# RoboTaxi Bench: independent variables, in boxes

*Variable list for the simulator and harness, 19 Oct 2026. 836 distinct variables in 19 boxes (189 high, 386 medium and 261 low impact). Built by 12 domain researchers, each checked by a separate fact-checker, then three gap critics (a veteran NYC driver, a simulation engineer, a robotaxi fleet operator), a gap-fill round with its own fact-check, and an editor who sorted everything into boxes. Full dossiers with sources and fact-check logs: `research/research_notes/RoboTaxi Bench variables/`. Spreadsheet version: `research/reports/RoboTaxi Bench variables.csv`.*

**How to read each item.** Bold name, one-line description and example values, then tags in this order:
- **Impact** on profit per hour: High, Med or Low.
- **Role:**
  - *World input*: set by the scenario or replayed from data, such as weather or demand.
  - *Rule/price*: fixed by the designer, such as fares, tolls and laws.
  - *Agent decision*: what the model chooses.
  - *Changes during run*: state such as battery level, fatigue or position.
  - *Benchmark setting*: experiment knobs.
- **Visibility:** whether the agent sees it, can get it from public information or forecasts, or whether it is hidden.
- *core MVP* marks the first-version set.

Under each item, the smaller line gives the evidence for the impact rating, the public data source and how to model it. Source tags: [P] primary source read directly, [P\*] primary source via a copy, [S] snippet or secondary only, [U] unverified, [design] our judgement.

Roles: 305 world inputs, 217 rules and prices, 56 agent decisions, 46 run-time states, 212 benchmark settings.

## The 30 biggest levers on earnings

| # | Variable | Box | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Base request rate by zone and hour | Riders and demand | Base request rate by zone and hour is the main input behind every dollar earned. |
| 2 | Pickup concentration (spatial skew) | Geography | Pickup concentration: 16 of 263 zones give half of yellow pickups, so where you wait decides earnings. |
| 3 | Destination choice matrix | Riders and demand | Destination matrix sets where each trip leaves the car, and so the value of the next trip and the risk of stranding. |
| 4 | Weekday hourly demand curve | Time of day | Weekday hourly demand curve (with R02-007 weekend) decides when working pays at all. |
| 5 | Competitor cars online by zone and hour | Competition and matching | Competitor cars online by zone and hour set the ping rate and the agent's share of demand. |
| 6 | Matching algorithm | Competition and matching | Matching algorithm decides which car gets each request, so positioning only pays through it. |
| 7 | Time-dependent link speed profile | Traffic and travel time | Time-dependent link speeds set trips per hour and how per-minute vs per-mile pay adds up. |
| 8 | NYC minimum pay: per-mile component | Fares and pay | NYC minimum pay per mile (with R07-026 per minute) is the floor that sets app driver revenue per trip. |
| 9 | Surge (dynamic pricing) | Fares and pay | Surge raises pay where supply is short; chasing or ignoring it is a major strategic lever. |
| 10 | Taxi metered rate of fare | Fares and pay | Taxi metered rate is the whole revenue formula for the taxi track. |
| 11 | Upfront offer information | Fares and pay | Upfront offer information decides whether accept/decline is an informed choice or a gamble. |
| 12 | Reposition to a target zone | Agent decisions | Repositioning to a target zone is the core geographic skill the benchmark measures. |
| 13 | Accept or decline an offer | Agent decisions | Accept or decline: the per-offer choice that compounds over hundreds of offers per day. |
| 14 | Airport queue length and position | Competition and matching | Airport queue state: airports are 7.9% of yellow trips but 22% of revenue, against 2-3 hour waits. |
| 15 | Airport trip share and value | Geography | Airport trip share and value: few, long, lucrative trips that dominate positioning trade-offs. |
| 16 | Rain demand uplift for app requests | Weather | Rain demand uplift is the largest routine weather effect on app requests. |
| 17 | Event catalogue for the scenario day | Events | Event catalogue drives the event spikes and the with/without public-info experiment. |
| 18 | Information level ladder | Information and data | Information ladder defines what the model may know and how the value of each layer is measured. |
| 19 | Public vs insider boundary rule | Information and data | Public vs insider boundary rule makes the comparison fair; break it and scores are meaningless. |
| 20 | Demand source mode | Information and data | Demand source mode (replay, resample, generator) sets realism, leakage risk and variance. |
| 21 | Supply elasticity setting | Competition and matching | Supply elasticity: whether rivals react to surge and to the agent changes every payoff. |
| 22 | Latent vs observed demand (censoring) | Information and data | Latent vs observed demand: trip records show only served trips, which biases the base rate low where it matters. |
| 23 | Modifier composition and saturation rule | Riders and demand | Modifier composition and saturation: how hour x weather x event multipliers stack can blow demand up or flatten it. |
| 24 | Powertrain type | Vehicle and energy | Powertrain (electric vs gas) sets energy cost per mile, refuelling downtime and licence eligibility. |
| 25 | Cost accounting basis for profit | Running costs | Cost accounting basis (marginal vs fixed costs) defines what 'profit' means in the score. |
| 26 | Wall-clock coupling and model latency | Benchmark settings | Wall-clock coupling: whether slow model answers cost sim time and missed offers. |
| 27 | Seeds and common random numbers | Benchmark settings | Seeds and common random numbers make profit differences reflect decisions rather than luck. |
| 28 | Shift end and total hours (daily, weekly) | Agent decisions | Shift end and total hours: when and how long to work is the biggest single lever on daily profit. |
| 29 | Fleet size N | Fleet | Fleet size N sets the fleet level, market impact and self-cannibalisation. |
| 30 | Idle-car repositioning decision | Fleet | Idle-car repositioning is the fleet positioning strategy the team wants to test. |

## Your items and where they live

- **Geography: road vs foot-traffic density ('pickup-ability')**: Road-to-pedestrian ratio ('pickup-ability'); Pedestrian volume by location; Pedestrian demand class of each street; Subway entries and exits by hour; Hail visibility by street type; Cruising route for street hails; Street-hail encounters and hail rights; Curb availability and stopping search
- **Time of day: average time of having lunch**: Lunch hour (rider demand); Meal breaks: timing and duration; Take a break (when, what, where); Driver meal, rest and prayer break timing; Dinner and evening out; Restrooms, food and break spots; Competitor breaks
- **Events with vs without public info (e.g. Coachella)**: Event information level (experiment arm); Event-awareness prompt arm; Public event calendar content; Information level ladder; Public event data feeds (agent tools); Information latency for unplanned events; Forecast error and false alarms; Memorisation guard for real events; Information-value metric; Event-window scoring metrics; Out-of-NYC event scenario (Coachella-style)
- **'Take me home' feature**: Go home and use the destination filter; Destination filter quota and mechanics; Value of ending near home ('take me home'); Driver home location; Commute deadhead at shift start and end; Distance and time to home; Trips heading toward the driver's home; Off-duty 'returning home' pickup exception; End-of-episode valuation and go-home rule; Destination filter (take me home); App controls: pause, last trip, auto-accept, filter to any place
- **Electric vs gas**: Powertrain type; Vehicle choice and fleet mix; Battery capacity and usable range; Fuel level or state of charge; Energy use per mile by speed (drive cycle); Gas refuelling stop; Gasoline retail price (NYC); Home electricity price; Public DC fast-charging price; Cold- and hot-weather EV range and charging loss; Home or overnight charging access; Refuel or charge (when and where)
- **Fuel and maintenance costs**: Gasoline retail price (NYC); Station-level price spread and fuel detours; Home electricity price; Public DC fast-charging price; Public Level 2 and curbside charging price; Routine maintenance per mile; Tyre wear and replacement; Brake wear; Simplified all-in per-mile cost (calibration); Depreciation per mile and per year; Cost accounting basis for profit; Refuel or charge (when and where)
- **Fleet of N cars: positioning strategy**: Fleet size N; Idle-car repositioning decision; Rebalancing objective and target distribution; Repositioning reach cap; Airport allocation across the fleet; Event pre-positioning; Anti-clustering rule; Territory partition; Starting positions; Operator-chosen service area and request admission control; Agent fleet share of total supply; Fleet saturation curve (value of the N-th car)
- **No insider info: only public data counts**: Public vs insider boundary rule; Information level ladder; As-of timestamp discipline; Historical trip aggregates given; Weather forecast as issued; Agent-visible history consistent with the sim's demand; Driver information networks; Memorisation guard for real events; Date and identity masking; Operations-research planner baseline

## The boxes

### 1. Geography (56)
*Where things are: zones, roads, foot traffic, hubs, curbs and the places that pull riders in or strand the car.*

* **Zones, boundaries and resolution**
   * **Green taxi hail exclusionary zone**: Green (boro) taxis may not accept street hails or e-hails in Manhattan south of E 96th St and W 110th St or at JFK and LGA, and may not begin base-dispatched (pre-arranged) trips in that part of Manhattan; they may drop off anywhere. [corrected by fact-check: the rule also bans pre-arranged starts in Manhattan south of E 96th / W 110th, and an e-hail counts as a hail] *Values:* Excluded: 55 Yellow Zone zones + 2 airports. Green Oct 2019 pickups: 64.6% outer boroughs, 32.3% upper Manhattan, 3.1% Yellow Zone; 18.5% of drop-offs in Yellow Zone; fine $500 plus suspension for a first violation. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi (green)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 51-03 (Hail Exclusionary Zone and Pre-Arranged Exclusionary Zone definitions), 82-13 and 80-20(b)(11), read from a verbatim copy current through 13 Aug 2026; [P] own computation, green Oct 2019 (473,824 trips, reproduced) · Data: 35 RCNY 51-03 and 82-13 (verbatim copy https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive); TLC green cab page (https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/businesses/green-cab.page); TLC green trip records · Model: Street-hail and e-hail generation disabled for green cars inside the excluded polygons; base-dispatched trips may not start in excluded Manhattan but may start at JFK/LGA [corrected by fact-check: the original said e-hail was still allowed] · [R01-006]</sub>
   * **Pickup concentration (spatial skew)**: How unevenly trip origins are spread across zones; the more concentrated, the more location choice matters. *Values:* Yellow Oct 2019: 16 zones = 50% of pickups, 32 = 80%, 48 = 95%; HVFHV Jun 2021: 60 / 125 / 188 zones. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own computation on TLC yellow (Oct 2019, 7.08M trips) and HVFHV (Jun 2021, 14.96M trips) records · Data: TLC trip records (yellow, green, FHV, HVFHV) · Model: Demand generator samples origins from a zone x hour intensity fitted to trip records; historic heatmap can be given to the agent as public info · [R01-003]</sub>
   * **Service-zone class**: Each zone belongs to a TLC service class that decides who may street-hail there and which fare rules apply. *Values:* Yellow Zone 55 (Manhattan core) / Boro Zone 205 (14 upper Manhattan + 191 outer borough) / Airports 2 (JFK 132, LGA 138) / EWR 1. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · all; decisive for taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Lookup counts; 89.0% of yellow pickups were in the 55 Yellow Zone zones (Oct 2019, own computation) · Data: Same as R01-001 (service_zone column) · Model: Zone attribute checked by hail-eligibility and fare rules · [R01-002]</sub>
   * **Taxi vs app geographic footprint**: Street-hail demand sits in the Manhattan core while app demand is spread over the outer boroughs, so the same map rewards different positioning by driver type. *Values:* Share of pickups in Yellow Zone: yellow 89.0% vs HVFHV 32.2%; outer boroughs: 2.2% vs 59.5%. Manhattan HV trips fell 6.1% Jan 2025 to Jan 2026 while other boroughs grew. `High · World input · public/forecast · taxi, human app driver, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own computation (yellow Oct 2019 vs HVFHV Jun 2021); [S] TLC license pause report Feb 2026 [uncertain: the -6.1% Manhattan figure from the Feb 2026 licence pause report was not re-checked; nyc.gov blocked] · Data: TLC trip records; TLC license pause reports (https://www.nyc.gov/assets/tlc/downloads/pdf/license-pause-report-2026-02.pdf) · Model: Separate demand layers per channel (street hail, e-hail) with their own zone weights · [R01-005]</sub>
   * **Zone adjacency and travel-time skim**: A zone-to-zone neighbour list and travel-time matrix used for repositioning and fleet rebalancing. *Values:* 263 x 263 matrix of travel times by hour (minutes); neighbour lists. `High · World input · agent sees · core MVP · all; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] Needed by both single-car repositioning and fleet rebalancing; R10 supplies the times · Data: Derived from OSM graph + TLC trip durations; NYC DOT traffic speeds (cross-link R10) · Model: Precompute skims per hour band; agent tools query ETA between zones · [R01-010]</sub>
   * **Zone pickup/drop-off imbalance**: Ratio of pickups to drop-offs per zone; zones well below 1 are 'sinks' where a driver lands with few nearby requests. *Values:* Yellow Oct 2019 PU/DO: Manhattan 1.02, Queens 1.30, Brooklyn 0.27, Bronx 0.24, Staten Island 0.14; zone extremes JFK 3.81, Windsor Terrace 0.08, Washington Heights North 0.09 [corrected by fact-check: Windsor Terrace, not Washington Heights North, is the lowest zone with >3,000 drop-offs; JFK is 3.53 under a lighter cleaning filter that keeps zero-distance trips]. HVFHV Jun 2021: every borough 1.02-1.06. `High · World input · public/forecast · all; strongest for taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own computation, TLC yellow Oct 2019 and HVFHV Jun 2021 (reproduced by fact-check) · Data: TLC trip records · Model: Emerges from the OD matrix; expose historic PU/DO ratio per zone x hour as a public layer · [R01-004]</sub>
   * **Licensing jurisdiction boundary**: The area where the vehicle's licence allows pickups (NYC for TLC vehicles); trips may end outside it, but pickups outside are effectively unavailable. *Values:* Inside five boroughs: pickups allowed. Outside: drop-off only. EWR: yellow 57 pickups vs 15,829 drop-offs (Oct 2019); HVFHV 228 vs 63,278 (Jun 2021). Uber JFK pickups only for TLC-licensed drivers. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own counts from TLC records; [S] Uber JFK driver-information page · Data: TLC trip records; Uber airport driver pages; county taxi rules [U] · Model: Pickup-eligibility mask by polygon and licence type · [R01-007]</sub>
   * **Spatial resolution**: The unit at which positions and demand are represented: taxi zone, hex grid or road segment. *Values:* Taxi zone (~3 km2 mean, 263 units) / H3 hex res 8 (~0.7 km2) / OSM drivable road graph (tens of thousands of nodes). `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] Finer resolution lets positioning skill show but costs compute and data; zone level matches public trip data · Data: TLC zones; OpenStreetMap (https://www.openstreetmap.org); NYC DCP LION · Model: Start at zone level with a random point inside the zone; upgrade to a road graph for routing, curbs and hails · [R01-009]</sub>
   * **Taxi zone partition**: The city is split into the TLC's 263 taxi zones (plus 2 unknown codes), the spatial unit of every public trip record. *Values:* 263 zones: Manhattan 69, Queens 69, Brooklyn 61, Bronx 43, Staten Island 20, EWR 1; IDs 264/265 = unknown / outside NYC [corrected by fact-check: in the current TLC lookup 264 = Unknown and 265 = 'Outside of NYC'; older copies label both Unknown (NV / NA)]. `Med · World input · agent sees · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Counted 265 rows in taxi_zone_lookup.csv (GitHub copy of the TLC file; borough and service-zone counts reproduced); all trip data is keyed to these IDs; [S] dataset ID 755u-8jsi confirmed by many GitHub pipelines that download it [corrected by fact-check: impact high -> medium; the partition is a structural layer whose profit effect flows through R01-003, R01-004 and R01-009] · Data: NYC Taxi Zones, NYC Open Data 755u-8jsi (https://data.cityofnewyork.us/d/755u-8jsi); TLC lookup + shapefile (https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page) · Model: Load zone polygons; map every car and request position to a zone ID; demand, fares and stats keyed by zone · [R01-001]</sub>
* **Road network and chokepoints**
   * **One-way streets and circuity**: Manhattan's mostly one-way grid and the rivers make driving distance longer than straight-line distance, and a pickup on the wrong side costs a loop. *Values:* Most avenues and most cross streets one-way [corrected by fact-check: some avenues are two-way, e.g. Park Ave, Central Park West, West End Ave, York Ave [U]]; major two-way crosstown streets (14th, 23rd, 34th, 42nd, 57th, 72nd, 79th, 86th, 96th, 110th, 125th); circuity ~1.2-1.4; turn-around loop ~2-4 blocks. `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Background knowledge of the NYC grid; LION has a traffic-direction field · Data: NYC DCP LION (TrafDir); OSM oneway tag · Model: Directed graph; car heading matters for pickup ETA · [R01-013]</sub>
   * **River crossings as chokepoints**: Manhattan and Staten Island are islands and the Bronx is cut off from Manhattan and Queens by water, so most links between boroughs and all links to New Jersey are bridges or tunnels; they concentrate delay, incidents and tolls. [corrected by fact-check: Brooklyn and Queens share a land border and the Bronx joins Westchester by land, so bridges and tunnels are not the only links] *Values:* East River: Brooklyn, Manhattan, Williamsburg, Queensboro bridges (free), Queens-Midtown and Hugh Carey tunnels and RFK (tolled); Hudson: GWB, Lincoln, Holland (PANYNJ); Harlem River bridges free except the MTA Henry Hudson Bridge (tolled); Verrazzano, Throgs Neck, Whitestone, Cross Bay, Marine Pkwy (MTA, tolled); Staten Island to NJ: Goethals, Bayonne, Outerbridge (PANYNJ, tolled) [corrected by fact-check: added the Henry Hudson Bridge and the Staten Island-NJ crossings [U]]. `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] 5.9% of yellow trips paid a toll, mean $6.63 (Oct 2019, own computation, reproduced); [U] network facts [corrected by fact-check: impact high -> medium; tolls are mostly passed to riders and crossing delay is modelled in R10] · Data: OpenStreetMap; NYC DOT bridge traffic volumes [U] · Model: Crossing edges with their own capacity, incident probability (R03) and travel-time variance (R10) · [R01-019]</sub>
   * **Road hierarchy class**: Each road segment's class (expressway or parkway, arterial avenue, collector, local) sets its speed, capacity and how good it is for finding hails. *Values:* OSM highway = motorway / trunk / primary / secondary / tertiary / residential. Mean in-trip speed: ~10.2 mph for Yellow Zone pickups vs ~20.9 mph for airport pickups (yellow Oct 2019). `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own computation (distance / duration) from TLC yellow Oct 2019 · Data: OpenStreetMap; NYC DCP LION street centerline (lanes, width, direction) · Model: Graph edges carry class: free-flow speed and capacity; congestion handled in R10 · [R01-012]</sub>
   * **Busways and transit-priority streets**: Streets where through car traffic is banned for set hours, but taxis and FHVs may enter for local pickups and must turn off at the next street. *Values:* 14th St busway (3rd to 9th Ave, 6am-10pm daily; cars may enter to pick up or drop off and must turn right within a block or two, no left turns) [corrected by fact-check: the original said 3rd to 8th Ave]; others such as Main St Flushing, Jamaica Ave, Archer Ave, 181st St [U]; 34th St bus priority (2025). `Low · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 34 RCNY 4-12(m)(2): only buses and Access-A-Ride vans during restricted hours except where signs allow; [S] NYT (Oct 2019, GitHub copy) and Village Sun / amNY on 14th St busway rules; NYC DOT 34th St documents · Data: NYC DOT busways page (https://www.nyc.gov/html/brt/html/busways/busways.shtml) · Model: Edges with through-traffic ban + forced turn after a stop · [R01-028]</sub>
   * **Car-free park drives and plazas**: Roads closed to cars inside demand areas, so pickups move to the edges and routes detour. *Values:* Central Park loop drives car-free since 27 Jun 2018 (transverses open); Prospect Park loop car-free; Times Square / Broadway plazas; Open Streets time windows. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] ABC7, Central Park Conservancy (2018) · Data: OSM; NYC DOT Open Streets data [U] · Model: Remove edges (permanently or by time); snap pickups to the nearest legal curb · [R01-029]</sub>
   * **Elevation and grade**: Hills slightly raise energy use; NYC is mostly flat with steep spots (Washington Heights, Staten Island, parts of the Bronx). *Values:* Grade 0-10%+; elevation 0 to ~125 m. `Low · World input · agent sees · all; EV range`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Background knowledge · Data: USGS 3DEP; NYC 1-ft DEM on NYC Open Data [U] · Model: Edge grade feeds an energy multiplier in R08 · [R01-018]</sub>
   * **Gated and single-access destinations**: Destinations behind gates or ID checks, or reachable by only one road, add a security wait or a long detour that a zone-level map misses. The examples are NYC places; other cities can be generalised from OSM tags. *Values:* Brooklyn Navy Yard (gated); Fort Hamilton (military ID check); Rikers Island (single bridge, security); Breezy Point (gated private cooperative); Roosevelt Island (cars only via the bridge from Queens); City Island (single bridge); JFK cargo areas. Gate delay 2-15 min. Detour up to 10-20 min, e.g. reaching Roosevelt Island from Manhattan [design].. `Low · World input · agent sees · all; robotaxi may be barred (ODD)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Local geography; [design]. Could be measured from OSM barrier and access tags. · Data: OpenStreetMap (barrier=gate, access=private/permissive) https://www.openstreetmap.org · Model: Gate nodes with a delay distribution and an access class per vehicle type. Single-access zones are routed only through their one link. · [G1-015]</sub>
   * **Road access by vehicle class**: Some roads are closed to some vehicles: parkways ban commercial vehicles (taxis and FHVs count as passenger cars) and robotaxis may be kept off freeways. *Values:* Parkway allowed yes/no; robotaxi freeway allowed yes/no (scenario). `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi; all`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Background knowledge; medium for robotaxi if freeways are excluded · Data: OSM access tags; NYSDOT parkway rules [U] · Model: Edge mask per vehicle class · [R01-017]</sub>
* **Foot traffic vs road density**
   * **Pedestrian volume by location**: How many people walk past a point; drives street-hail chances and is a proxy for latent ride demand. *Values:* DOT counts at 114 locations (100 on-street, mainly retail corridors; 13 bridges; Hudson River Greenway), counted each May and September. `High · World input · public/forecast · taxi (hails); all`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NYC Open Data dataset description (confirmed via GitHub copies: 114 locations, 100 on-street mostly retail corridors, 13 East River and Harlem River bridge points, Hudson River Greenway); Buchholz (2022, REStud): taxis are drawn to search in the most concentrated pedestrian areas [uncertain: this paraphrase of Buchholz was not checked against the paper] · Data: NYC DOT Bi-Annual Pedestrian Counts (https://data.cityofnewyork.us/d/2de2-6x2h) · Model: Street-hail arrival rate per edge proportional to pedestrian intensity x hour profile, calibrated to yellow pickups · [R01-034]</sub>
   * **Population and job density**: Residents and workers per zone are the base of trip generation. *Values:* Residents/km2 and jobs/km2 per zone. `High · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Standard trip-generation practice; Correa, Chow and Ozbay model demand from the spatial distribution of activities [S] · Data: US Census ACS 5-year (https://data.census.gov); LEHD LODES (https://lehd.ces.census.gov/data/) · Model: Demand-generator covariates when extrapolating beyond replayed data · [R01-039]</sub>
   * **Hail visibility by street type**: Street hails happen mostly on avenues and busy corridors that cabs pass often; side streets yield few hails. *Values:* Hail multiplier: avenue 1.0, cross street 0.2-0.5 [design guess]. `Med · World input · hidden · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] / [U]; yellow records carry exact coordinates only until June 2016 (zone IDs from July 2016) [S] toddwschneider/nyc-taxi-data README · Data: Pre-2016 TLC yellow records with lat/lon · Model: Generate hails per edge; a cruising agent only sees hails on edges it drives · [R01-040]</sub>
   * **Pedestrian demand class of each street**: NYC DOT's Pedestrian Mobility Plan puts every street in one of five pedestrian-demand classes. *Values:* Baseline (~60% of roads), Community Connector (~25%) [uncertain: shares not re-checked], Neighborhood Corridor, Regional Corridor, Global Corridor (mostly Manhattan). `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NYC DOT Pedestrian Mobility Plan and data.gov dataset (modified Mar 2026); class names confirmed in GitHub uses of the Pedestrian_Demand file; [P*] 34 RCNY 5-11 uses the plan's corridor categories; MIT critique that some outer-borough streets are under-classified · Data: Pedestrian Mobility Plan Pedestrian Demand (https://catalog.data.gov/dataset/pedestrian-mobility-plan-pedestrian-demand) · Model: Categorical edge attribute feeding hail rate and curb friction · [R01-035]</sub>
   * **Road-to-pedestrian ratio ('pickup-ability')**: The user's idea: compare how many pedestrians gather on a block with how much drivable road and stoppable curb it has; busy but curb-starved streets mean many riders but slow, congested pickups. *Values:* Index = legal-stop curb metres / pedestrians per hour (or lanes / pedestrian class); bands low / medium / high; low examples Times Sq, SoHo. `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; Buchholz (2022): competition pushes taxis to spread search beyond the densest pedestrian areas [S] [uncertain: paraphrase not checked against the paper] · Data: DOT pedestrian counts + Pedestrian Mobility Plan + LION width/lanes + DOT parking signs · Model: Per-edge index raises hail rate (+) and pickup dwell / lowers through-speed (-) · [R01-037]</sub>
   * **Subway entries and exits by hour**: Hourly subway ridership per station shows where crowds appear and when transit thins out, shaping first/last-mile and substitute demand. *Values:* Hourly counts by station complex: 2017-19 (t69i-h2me), 2020-24 (wujg-7c2s), 2025+ (5wq4-mkjj); OD estimates (jsu2-fbtj) [uncertain: one GitHub project reports wujg-7c2s data starting in Feb 2022; t69i-h2me and jsu2-fbtj exist in the MTA catalog but their titles were not confirmed]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] data.ny.gov catalog entries; IDs 5wq4-mkjj (2025+) and wujg-7c2s (2020-24) confirmed by several GitHub pipelines · Data: MTA Subway Hourly Ridership (https://data.ny.gov/d/5wq4-mkjj) · Model: Demand covariate; subway outages (R03) move demand to cars near affected stations · [R01-036]</sub>
   * **Transit accessibility**: Distance from a zone to the nearest subway station; areas far from rail rely more on cars for hire. *Values:* 0-3+ km to nearest station; transit-desert flag. `Med · World input · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Top HVFHV pickup zones include East New York, Crown Heights North, Bushwick South (Jun 2021); [U] transit-substitution mechanism · Data: MTA GTFS static (https://new.mta.info/developers); NYC subway entrances (NYC Open Data) · Model: Demand covariate; raises late-night demand when subway headways lengthen · [R01-038]</sub>
* **Land use, trip generators and neighbourhood character**
   * **Land-use mix per zone**: Share of residential, office, retail, nightlife, industrial and park land; sets the daily shape of demand. *Values:* MapPLUTO land-use classes (11) by lot area per zone. `High · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Share of core yellow pickups ending in outer boroughs: 1.4% at 8am vs 17.7% at 4am (Oct 2019), showing home-bound night flows · Data: NYC DCP MapPLUTO (https://www.nyc.gov/site/planning/data-maps/open-data/dwn-pluto-mappluto.page) · Model: Zone feature vector; demand time profile depends on land-use mix · [R01-042]</sub>
   * **Neighbourhood income**: Median household income of pickup and drop-off areas, which tracks card use, trip length and tipping. *Values:* ACS median income per tract mapped to zone; card-trip tip/fare 25.9% for Yellow Zone pickups vs 19.3% outer boroughs; lowest drop-off zones 18.8-19.2% (Stuyvesant Heights, Central Harlem North, Crown Heights North) vs ~27% (UES, UWS, West Village). `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own computation, yellow Oct 2019 card trips (tips are recorded only for card payments); reproduced, and robust alternatives give the same gap (tip/fare ratio of sums 23.9% vs 17.9%; medians 26.3% vs 22.2%) · Data: US Census ACS; TLC tips (yellow card trips; HVFHV tips column) · Model: Zone covariate for tip probability and size (behaviour owned by R05) · [R01-067]</sub>
   * **Nightlife density**: Bars, clubs and late restaurants create late-night demand and longer, outbound trips. *Values:* On-premises liquor licences per zone; key areas LES, East Village, Meatpacking, Williamsburg, Bushwick. `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] East Village is the #2 HVFHV pickup zone (Jun 2021); Meatpacking among the highest-tip drop-off zones (27%) · Data: NYS Liquor Authority licences (data.ny.gov) [U]; DOHMH restaurant inspections [U] · Model: Late-night demand multiplier; destinations drawn toward residential zones · [R01-044]</sub>
   * **Office and business districts**: Midtown, Financial District, Hudson Yards, Downtown Brooklyn and Long Island City pull weekday daytime trips. *Values:* Jobs per zone; weekday vs weekend ratio. `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Midtown Center 4.2% and Midtown East 3.7% of all yellow pickups (Oct 2019) · Data: LEHD LODES; MapPLUTO office area · Model: Work-hour peaks per zone (remote-work days in R02) · [R01-043]</sub>
   * **Tourist and hotel density**: Hotels and attractions create airport trips, taxi-stand queues and riders less likely to use transit. *Values:* Hotel rooms per zone; attraction POIs. `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Times Sq/Theatre District = 3.5% of yellow pickups (Oct 2019) · Data: OSM tourism tags; NYC hotel lists on NYC Open Data [U] · Model: Hotel POIs as taxi-stand queues and airport-bound demand · [R01-045]</sub>
   * **Venues, stadiums and convention centres**: Fixed big venues whose location, capacity and exits shape event surges (the events themselves are in R03). *Values:* Capacity 2k-50k (MSG, Barclays, Yankee Stadium, Citi Field, USTA, UBS Arena, Javits, Lincoln Center); exit curb capacity. `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Background knowledge; MSG sits on Penn Station (zone 186, top-5 yellow pickup zone) [P] · Data: OSM; venue websites · Model: Venue nodes with capacity; R03 schedules events on them · [R01-047]</sub>
   * **Hospitals, universities and institutions**: Large hospitals produce steady trips around shift changes and discharges (plus paratransit rides); campuses shift demand with the academic calendar. *Values:* Beds per site; shift changes ~7am / 7pm [U]; enrolment per campus, term on/off. `Low · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Background knowledge; [P*] HVFHV records carry access_a_ride_flag (confirmed in the Jan 2023 schema) · Data: NYS DOH health facility data (health.data.ny.gov) [U]; NCES IPEDS (campuses) · Model: POI with a daily time profile and seasonal multiplier (R02) · [R01-046]</sub>
   * **Payment mix by zone**: Share of cash vs card trips by area; affects recorded tips and cash handling for taxis (app trips are cashless). *Values:* Card share, yellow Oct 2019: Yellow Zone 74.2%, upper Manhattan 58.8%, outer boroughs 51.7%, airports 64.7%. `Low · World input · hidden · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own computation · Data: TLC yellow payment_type · Model: Per-zone payment-type probability · [R01-068]</sub>
   * **Perceived safety and crime**: Crime and drivers' sense of risk make some drivers avoid areas at night, thinning supply there and raising surge for those who stay, with a small real incident risk. *Values:* Complaints per 1,000 residents by precinct and hour; avoidance probability 0-1 [design]. `Low · World input · public/forecast · human app driver, taxi; robotaxi (vandalism)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] / [design]; benchmark must not reward illegal refusal · Data: NYPD Complaint Data (NYC Open Data) · Model: Lower competitor supply in high-perceived-risk zones at night; small incident probability · [R01-069]</sub>
   * **Retail and shopping corridors**: Shopping streets create daytime and weekend short trips. *Values:* Retail floor area per zone. `Low · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Background knowledge; most DOT pedestrian count sites are retail corridors [S] · Data: MapPLUTO retail area; DOT pedestrian counts · Model: Daytime / weekend demand covariate · [R01-048]</sub>
* **Airports, stations and other hubs**
   * **Airport trip share and value**: Airport trips are few but long and lucrative. *Values:* Yellow Oct 2019: 7.9% of trips, 22.2% of driver revenue (fare + extras + tips); JFK pickup mean $51.19 over 15.3 mi, LGA $37.81 over 9.1 mi. HVFHV Jun 2021: 5.4% of trips touch an airport. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own computation, TLC yellow Oct 2019 and HVFHV Jun 2021 · Data: TLC trip records · Model: Airport zones with their own demand profile tied to flight schedules (R03/R12) · [R01-050]</sub>
   * **Intercity rail and bus hubs**: Penn Station/Moynihan, Grand Central, Port Authority Bus Terminal, Atlantic Terminal and Jamaica produce rider pulses timed to public train and bus arrivals. *Values:* Penn Station/Madison Sq West = 3.6% of yellow pickups, PU/DO 1.40 (Oct 2019); arrival schedules public in GTFS. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own computation, TLC yellow Oct 2019 · Data: GTFS feeds (MTA, NJ Transit, Amtrak); TLC records · Model: Hub nodes with arrival-driven pulses + taxi-stand queues · [R01-049]</sub>
   * **Airport pickup/drop-off asymmetry**: For yellow cabs airport pickups far exceed drop-offs, while app trips are roughly balanced. *Values:* Yellow PU/DO: JFK 3.81, LGA 2.06 (Oct 2019); HVFHV: JFK 0.84, LGA 0.88 (Jun 2021). `Med · World input · public/forecast · taxi, human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own computation · Data: TLC trip records · Model: Emerges from the OD matrix; decides whether an airport drop-off can be followed by an airport pickup · [R01-051]</sub>
   * **Cruise-ship terminal days**: On ship days, thousands of passengers with luggage leave the Manhattan (Piers 88-90) and Brooklyn (Red Hook) cruise terminals in a morning wave, with a high airport share. Generalises to any cruise port (Miami, Seattle) through a ship-call calendar. *Values:* 2024: about 1.5M passengers and 238 ship calls across both terminals. Manhattan had 159 calls (down from 183 in 2023) and about 1M passengers, which leaves about 79 calls for Brooklyn. Manhattan can berth two ships of up to about 4,000 passengers at once. About 2-6k passengers disembark per ship, mostly 07:00-11:00 [U]. Red Hook (zone 195), yellow pickups 07-11h: median 51 on 53 'ship-like' days (>= 30 pickups, Jan 2023-Jan 2024) vs 1 on other days. Ship-like days are mostly in Jul-Dec (6-9 a month) and mostly on Saturdays and Sundays. The threshold catches only part of the calls. The Manhattan terminal sits in Clinton West (zone 50), where other demand hides the signal (07-11h median 170 pickups, no visible ship pattern).. `Med · World input · public/forecast · all (taxi stand at the terminals, app pickups, robotaxi if in ODD); fleet pre-positioning`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own calc on TLC yellow Jan 2023-Jan 2024 pickups by zone-hour (g1/a3.py, a5.py), re-run in this fact-check. [corrected: 'peak Jul-Oct' -> spread over Jul-Dec, mostly weekends]. [S] NYCEDC Cruise NYC page, Crain's (2024 record year) and an EDC Community Board 4 deck (Jan 2026) for passenger, call and berth counts. Impact is medium on ship-day mornings for a car near a terminal, and low citywide. · Data: NYCEDC Cruise NYC https://edc.nyc/project/cruise-nyc ; WorldShipNY arrivals table https://worldshipny.com [U] ; CruiseMapper port schedule https://www.cruisemapper.com/ports/new-york-port-98 [U] ; TLC trip records (zones 50 and 195) · Model: One event object per ship call (terminal, ship capacity, arrival time). Each creates a disembarkation pulse 07:00-11:00 with an airport-heavy destination mix and a high XL share [design]. Pulse size is fitted from zone-195 morning residuals. · [G1-003]</sub>
   * **Newark Airport (EWR) special status**: EWR is in New Jersey: NYC drivers drop off there but almost never pick up, so each EWR drop-off implies an empty return. [corrected by fact-check: for taxis the passenger pays a $20 Newark surcharge and tolls both ways (35 RCNY 58-26(c)(3)), so the return toll is reimbursed; app drivers are paid the out-of-town rate] *Values:* Yellow: 15,829 drop-offs vs 57 pickups (Oct 2019; 829 pickups under a lighter filter, mostly zero-distance records), rate code 3 = 0.24% of trips; HVFHV: 63,278 vs 228 (Jun 2021); return toll $14.79-16.79 E-ZPass; a taxi may refuse an EWR trip after 8 h of operation. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own counts (reproduced); [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(c)(3), 80-20(b)(8); [S] PANYNJ 2026 tolls · Data: TLC records; PANYNJ · Model: EWR zone = drop-off only for NYC-licensed cars; forced deadhead; inbound toll paid by the driver for app cars and reimbursed by the passenger for taxis · [R01-056]</sub>
   * **Airport terminal layout and pickup rules**: Where at the airport pickups are allowed (terminal curbs, FHV zones, remote lots), adding minutes and confusion. *Values:* JFK: FHV pickups barred at Terminals 5 and 7 'until further notice' [uncertain: from 2021-era driver guides; may be outdated], with Howard Beach FHV Hold Lot 3; arrivals-level pickups only; JFK rebuilding (New Terminal One) changes roads [U]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · all; robotaxi airport access needs Port Authority approval [U]`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber JFK driver page (French version); Inshur driver guide (2021) · Data: PANYNJ ground transport pages; Uber airport driver pages · Model: Airport sub-graph with pickup nodes per terminal and extra walk/drive time · [R01-055]</sub>
   * **Ferry terminals and heliports**: Staten Island Ferry terminals, NYC Ferry and NY Waterway landings, and Manhattan heliports produce small, timed pulses of pickup demand. *Values:* The Staten Island Ferry runs 24/7, every 15-20 min at rush hours and at least every 30 min at other times (2015 city policy; cut to hourly during COVID, later restored to half-hourly) [S; corrected by fact-check, was 'every 15-60 min [U]']. NYC Ferry hourly boardings by landing are public [S]. Heliports: Downtown Manhattan (Pier 6), E 34th St, W 30th St [U]. Each arrival yields from a few to tens of pickups [design].. `Low · World input · public/forecast · all (taxi via hails and the stand at Whitehall)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] The NYC Ferry Ridership dataset (t5n6-gx8c) gives hourly boardings by date, route, direction and landing. [S] Staten Island Ferry Ridership Counts (6eng-46dm) give daily riders at Whitehall and St. George. Whitehall late at night is a known taxi spot [U]. · Data: NYC Ferry Ridership, NYC Open Data t5n6-gx8c https://data.cityofnewyork.us/d/t5n6-gx8c; Staten Island Ferry Ridership Counts, NYC Open Data 6eng-46dm (daily; trip-level counts only in an NYC DOT folder) [S, corrected: was 'none found']; NYC Ferry and Staten Island Ferry GTFS [U] · Model: Treat ferry landings and heliports as timed trip generators like rail hubs (R01-049): arrivals x riders per boat x car share, released 2-8 min after docking. Split Staten Island Ferry daily counts over boats with the schedule. · [G2-009]</sub>
* **Curbs, stands and legal waiting spots**
   * **Curb regulation at the stop point**: The sign type at the curb decides whether a quick pickup or drop-off is legal. *Values:* No Parking (stop OK) / No Standing (quick passenger pickup OK, no waiting) / No Stopping (not allowed) / bus stop (OK if no bus is blocked) / taxi stand (taxis only; other cars may only briefly load, and TLC fines an FHV driver $350 for a first pickup at a taxi stand) / 'For-Hire Vehicles Only' curb ($115 if standing beyond a quick pickup [uncertain: fine amount not checked]) [corrected by fact-check: added the taxi-stand pickup ban for FHVs]. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · all; robotaxi must comply exactly`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 34 RCNY 4-11(c): taxis and FHVs may briefly stop to pick up or drop off where standing or parking is banned, within 12 in of the curb, but not where stopping is banned, in a bike lane or in a crosswalk; 34 RCNY 4-08(c)(1); 35 RCNY 80-19(d)(2); [S] NYC DOT parking sign legend; NYC DOF violation codes · Data: NYC DOT Parking Regulation Locations and Signs (NYC Open Data) [U] · Model: Curb-segment attribute; illegal stop = fine probability or forced re-loop · [R01-025]</sub>
   * **Idle staging capacity**: Number of legal places in a zone where idle fleet cars can wait without circling, double parking or paying a garage. *Values:* 0-50 cars per zone [design]; garage cost per hour; robotaxis need a legal stop.. `Med · World input · public/forecast · fleet, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; legal waiting spots (R01-032) and hotspot curb queues (R10-040). · Data: NYC parking regulations and licensed garages on NYC Open Data [U] · Model: Staging capacity per zone; extra idle cars must circle (miles) or use a paid lot. · [R11-023]</sub>
   * **Legal waiting spots (staging)**: Whether the car can legally sit still near a hotspot while waiting for the next request instead of cruising. *Values:* Share of curb where standing is legal per zone; parking $0-15+/h [U]; NYC anti-idling limit 3 min (1 min next to schools); a taxi awaiting a fare may stand only at a taxi stand (or at a hydrant with the driver in the seat); non-taxis may not cruise for passengers [corrected by fact-check: idling limits confirmed and staging rules added]. `Med · World input · agent sees · all; robotaxi needs staging lots`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] NYC Admin Code 24-163(a), (f); 34 RCNY 4-11(a)-(b); [S] Schaller (2017): in the Manhattan CBD, app cars had 55K passenger hours vs 37K empty hours (60% busy), and utilisation fell from 67% (taxis, 2013) to 62% (taxis and apps, 2017) [uncertain: the '11 min vs 8 min empty between trips' figures were not found in the checked excerpts] · Data: NYC DOT parking regulations; licensed garages on NYC Open Data [U] · Model: Per-zone wait cost (fuel, ticket risk, parking fee) vs cruise cost; agent chooses wait, cruise or reposition · [R01-032]</sub>
   * **Taxi stands and hotel queues**: Designated stands (hotels, transit hubs) where only taxis may wait in line for passengers. *Values:* Stand present yes/no; queue length 0-30 cabs [U]; the first two cabs must stay in the driver's seat; green cabs may use stands only in the Hail Zone. `Med · World input · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 34 RCNY 4-08(c)(1): only taxis may stand at a taxi stand; 35 RCNY 80-19(d): queue rules and green-cab limits; [S] NYC DOT sign legend · Data: NYC DCP taxi-stand study (https://www.nyc.gov/assets/planning/download/pdf/plans/transportation/td_taxistands.pdf) · Model: FIFO queue node fed by hotel or hub demand · [R01-030]</sub>
   * **Doorman and hotel-starter relationships**: Doormen and hotel starters may steer the best fares, such as airport runs, to cabs or black cars they know, sometimes for a tip. *Values:* 0-20% of hotel-stand airport fares steered to 'regular' drivers [design]; $5-20 tip to the starter [U].. `Low · World input · hidden · taxi; human black-car driver (not app pool, not robotaxi)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Industry lore and press; no data found. · Data: none found · Model: At hotel stands (R05-044), a 'regular' state, built by repeat visits and tips, raises the chance of getting a long fare. · [G1-018]</sub>
   * **Taxi/FHV relief stands**: Curb spaces where taxi and FHV drivers may park for up to one hour to take a break. *Values:* 113 stands: Manhattan 70, Queens 28, Bronx 12, Brooklyn 3, Staten Island 0 [uncertain: count not re-checked]; one-hour limit. `Low · World input · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 34 RCNY 4-08(c)(2): taxis and FHVs may park at a relief stand for no more than one hour; [S] Manhattan CB1 letter (2026) citing NYC DOT; NYC DOT relief-stand page · Data: NYC Open Data 'Taxi and For-Hire Vehicle (FHV) Relief Stands' (https://catalog.data.gov/dataset/taxi-and-for-hire-vehicle-fhv-relief-stands) · Model: POI layer; a break elsewhere costs parking search time or ticket risk · [R01-031]</sub>
* **Out-of-city trips and the empty return**
   * **Night outward drift**: At night more trips leave the core for residential outer boroughs, leaving drivers far from demand. *Values:* Share of core yellow pickups ending in outer boroughs: 1.4% at 8am, 9.5% at 10pm, 13.1% at midnight, 17.7% at 4am (Oct 2019). `High · World input · public/forecast · all; taxi most`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own computation, TLC yellow Oct 2019 · Data: TLC trip records · Model: Emerges from hour-specific OD matrices; makes 'go home after last trip' and repositioning decisions matter · [R01-060]</sub>
   * **Empty-return distance and legality**: After an out-of-city drop-off the driver normally returns to NYC empty, because pickups outside the city need a local licence. *Values:* Return 10-60+ km; street hails outside NYC not allowed; app pickups outside NYC depend on NJ / county licensing [U]; taxi passengers reimburse tolls both ways for EWR, Westchester and Nassau; taxis may refuse those trips after 8 h of operation. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Near-zero pickups at EWR and zone 265 in TLC records; [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(c), 80-20(b)(8); [U] county licensing details [corrected by fact-check: impact high -> medium; such trips are ~0.5% of yellow and ~4% of app trips and are partly compensated, though a single one can cost up to an hour] · Data: TLC records; county taxi/limousine rules [U] · Model: Compute deadhead route back to the city line or home; charge time, energy and inbound toll · [R01-059]</sub>
   * **Network extent and off-map trips**: How far the road graph and travel-time model reach beyond the five boroughs, and how trips that end off the map are timed, located and returned. *Values:* HVFHV Jan 2023: 3.92% of trips end 'Outside of NYC' (zone 265; mean 16.6 mi and 34.4 min) and carry 10.4% of base fare; 0.63% end at EWR (17.2 mi, 39.1 min, 1.8% of fare) [P, re-verified exactly by fact-check]. Pickups from zone 265 are about 0.005% [P]. Zone 265 has no location, so the end point must be imputed from trip_miles and direction [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden · all (taxi out-of-city rate codes; robotaxi only if the ODD allows)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own calc on the local HVFHV Jan 2023 file: about 4.6% of trips and about 12% of base fare end off the NYC map. A fixed-time or instant-return shortcut misprices exactly these long trips; R01-057 gives only the share. · Data: NYC TLC trip records (zone 265, trip_miles, trip_time); OpenStreetMap roads for NJ, Westchester, Nassau and Suffolk; NJDOT/NYSDOT speed data [U] · Model: Extend the road graph to a buffer of about 50 km around NYC, impute the off-map drop-off point by matching trip_miles to county centroids, and route the empty return (R01-059). · [G2-031]</sub>
   * **Out-of-city fare and pay rules**: How trips leaving the city are priced and paid, which decides whether the empty return is compensated (amounts owned by R07). *Values:* Yellow: meter to the city line, then double meter beyond for Westchester/Nassau, plus tolls both ways; other places negotiated flat fare. HVFHV minimum pay from 1 Mar 2026: in-city $1.283/mi + $0.681/min (non-WAV); out-of-town $1.757/mi (non-WAV), $2.193/mi (WAV), $0.725/min [uncertain: 2026 posted values not re-checked; they match the rule's base rates after yearly CPI indexing]. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi; robotaxi pricing is a setting`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(c)(1)-(2), (f)(2): double meter and tolls both ways confirmed; 35 RCNY 59D-22(a): out-of-town base rates ($1.700/mi, WAV $2.122, $0.574/min) are double the in-city base and are not divided by the utilisation rate; [S] TLC driver-pay-rates page · Data: TLC driver pay rates (https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/driver-pay-rates.page) · Model: Fare-engine branch when the destination is outside the NYC polygon · [R01-058]</sub>
   * **Out-of-city trip share**: Share of trips ending outside NYC (NJ, Westchester, Nassau, Long Island, Connecticut). *Values:* HVFHV Jun 2021: 3.67% end outside NYC (zone 265) + 0.42% at EWR. Yellow Oct 2019: rate code 4 (Nassau/Westchester) 0.08%, negotiated (code 5) 0.17%, Newark (code 3) 0.24%. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own computation · Data: TLC trip records · Model: OD matrix includes external destination nodes by county · [R01-057]</sub>
* **Places the driver and car need (fuel, chargers, restrooms, garages)**
   * **EV charger locations, power and access**: Location, speed and availability of chargers (public, curbside, fleet-only hubs), which set detour and downtime for EVs. *Values:* DC fast 50-350 kW; Level 2 7-19 kW; NYC DOT curbside chargers; fleet hubs. `Med · World input · agent sees · EV drivers, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] TLC license reviews weigh EV charging availability; [U] charger mix [uncertain: TLC licence-review claim not re-checked] · Data: AFDC station locator (https://afdc.energy.gov/stations) · Model: Charger POIs with ports, power and queues; charge action (R08) · [R01-065]</sub>
   * **Garages, depots and shift-change points**: Yellow cab garages and robotaxi depots are where shifts start or end and cars are cleaned or charged, adding deadhead. *Values:* Garage zone; two-shift taxis change around 4-5pm [U]; robotaxi depot locations set by scenario. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · taxi, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Background knowledge; [design] for robotaxi depots · Data: None found · Model: Depot nodes; mandatory returns for shift change, charging or cleaning · [R01-063]</sub>
   * **Fuel station locations**: Where gas stations are and the detour to reach them; Manhattan has few. *Values:* Stations per borough; detour 0-15 min. `Low · World input · agent sees · gas vehicles`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Background knowledge · Data: OSM amenity=fuel · Model: POI layer; refuel action routes to the nearest station · [R01-064]</sub>
   * **Restrooms, food and break spots**: Where a driver can take a legal break with a toilet and food, which affects break length and dead time. *Values:* Public restroom POIs; 113 relief stands [uncertain: count not re-checked, see R01-031]; break detour 0-20 min. `Low · World input · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] WNYC 'Relief in short supply for the city's taxi drivers'; [U] · Data: NYC Parks public restrooms (NYC Open Data) [U]; OSM amenity=toilets · Model: Break action needs a nearby POI; cost = detour + dwell · [R01-066]</sub>

### 2. Time of day (23)
*How the clock shapes demand, trip value, routines (lunch, prayer, going home) and time-based prices.*

* **Clock, tick and the episode day**
   * **Simulation clock and hour-of-week key**: The local wall-clock time (America/New_York) that every other profile is looked up by, stored as date, minute and a 168-slot hour-of-week index. *Values:* 1-minute resolution; 168 hour-of-week slots; 365/366 calendar days; time zone UTC-5/UTC-4. `High · Changes during run · agent sees · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023: same-city demand ranges 4,892 to 48,069 trips/hour depending only on hour and weekday · Data: None needed (derived); profiles from NYC TLC trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Global clock object; every generator queries rate(slot, day_type, month) multipliers · [R02-001]</sub>
   * **Episode day boundary**: Where one simulated day ends and the next begins; cutting at midnight splits Saturday night. *Values:* Boundary at 04:00-05:00 (demand trough) recommended vs 00:00. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; HVFHV trough at 03-05h while Sun 00-01h still carries 41k and 33k trips/h of Saturday night [P*] · Data: none (benchmark setting) · Model: Day runs 05:00 to 04:59 next day; multi-day episodes chain days · [R02-068]</sub>
   * **Simulation tick granularity**: The length of one simulation step, which limits how precisely the agent can time pulses like let-outs. *Values:* 1 min recommended; 5 or 15 min coarser; pulses last 15-30 min [corrected by fact-check: in zone 230 let-out pickups stay raised for about 45-60 min at 15-min resolution, peaking about 30 min after onset]. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; let-out spikes last 15-30 min in zone 230 data [P*] [corrected by fact-check: 45-60 min] · Data: none (benchmark setting) · Model: Event-driven or 1-minute ticks; agent decision every N minutes · [R02-067]</sub>
* **Hourly demand curve (rush, lunch, dinner, nightlife, dead hours)**
   * **Dead hours (03-05 am)**: The overnight trough when demand is lowest but trips are long, fast and airport-heavy. *Values:* HVFHV weekday 03h 4,892 trips/h; 04h avg 7.2 mi, 25 mph, $64.4 per engaged hour, 28.6% of trips go to airports; yellow 274/h. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Low arrival rate with OD skewed to JFK/LGA/EWR; idle time dominates the agent's hourly profit · [R02-014]</sub>
   * **Evening rush**: The 4-8 pm peak when offices empty, the highest weekday volume but the slowest traffic and lowest pay per engaged hour. *Values:* HVFHV 17-18h 35-38k trips/h; 12.3 mph; $49.7-49.9 per engaged hour; yellow adds $2.50 surcharge [corrected by fact-check: 12.3 mph is 17h only (18h 13.3 mph); $49.7-49.9 is 16-17h, 18h pays $52.4]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023; [P*] surcharge visible in yellow 'extra' field · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Profile peak plus outbound OD bias; couples with R10 speed curve · [R02-010]</sub>
   * **Late-night nightlife demand**: Thursday-Saturday 10 pm-3 am demand from bars, clubs and parties, concentrated in a few neighbourhoods. *Values:* Nightlife zones (East Village, LES, West/Greenwich Village, Williamsburg etc.) 1,680-1,920 pickups per 15 min at midnight Sat/Sun vs 456 on weekdays; HVFHV 21-24h: Sat 133k, Fri 120k, Mon 64k. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Zone-specific late-night multipliers by weekday; waits rise (Sat 23h median 5.2 min) · [R02-012]</sub>
   * **Morning rush**: The 7-10 am commuter peak, with short Manhattan-bound trips, slow traffic and the longest rider waits of a weekday. *Values:* HVFHV 08h 36.3k trips/h; median request-to-pickup 5.0 min at 07h (vs 3.3 at 10h); 14 mph. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Profile peak plus commute-direction bias (outer boroughs to Midtown/FiDi) in the OD matrix · [R02-008]</sub>
   * **Weekday hourly demand curve**: The number of ride requests per hour on an ordinary Monday-Friday, shaped by commuting and evening activity. *Values:* HVFHV citywide trips/hour: 03h 4,892; 08h 36,283; 12h 23,630; 18h 37,694; 23h 25,607 (peak/trough 7.7x). Yellow: 03h 274 vs 18h 7,534 (27x). `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023; [P*] own calc yellow Jan 2023 · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Non-homogeneous Poisson arrivals per zone with rate = base(zone) x hour-of-week profile x day-type x month · [R02-006]</sub>
   * **Weekend hourly demand curve**: The hourly shape on Saturday and Sunday, with late mornings, strong evenings and Friday/Saturday nights running into the next morning. *Values:* HVFHV Sat: 05h 9,765; 19h 48,069; 23h 46,648. Sun: 00h 41,287 (Sat night); 05h 10,221; 17h 34,312. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Separate Sat and Sun profiles; the 00-04h of Sat/Sun belong to the previous evening · [R02-007]</sub>
   * **Dinner and evening out**: The 7-10 pm leisure wave of restaurant, show and social trips, stronger Thursday-Saturday. *Values:* HVFHV weekday 19-21h 31-36k trips/h; Sat 19h 48.1k; trip length rises from 4.1 to 5.2 mi after 8 pm. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Leisure-purpose demand layer by hour and day with nightlife/restaurant zone weights · [R02-011]</sub>
   * **Sector-specific hourly shape**: The yellow taxi and app-ride markets have different hourly and weekly shapes, so a taxi agent and an app agent face different best hours. *Values:* Yellow-to-HVFHV trip ratio 0.23 at noon falling to 0.175 at 8 pm; yellow busiest day Thursday, HVFHV busiest Saturday. `Med · World input · public/forecast · taxi, human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 and yellow Jan 2023 · Data: NYC TLC trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Fit profiles per sector (street hail vs e-hail) rather than one shared curve · [R02-015]</sub>
   * **Bar-closing taper**: The end-of-night exit when bars close (4 am in NYC; 2 am in many US cities), which in NYC tapers smoothly rather than spiking. *Values:* NYC nightlife pickups fall steadily from 1,700/15 min at 01:00 to 590 at 04:00 and 222 at 05:00 (Sat); no spike at 04:00. Close time 04:00 NYC; 02:00 elsewhere [U]. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 (re-computed: 1,699 at 01:00, 992 at 03:00, 591 at 04:00, 222 at 05:00); NY on-premises closing 4 am [U] [uncertain: NY Alcoholic Beverage Control Law hours not read this session] [corrected by fact-check: was medium; in NYC the taper is already inside the late-night curve (R02-012) and has no closing spike, as the dossier summary itself says; medium only for a 2 am city] · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page; NY State Liquor Authority hours rules · Model: Exponential decay after 01:00 in nightlife zones; for 2 am cities add a closing pulse · [R02-013]</sub>
   * **Lunch hour (rider demand)**: Midday trips around the average lunch time; small in ride-hail because most lunch trips are walked. *Values:* Midtown+FiDi weekday pickups per 15 min: 590-613 at 10:00-10:45, 681-696 at 11:30-12:00 (about +12%), dip 12:30-12:45 [corrected by fact-check: 571-613 at 10:00-10:45; pickups recover to 664-700 at 13:00-13:45, so most of the +12% is the late-morning ramp and the lunch-specific bump is under 5%]; lunch typically 12:00-13:00, 30-60 min [U]. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023; ATUS eating-time pattern not verified this session [U] · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page; BLS American Time Use Survey, https://www.bls.gov/tus/ · Model: Small Gaussian bump centred 11:45 on business-zone pickups; slower midday speeds come from R10 · [R02-009]</sub>
   * **Minute-of-hour request pulses**: Ride requests bunch slightly at round clock times (on the hour, the half hour, and 10 or 40 past) as meetings, classes and shows end on the clock. *Values:* HVFHV Jan 2023, non-holiday weekdays, requests 07-20h. Midtown/Penn zones (100, 161-164, 186, 230), on-demand requests (wait <= 15 min): minute :10 is 1.15x the mean, :40 1.14x, :30 1.10x, :00 1.09x, :55 1.08x; trough 0.93-0.94x at :19-:23. Citywide (8.4M requests): peaks are 1.06-1.12x (at :40, :10, :45, :15, :30 and :00), and 5-minute block averages stay within about +/-3% (0.98-1.03). Airports peak at :55-:59 (1.04-1.06x). Pickup minutes are almost flat (+/-6%).. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own calc on HVFHV Jan 2023 request_datetime (g1/a1.py, a2.py), re-run in this fact-check, with 5-minute blocks added. [corrected: pickup minutes +/-6%, not +/-5%]. Median waits are slightly shorter at the peak minutes (3.35-3.43 vs 3.65 min), so the peaks are not just scheduled reservations. · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records (request_datetime to the second) · Model: Optional periodic term on each zone's arrival rate (minute weights per zone class). It matters only with 1-minute ticks (R02-067). · [G1-008]</sub>
* **Trip quality by hour (trip length, pay per hour)**
   * **Hourly fare intensity and pay per engaged hour**: Revenue per minute of driving with a passenger, which mixes surge, speed and trip mix and varies by hour. *Values:* HVFHV driver pay per engaged hour $49.7-64.4 (weekday); base fare per mile $3.38-5.15; NYE 01-02h $97-101. `High · World input · public/forecast · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Emerges from pricing rules (R07) x speed x surge state; validate against these hourly targets · [R02-018]</sub>
   * **Hourly trip length and destination mix**: How long trips are and where they go changes by hour (commutes, airport runs, outer-borough returns). *Values:* HVFHV weekday avg trip 4.1 mi at 18h vs 7.2 mi at 04h; airport drop-off share 28.6% at 04h vs 0.3% at 23h; airport pickup share 8.2% at 00h. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Hour-of-week-specific OD matrices (or OD multipliers) fitted from TLC zone pairs · [R02-017]</sub>
* **Meal times and daily routines**
   * **Meal breaks: timing and duration**: When the driver eats and for how long. *Values:* 1-2 meals per 10-12 h shift, 15-45 min each; general-population peaks 12:00-13:00 and 18:00-19:00; drivers often move meals off demand peaks.. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet (per driver)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] USDA ERS ATUS module 2006-08: over 35% of Americans eat as a main activity at noon-1 pm and 6-7 pm [uncertain: share not re-checkable; the peaks themselves are well established (U)]; ATUS 2024: 74 min/day eating and drinking [uncertain: recent ATUS values are about 1.1-1.25 h (66-75 min) a day (U)]; a Wuhan study detects cab drivers' meal stops from GPS. · Data: BLS ATUS https://www.bls.gov/tus/ ; ERS Eating and Health Module https://www.ers.usda.gov/data-products/eating-and-health-module-atus/ · Model: Hunger timer; after 5-6 h without a meal apply a rising fatigue or discomfort penalty; a meal action takes 15-45 min in a zone with food. · [R09-010]</sub>
   * **Prayer and religious observance**: Prayer stops, Friday midday prayers and Ramadan fasting that some drivers observe. *Values:* 5 daily prayers of about 10-15 min at sun-based times; Friday Jumu'ah about 45-60 min at midday; Ramadan iftar stop of 30-60 min at sunset.. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, taxi (persona option)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] In The Fray: roughly half of NYC's taxi drivers are Muslim (anecdotal); Religion News Service 2023: drivers combine prayers when they cannot park; CNN: drivers give up '$30 or $40' in the iftar hour. · Data: Astronomical prayer-time calculation [U]; Islamic calendar for Ramadan dates [U] · Model: Optional persona schedule of fixed-time breaks that need a parking spot or mosque zone; a Friday midday block. · [R09-013]</sub>
* **Time-of-day rules and prices**
   * **Time-of-use electricity price**: EV charging costs depend on the hour, cheapest overnight, which shapes when EVs and robotaxis should charge. *Values:* Off-peak roughly 00:00-08:00; summer weekday afternoons most expensive; DC fast-charging prices may also vary by hour [U] [uncertain: Con Edison tariff pages unreachable (DNS blocked)]. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet, human app driver (EV)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Con Edison TOU tariff not read this session; cross-link R08 · Data: Con Edison time-of-use rates, https://www.coned.com/en/accounts-billing/your-bill/time-of-use ; AFDC stations, https://afdc.energy.gov/ · Model: Price per kWh by hour-of-week in the energy model · [R02-057]</sub>
   * **Curb and bus-lane time windows**: Kerbside and bus-lane rules change by hour, limiting where a car may stop or wait at peak times. *Values:* Typical 'No Standing 7-10 am / 4-7 pm' on avenues; bus-lane camera hours vary (peak only to 24/7) [U] [uncertain: NYC DOT regulation data not read]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] not measured; cross-link R01/R07 · Data: NYC DOT parking regulations (NYC Open Data), https://data.cityofnewyork.us/ · Model: Time-windowed curb attributes on links; fines if agent stops illegally · [R02-058]</sub>
   * **Holiday exemptions in time rules**: Some time-of-day charges and rules switch off on legal holidays. *Values:* Yellow $2.50 rush surcharge absent 16-20h on July 4, Labor Day, Thanksgiving, Christmas (mode 'extra' $0 vs $2.50 next day) [fact-check: also $0 on MLK, Presidents' and Memorial Day 2023]; alternate-side parking suspended on listed holidays [U]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi, human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc yellow 2023 'extra' field; TLC fare page says 'excluding holidays' [S] [upgraded by fact-check: 35 RCNY 58-26(a)(2)(i) and (b)(1)(i), both rush surcharges 'will not be applied on legal holidays', read via verbatim copy [P*]] · Data: TLC taxi fare page, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/passengers/taxi-fare.page; NYC DOT ASP calendar · Model: Rule engine checks day_type before applying time-band surcharges · [R02-037]</sub>
* **Daylight, DST and timestamps**
   * **DST transition days**: Clocks jump forward in March (23-h day) and back in November (25-h day), shifting late-night demand in clock time. *Values:* Spring 2023: no 02h; 03h yellow trips 4,267 vs ~2,300 normal (1.8x); Fall: 01h 8,435 (two hours) vs 5,400-6,000; 02h 2,217 vs 4,000-5,300. `Low · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023 · Data: IANA tz database (America/New_York) · Model: Run the clock in UTC, convert to local for profiles; nightlife demand follows body time across the switch · [R02-064]</sub>
   * **Sunrise, sunset and darkness**: Day length and the time of dusk, which change through the year and drive lighting, prayer times and perhaps some mode choice. *Values:* NYC sunset 16:28 (8 Dec) to 20:30 (21 Jun); daylight 9.2-15.1 h; darkness effect on demand undetected (17-19h share -1% after both DST shifts). `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] astral/NOAA solar calc; [P*] own calc yellow 2023 DST test · Data: NOAA solar calculator, https://gml.noaa.gov/grad/solcalc/ · Model: Compute sun position per minute; feed lighting, Ramadan and Sabbath windows · [R02-063]</sub>

### 3. Calendar and season (23)
*Which kind of day it is: weekday or weekend, season, holiday, school and shift calendars, timed schedules.*

* **Day type and day of week**
   * **Day-of-week demand level**: Total demand differs by weekday, and differently for taxis and app rides. *Values:* HVFHV Jan 2023 per day: Sat 724k, Fri 662k, Sun 627k, Thu 609k, Wed 563k, Tue 528k, Mon 516k. Yellow 2023: Thu 121k, Wed 117k, Fri 114k, Sat 112k, Tue 111k, Mon 98k, Sun 95k. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023; [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023 · Data: NYC TLC trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Day-of-week multiplier on base demand per sector · [R02-023]</sub>
   * **Day-type classification**: The category a calendar date falls into, which selects the demand, speed and rule profile for that day. *Values:* Mon, Tue-Thu, Fri, Sat, Sun, federal holiday, bridge day, major-event day, DST day. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023: holidays run at 0.45-0.90 of a normal same-weekday, bridge days 0.56-0.79 · Data: US federal holiday list (OPM), https://www.opm.gov/policy-data-oversight/pay-leave/federal-holidays/ ; NYC DOE calendar · Model: Lookup table date -> day_type; profiles fitted per day_type from TLC history · [R02-002]</sub>
   * **Thursday-Saturday nights**: Leisure nights start on Thursday and peak on Friday and Saturday [corrected by fact-check: true for app rides; for yellow taxis Thursday 21-24h (20.0k) is the busiest night, ahead of Friday and Saturday]. *Values:* Yellow 21-24h per day: Thu 20.0k, Fri 19.0k, Sat 18.7k vs Mon 12.4k; HVFHV 21-24h: Sat 133k, Fri 120k, Thu 93k, Mon 64k. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023; [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 · Data: NYC TLC trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Night layer multipliers by weekday and zone · [R02-025]</sub>
   * **Hybrid-work office days**: Office attendance concentrates on Tuesday-Thursday, raising morning business demand on those days. *Values:* Yellow 7-10 am trips/day: Tue 14.9k, Wed 15.2k, Thu 15.3k vs Mon 13.4k and Fri 12.3k (-12% and -19% vs Thu); office occupancy ~55% midweek vs ~32% Fri [S] [uncertain: Kastle figure not re-read; whether it is NYC or the 10-city average, and for which year, is not stated]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023; Kastle Back to Work Barometer via repo note D02 [S] · Data: NYC TLC yellow taxi trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page; Kastle Back to Work Barometer, https://www.kastle.com/safety-wellness/getting-america-back-to-work/ · Model: Weekday x business-zone multiplier on AM and PM commute layers · [R02-024]</sub>
   * **Summer Fridays and getaway afternoons**: Friday afternoons empty out in summer and before long weekends as people leave the city early. *Values:* Yellow Fri/Thu ratio 13-17h: Jul 0.91, Aug 0.87 vs 0.95-1.03 other months; Mon before July 4 at 0.69 of normal [corrected by fact-check: September 2023 computes to 0.90, an artefact of the 21-24 Sep 2023 data gap (R02-065)]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023 · Data: NYC TLC yellow taxi trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Seasonal Friday-afternoon multiplier; outbound airport/Penn Station OD boost · [R02-027]</sub>
   * **Sunday pattern**: Sunday mornings are the quietest daytime of the week and Sunday evenings bring airport returns. *Values:* Yellow 7-10 am Sun 6.2k vs ~15k weekdays; HVFHV airport pickup share 6.2-7.6% at Sun 21-23h vs mostly 1-5% at other times [corrected by fact-check: weekday 23-01h shares are similar (5.5-8.2%); the Sunday-evening signal is in volume, about 1,770-1,790 airport pickups/h at Sun 21-23h vs 1,170-1,460/h at weekday 21h-00h]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023; [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023 · Data: NYC TLC trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Sunday profile plus airport-arrival layer weighted to Sunday evening · [R02-026]</sub>
* **Month, season and year**
   * **Holiday shopping season**: Late November to December brings shoppers, office parties and slow traffic in Midtown. *Values:* Weekday 00h yellow trips Dec 2,554/h (similar to Jun 2,537) vs Jan 1,671/h; Dec 17h speed 9.3 mph (slowest month); Fri before Christmas 0.84 of normal. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023 · Data: NYC TLC yellow taxi trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Seasonal layer Black Friday-Dec 23 on Midtown retail zones and late-night parties · [R02-031]</sub>
   * **Monthly seasonal demand index**: How average daily demand varies by month. *Values:* HVFHV +/-7%: Jul-Aug 0.93-0.97, Dec 1.04-1.07. Yellow +/-15%: Jan 0.84-0.94, Aug 0.85-0.87, May 1.07-1.11, Oct 1.07-1.10 (2023-2025) [corrected by fact-check: values re-computed and confirmed, but yellow Sep 2023 reads 0.90 only because the TLC file lacks about 95% of trips from midday 21 Sep to 24 Sep 2023; Sep is 1.06-1.08 in 2024-25]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] TLC monthly row counts via SHA-256 catalog of CloudFront files (own calc) · Data: NYC TLC trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Month multiplier per sector applied to base rates · [R02-028]</sub>
   * **Tourist season**: Visitor numbers peak in summer and the December holidays and are lowest in January-February, shifting demand toward hotels, attractions and airports. *Values:* Low season Jan-Feb; peaks Jun-Aug and late Nov-Dec [U] [uncertain: no visitor statistics read]; yellow monthly index tracks business more than tourism. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] NYC Tourism + Conventions visitor statistics not read this session · Data: NYC Tourism + Conventions, https://www.nyctourism.com/ ; Broadway League weekly grosses, https://www.broadwayleague.com/ · Model: Month multiplier on hotel/attraction zones and airport OD · [R02-030]</sub>
   * **Summer recreation destinations**: Warm-weather trips to beaches and parks (Rockaway, Coney Island, Orchard Beach) and out-of-town getaways pull cars to dead-end peninsulas where return trips are scarce. The zones are NYC-specific; any coastal city has an equivalent. *Values:* Yellow 2023 drop-offs into the Rockaway zones (27, 86, 117, 201): about 49/day in Jun-Aug vs 36/day in January (+35%). October also reaches 46/day, so part of the rise is a broad Apr-Oct season rather than beach days. Coney Island (55): 21/day in Jul-Aug vs 14 in January (+50%). Summer yellow pickups per drop-off: only 0.24 in Rockaway and 0.32 in Coney Island. The effect is likely concentrated on hot, dry weekends [U].. `Low · World input · public/forecast · all; single car (stranding risk), fleet (rebalancing out)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own calc on TLC yellow 2023 drop-offs by zone and month, re-run in this fact-check. September is depressed by the 21-24 Sep 2023 data gap (R02-065). Volumes are small (tens of yellow trips a day). App trips, which dominate outer-borough demand, are not measured. · Data: NYC TLC trip records https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page ; NOAA hourly weather; NYC Parks beach attendance (no machine-readable source found) · Model: Season x temperature x weekend multiplier on drop-offs to beach zones. The low pickup rate there feeds destination re-dispatch value (R05-058). · [G1-009]</sub>
* **Holidays and observances**
   * **Bridge and holiday-adjacent days**: Days next to holidays behave like partial holidays, with heavy airport flows before and after. *Values:* Black Friday 0.61; Wed before Thanksgiving 0.74; Sun after Thanksgiving 0.79; Christmas Eve 0.61; Dec 26 0.56; Dec 29 0.65; Christmas-New Year week AM trips -56%, speed +50%. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023 · Data: NYC TLC yellow taxi trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Bridge day-type; airport OD boost on getaway and return days · [R02-035]</sub>
   * **Federal and civic holiday multiplier**: Demand and speed on legal holidays compared with a normal same weekday. *Values:* Yellow 2023: Christmas 0.45, Thanksgiving 0.50, July 4 0.54, Memorial Day 0.73, NYD observed 0.77, Presidents 0.86, Columbus 0.86, Labor 0.87, Juneteenth 0.85, MLK 0.89; speeds +20-45% [corrected by fact-check: +14-45%; Presidents', Columbus and Juneteenth run +14-23%]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023 · Data: NYC TLC yellow taxi trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page; OPM federal holiday list · Model: Holiday day-type with its own hourly profile and speed multiplier · [R02-034]</sub>
   * **Party and dining nights**: Non-federal dates with outsized bar and restaurant demand reshape the evening and late-night peaks: Halloween, Thanksgiving Eve, Valentine's Day, St Patrick's Day, Mother's Day and Super Bowl Sunday. The dates are US-specific; the method generalises. *Values:* Yellow 2023 pickups vs the median of the same weekday +/-4 weeks, excluding holidays and event days. Halloween (Tue): 17-22h 0.73-0.89 (23h 0.95); 00-03h next day 1.97. Thanksgiving Eve: 18-23h 0.56-0.57; 00-03h 1.40. Valentine's (Tue): 18-21h 1.10; 22-23h 1.21; 00-03h 1.39. St Patrick's (Fri): 12-17h 0.90; 00-03h 1.22. Mother's Day: 12-23h 1.12-1.19; 00-03h 0.86. Super Bowl Sunday (12 Feb 2023): 17-18h 1.13-1.24; 19-20h (game) 0.79-0.83; 22h 1.85; 23h 1.49. Cinco de Mayo: at most 1.16. SantaCon is in R03-035.. `Med · World input · public/forecast · all; single car and fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own calc on TLC yellow 2023 hourly pickups (g1/a4.py, a5.py), re-run in this fact-check. [corrected: Halloween 0.73-0.89 is 17-22h, not 18-23h].
