Each ratio rests on one date, so expect a few percent of noise [design]. App (HVFHV) effects are not measured, since only Jan 2023 HVFHV is available locally. · Data: NYC TLC yellow and HVFHV trip records https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page ; NFL schedule (Super Bowl kickoff) · Model: Date x hour multiplier table added to the day-type calendar (R02-002), tilted toward nightlife zones (R01-044). For Halloween, combine with the Village parade closure (R03-030). · [G1-004]</sub>
   * **Religious and cultural holidays**: Observances that barely move citywide demand but shift it strongly in particular neighbourhoods and driver groups. *Values:* Citywide yellow ratio: Yom Kippur 0.92, Eid al-Fitr 0.95, Easter 0.90, Lunar New Year 1.01, Diwali 1.02; local effects (Flushing, Chinatown, Borough Park) not measured. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023; neighbourhood effects [U] · Data: NYC TLC yellow taxi trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page; interfaith calendars · Model: Zone-level multipliers on dated observances (neighbourhood x holiday table) · [R02-038]</sub>
   * **Weekly Sabbath in observant neighbourhoods**: Orthodox Jewish areas see almost no rides from Friday sunset to Saturday nightfall. *Values:* Friday sunset to Saturday nightfall (~25 h); zones such as Borough Park, Williamsburg South [U] [uncertain: zone-level effect not measured]. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] not measured this session · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page; sunset times · Model: Zone multiplier near 0 within the sun-defined window · [R02-040]</sub>
* **Institutional calendars and shift changes**
   * **Public school calendar**: School terms and recesses change morning traffic and some family trips. *Values:* Recess weeks: yellow 7-10 am trips -3 to -14% [corrected by fact-check: the dossier's own §2.5 weeks give -3% (April) and -12% (February)], AM speed +7-11% (about 12.2 to 13.4 mph; Feb week includes Presidents' Day); school start Sept 2023: AM speed 13.6 to 10.9 mph (-20%), AM trips +39% vs late Aug [uncertain: the September jump also reflects offices returning after summer and Labor Day, not only schools]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023; DOE recess dates [U] · Data: NYC DOE school year calendar, https://www.schools.nyc.gov/calendar · Model: School-in-session flag modifies AM speeds and family demand · [R02-041]</sub>
   * **Hospital and healthcare shift changes**: Large hospitals change shifts at fixed times, creating small pulses of staff trips. *Values:* Typical 12-h shifts 07:00/19:00, 8-h shifts 07/15/23h [U] [uncertain: no NYC hospital schedule read]. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] not measured · Data: none found (hospital locations from NYC Open Data facilities) · Model: Small pickups pulse at hospital zones at shift ends · [R02-043]</sub>
   * **Paydays and month-end**: Spending may rise just after common paydays (1st, 15th, Fridays), lifting discretionary rides. *Values:* Possible small uplift on 1st/15th and Fridays [U]; not tested [uncertain: no evidence found either way]. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] no ride-hail evidence found · Data: none found · Model: Optional day-of-month multiplier (default 1.0) · [R02-046]</sub>
   * **Taxi refuelling before shift handover**: Yellow-cab drivers on driver-pays leases customarily hand the cab back with a full tank, so gas stations near the Queens garages queue up in the late afternoon. *Values:* Refuel stop of 10-25 min including the queue, between 15:30 and 17:00 [design]. If the owner supplies gasoline under the optional gas-surcharge lease ($13-31 per shift, set by the EIA New York City gasoline price index), the driver pays no fuel and does not refuel before handover (35 RCNY 58-21(c)(6)) [P*, added by fact-check]. Hybrids need about one fill per 1-2 shifts (R08-012) [design]. It deepens the 4-5 pm taxi supply dip (R02-044, R06-018).. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi (day-shift lease drivers); competitor taxi bots`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] full-tank custom, which is not in 35 RCNY 58-21; that rule only provides the optional owner-supplied gas surcharge [P*, re-read]. [S] A 2011 TLC count found cabs on the road fell from 6,688 to 5,452 between 4 and 5 pm (via R02-044). · Data: 35 RCNY 58-21 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; OpenStreetMap amenity=fuel near garage zones; none found for queue times · Model: A lease-type flag: gas-included (no refuelling) or driver-pays (must refuel before handover when the tank is below a threshold). Competitor taxi bots on driver-pays leases leave service 20-40 min earlier. · [G2-017]</sub>
   * **University terms**: College move-in, term time, finals and commencement shift demand near campuses and luggage trips. *Values:* Move-in late Aug, commencement May, winter break mid-Dec to late Jan [U] [uncertain: academic calendars not read]. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] not measured · Data: University academic calendars (NYU, Columbia, CUNY) · Model: Campus-zone multipliers on dated windows · [R02-042]</sub>
* **Timed schedules (flight banks, transit timetables)**
   * **Airport arrival banks**: Flights land in banks, so airport pickup demand rises and falls by hour differently at JFK, LGA and EWR. *Values:* HVFHV weekday JFK pickups/h: 60 at 04h, 409-428 at 06-07h, 593-682 at 14-17h, 747-799 at 21-23h; LGA ~0 at 02-06h, 500-760 at 09-24h [corrected by fact-check: LGA 11-28/h at 02-06h and 502-764/h at 09-24h]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023; [U] scheduled-arrival data not read · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page; BTS on-time performance (scheduled arrivals), https://www.transtats.bts.gov/ · Model: Airport pickup arrivals driven by scheduled arrivals per hour x passengers x ground-transport share · [R02-047]</sub>
   * **Airport departure banks**: Early-morning departure banks create long, high-fare trips to the airports before dawn. *Values:* Weekday drop-offs/h: JFK 1,137 and LGA 958 at 05h; LGA drop-offs end ~21h (37/h); 23.8-28.6% of all 04-05h trips go to airports. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page; BTS scheduled departures · Model: Airport-bound OD weight by hour from scheduled departures minus 1.5-3 h · [R02-048]</sub>
   * **Transit service schedule**: Subway headways, last commuter trains and overnight service gaps shift demand to cars at night. *Values:* Overnight subway headways ~20 min [U]; last LIRR/Metro-North/PATH trains ~00:30-01:30 [U] [corrected by fact-check: PATH and the LIRR run all night with long gaps, so they have no last train; only Metro-North has a true overnight gap (last Grand Central departures roughly 01:00-02:00) [U]] [uncertain: MTA GTFS not read]; 2020-21 overnight subway closure 01-05h [U]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] MTA GTFS not read this session · Data: MTA GTFS feeds, https://new.mta.info/developers ; MTA ridership on NYC Open Data · Model: Mode-choice term: car demand rises when transit wait or gap is long at that hour · [R02-052]</sub>

### 4. Events (57)
*Planned and unplanned happenings that move riders or block roads: concerts, games, parades, crashes, outages.*

* **Event calendar and venues**
   * **Broadway curtain and let-out pattern**: Weekly theatre schedule that creates evening and matinee let-out waves around Times Square. *Values:* Tue 7pm curtain (let-out ~21:30); Wed and Thu a mix of 7pm and 8pm curtains; Fri-Sat 8pm (let-out ~22:30-22:45); matinees Wed and Sat 2pm (let-out ~16:30), Sun mostly 3pm (let-out ~17:30); most theatres dark on Monday [corrected by fact-check: originally 'Wed-Sat 8pm' and 'Sun 2pm'; own 2023 zone-230 15-min counts show a 21:30 peak on Thursdays (84 vs 57 on Mondays) as on Tuesdays, both 21:30 and 22:30-22:45 peaks on Wednesdays, and a Sunday bump at 17:30 (63 vs 47 at 17:15), P*; evening curtains 'typically 7pm or 8pm', S]; ~12M admissions a season [uncertain: about 12.3M in 2022-23 and 2023-24 from memory, and 2024-25 reportedly a record of about 14.7M; not re-checkable this session]. `High · World input · public/forecast · all (strongest for taxi)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, zone 230 yellow pickups 22:00-23:00 in 2023: Mon 177/day vs Tue 270, Wed 331, Fri 298, Sat 285, Sun 129; Tuesday peak moves to 21:30 (88 vs 57 per 15 min on Mondays) · Data: Broadway League weekly grosses and attendance (broadwayleague.com); show schedules (Playbill, IBDB) [U] · Model: Recurring weekly event set per theatre with a let-out distribution; scale by the week's reported attendance · [R03-003]</sub>
   * **Event catalogue for the scenario day**: The list of planned events active on the simulated day, each with type, venue, start, end, attendance and closure footprint. *Values:* Typical NYC evening: ~30-40 Broadway shows, 0-3 arena or stadium events, 0-2 conventions, dozens of permitted street events on weekends; peak days (NYE, Marathon, UNGA) add a citywide event [U]. `High · World input · public/forecast · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, 2023 yellow trips: event days move venue-zone pickups from 0x normal (Times Sq after 17:00 on NYE) to 15x normal (Flushing Meadows during the US Open) · Data: NYC Permitted Event Information (NYC Open Data tvpp-9vvx, next month only) and NYC Permitted Event Information - Historical (bkfu-528j, approved events from 2008) for replay dates [corrected by fact-check: tvpp-9vvx lists only approved events 'within the next month', so past scenario days need bkfu-528j; IDs and descriptions read from verbatim copies of the Socrata metadata, P*]; plus venue and league schedules (see R03-061) · Model: Load event objects per scenario date; each adds arrival and let-out demand, closures and speed effects through the variables below · [R03-001]</sub>
   * **Multi-session tournaments (US Open tennis)**: Two-week events with day and night sessions every day, giving repeated arrival waves and late-night let-outs. *Values:* US Open: late Aug to early Sep, 14 days (2023: Aug 28-Sep 10) [uncertain: from 2025 the main draw reportedly starts on a Sunday, giving 15 days; not re-checkable this session]; day session ~11:00, night session ~19:00; night matches often end after midnight [U]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc 2023: Flushing Meadows (zone 93) yellow pickups 188.7/day vs 12.5 on other days (15x); 21-24h 29x; 0-2h pickups up to 162 a night vs 0.7; these trips average 10.0 mi and a $45 fare · Data: USTA schedule (usopen.org) [U]; TLC trip records · Model: Daily two-session event with a heavy-tailed end time for the night session · [R03-005]</sub>
   * **New Year's Eve in Times Square**: The year's biggest single night: an afternoon-to-midnight frozen zone around Times Square, then a citywide after-midnight spike. *Values:* Times Sq vehicle access restricted from late morning on Dec 31 until after midnight [corrected by fact-check: originally 'closed to cars from ~16:00-17:00'; own data show zone-230 pickups already 75-86% below a normal Sunday from 12:00 (37-66 vs 238-277 an hour), 10 at 16:00 and zero from 17:00, P*; official closure times U]; citywide demand 1.4-3x normal 00:00-04:00; app prices ~1.8x. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc: zone 230 yellow pickups fell to 0 from 17:00 on Dec 31 2023 (normal Sunday ~240/h); Jan 1 2024 0-4h citywide yellow pickups 1.4-3.2x, fare per trip $19.80 vs $15.80; HVFHV Jan 1 2023 1-3h fare per mile 7.97-8.40 vs 4.49-4.58, driver pay $97-101 vs $60-61 per engaged hour, waits 8.3-8.7 vs 4.7-4.9 min [corrected by fact-check: 8.40 vs 4.58 was the 01:00 hour only; re-run on the HVFHV file reproduced the rest] · Data: TLC trip records; NYPD and NYC DOT closure notices [U] · Model: Scripted closure polygon plus a citywide demand and surge multiplier after 00:00 · [R03-008]</sub>
   * **Venue registry and zone mapping**: Fixed table of venues with location, taxi zone, capacity, pickup and drop-off nodes and taxi stands. *Values:* MSG/Penn zone 186 (~19-21k); Yankee Stadium zone 247 (46.5k); Citi Field and USTA tennis centre zone 93 (~42k; Ashe ~24k); Javits zone 246; Broadway zone 230 (~40 theatres); Randalls Island zone 194; Barclays zone 181 (Park Slope), at the corner with 189 (Prospect Heights) and 97 (Fort Greene) [corrected by fact-check: originally 'zone 25 or 189 (verify)'; point-in-polygon test on a GitHub copy of the TLC zone GeoJSON, S]; outside NYC: MetLife, UBS Arena, Prudential Center (zone 265). Capacities [U]. `High · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc: Yankee night games lift zone-247 drop-offs 16-20h 4.7x (34.1 vs 7.2 per day) [corrected by fact-check: originally 4.5x (31.9 vs 7.1); the Sep 21-24 data-gap days are now excluded]; Mets and US Open lift zone 93, confirming the zone mapping · Data: TLC taxi zone shapefile (NYC Open Data 755u-8jsi); OpenStreetMap venue polygons · Model: Geocode each venue to a road node and taxi zone; attach capacity, pickup lot node and taxi stand · [R03-002]</sub>
   * **Annual fixed-date civic events**: Recurring yearly events with known dates and road closures (Macy's parade, Marathon, Pride, UNGA week, NYE ball drop, Fashion Week). *Values:* Pride Sunday 2023 yellow 1.11x; Marathon Sunday 1.03x (25-h DST day); UNGA Tuesday 0.94x with 10.9 vs 11.6 mph. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023; event sizes cross-link R03 · Data: NYC permitted event information (NYC Open Data); NYC DOT closure notices · Model: Calendar of dated closures and demand pulses passed to R03 events engine · [R02-053]</sub>
   * **Concerts and music festivals**: One-off or multi-day concerts and festivals at arenas, parks and islands, mostly with young, transit-heavy crowds. *Values:* Arena shows 15-20k; park festivals 30-60k a day (Governors Ball, Electric Zoo) [U]; stadium shows 50-80k. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc 2023: Electric Zoo (Randalls Island, zone 194) drop-offs 12-22h 45-48 vs 5-8 on normal days; Governors Ball (zone 93) 1.85x; yellow taxis carry only a small share of these crowds · Data: Ticketmaster Discovery API, SeatGeek API, setlist.fm (history) [U] · Model: Event objects with an audience profile (young, transit-heavy) that gives a low car share · [R03-007]</sub>
   * **Conventions and trade shows (Javits)**: Multi-day exhibitions that add morning arrivals and evening departures at the convention centre, plus hotel and airport trips. *Values:* NY Comic Con ~200k over 4 days [U]; Auto Show 10 days; 100+ events a year at Javits [U]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc 2023 zone 246: Comic Con (Oct 12-15) drop-offs 7-13h 1.97x and pickups 16-21h 1.39x the same weekdays a week before and after; Auto Show (Apr 7-16) pickups 1.22x · Data: Javits event calendar (javitscenter.com/events) [U] · Model: Daily open and close times with attendance and a business-traveller price profile · [R03-006]</sub>
   * **Events outside the city boundary**: Large events just outside NYC (MetLife, UBS Arena, Prudential Center, Belmont Park [uncertain: the Belmont Stakes was run at Saratoga in 2024 and 2025 while Belmont Park was rebuilt; check the scenario year]) whose trips cross licence borders, so drivers drop off but rarely pick up there. *Values:* MetLife 82.5k; UBS Arena ~17k [U]; yellow and green taxis cannot take street hails outside NYC, and Newark airport is drop-off-only for NYC cars (R01); whether NYC-licensed app drivers may accept app requests in NJ or Nassau depends on those jurisdictions' rules [corrected by fact-check: originally 'NYC-licensed app cars generally cannot take street pickups outside the city'; app cars never take street hails, so the limit is on app dispatch, which is uncertain: not re-checkable this session]. `Med · World input · public/forecast · human app driver, taxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc: the Taylor Swift MetLife weekend (May 26-28 2023) left yellow drop-offs outside NYC unchanged (ratio 1.00), so taxis capture almost none of it; app share not measured · Data: Venue calendars [U]; TLC zone 265 (outside NYC) · Model: External event that creates outbound trips and only app-dispatched return trips; charge the empty return · [R03-010]</sub>
   * **Pro sports home schedules**: Home games of New York teams, which create arrival and let-out waves at stadiums and arenas, including playoff games whose dates are uncertain. *Values:* MLB Yankees and Mets: 81 home games each (2023: 63% and 69% at night); NBA Knicks and Nets, NHL Rangers ~41 each; Islanders at UBS Arena; NFL Giants and Jets at MetLife (NJ); MLS, WNBA; playoffs add home dates, and games 5-7 of a series are 'if necessary' and confirmed only 1-2 days ahead [corrected by fact-check: '0-11' had no basis; under current formats a team can host up to 14 MLB postseason games (Wild Card 3, Division Series 3, LCS 4, World Series 4) and up to 16 NBA or NHL playoff games, U]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Retrosheet 2023 game logs + own calc: yellow drop-offs near Yankee Stadium 8.4 per 10k attendees on night games (Mets 4.9); corr(attendance, drop-offs) 0.53 for the Yankees [corrected by fact-check: originally 8.1 and 0.52, with two data-gap night games included] · Data: Retrosheet game logs (github.com/chadwickbureau/retrosheet); MLB Stats API, NBA and NHL schedule feeds [U] · Model: Import per-date games with start time, attendance and duration; generate waves through R03-011 to R03-016 · [R03-004]</sub>
   * **Commencements and move-in days**: University graduations at large venues and dorm move-in days that add family groups with luggage. *Values:* May-June graduations at Yankee Stadium, MSG, Barclays, Radio City; late-August move-in; 1-5 days a year per venue [U] [uncertain: not re-checkable this session; web search budget used up and source hosts blocked]. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] recurring venue and university calendars; not measured here · Data: University calendars; venue calendars [U] · Model: Small daytime events with high XL and luggage share · [R03-009]</sub>
   * **Unlisted private events**: Weddings, proms, quinceaneras, funerals and banquet-hall parties that no public calendar lists but that create local demand spikes, mostly on spring and summer Saturdays. *Values:* 0-3% of Saturday-evening demand citywide, concentrated in zones with catering halls. Single spikes of 20-150 trips in one zone-hour [design guess].. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] These spikes are an irreducible floor of 'unexplained' demand. That floor limits how much event information can add in the with/without-info test (R03-060). · Data: none found for events. NYS Liquor Authority 'Current Liquor Authority Active Licenses' on data.ny.gov (dataset 9s3h-dpkz, with licence type, so catering establishments can be located) [S]. Residual Saturday spikes in TLC data (method of R03-066). · Model: Schedule small Poisson events at catering-hall zones (Saturday evenings, seasonal weights) using TLC residual templates. They never appear in any information arm. · [G2-008]</sub>
* **Event size, timing and crowd behaviour**
   * **Attendance**: Number of people at an event, the main scale factor for event demand. *Values:* Yankees 2023 mean 40,863 (sd 4,180; 30.7k-47.3k); Mets 32,994 (sd 6,141); arenas 15-21k; Broadway ~0.6-1.9k a theatre [corrected by fact-check: originally 1-1.9k; a Broadway house has 500 or more seats by definition and the smallest have about 600, U]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Retrosheet 2023 game logs; corr(attendance, yellow drop-offs) 0.53 Yankees (0.52 with the two data-gap games) and 0.27 Mets [P*] · Data: Retrosheet game logs; league box scores; Broadway League grosses [U] · Model: Event demand = attendance x car share x trips per party; capacity is public, realised attendance is sampled · [R03-011]</sub>
   * **Audience car-for-hire mode share**: Fraction of attendees who take a taxi or app ride, which depends on transit access and audience. *Values:* Yellow taxis: ~3-7 extra drop-offs per 10k attendees at the ballparks [corrected by fact-check: originally ~2.5-6; recomputed 6.6 (Yankees) and 2.9 (Mets) above non-game baselines of 7.2 and 6.4 a night]; app share 2-10% at Manhattan venues, 10-30% at transit-poor venues [design]. `High · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc 2023: 8.4 (Yankees) and 4.9 (Mets) yellow drop-offs per 10k attendees, including non-game baselines of 7.2 and 6.4 a night [corrected by fact-check: originally 8.1 and '~7'; US Open fortnight and data-gap days removed]; app share not yet measured [design] · Data: TLC yellow and HVFHV records joined to event calendars · Model: Venue x audience coefficient; calibrate from HVFHV residuals on event nights · [R03-016]</sub>
   * **Event duration and end-time uncertainty**: How long an event runs and how predictable its end is, which decides when let-out demand appears. *Values:* MLB 2023 mean 162 min, sd 19.7 (Yankees 162 +/- 18, p10-p90 143-186; 16.5% over 180 min; 6-9% extra innings); Broadway +/- 5 min [U]; NBA ~135 +/- 15 min [U]; concerts end by ~23:00 curfew [U]; tennis night sessions open-ended. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Retrosheet 2023 durations (first pitch-clock season); [P*] US Open after-midnight pickups · Data: Retrosheet game logs; live score feeds [U] · Model: Sample end = start + duration from an event-type distribution; reveal live progress (score feed) only at higher information levels · [R03-013]</sub>
   * **Let-out wave shape**: How fast post-event demand rises and decays near the venue. *Values:* Peak 15-30 min after the end; back near normal 60-75 min after. `High · World input · hidden · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc HVFHV Jan 2023 zone 186 (MSG/Penn): on the busiest fifth of nights pickups peak at 22:00 with 131 per 15 min vs 53 on quiet nights, down to 69 by 23:15 · Data: TLC HVFHV trip records (request and pickup times) · Model: Gamma-kernel or Hawkes burst triggered at the realised end time · [R03-015]</sub>
   * **Audience price sensitivity and income**: How willing an event crowd is to pay surge prices and wait, by audience type. *Values:* Low sensitivity: Broadway, US Open, conventions (tourists, expense accounts); high: EDM festivals, students, outer-borough fans [design]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc HVFHV Jan 2023: Times Sq night volume rose together with fare per mile (corr 0.76; +42% on the busiest nights), so riders kept booking at higher prices; segment values [design] · Data: none found · Model: Per-event elasticity applied to surge acceptance and cancellation · [R03-017]</sub>
   * **Event cancellation and postponement**: Chance that a scheduled event is cancelled, postponed or delayed, which strands drivers who moved there. *Values:* 1-3% per outdoor event, higher with storms [design]; MLB rain delays 30-120 min [U]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc: Electric Zoo day 1 (Fri Sep 1 2023) was cancelled the day before; drop-offs were 7 vs 45-48 on Sep 2-3 [uncertain: the cancellation and its timing are background knowledge; the trip data confirm only that Sep 1 had no event demand] · Data: Venue announcements; news [U] · Model: Per-event Bernoulli with an announcement lead time; R04 storms raise the probability · [R03-020]</sub>
   * **Event-crowd destinations and trip value**: Where event leavers go, and so how long and lucrative their trips are. *Values:* US Open leavers: 10.0 mi average, $45 fare; MSG leavers partly to Penn Station rail; ballparks to Manhattan and the suburbs. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc: 2023 US Open zone-93 pickups; HVFHV zone 186 busiest nights 5.8 mi vs 5.0 mi on quiet nights · Data: TLC origin-destination on event nights · Model: Per-venue destination distribution fitted from TLC OD on event nights · [R03-018]</sub>
   * **Start time and arrival spread**: Scheduled start and how arrivals spread before it, creating trips into the venue. *Values:* Arrivals from T-120 to T+20 min, peak about T-45 [design]; MLB night games 19:05-19:10; Broadway 19:00 or 20:00. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc: Yankee night-game drop-offs concentrated 16-20h (34.1 vs 7.2) [corrected by fact-check: originally 31.9 vs 7.1, with data-gap days included]; Comic Con drop-offs 7-13h 2x · Data: Venue and league schedules · Model: Sample arrival trips from a beta distribution ahead of start, with origins from the audience origin map · [R03-012]</sub>
   * **Weather x event interaction**: Rain or cold at let-out raises the car share and delays or cancels outdoor events. *Values:* Rain at let-out: car share 1.3-2x [design]; outdoor events halted in thunderstorms. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; cross-link R04 · Data: NOAA and NWS observations and forecasts (R04) · Model: Multiply mode share by a weather factor at the realised end; storms raise cancellation and delay odds · [R03-021]</sub>
   * **Early leavers and blowouts**: Share of attendees who leave before the end, which spreads the let-out wave earlier. *Values:* 5-30% depending on score margin, weather and last trains [design]. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] no public measure; visible as early pickups in TLC 15-minute data · Data: none found · Model: Shift a fraction of let-out demand to the 30 min before the realised end · [R03-014]</sub>
   * **Post-event group size and rider state**: Groups, intoxication and mess risk among event riders. *Values:* 2-4 riders a trip; more XL requests; mess and cancellation risk 2-3x on late nights [design]. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; cross-link R05 (party size) and R09-046 (mess events) · Data: none found · Model: Event riders draw from a separate group-size and incident profile · [R03-019]</sub>
* **Venue let-out: surge, queues and pickup zones**
   * **Post-event surge multiplier and decay**: How much app prices rise around a let-out and how quickly they fall back. *Values:* 1.2-1.4x at arena let-outs, ~1.8x on New Year's night; decays to ~1.05-1.10x within 45-60 min [corrected by fact-check: originally ~1.05x; at 23:00 the busiest nights' fare per mile at zone 186 was 4.91 vs 4.48 on quiet nights, 1.10x]. `High · World input · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc HVFHV Jan 2023: zone 186 busiest nights fare per mile 6.27 vs 4.73 at 22:00, 4.91 by 23:00; zone 230 +42% fare per mile and +27% driver pay per engaged hour; NYE 1.83x; Brooklyn zones 25 and 189 next to Barclays showed no pay gain on their busiest nights (-2% and -5% pay per engaged hour), so let-out surge is venue-specific [added by fact-check from hv_events_out.txt, P*] · Data: TLC HVFHV base_passenger_fare and driver_pay · Model: Surge engine (R07) reacts to the local request-to-idle-car ratio; calibrate to these ratios · [R03-022]</sub>
   * **Rider wait and pickup time at let-out**: Longer request-to-pickup times when crowds swamp nearby supply, which also raises cancellations. *Values:* Waits +50-100% (3.5-3.8 min to 5.4-6.8 min). `Med · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc HVFHV Jan 2023: zone 230 busiest nights 6.8 vs 3.5 min, zone 186 5.4 vs 3.6 min; corr(volume, wait) 0.88 at zone 230 · Data: TLC HVFHV request_datetime and pickup_datetime · Model: Emerges from matching; validate against these values · [R03-024]</sub>
   * **Taxi meter vs app surge at let-out**: Yellow and green taxis charge the meter with no surge, so at let-outs they win price-sensitive riders and street hails. *Values:* Taxi fare fixed by the TLC meter plus flat surcharges; app prices 1.2-1.8x. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] TLC metered fare rules; cross-link R07 · Data: TLC fare rules (R07) · Model: Rider mode choice compares the taxi meter estimate with the surged app price · [R03-023]</sub>
   * **Taxi stands and hail lines at venues**: Formal taxi queues at venues and hubs (Penn/MSG, Javits, Port Authority) where taxis wait in order. *Values:* Queue 0-60 cabs [design]; zone 186 yellow pickups 22:00-24:00 range 142-581 a night (p10 213, p90 427). `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc 2023 zone 186 nightly pickups; stand rules [U] · Data: TLC yellow records; NYC taxi stand locations (NYC DOT) [U] · Model: First-in-first-out stand queue fed by hail riders at let-out · [R03-026]</sub>
   * **Venue egress congestion**: Gridlock around a venue as crowds, buses and cars leave at once. *Values:* Speeds within ~1 km fall to 3-6 mph for 30-60 min [design]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc: trips starting in parade, Pride and UNGA zones ran 10-30% slower; venue-level values [design] · Data: NYC DOT traffic speeds (R10); TLC trip durations · Model: Speed multiplier on links within a radius during the let-out window · [R03-027]</sub>
   * **Venue pickup geofence and rideshare lot**: Venue rule that app pickups happen only at a set lot or street, with a staging queue for drivers. *Values:* Rider walk 5-15 min; lot holds 50-500 cars [design]; stadiums and festival sites use rideshare lots [U]. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`

* **Planned street closures (parades, races, VIP, filming)**
   * **Event and VIP traffic closures**: Road closures and spill-over congestion from parades, marathons, UNGA motorcades and presidential visits; the events themselves are R03. *Values:* UNGA week Midtown under 4 mph; parade or marathon route closures 6-12 h [U]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: NYC DOT 2025 Gridlock Alert statement on UNGA speeds [S] · Data: NYC DOT gridlock alerts; NYC event permits (R03) · Model: Event objects carry a closure polygon, a time window and a surrounding speed multiplier. · [R10-027]</sub>
   * **NYC Marathon**: First-Sunday-of-November race that closes bridges and avenues in all five boroughs from early morning to late afternoon, on the same day clocks fall back. *Values:* ~50k finishers [U]; Verrazzano-Narrows, Pulaski, Queensboro, Willis Ave and Madison Ave bridges, 1st Ave, 5th Ave and Central Park closed ~07:00-17:00 [corrected by fact-check: the course crosses five bridges and the Madison Ave Bridge was missing, S via a GitHub course file; closure times U]; 25-hour day (DST end). `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc Nov 5 2023 vs 3 nearby Sundays: citywide yellow trips 7-17h -5%; Central Park zone pickups -49%; Manhattan trip speed +8-10% 9-15h; fare per engaged hour $81 vs $76; the 1am hour shows 8,435 vs 5,425 trips because it occurs twice · Data: NYRR course map; NYC DOT advisories [U] · Model: Time-windowed edge closures along the route plus crossing barriers for east-west traffic · [R03-029]</sub>
   * **Parades on Manhattan avenues**: Recurring parades that close an avenue and its cross streets for 4-8 hours and suppress pickups along the route. *Values:* ~15-25 major parades a year: St Patrick's (Mar), Easter, Puerto Rican (Jun), Pride (last Sun in Jun), Dominican (Aug), Steuben (Sep), Columbus, Hispanic, Pulaski (Oct), Halloween (Oct 31, evening), Veterans (Nov 11), Thanksgiving [U]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`

   * **UN General Assembly high-level week**: Late-September week of rolling security zones and motorcades around the UN and Midtown East. *Values:* ~5 weekdays; 1st Ave in the 40s frozen, FDR holds; NYC DOT Gridlock Alert days [U]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc Sep 18-20 2023 vs Mon-Wed of adjacent weeks: Midtown East pickups -41%, UN/Turtle Bay South -66%, citywide -11% (7-20h); speeds 8-11h 7.6 vs 8.7 mph; mean trip 20.7 vs 18.8 min; fare per engaged hour -3% · Data: NYC DOT gridlock alerts; UN calendar (un.org) [U] · Model: Zone pickup suppression, speed multipliers and random motorcade holds · [R03-033]</sub>
   * **Film and TV shoots**: Permitted shoots that take parking lanes and sometimes hold traffic briefly. *Values:* Thousands of permits a year [U]; traffic holds up to ~5 min [uncertain: the permitted hold length was not re-checkable this session]; parking taken 1-3 days. `Low · World input · public/forecast`

   * **Holiday spectacles and fireworks**: Short events with frozen zones and crowds: the Rockefeller tree lighting, July 4 fireworks, SantaCon. *Values:* 2-6 h closures; local pickups -40 to -60% during, a burst right after. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc 2023: tree lighting (Nov 29) Midtown Center pickups -59% 17-22h; July 4 east-side pickups 21-24h were 1.46x the 18-21h count vs 0.69-0.79x on normal Tuesdays; SantaCon +18% in downtown bar zones 12-20h · Data: NYC Permitted Event Information; organiser announcements [U] · Model: Event objects with a closure and a post-event burst · [R03-035]</sub>
   * **Other races and mass rides**: Bike tours, half marathons and triathlons that close highways and bridges on weekend mornings. *Values:* Five Boro Bike Tour (May, ~32k riders; FDR, BQE, Verrazzano) [U]; NYC Half (Mar); NYC Triathlon (Jul, West Side Hwy) [U] [uncertain: not re-checkable this session; web search budget used up and source hosts blocked]. `Low · World input · public/forecast`

   * **Outer-borough parades and festivals**: Big parades and street festivals outside Manhattan, such as West Indian Day (Eastern Parkway, Labor Day), Lunar New Year (Flushing) and the Mermaid Parade (Coney Island). *Values:* Up to 1-2M spectators for West Indian Day [U]; closures 6-10 h [uncertain: organiser crowd claims and closure hours not re-checkable this session]. `Low · World input · public/forecast · all (app drivers most)`

* **Unplanned incidents and emergencies**
   * **Weather-triggered emergencies, travel bans and transit suspensions**: Official orders in big storms that ban non-emergency driving, suspend subway or bus service, or close bridges. Weather thresholds and examples only; the emergency object itself is R03-044. *Values:* Jan 2016 travel ban during the 27.3 in storm [U]; 26-27 Jan 2015: travel ban and full subway shutdown for a storm forecast at 20-30 in that gave 9.8 in at Central Park [U for ban and forecast; P* for 9.8 in]; above-ground subway lines suspended in heavy snow [U]. `High · Rule/price · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Snowfall totals [P*]; ban and shutdown details [U] · Data: NYC Emergency Management / NYC Notify; MTA service alerts; no machine-readable archive found · Model: Scenario flag with start and end time: ban stops new trips except exempt classes (configurable); transit suspension boosts car demand on affected corridors (R04-026). · [R04-016]</sub>
   * **Power outages and dark signals**: Grid failures that darken traffic signals and stop subway lines in an area. *Values:* Rare large ones, e.g. the West Side blackout of Jul 13 2019 (~73k customers, ~5 h) [U]; dark signals act as all-way stops. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U]; robotaxis stalled at dark signals in the San Francisco blackout of Dec 2025 [U] [uncertain: news recollection, not re-checkable this session] · Data: Con Edison outage map; DOE Form OE-417 electric emergency incident and disturbance reports (oe.netl.doe.gov) [corrected by fact-check: the form is the Department of Energy's OE-417, not an EIA form, S] · Model: Area polygon with higher intersection delay, subway down and robotaxi pause · [R03-043]</sub>
   * **Protests and demonstrations**: Marches and blockades of streets, bridges and tunnels, sometimes with curfews or vehicle restrictions. *Values:* Tens to hundreds a year; bridge or tunnel blockades 0.5-3 h [U]; 2020 curfew with Manhattan vehicle restrictions [U] [uncertain: not re-checkable this session; web search budget used up and source hosts blocked]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`

   * **Traffic crashes and breakdowns (incident object)**: Random crashes that block lanes, bridges or tunnels; this domain owns the incident object and alert, R10-025 owns the speed effect. *Values:* ~100k reported crashes a year after 2020 [U]; major bridge or highway blockages 15-90 min. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NYPD collisions dataset (see R10-025) · Data: NYPD Motor Vehicle Collisions - Crashes (NYC Open Data h9gi-nx95; police-reported crashes with an injury, a death or at least $1,000 damage, P* metadata copy); 511NY incidents · Model: Poisson by road class x hour x weather; published to the agent after a latency (R03-062) · [R03-038]</sub>
   * **Cellular or platform outages**: Phone network or app outages that stop app matching while street hails continue. *Values:* 0-2 a year, 1-12 h (e.g. the nationwide carrier outage of Feb 2024) [U] [uncertain: not re-checkable this session; web search budget used up and source hosts blocked]. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U]; cross-link R06-051 · Data: none found · Model: Switch off app matching for a share of drivers and riders · [R03-045]</sub>
   * **Emergency-vehicle encounters and first-responder scenes**: How often a robotaxi must yield to sirens or get past an active fire, EMS or police scene, and how long it stalls or waits for remote help. *Values:* The San Francisco Fire Department logged 55 AV interference incidents by 8 Aug 2023, most since May (34 Cruise, 18 Waymo, 3 unclear), and 90-odd by Nov 2023, about two-thirds Cruise. The counts are self-reported and probably low. Delay 1-10 min per stalled encounter [design]. Encounter rate by zone from NYC EMS and fire dispatch counts.. `Low · World input · hidden · robotaxi; fleet (human drivers: small yield delay only)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Mission Local (Aug and Nov 2023) and Axios/SF Chronicle via snippets. [corrected: '55 from Jan to Aug' -> logged by 8 Aug, mostly since May]. NYC rates are [design]. · Data: NYC Open Data EMS Incident Dispatch Data https://data.cityofnewyork.us/Public-Safety/EMS-Incident-Dispatch-Data/76xm-jjuj ; Fire Incident Dispatch Data https://data.cityofnewyork.us/Public-Safety/Fire-Incident-Dispatch-Data/8m42-w767 (both confirmed in the data.gov catalog [S]) · Model: Poisson encounters per car-hour by zone and hour from dispatch counts, each with an AV stall probability and delay, plus an optional regulator-complaint counter. · [G1-041]</sub>
   * **Fires, gas leaks, steam and manhole incidents**: Emergencies that close one or several blocks for hours. *Values:* Closures 1-6 h; several a day citywide [U] [uncertain: not re-checkable this session; web search budget used up and source hosts blocked]. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] FDNY incident data · Data: FDNY Incidents Responded to by Fire Companies (NYC Open Data) [U] · Model: Random block closures with a duration distribution · [R03-039]</sub>
   * **Police activity, crime scenes and security alerts**: Closures for investigations, suspicious packages or shootings, including station evacuations. *Values:* Minor ones daily; major ones (e.g. the 2022 Sunset Park subway shooting) 0-3 a year [U]; closures 1-8 h [uncertain: not re-checkable this session; web search budget used up and source hosts blocked]. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] · Data: none found (news only) · Model: Random closure polygons; major cases also trigger a transit outage and a car-demand surge · [R03-041]</sub>
   * **Water main breaks**: Burst mains that flood streets and sometimes subway stations, closing roads for hours to days. *Values:* ~400-500 breaks a year [U] [uncertain: DEP figure not re-checkable this session]; closures 4-48 h; sometimes a subway line suspension. `Low · World input · hidden`

   * **Planned subway service changes**: Scheduled weekend and overnight suspensions with shuttle buses that raise car demand along affected corridors. *Values:* Several lines most weekends; 22:00-05:00 weeknight work [U] [uncertain: not re-checkable this session; web search budget used up and source hosts blocked]. `Low · World input · public/forecast`

* **Construction and lane closures**

   * **Overnight and weekend bridge, tunnel and highway work**: Scheduled closures of lanes or whole crossings for maintenance at night or on weekends. *Values:* Typically 22:00-05:00, or Fri 23:00-Mon 05:00 [U] [uncertain: not re-checkable this session; web search budget used up and source hosts blocked]. `Low · World input · public/forecast`

### 5. Weather (43)
*Rain, snow, heat, storms and visibility, and how each changes demand, driving and the people involved.*

* **Rain and precipitation**
   * **Precipitation state and hourly intensity**: Whether it is dry, raining or snowing in each hour, and how hard; the master input that drives the demand, speed, risk and robotaxi rules. *Values:* dry; trace (<0.01 in/h); light (<0.10 in/h, ~2.5 mm); moderate (0.10-0.30 in/h); heavy (>0.30 in/h); extreme (>1 in/h, Ida 2021 ~3.15 in in one hour [U]). Central Park 2009-2025 per year: 126 days >=0.01 in, 80 >=0.1 in, 56 >=0.25 in, 34.5 >=0.5 in, 13.6 >=1 in, 2.5 >=2 in; mean 50.5 in/yr [P*]. `High · World input · agent sees · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: Brodeur & Nield 2018: Uber rides +25% per rainy hour vs taxi +4% [S] [uncertain: IZA/JEBO hosts unreachable and the search budget was exhausted, so the +25%/+4% figures were not re-read at source]; our TLC Jan 2023: Uber trips +6.3% on wet days (t=2.3), yellow -0.5% [P] · Data: NOAA NCEI Local Climatological Data (hourly), Central Park USW00094728 https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/products/land-based-station/local-climatological-data ; GHCN-Daily https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/cdo-web/datasets/GHCND/stations/GHCND:USW00094728/detail ; IEM ASOS https://mesonet.agron.iastate.edu/request/download.phtml · Model: Hourly series per scenario day (replayed from LCD or generated), binned into 5 intensity classes that index the demand, speed, crash and robotaxi tables. · [R04-001]</sub>
   * **Rain demand uplift for app requests**: Extra ride requests when it rains; completed app trips rise but completed taxi trips do not, because taxi supply is fixed and fully used [uncertain: an inference; TLC files cannot show taxi occupancy or unmet hails]. *Values:* App ride volume +10-30% per rainy hour [design range anchored on Brodeur & Nield's +25% for Uber] [corrected by fact-check: was 'requests' tagged [S range]; no source gives this range, and Brodeur & Nield count completed rides, not requests]; daily, wet day (>=0.10 in) vs dry, Jan 2023: Uber +6.3% (t=2.3), Lyft +3.9% (t=1.5), yellow -0.5% (n.s.) [P]; heavy rain larger than light [U]. `High · World input · hidden · core MVP · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet; taxi (as latent hail demand)`
      * <sub>Evidence: Brodeur & Nield 2018 IZA DP 9986: Uber +25%/rainy hour vs taxi +4% [S] [uncertain: IZA/JEBO hosts unreachable and the search budget was exhausted, so the +25%/+4% figures were not re-read at source]; our own TLC x NOAA daily regressions with weekday and trend controls [P] · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV and yellow trip records https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page joined to NOAA LCD hourly precipitation · Model: Multiply the zone x hour request rate by m_rain(intensity, hour, day type), fitted by Poisson regression of HVFHV counts on hourly rain with hour-of-week fixed effects. · [R04-002]</sub>
   * **Pickup ETA inflation in rain**: Rain lengthens the time from request to driver arrival; the curb dwell after arrival does not change. *Values:* Uber median request-to-on-scene +0.23 min on wet days (base 2.6 min, +9%, t=3.0); 17:00-20:00 wet 2.6-3.1 vs dry 2.0-2.5 min (+22% to +40%) [corrected by fact-check: this evening comparison uses the 4 Tue-Thu days with >=0.4 in against dry Tue-Thu days, without a trend control; only the +9% daily figure uses the >=0.10 in definition with weekday and trend controls]; on-scene-to-pickup dwell 0.79 min, unchanged (t=0.2) [P]. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 analysis [P] · Data: TLC HVFHV request_datetime, on_scene_datetime, pickup_datetime · Model: Emerges from matching (R06) under slowed speeds and tighter supply; use +9% daily and +22% to +40% in the evening as validation targets. · [R04-009]</sub>
   * **Rain effect by hour, day type and trip purpose**: The same rain raises demand more at some hours and on some day types (errands, evening, nightlife) and can cut discretionary weekend trips when heavy. *Values:* Uber, wet vs dry Tue-Thu, Jan 2023: +2% to +6% at 05:00-09:00, +14% to +25% at 10:00-23:00 [P, not trend-adjusted] [corrected by fact-check: 'wet' here means the 4 Tue-Thu days with >=0.4 in (3, 19, 25 and 26 Jan) against dry Tue-Thu days; hours 00:00-04:00 also show +13% to +27%, so part of the gap is the upward trend within January, not rain]; heavy-rain Sunday 30 Apr 2023: yellow -24% to -29% at 19:00-21:00 [P]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our TLC hourly comparisons [P]; R02 notes rain at 6 pm hits a market already short of cars [design] · Data: TLC trip records + NOAA LCD hourly · Model: Interact the rain multiplier with hour-of-week buckets and trip-purpose shares (commute vs discretionary) from R05. · [R04-004]</sub>
   * **Rain onset surge and decay**: Demand jumps most in the first hour or two after rain starts, when people are caught out, then fades as trips are postponed or already made. *Values:* First-hour peak 1.2-1.6x, decay half-life 1-3 h [design guess]; 29 Sep 2023 flood: yellow trips +46% to +63% from 05:00-09:00, back to about -3% to +5% by 12:00-15:00 vs 4 reference Fridays [P]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our TLC hourly event study of 29 Sep 2023 [P]; curve shape is a [design guess] · Data: TLC trip records + NOAA LCD hourly or IEM 1-minute ASOS for onset times · Model: Multiplier = 1 + a*intensity + b*exp(-(t - onset)/tau); fit a, b, tau by event study on rain onsets. · [R04-003]</sub>
   * **Street-hail intensity in rain (taxi)**: How many more people try to hail a yellow cab per mile cruised when it rains, mostly in high-foot-traffic zones. *Values:* Hail attempts +20-50% in rain in the Manhattan core [design guess]; completed yellow trips flat on wet days (-0.5%), so the cab is full rather than demand-limited [P]. `Med · World input · hidden · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our TLC: yellow completed trips flat while app trips rise [P]; Farber 2015 'Why You Can't Find a Taxi in the Rain' [U for rain result] · Data: TLC yellow trip records; R01 pedestrian density · Model: Raise the street-hail arrival rate per road segment by weather, scaled by pedestrian density, so the hail-encounter probability per cruising minute rises. · [R04-007]</sub>
   * **Surge response to weather**: How much app prices and driver pay rise when rain creates a supply shortage; the yellow meter does not change. *Values:* Uber daily averages on wet days, Jan 2023: base fare per mile +$0.09 (+2%, t=1.4), driver pay per engaged hour +$0.46 (+0.8%, t=1.8) [P] (weekday controls only, no trend control); hourly peaks much larger [U]; yellow tariff fixed. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our TLC HVFHV analysis [P]; Brodeur & Nield: surge lets app supply respond to rain [S] [corrected by fact-check: impact lowered from high to medium; the measured daily price and pay responses are only 1-2%, and the larger hourly surge peaks are unmeasured [U]] · Data: TLC HVFHV base_passenger_fare, driver_pay · Model: Surge multiplier from zone demand/supply ratio (R07); weather enters only through demand and speed, not as a separate weather fee. · [R04-011]</sub>
   * **Mode substitution from walking, cycling and transit**: Bad weather moves trips off walking, Citi Bike, e-bikes and outdoor bus waits onto cars; this is the pool the extra car demand comes from. *Values:* Citi Bike trips drop sharply on rainy days (order of -30% to -60%) [U]; subway ridership dips in storms [U]. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Background knowledge of Citi Bike and MTA ridership on rain days; not computed here [U] [corrected by fact-check: impact lowered from medium to low; this variable is only a calibration check, and its demand effect is already carried by R04-002] · Data: Citi Bike System Data https://citibikenyc.com/system-data ; MTA Daily Ridership https://data.ny.gov/ · Model: Use Citi Bike and subway drops on weather days as a calibration check on the car-demand uplift, not as a separate generator. · [R04-006]</sub>
   * **Shift toward short trips in bad weather**: Rain and cold add short trips that people would otherwise walk, changing the trip-length mix and the number of pickups per hour. *Values:* Yellow share of trips under 1 mile: 20.5% on dry days; +0.5 pp light, +1.2 pp moderate, +1.4 pp heavy rain; +2.5 pp on cold days (max <=35°F); Uber +1.1 pp on wet days (base 10%) [P]. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our 2023 yellow daily regressions, t=2.7 to 6.2; Uber Jan 2023 t=3.7 [P] [corrected by fact-check: impact lowered from medium to low; the shifts are 0.5-2.5 points of the trip mix and the yellow fare per trip did not change significantly (heavy rain +$0.1, t=0.7), so the effect on profit per hour is small] · Data: TLC trip records (trip_distance, trip_miles) + NOAA GHCN-Daily · Model: Weather-dependent re-weighting of the trip-distance / OD distribution toward intra-zone and adjacent-zone trips. · [R04-005]</sub>
   * **Weather-driven cancellations and no-shows**: Riders cancel when ETAs grow or they find another car; drivers cancel long or flooded pickups. *Values:* Cancellation rate +2 to +5 pp in heavy rain [design guess]; public TLC files contain completed trips only. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: No public data found [design guess] · Data: none found (TLC HVFHV has completed trips only) · Model: Rider cancel hazard rising with ETA, scaled up in rain; driver-side cancel exposed as an agent action. · [R04-010]</sub>
* **Snow, ice and winter roads**
   * **Snowfall rate and accumulation**: How much snow falls per hour and in total, which slows traffic, changes demand and can stop robotaxis. *Values:* Central Park 2009-2025: mean 28.9 in/season (2.3 in 2022-23 to 61.9 in 2010-11); per year 10.8 days >=0.1 in, 6.9 >=1 in, 2.7 >=4 in, 1.4 >=6 in, 0.4 >=10 in; record 27.3 in on 23 Jan 2016 (fact-check: with 0.2 in on 22 Jan the storm total is 27.5 in [P*], the largest Central Park storm on record [U]); 2026: 11.4 in on 25 Jan, 19.7 in on 22-23 Feb [P*]. `High · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: NYC DOT sensor speed x0.51 in heavy snow [S]; 16 Jan 2024 (1.3 in): yellow trips -5%, morning speeds -14% to -23% [P] · Data: NOAA GHCN-Daily Central Park (SNOW, SNWD) via GitHub copy https://github.com/toddwschneider/nyc-taxi-data ; NOAA LCD present-weather codes · Model: Hourly snowfall rate and running depth drive speed (R04-008), demand (R04-017), crash risk (R04-041) and robotaxi limits (R04-039). · [R04-013]</sub>
   * **Freezing rain, sleet and black ice**: Icy precipitation and refreezing that make bridges and ramps slippery and raise crash risk more than snow does. *Values:* A few days per year [U]; highest crash multiplier of any weather type [U]; bridges and elevated roads ice first [U]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Road-weather background knowledge; FHWA pages unreachable [U] · Data: NOAA LCD present-weather codes (FZRA, PL); NWS winter weather advisories (IEM archive) https://mesonet.agron.iastate.edu/ · Model: Precipitation-type flag that applies the snow speed penalty plus a higher crash multiplier, larger on bridges and elevated links. · [R04-015]</sub>
   * **Snow cover and plowing state after a storm**: Snow left on roads and curbs for days after a storm: narrower lanes, blocked curbs and slow side streets. *Values:* Snow depth >=1 in on 22.5 days/yr at Central Park [P*]; side streets 1-3 days to clear [U]. `Med · Changes during run · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Depth statistics [P*]; clearing times [U] · Data: GHCN-Daily SNWD; NYC DSNY snow-plow tracking (PlowNYC) [U, URL not verified] · Model: Per-borough 'cleared' state that recovers over 1-3 days, adding speed and pickup-dwell penalties on uncleared streets. · [R04-014]</sub>
   * **Snow-day demand mix**: Snow changes who travels: closed schools and remote work cut commutes, while people who must travel switch from walking and buses to cars. *Values:* Small snows (0.1-1.3 in), 2023-24: yellow trips -3% (n.s.); 16 Jan 2024 -5% [P]; big storms likely -30% or more during the storm, rebound afterwards [U]; NYC public schools now go remote on snow days [U]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our TLC daily regressions [P]; big-storm effect [U] · Data: TLC trip records + GHCN-Daily; NYC public school closure announcements · Model: Snow multiplier on demand by trip purpose: commute down, airport and medical stable, short trips up. · [R04-017]</sub>
   * **Digging out after a storm**: Street-parked cars get plowed in after snow, so drivers lose 30-90 minutes clearing the car before the first trip on high-demand snow days. *Values:* Delay of 30-90 min after >= 4 in of snow for street-parked cars; about 0 for garaged cars and depot fleets [design]. NYC suspends alternate-side parking during snow operations, while parking meters stay in force.. `Low · World input · agent sees · human app driver; taxi (garages and robotaxi depots exempt)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; [S] 311 and press (PIX11, Newsweek) on alternate-side parking suspensions for snow operations. · Data: NOAA GHCN-Daily snowfall (Central Park USW00094728); NYC 311 alternate-side parking status · Model: Draw a shift-start delay when overnight snowfall passes a threshold, depending on parking type (R08-052). · [G1-016]</sub>
   * **Winter road damage and salt**: Potholes, road salt and slush add tyre, alignment, corrosion and car-wash costs in late winter. *Values:* Pothole complaints peak Feb-Apr [U]; +$5-15 per week in washing and wear in winter [design guess]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] and [design guess] · Data: NYC 311 Service Requests (potholes) https://data.cityofnewyork.us/Social-Services/311-Service-Requests-from-2010-to-Present/erm2-nwe9 · Model: Seasonal maintenance cost-per-mile multiplier (cross-link R08). · [R04-018]</sub>
* **Heat, cold and temperature**
   * **Extreme cold and wind chill**: Very cold hours that cut EV range, slow charging, fail weak batteries and push people into cars. *Values:* Daily min <=20°F on 14.6 days/yr, <=10°F on 2.1 days/yr; lowest -1°F on 14 Feb 2016 [P*] [corrected by fact-check: this is the 2009-2025 minimum in our copy, not the all-time record; the Central Park record low is -15°F in Feb 1934 [U]]. `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] frequency; effects via R04-037/038 [S]/[U] · Data: NOAA LCD; GHCN-Daily · Model: Cold flag triggering EV range and charging penalties, a small 12V battery failure chance [design guess] and the short-trip shift. · [R04-022]</sub>
   * **Heat waves and heat advisories**: Multi-day hot spells with official advisories that raise short-trip demand, strain power and transit, and tire drivers. *Values:* NWS NYC heat advisory roughly heat index >=95°F for 2+ days or >=100°F [U]; 2.8 days/yr >=95°F [P*]; subway heat and A/C failures [U]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] frequency; effects [U] · Data: NWS alerts (IEM archive) https://mesonet.agron.iastate.edu/ ; NOAA LCD · Model: Heat-event flag raising short-trip demand, A/C energy and fatigue, with a small chance of correlated power or transit outages. · [R04-021]</sub>
   * **Hourly air temperature, humidity and heat index**: Outdoor temperature (with dew point for heat index and wind for wind chill) each hour. *Values:* Central Park 2009-2025 mean daily max 39.6°F (Jan) to 86.0°F (Jul); per year 62.9 days with min <=32°F, 18.1 days max >=90°F, 2.8 days >=95°F; highest 104°F on 22 Jul 2011 [P*] [corrected by fact-check: this is the 2009-2025 maximum in our copy, not the all-time record; the Central Park record is 106°F on 9 Jul 1936 [U]]. `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our climate statistics from GHCN-Daily [P*]; drives EV energy, demand and comfort · Data: NOAA LCD hourly dry-bulb and dew point; GHCN-Daily TMAX/TMIN · Model: Hourly series feeding the demand (R04-020), HVAC energy (R04-023) and EV range (R04-037) functions. · [R04-019]</sub>
   * **Temperature demand effect**: How hot and cold days change the number of rides and how short they are. *Values:* Yellow trips vs 51-80°F days, with month and weekday controls, 2023: max >=90°F +22% (t=1.9), 81-89°F +11% (t=1.6), max <=35°F +4% (n.s.); short-trip share +2.5 pp on cold days (t=5.2), +1.3 pp on hot days (t=3.2) [P]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our 2023 TLC regressions, small samples (12 hot, 9 cold days) [P] · Data: TLC trip records + GHCN-Daily · Model: Temperature-band multipliers on demand and short-trip share, refitted on several years before use. · [R04-020]</sub>
* **Storms, wind and flooding**
   * **Flash flooding and flooded roads**: Extreme rain that floods parkways, underpasses and basements, closing links for hours. *Values:* Central Park extreme days: 7.13 in (1 Sep 2021, Ida), 5.48 in (29 Sep 2023), 4.45 in (21 Aug 2021); >=2 in on 2.5 days/yr [P*]; flood-prone FDR, BQE, Major Deegan, Grand Central and Bronx River parkways [U]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: 29 Sep 2023: yellow-trip average speeds at 08:00-09:00 -29% to -35% [P] [corrected by fact-check: all yellow trips citywide (mostly Manhattan), not a Manhattan-only measure]; Waymo fleet recall May 2026 after flooded-road incidents [S, via R06] [uncertain: not re-checked at source; news hosts unreachable] · Data: FloodNet NYC street-flood sensors https://www.floodnet.nyc/ [U]; NYC 311 street-flooding complaints; GHCN-Daily · Model: When hourly rain passes a threshold (e.g. 1.5 in/h [design guess]), close or slow a list of flood-prone links for 1-6 h; entering a closed link carries damage risk (R04-043). · [R04-025]</sub>
   * **Hurricanes, tropical storms and nor'easters**: Rare multi-hazard storms combining heavy rain, wind, storm-surge flooding, power cuts and transit shutdowns over 1-3 days. *Values:* About one major event every few years [U]; Irene 28 Aug 2011 (3.99 in; 6.87 in over 27-28 Aug), Sandy 29 Oct 2012 (mean wind 18.8 mph), Henri and Ida Aug-Sep 2021 [P*]; evacuation zones 1-6 [U]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] station data; scenario consequences [U] · Data: NOAA NHC HURDAT2 https://www.nhc.noaa.gov/data/ ; NYC hurricane evacuation zones (NYC Open Data) [U] · Model: Scripted scenario pack combining R04-025/026/029/030, with demand collapse during the storm and a surge afterwards. · [R04-028]</sub>
   * **Weather-driven transit disruption (weather cause of R03-046)**: Subway and rail suspensions caused by flooding, snow, heat or storms, which push riders onto cars. R03-046 owns the subway-incident object; R04 supplies the weather trigger and its demand evidence. *Values:* 29 Sep 2023 flood with subway suspensions [uncertain: the suspensions are background knowledge; only the trip changes come from our data]: yellow trips +17% for the day and +46% to +63% from 05:00-09:00 [P]; full MTA shutdowns for Irene (2011), Sandy (2012) and the Jan 2015 blizzard [U]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our TLC hourly event study [P] · Data: MTA ridership https://data.ny.gov/ ; MTA GTFS-realtime alerts (no historical archive found) · Model: Disruption event that boosts car demand in the catchment of suspended lines (cross-link R03, R05). · [R04-026]</sub>
   * **Airport weather delays and cancellations (weather cause of R03-049)**: Thunderstorms, snow, wind and low cloud cause ground stops and cancellations at JFK, LGA and EWR, shifting airport pickup demand in time. R03-049 owns the airport-disruption object. *Values:* Summer thunderstorm ground stops; mass cancellations in snowstorms [U]; airport pickup share did not change on rain days in 2023 (+0.2 to -0.4 pp, n.s.) [P]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our TLC airport share [P]; delay mechanisms [U] · Data: BTS Airline On-Time Performance https://www.transtats.bts.gov/ ; FAA airport status (live) · Model: Weather perturbs flight-arrival banks: delays shift airport demand later, cancellations remove it (cross-link R01-052, R05-063). · [R04-031]</sub>
   * **Thunderstorms and lightning**: Short, intense, often local summer storms that cause sudden demand spikes, airport ground stops and street flooding. *Values:* Mostly May-Sep afternoons; 30-120 min; rain rates can exceed 1 in/h [U]; GSOD thunder flag shows 0 days in 2023 at Central Park, so station flags are unreliable [P*]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U]; station-flag check [P*] · Data: NOAA LCD weather-type codes (TS); NEXRAD on AWS https://registry.opendata.aws/noaa-nexrad/ ; NWS warnings (IEM) · Model: Convective-cell generator: short heavy-rain bursts over a subset of zones, coupled to airport delays (R04-031). · [R04-024]</sub>
   * **Weather cancellation and delay of outdoor events (input to R03-020, R03-021)**: Rainouts, rain delays and postponed outdoor concerts move or remove event egress surges. R03-020/R03-021 own event cancellation and weather-at-let-out effects. *Values:* Baseball rainouts and delays; outdoor concerts and parades postponed [U]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] · Data: Retrosheet game logs (rainouts) https://www.retrosheet.org/ [uncertain: game logs list games played; postponements and their reasons are recalled to sit in Retrosheet's schedule files, not re-read]; NYC permitted events (NYC Open Data) · Model: Event cancel/delay probability conditioned on rain forecast and observed rain; shifts or removes the R03 egress surge. · [R04-032]</sub>
   * **Post-storm fuel and charging shortages**: Fuel or charging scarcity after a major storm. *Values:* Odd-even gas rationing in NYC in Nov 2012 after Sandy [U]. `Low · World input · public/forecast · human app driver, taxi, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] · Data: none found · Model: Fuel-availability flag: refuelling takes longer or fails (cross-link R08). · [R04-030]</sub>
   * **Wind speed and gusts**: Wind that brings down trees and branches and triggers bridge restrictions; little direct effect on cars otherwise. *Values:* Central Park mean daily wind 5.3 mph (sensor sheltered by trees; LGA/JFK windier); 2023: 5 days with gusts >=30 kn, max 34 kn (gusts reported on only 213 of 365 days); Sandy day mean 18.8 mph [P*]; bridge wind limits mostly hit trucks and motorcycles [U]. `Low · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] station data; restrictions [U] · Data: NOAA LCD (LGA USW00014732, JFK USW00094789); NOAA GSOD · Model: Gust threshold triggers random tree-down link closures and bridge restrictions. · [R04-027]</sub>
* **Visibility, light and air quality**
   * **Fog and low visibility**: Fog and mist that slow traffic slightly, raise crash risk, limit robotaxis and delay flights. *Values:* Central Park 2023 (GSOD): fog flagged on 27 days; daily mean visibility <5 mi on 18 days, <3 mi on 2, never <1 mi [P*]. `Low · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] station data; effects [U] · Data: NOAA LCD visibility; NOAA GSOD FRSHTT flags · Model: Visibility class adds a small speed and crash penalty and feeds robotaxi limits (R04-039) and airport delays (R04-031). · [R04-033]</sub>
   * **Low-sun glare**: Low sun shining straight down a street blinds drivers and cameras, especially along Manhattan's crosstown grid. *Values:* Sunset azimuth ~299° (grid-aligned) around 24-25 May and 17-18 Jul; sunrise ~119° around 2-3 Dec and 8-9 Jan (2026, our calculation) [P] (fact-check: reproduced with an independent NOAA solar-position calculation) [uncertain: these are dates for the sun at the geometric horizon; the published 'Manhattanhenge' dates, with the sun just above the real horizon, are about 28-30 May and 11-13 Jul, recalled not re-read; glare risk spans several days around both]; camera-based driving struggles in glare and low visibility (NHTSA probe) [S]. `Low · World input · agent sees · all; camera-only robotaxi most`
      * <sub>Evidence: Sun-angle calculation [P]; NHTSA FSD low-visibility probe reported in news [S] [uncertain: the cited GitHub source is near-empty on re-check; the probe was opened in Oct 2024 [U] and its later status was not re-checked] · Data: astral sun position; OSM street bearings https://download.geofabrik.de/north-america/us/new-york.html · Model: When sun elevation <10° and street bearing within ±10° of sun azimuth, small speed and crash penalty on those links. · [R04-036]</sub>
   * **Wildfire smoke and air quality alerts**: Smoke or ozone episodes that push people from walking and cycling into cars and cancel outdoor events. *Values:* 7 Jun 2023 Canadian smoke (AQI near 480 at worst [U]): yellow trips +4.8% for the day, +15% to +17% at 14:00-15:00 vs nearby Wednesdays [P]; NYS air quality health advisories on several summer days a year [S]. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our TLC event comparison [P]; NYS DEC advisory texts in a public dataset [S] · Data: EPA AirNow API https://docs.airnowapi.org/ ; EPA AQS https://aqs.epa.gov/aqsweb/documents/data_api.html ; Open-Meteo air-quality API · Model: AQI series; above ~150, shift walking and cycling trips to cars, cancel outdoor events, raise driver discomfort. · [R04-034]</sub>
* **Weather limits on vehicles**
   * **Cold-battery charging speed and preconditioning (see R08-010)**: Cold batteries charge slowly unless warmed first; hot batteries can throttle. Shared with R08-010. *Values:* DC fast-charge power reduced with a cold battery; preconditioning 10-30 min uses energy [U]. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver (EV), robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] · Data: AFDC station locator https://afdc.energy.gov/stations ; no public charging-curve-by-temperature data found · Model: Charging-power curve scaled by battery temperature; agent may precondition while driving to a charger at an energy cost. · [R04-038]</sub>
   * **Flood water damage and stranding risk**: Chance that driving into standing water stalls or wrecks the car. *Values:* About 6 in of water can stall a car; flood-damaged cars are often written off [U]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] · Data: FloodNet depths https://www.floodnet.nyc/ ; no damage data found · Model: If the route crosses a flooded link above a depth threshold, stochastic stall: car stuck for hours plus repair cost. · [R04-043]</sub>
   * **Weather crash-risk multiplier for the agent's car**: Higher chance that the agent's own car crashes in rain, snow, ice or fog, with repair cost and lost days. *Values:* NYC crashes per hour in rainy vs dry hours at the same hour of day (2023-2025, not exposure-adjusted): about -1% to +5% at 08:00-17:00, about +10% at 00:00, 07:00 and 18:00, and +20% to +34% at most hours from 19:00 to 06:00 [S] [corrected by fact-check: was '10-35% higher' at every hour; the notebook output shows almost no difference in daytime, and its 'rain' hours include trace amounts]; snow and ice higher [U]; cost: deductible $500-2,500 plus 2-10 days off the road [design guess]. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Jususs/NYC_Big_Data notebook joining NYPD crashes to NOAA LCD [S] [corrected by fact-check: impact lowered from medium to low; crashes are rare per driver-hour, so even a 20-30% weather multiplier changes expected profit per hour very little, though one crash can dominate a single run [design guess]] · Data: NYPD Motor Vehicle Collisions https://data.cityofnewyork.us/Public-Safety/Motor-Vehicle-Collisions-Crashes/h9gi-nx95 + NOAA LCD · Model: Poisson crash hazard per mile x weather multiplier; a crash ends the shift and charges cost (cross-link R08, R10-025). · [R04-041]</sub>
* **Weather and people (riders, tips, drivers)**
   * **Driver weather log-off thresholds**: How human drivers (competitors and the agent's persona) cut hours in snow, ice or floods, which they do not do in ordinary rain. *Values:* Rain: taxi supply about flat (Farber 2015) [U]; heavy snow: supply -10% to -40% [U, as R06-050]; NYC cab drivers respond positively to higher earnings opportunities (Farber 2015 abstract) [P*]. `Med · Rule/price · hidden · taxi, human app driver, competitor supply`
      * <sub>Evidence: Farber 2015 QJE abstract via NBER metadata copy [P*] (fact-check: confirmed in the NBER w20604 metadata, Oct 2014; the abstract does not mention rain, so the rain result stays [U]); snow numbers [U] · Data: TLC trip records (no driver IDs in current files); Farber used 2009-2013 TLC data · Model: Weather-dependent log-off hazard in the competitor supply model (cross-link R06-050). · [R04-046]</sub>
   * **Rider willingness to pay in bad weather**: Riders accept higher prices when it is wet or very cold, so surge loses fewer of them. *Values:* Price elasticity 10-30% lower in rain [design guess]. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] general ride-hail pricing literature; none NYC-specific found · Data: none public at request level · Model: Weather term in the rider price-acceptance function (R05, R07). · [R04-045]</sub>
   * **Driver comfort and fatigue in heat and cold**: Extreme heat or cold makes a human driver tire faster. *Values:* Fatigue rate +10-20% on extreme-temperature days [design guess]. `Low · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design guess]; cross-link R09-007 · Data: none found · Model: Temperature term in the fatigue accumulation function. · [R04-048]</sub>
   * **Tipping response to weather**: Whether riders tip more in bad weather; in the data they barely do. *Values:* Yellow card tips 22.3% of fare on dry days; rain days +0.1 to +0.2 pp (n.s.); Uber tip incidence 19.6%, +0.3 pp on wet days (n.s.) [P]; sunnier days slightly higher tips (Devaraj & Patel 2017) [U]. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our 2023 yellow and Jan 2023 HVFHV regressions [P] · Data: TLC tip_amount (card trips) and HVFHV tips · Model: Keep tip rates weather-independent by default; optional small multiplier. · [R04-044]</sub>
* **Weather scenarios and data**
   * **Seasonal climate mix per scenario day**: How likely each weather type is in each month, which sets how often each effect appears in randomly drawn scenario days. *Values:* Central Park 2009-2025: wet-day (>=0.01 in) probability 0.28 (Sep) to 0.39 (May, Dec); snow-day probability Jan 0.11, Feb 0.13, Mar 0.05, Dec 0.06; days >=90°F per month Jul 8.1, Aug 4.8, Jun 2.7; freezing nights Jan 19.7 [P*]. `High · Benchmark setting · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our GHCN-Daily climate statistics [P*] · Data: NOAA GHCN-Daily Central Park · Model: Scenario-day sampler stratified by month and weather type; report the weather class with every score. · [R04-054]</sub>
   * **Spatial resolution of weather**: Whether one station represents the whole city or weather varies by zone. *Values:* One station (Central Park) / 4-5 stations (Central Park USW00094728, LGA USW00014732, JFK USW00094789, EWR USW00014734, Teterboro USW00094741) / 1-km radar grid [P* station IDs; design]; summer storms can be very local [U]. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Station list from a public NOAA LCD analysis [P*]; locality [U] · Data: NOAA LCD for the listed stations; NOAA MRMS radar · Model: Nearest-station or radar interpolation to taxi zones. · [R04-057]</sub>
   * **Temporal resolution of weather**: Daily, hourly or minute weather data; coarse data hide most of the rain effect. *Values:* Daily (GHCN) / hourly (LCD routine reports at :51) / 1-5 min (ASOS); our daily estimate (+6% Uber on wet days) vs ~+25% per rainy hour in Brodeur & Nield [P, S] [uncertain: the Brodeur & Nield figure was not re-read at source]. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Comparison of our daily estimate with the hourly literature [P, S] · Data: NOAA LCD; IEM 1-minute ASOS https://mesonet.agron.iastate.edu/request/asos/1min.phtml · Model: Hourly by default; finer only for onset dynamics. · [R04-058]</sub>
   * **Weather counterfactual and ablation pairs**: Running the same demand day with and without weather effects, or with rain injected at a chosen time, to measure how much of an agent's earnings come from handling weather. *Values:* Weather on/off; rain injected at time t; information level L0-L3 crossed with weather on/off [design]. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] · Data: n/a · Model: Harness toggles weather multipliers while holding other random draws fixed. · [R04-056]</sub>
   * **Climate trend in extremes**: Whether to include more frequent extreme rain in 'future NYC' scenarios. *Values:* 4 of the 12 wettest Central Park days in 2009-2025 fell in 2021-2023 (21 Aug, 1 Sep and 26 Oct 2021; 29 Sep 2023), 5 counting 23 Mar 2024 [corrected by fact-check: was '3 of the 12'; 3 of the 12 also fell in Aug-Sep 2011, so this clustering is weak evidence of a trend]; seasonal snowfall swings 2.3-61.9 in [P*]; heavier downpours in the Northeast long-term [U]. `Low · Benchmark setting · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] ranking; trend [U] · Data: NOAA GHCN-Daily; NYC Panel on Climate Change reports [U] · Model: Optional scenario knob raising extreme-rain frequency. · [R04-060]</sub>

### 6. Traffic and travel time (43)
*How long it takes to get from A to B, and what slows it down.*

* **Speeds and congestion**
   * **Congestion-pricing speed regime (since 5 Jan 2025)**: The step change in speeds inside and around the Manhattan Congestion Relief Zone after tolling began, which shifts all calibrated travel times. *Values:* CBD speeds +15% vs synthetic control [S] (8.2 to 9.7 mph [uncertain: implies +18%, source not traced]; a later paper version reportedly +11% [uncertain: not re-read]); >20% gains weekday 1-7 PM; trips to/within CBD ~8% faster; 2025 vs 2024 AM-peak crossing times: Holland -36%, Williamsburg -23%, Queensboro -21%, Hugh Carey -16%, Brooklyn Br -13%, Lincoln -10% (some reposts -5%) [uncertain: conflicting secondary figures], Queens-Midtown -4%. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Cook et al. NBER w33584 (Google Maps data, synthetic control vs 5 cities) [S]; NY Governor release 9 Sep 2025 [S] · Data: NBER w33584 https://www.nber.org/papers/w33584 ; MTA Congestion Relief Zone entries dashboard (data.ny.gov) [uncertain: exact dataset not re-checked]; TLC 2025+ trip records (HVFHV files carry a cbd_congestion_fee column, so post-pricing trips are identifiable [P*]) · Model: Calibrate speed profiles on post-Jan-2025 trips for current scenarios; keep a pre-2025 profile as a separate regime for historical or counterfactual scenarios. · [R10-005]</sub>
   * **Core-versus-periphery speed gradient**: How much slower the dense core (Midtown, Lower Manhattan) is than the rest of Manhattan and the outer boroughs at the same hour. *Values:* Midtown ~4.3-5 mph vs Manhattan south of 60th St ~6.9-7.1 mph (2017-2019) [S]; HVFHV Jan 2023 weekday 9 AM-6 PM trip speeds (both trip ends inside the area): Midtown core (zones 100, 161-164, 186, 230) 5.4-6.0 mph, Manhattan below 60th St 8.2-8.5 mph, citywide 12.3-15.1 mph [P*]; outer-borough arterials ~10-20 mph daytime [U]; core/citywide speed ratio 0.37-0.45 measured, ~0.3-0.6 as a design range [P*]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Sam Schwartz report from taxi+FHV GPS: Midtown 4.8 mph vs Manhattan below 60th St 6.9 mph [S] · Data: TLC trip records + taxi zone shapefile; MTA CBD Taxi and FHV Speeds (beginning Oct 2019) https://catalog.data.gov/dataset/mta-central-business-district-vehicle-speeds-beginning-october-2019 [uncertain: dataset title and URL not re-checked] · Model: Zone-level speed multiplier by hour fitted from TLC intra- and inter-zone trip speeds; creates the core trade-off of high demand vs slow trips. · [R10-002]</sub>
   * **Peak-hour slowdown curve**: Shape of the within-day speed curve (AM peak, midday plateau, PM peak, late-night free flow) applied to travel times. *Values:* Midtown to JFK median 38 min (10-11 AM) vs 64 min (4-5 PM) in 2009-2015 [S] [corrected by fact-check: 2023 TLC yellow weekday trips from the Midtown core to JFK took a median 29 min at 3-5 AM, 54 min at 10-11 AM and 75 min at 4-5 PM (P*); the 38-min figure is not reproduced and may reflect an older era or another hour]; CBD worst on weekdays 1-7 PM [S]; core Manhattan 8-9 AM ~9 mph (14.5 km/h) [S; consistent with 8.5-8.8 mph in TLC 2023 data, P*]; overnight ~1.5-3x faster than midday [corrected by fact-check: 1.6-2.2x in TLC 2023 data, e.g. Manhattan below 60th St 13.6 mph at 4 AM vs 6.8 mph at noon, Midtown core 11.8 vs 5.4 mph, P*]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Schneider analysis of TLC taxi trips 2009-2015: Midtown to JFK median 38 vs 64 min depending on hour [S]; TLC 2023 yellow trips: 29-75 min by hour [P*] · Data: TLC trip records; NYC DOT Traffic Speeds NBE (i4gi-tjb9) · Model: Hour-of-week multiplier per zone pair or edge class; the calendar that indexes it lives in R02. · [R10-003]</sub>
   * **Time-dependent link speed profile**: Typical speed on each road segment for each time bucket of the week; the base layer from which every travel time is computed. *Values:* Manhattan south of 60th St 9.1 mph (2010) to 7.1 mph (2017) [S]; 2023 yellow trips within Manhattan below 60th St: weekday 6.8-7.6 mph from 9 AM to 6 PM, 8.5 mph at 8 AM [P*: own analysis of 38.3M TLC 2023 yellow trips]; CBD 8.2 to 9.7 mph after Jan 2025 [uncertain: 8.2 to 9.7 mph is +18%, not the +15% headline, and the mph pair could not be traced to the NBER paper]; overnight local streets ~15-25 mph [corrected by fact-check: TLC trip-average speeds at 0-5 AM are 11.6-13.6 mph within Manhattan below 60th St (yellow 2023) and about 20-25 mph citywide including highways (HVFHV Jan 2023); 15-25 mph fits the outer boroughs, not Manhattan]; expressways 30-50 mph off-peak [U]; 2016 five-minute buckets per week (Valhalla [P]) or 168 hourly buckets. `High · World input · public/forecast · core MVP`
