      * <sub>Evidence: NYC DOT Mobility Report 2018: speeds south of 60th St fell 9.1 to 7.1 mph 2010-2017; trip time is the denominator of $/hour [S]; TLC 2023 trip speeds reproduce ~7 mph daytime [P*] · Data: NYC TLC trip records https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page ; NYC DOT Traffic Speeds NBE https://data.cityofnewyork.us/Transportation/DOT-Traffic-Speeds-NBE/i4gi-tjb9 ; MTA Bus Route Segment Speeds https://data.ny.gov/d/58t6-89vi · Model: Per-edge weekly speed profile (Valhalla predicted-speed format) or road-class x zone x hour-of-week multipliers on OSM free-flow speeds, calibrated so zone-pair durations match TLC. · [R10-001]</sub>
   * **Weather speed-penalty table (input to R10-006)**: How much slower traffic moves in each weather class; R10 applies it to link speeds. *Values:* NYC DOT sensors 2023-2025, median speed vs dry baseline (same sensor, day type, hour): light rain 0.95, moderate 0.91, heavy 0.91; light snow 0.91, moderate 0.80, heavy 0.51 [S] [uncertain: the ratios were re-read in the notebook output (0.946, 0.914, 0.913; 0.908, 0.798, 0.513), but its bins use thresholds of 2.5 and 10 (rain) and 2 and 5 (snow) in undocumented units, probably mm per hour, with each sensor matched to its nearest LCD station, so its classes do not map exactly onto the in/h bins in R04-001]. Our TLC: heavy-rain days -0.4 mph (t=-2.2); 29 Sep 2023 08:00-09:00 7.5-8.4 vs 11.6-11.8 mph (-29% to -35%); snow 16 Jan 2024 08:00-10:00 -14% to -23% [P]. `High · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Jususs/NYC_Big_Data notebook joining NYC DOT speeds to NOAA LCD [S]; our TLC hourly speeds [P] · Data: NYC DOT Traffic Speeds NBE https://data.cityofnewyork.us/Transportation/DOT-Traffic-Speeds-NBE/i4gi-tjb9 + NOAA LCD; TLC trip speeds · Model: Lookup multiplier by precipitation class x road class (highway vs street) x hour, applied on top of the R10 weekly profile. · [R04-008]</sub>
   * **Day-type and seasonal speed shifts**: Speed differences between weekdays, weekends, holidays, summer and the December shopping season, including official Gridlock Alert Days. *Values:* 20 Gridlock Alert Days in 2025 (UNGA week 22-26 Sep; 19, 20, 25 Nov; 12 days in Dec) [S]; Midtown under 4 mph in UNGA week [S]; weekend mornings and holiday weeks faster [U]; TLC 2023 check: weekday daytime speed within Manhattan below 60th St fell from 8.3 mph (Jan) to 6.5 mph (Sep, Dec), a ~22% seasonal swing, and the slowest weekdays were 18-20 Sep (UNGA week), 21 and 29 Nov and 5-7 Dec [P*]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: NYC DOT 2025 Gridlock Alert Days announcement: Midtown average speeds fell under 4 mph during UNGA [S] · Data: NYC DOT Gridlock Alert Days https://nyc.gov/gridlockalert ; TLC trip records by date · Model: Day-type factor (weekday / Sat / Sun / holiday / gridlock-alert) multiplying the weekly profile; gridlock days flagged in the scenario calendar. · [R10-004]</sub>
   * **Endogenous congestion from ride-hail volume**: Feedback whereby the simulated fleet's own vehicles, and FHV traffic overall, slow the streets they use. *Values:* FHVs ~30% of Manhattan-core traffic (2019); SF weekday delay +62% 2010-2016 vs +22% counterfactual without TNCs; a benchmark fleet of 1-500 cars is tiny vs NYC's tens of thousands of FHVs [U]. `Low · World input · hidden · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Erhardt et al. 2019 Science Advances [S]; TLC 23 Jul 2019 presentation [S] · Data: none needed for small N; TLC FHV trip volumes for background · Model: Ignore for N up to ~1,000; for stretch scenarios add fleet vehicles to zone-level background volume and apply a BPR-style volume-delay function. · [R10-007]</sub>
* **Travel-time variability**
   * **Airport-corridor tail risk**: Heavy right tail of trip times to and from JFK, LGA and EWR via the Van Wyck, Grand Central Parkway, BQE and Hudson crossings. *Values:* Midtown to JFK median 38-64 min by hour, P90 up to 84 min (2009-2015) [S] [corrected by fact-check: 2023 weekday Midtown core to JFK median 29-75 min, P90 up to 94 min at 4-5 PM]; LGA ~20-50 min [U] [corrected by fact-check: 2023 Midtown core to LGA median 21 min (3-5 AM) to 44 min (4 PM), P90 up to 60 min; to EWR median 30 min (night) to 54 min (3-7 PM), P90 76 min; all P*]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Schneider TLC airport analysis [S]; TLC 2023 yellow trips by hour [P*] · Data: TLC trip records (taxi zones 132 JFK, 138 LGA, 1 EWR) · Model: Separate per-corridor duration distributions with fatter tails (lognormal mixture) fitted on airport zone pairs. · [R10-010]</sub>
   * **Day-level and area-level correlated slowdowns**: Shared shocks that make a whole day or area slower than typical, so errors on consecutive trips are correlated rather than independent. *Values:* day factor 0.8-1.25 [design; confirmed by TLC 2023: weekday daily speed factor within Manhattan below 60th St P5-P95 0.82-1.20, P1-P99 0.77-1.23, extremes 0.75 on 6 Dec and 1.40 on 3 Jul (bridge day), P*]; zone-hour factor 0.85-1.2 [design]; extremes on gridlock and storm days (the slowest 2023 weekdays were UNGA week and early December) [P*]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] correlated shocks are what make a learnt 'today is slow' signal valuable; independent noise averages out; TLC 2023 day-level spread [P*] · Data: TLC trip records (day-to-day variance of zone-hour medians) · Model: Hierarchical noise: realised = typical x day_factor x zone_hour_factor x trip_noise, with AR(1) persistence within the day. · [R10-009]</sub>
   * **Trip-level travel-time noise**: Random deviation of a realised trip time from its expected time for the same origin, destination and departure time. *Values:* multiplicative lognormal with CV ~0.15-0.35 [design] [corrected by fact-check: TLC 2023 weekday zone-pair x hour cells (54k cells, 23M trips) give CV median 0.28 (P10 0.20, P90 0.46) and sd(log) median 0.26; sd(log) is 0.41 for trips under 1.5 mi, 0.29 at 1.5-3 mi, 0.23 at 3-6 mi and 0.21 above 6 mi; these include within-zone address spread, so they are upper bounds on traffic noise (P*)]; Midtown to JFK 4-5 PM P90 84 min vs median 64 (+31%) in 2009-2015 [S]; 2023: P90 94 vs median 75 min (+26%), daytime P90/median 1.26-1.33 [P*]; buffer index 20-60% on unreliable arterials [uncertain: no source given]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Schneider TLC airport analysis: P90/median about 1.3 [S]; FHWA buffer-index definition (95th pct vs mean) [S]; TLC 2023 dispersion analysis [P*] · Data: TLC trip records (dispersion of durations per zone pair x hour) · Model: Fit quantiles per zone pair x hour from TLC; realised time = expected x lognormal(0, sigma_zone,hour), seeded identically across models. · [R10-008]</sub>
* **Signals, grid and turn rules**
   * **Directional progression (avenue green waves vs crosstown)**: Avenues run coordinated signal progressions while crosstown streets do not, so north-south travel is much faster than east-west in Manhattan. *Values:* Avenues historically progressed at ~25-30 mph, retimed to 25 mph under Vision Zero; 3rd Ave 60th-96th St retimed to 15 mph in 2025 (one of 16 green waves citywide) [uncertain: segment and count not re-read]; Midtown crosstown 4.2-4.8 mph (2002). `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: NY1 Feb 2025 on 3rd Ave retiming [S]; NYC DOT THRU Streets document: Midtown crosstown 4.8 EB / 4.2 WB mph [S] · Data: VZV signal timing dataset; TLC trips (compare north-south vs east-west zone pairs) · Model: Direction-dependent speed factor by edge bearing and class (avenue vs street) in Manhattan, calibrated on TLC. · [R10-013]</sub>
   * **One-way grid and turn restrictions**: One-way streets, left-turn bans, no-U-turn rules and through-movement bans that lengthen paths relative to straight-line distance. *Values:* most Manhattan streets one-way; left turns off 14th St banned at all hours [S]; OSRM default turn penalty up to 7.5 s and U-turn 20 s [corrected by fact-check: 7.5 s is the maximum of an angle-based sigmoid, and turns across oncoming traffic add up to 2 s more, P]; U-turns banned in business districts (34 RCNY 4-05(b)) [P*]; network detour factor ~1.2-1.4 vs grid distance [U]. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: NYC DOT 14th Street rules [S]; OSRM car.lua defaults [P] · Data: OpenStreetMap (oneway, restriction relations) via Geofabrik New York extract https://download.geofabrik.de/north-america/us/new-york.html · Model: Edge-based routing graph with turn restrictions and turn costs (native in OSRM and Valhalla). · [R10-015]</sub>
   * **Signal delay and cycle length**: Average waiting time added at signalised intersections, set by cycle length and green split. *Values:* Manhattan avenue cycles ~90 s (2008 field note); ~10-40 s mean delay per signal [U]; ~20 short blocks per mile north-south [U]; OSRM's default car profile adds only 2 s per tagged traffic signal [P]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Blog field note: Manhattan avenues timed in sequence, cycle ~90 s [S]; NACTO cites 60-90 s cycles [S] · Data: NYC Open Data 'VZV Signal Timing/25MPH Signal Retiming' https://catalog.data.gov/dataset/vzv_signal-timing ; OSM highway=traffic_signals · Model: Implicit in calibrated speeds for v1; optional node penalty = f(cycle, approach class) in a graph engine. · [R10-012]</sub>
   * **Wrong-side approach and around-the-block loop**: Extra time when the vehicle arrives on the wrong side or direction of a one-way street for a pickup or drop-off and must circle the block. *Values:* 1-6 min in Midtown; under 1 min on two-way outer-borough streets [design]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] follows from the one-way grid and long Midtown blocks; Uber reports opposite-side meetings often fail [S] · Data: OSM one-way graph; no empirical loop-time data found · Model: Snap pickup to a curb side and route to that side with turn costs; the loop time emerges from graph routing. · [R10-016]</sub>
   * **Right-on-red ban**: NYC prohibits right turns on red unless a sign allows it, adding signal wait to right turns. *Values:* banned citywide by default: turns on red only where a sign authorises them (34 RCNY 4-03(a)(3)(ii)) [P*]; 336 signed exceptions in 2019 (Manhattan 6, Staten Island 198) [S]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: 34 RCNY 4-03 [P*]; NY1 2019 count of right-on-red locations [S] · Data: NYC DOT; OSM turn-restriction tags (sparse) · Model: Right turns at signals carry red-phase wait (no free right) in a graph engine; NYC-specific since most US cities allow it. · [R10-014]</sub>
   * **Turning delay from pedestrians**: Delay to turning vehicles that yield to heavy crosswalk volumes and leading pedestrian intervals, especially in Midtown. *Values:* 5-30 s per turn at busy Midtown corners [design]. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] a known contributor to Midtown slowness; no public NYC measurement found · Data: none found · Model: Zone-hour turn-penalty multiplier in a graph engine; otherwise implicit in calibrated speeds. · [R10-019]</sub>
* **Bridges, tunnels and highways**
   * **River-crossing chokepoint delay**: Queue delay at bridges and tunnels into Manhattan and between boroughs, which dominates many inter-borough trip times. *Values:* Holland Tunnel inbound 12 min 48 s (Jan 2024) vs 4 min 27 s (8 Jan 2025); AM-peak crossing times 4-36% lower in 2025; RPA cites >20 min saved via Lincoln Tunnel. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: MTA figures via City & State (Jan 2025) and Governor release (Sep 2025) [S] · Data: Congestion Pricing Tracker (Google Maps every 15 min, 19 routes); TLC trip records; NYC DOT Traffic Speeds NBE · Model: Dedicated chokepoint edges with hour-of-week delay curves plus stochastic queue shocks. · [R10-020]</sub>
   * **Highway versus local-street speed gap**: Speed difference between expressways (FDR, West Side Hwy, BQE, LIE, Van Wyck, Major Deegan) and surface streets, which drives route choice on longer trips. *Values:* expressways ~30-50 mph off-peak, can drop under 15 mph in peaks [U]; Manhattan surface streets 5-10 mph daytime. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: NYC DOT real-time speed feed covers mainly highways and major arterials, updated several times a minute [S] · Data: NYC DOT Traffic Speeds NBE https://data.cityofnewyork.us/Transportation/DOT-Traffic-Speeds-NBE/i4gi-tjb9 · Model: Road-class-specific weekly profiles: expressways from the DOT feed, surface streets from TLC calibration. · [R10-022]</sub>
   * **HOV-lane eligibility when carrying riders**: A car with enough riders aboard may use peak HOV lanes, while an empty car, and a robotaxi carrying a single rider, may not. *Values:* The LIE HOV lane in Nassau and Suffolk (about 40 miles [U]) is HOV-2+ on weekdays 6-10 am and 3-8 pm; a violation is a ticket of up to $150 and up to 2 licence points. The Clean Pass solo-EV exemption ended on 30 Sep 2025 [S]. Inside NYC the HOV lanes are HOV-3+: the LIE approach to the Midtown Tunnel in Queens (7-10 am), the Staten Island Expressway and the Gowanus Expressway, with hours varying by lane [S, 511NY page via search snippet; corrected by fact-check, was 'SIE and Gowanus HOV/bus lanes [U]']. So an app car or taxi with ONE rider (2 occupants) qualifies only on the Long Island HOV-2 lane, and inside the city needs 2+ riders. 511NY says Uber, Lyft and taxis may use HOV lanes when they meet the occupancy rule [S]. A robotaxi with one rider has one occupant and qualifies nowhere [design].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi; robotaxi (not eligible with one rider); fleet (routing)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] PIX11, CBS New York and News 12 (2025) on the LIE HOV rule and the end of Clean Pass; [S] 511NY HOV page (snippet). It affects only peak Long Island trips and group rides on the SIE, Gowanus or Midtown Tunnel approach, a small part of the ~4% of trips that end out of the city (G2-031). · Data: 511NY Rideshare HOV lane page https://511nyrideshare.org/hov-lanes (host not reachable from this session; snippet only); OpenStreetMap hov=* tags · Model: Link speeds depend on occupancy: an HOV link is usable when occupants are at or above its threshold (2 or 3) and its time window is active, so loaded and empty cars get different travel times on the same road. Generalises to HOV/HOT lanes in LA, the Bay Area, etc. · [G2-007]</sub>
* **Curb, pickup and drop-off time**
   * **Airport pickup access path**: Extra driving and waiting to reach designated airport pickup areas (FHV lots, taxi holds) rather than the terminal curb. *Values:* +3-15 min [U]; Waymo at Phoenix airport limits terminal wait to 4 min. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] JFK and LGA send FHV pickups to designated areas; Waymo PHX airport rules [S] · Data: PANYNJ ground transportation pages [U] · Model: Airport nodes with a mandatory staging-lot detour and service-type-specific access rules. · [R10-041]</sub>
   * **Curb availability and stopping search**: Extra time to find a legal or safe spot to stop at busy curbs (no-standing zones, bus lanes, bike lanes, double-parked trucks). *Values:* 0-3 min in Midtown and FiDi peaks; ~0 in residential outer boroughs [design]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] Manhattan has few loading docks so curbs are saturated [S on freight]; 34 RCNY 4-11(c): taxis and FHVs may stop briefly to pick up or drop off where standing is banned, must be within 12 in of the curb, and may double-park only if no curb space is free within 100 ft; never in bike lanes, crosswalks or no-stopping zones [P*] · Data: NYC DOT parking regulation signs dataset [U]; OSM · Model: Zone-hour 'curb friction' delay added to each pickup and drop-off, higher on bus-route blocks. · [R10-037]</sub>
   * **Double parking and delivery lane blockage**: Background lane capacity lost to double-parked delivery and service vehicles, peaking midday. *Values:* corridor capacity -6 to -12% (Manhattan parcel-delivery simulation); a travel lane blocked ~25% of the day on Church Ave, Brooklyn; delivery stops ~21 min, two-thirds double-parked [S]; 34 RCNY 4-08 lets commercial vehicles double-park up to 20 min while loading when no curb space is free on the block [P*]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: BTS/ROSAP Manhattan delivery study [S]; NYC DOT Church Ave analysis [S] · Data: none direct; captured in TLC-calibrated speeds · Model: Implicit in calibrated midday speeds; optional explicit blockage events on curb-lane edges. · [R10-038]</sub>
   * **Hotspot curb queues**: Queues of pickup vehicles at concentrated pickup points (stations, venues, airports) that grow with the number of cars converging, including the agent's own fleet. *Values:* 2-10 min approach and queue at Penn Station, Port Authority and venues at peaks [design]; airport lots can be longer (R06). `Med · Changes during run · hidden · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] no public queue-time data found within the search budget · Data: none found · Model: M/M/c queue at designated pickup nodes with service rate from curb length; fleet vehicles join the same queue. · [R10-040]</sub>
   * **Pickup dwell time**: Time from the vehicle reaching the pickup point until the rider is seated and the trip starts. *Values:* median ~1-2 min [U] [corrected by fact-check: Uber trips in Jan 2023 (13.6M) show pickup minus on-scene median 0.8 min (47 s), mean 1.0 min, P75 1.8 min; 17% of trips sit at 2.0-2.2 min and only 3.8% exceed 2.1 min, so the field looks censored at the 2-minute wait clock (P*)]; Uber/Lyft wait fees start 2 min after arrival (5 min for premium) [S] [uncertain for Lyft: not re-read]; Waymo waits up to 5 min (4 min at Phoenix airport) then charges a no-show fee; luggage or mobility aids add ~30-120 s (bus analogue: 15-19 s ordinary vs 28-137 s encumbered). `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Platform wait-fee policies and Waymo help page [S]; HVFHV records carry on_scene and pickup timestamps [P*]; on_scene_datetime is filled for 100% of Uber (HV0003) but 0.14% of Lyft (HV0005) trips in Jan 2023, despite the dictionary's 'accessible vehicles only' label [P*] · Data: TLC HVFHV trip records (pickup_datetime minus on_scene_datetime where populated: Uber only) · Model: Lognormal dwell by zone type (airport, station, residential), hour and luggage flag, calibrated on HVFHV on-scene to pickup gaps. · [R10-035]</sub>
   * **Rider pin accuracy and meeting friction**: Delay or failed pickups because the rider's GPS pin, the vehicle's position or the agreed side of the street is wrong. *Values:* GNSS error can reach 50 m or more in urban canyons; shadow matching roughly doubled accuracy [uncertain: not re-read]; 0.5-3 min extra or a cancellation [design]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber Engineering 'Rethinking GPS' and GPS World coverage [S] · Data: none public for NYC error rates · Model: Draw pin error (sigma 10-20 m outer boroughs, 30-60 m Midtown/FiDi [design]); if past half a block or on the opposite side, add search time or a cancellation chance. · [R10-039]</sub>
   * **Drop-off dwell and payment time**: Time from arrival at the destination until the vehicle can leave, including payment (taxi), unloading and door closing. *Values:* app trips 15-45 s [design]; taxi card or cash payment +30-90 s [design]; airport curb with luggage 1-3 min [design]. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] no public NYC measurement found · Data: none found (TLC trip end is the drop-off time, not the departure time) · Model: Add a dwell draw after each drop-off before the vehicle becomes available. · [R10-036]</sub>
   * **Night garbage-truck blockages**: Private carters and city sanitation trucks stop on narrow streets, mostly 22:00-06:00, and hold up traffic for minutes on one-lane streets. Daytime delivery blockage is R10-038. The Commercial Waste Zones are NYC-specific; night refuse blockage generalises. *Values:* 1-5 min per encounter; 2-10% chance per late-night trip on one-lane Manhattan and Brooklyn streets [design]. Commercial Waste Zones: 20 zones. The first (Queens Central) moved over between 3 Sep 2024 and 2 Jan 2025, with contracts with a zone carter required from 3 Jan 2025. The Bronx zones followed in late 2025, and Lower Manhattan sign-up opened on 1 Apr 2026. Full rollout is expected by the end of 2027 [S]. Fewer private trucks per street should follow [U].. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] DSNY CWZ rule and rollout-schedule pages via search snippets. [corrected: first-zone dates]. [uncertain: the claim 'about half of zones by Dec 2026' was not found]. Night timing [U]; delays [design]. · Data: DSNY Commercial Waste Zones https://www.nyc.gov/site/dsny/resources/reports/commercial-waste-zones-plan.page ; rollout schedule https://www.nyc.gov/site/dsny/businesses/cwz/rollout-schedule.page ; DSNY collection schedules (NYC Open Data) [U] · Model: Blocking hazard on local one-lane edges by hour. It is absorbed in calibrated night speeds unless traffic is modelled at link level. · [G1-017]</sub>
* **Routing, navigation and ETA**
   * **Navigation ETA error shown to agent**: Difference between the ETA the agent is shown and the realised travel time. *Values:* multiplicative error with MAE ~10-25% [design]; optimistic bias in peaks [design]. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber builds ETAs as routing-engine segment sums plus an ML residual correction (DeepETA, judged by MAE), implying raw router ETAs are biased [S] · Data: derived from the R10-008 noise model · Model: Shown ETA = expected time x (1 + bias + noise); realised time drawn separately from the true distribution. · [R10-044]</sub>
   * **Driver phone reliability and signal dead zones**: Phones overheating on summer dashboards, battery drain, lost signal in tunnels and under elevated tracks, and app freezes cause missed or timed-out offers. *Values:* 0-2% chance of missing an offer, higher in hot sun above about 30 C and in tunnels and under elevated tracks [design]. A second phone for multi-apping doubles the exposure [design].. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, taxi e-hail; robotaxi connectivity drop-outs are R03-056`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] driver-forum reports. Missed offers hurt the acceptance rate (R09-040) and can lose an airport queue place (R06-042). R03-045 covers only network-wide outages. · Data: none found (carrier coverage maps are a weak proxy [U]) · Model: An offer-delivery failure hazard set by temperature (R04-019) and link type (tunnel, under elevated track); a failure counts as a timeout. · [G2-016]</sub>
   * **Dynamic re-routing**: Whether routes are recomputed en route when conditions change, by a navigation autopilot or by the agent. *Values:* none / autopilot every N min / agent-controlled. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] · Data: none · Model: Re-plan the remaining path at each traffic update tick. · [R10-046]</sub>
   * **Map staleness**: Mismatch between the map the agent sees and the true network (recent turn bans, closures, new bus lanes). *Values:* 0-2% of edges stale [design]. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] robustness test · Data: OSM history (diff of two snapshots) · Model: Agent map built from an older OSM snapshot, simulator from a newer one. · [R10-049]</sub>
   * **Vehicle localisation and missed turns**: Navigation errors such as missed turns in urban canyons where the driver's own GPS drifts. *Values:* missed-turn probability 1-5% per Midtown trip for humans [design]; near zero for a robotaxi with lidar and HD-map localisation [U]. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber: GNSS errors of 50 m or more in dense cities [S] · Data: none found · Model: Small probability of a 1-4 min loop detour on Midtown and FiDi trips. · [R10-047]</sub>
* **Deadhead and empty driving**
   * **Pickup deadhead (en route to rider)**: Time and distance driven empty from accepting a request to reaching the rider. *Values:* NYC HVFHV average wait 4.4-6.6 min by borough (2018-Jan 2026) [S; consistent with a Jan 2023 check: mean request-to-pickup 4.3 min Manhattan, 4.7 Brooklyn, 4.8 Bronx, 5.0 Queens, 5.5 Staten Island, 4.6 citywide; Uber request-to-on-scene (pure en-route time) mean 3.0-4.3 min, median 2.4-3.7 min by borough, P*]; ~10% of ride-hail VMT is driving to pickups (six US metros); SF pickup ETAs late 2025: Waymo 5.74 min, Uber 3.15, Lyft 5.14. `High · Changes during run · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: TLC license pause report Feb 2026 [S]; TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 [P*]; Fehr & Peers 2019 [S]; Obi via Axios [S] · Data: TLC HVFHV trip records (request_datetime, on_scene_datetime, pickup_datetime; on_scene filled for Uber only) · Model: Same travel-time engine from vehicle position to pickup; logged per trip for utilisation metrics. · [R10-050]</sub>
   * **Repositioning travel cost**: Time and distance to move empty to a better zone, which the agent weighs against expected demand there. *Values:* 5-40 min depending on distance and hour [design]. `High · Changes during run · agent sees · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] repositioning is the core single-car and fleet decision and its cost is pure travel time · Data: same engine as trips · Model: Engine returns time and distance for any reposition order; optionally show the estimate before the agent commits. · [R10-053]</sub>
   * **Depot and charger deadhead**: Empty travel to depots, cleaning or chargers, which takes vehicles out of service. *Values:* 5-30 min each way [design]; 1-3 trips a day for EVs [U]. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet, human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] cross-link R08 charging · Data: AFDC charging station locations (R08) https://afdc.energy.gov/stations · Model: Same engine to charger or depot nodes; charge time from R08. · [R10-057]</sub>
   * **Empty return from airports and outer areas**: Deadhead back toward demand after a drop-off at an airport or a low-demand area when no return trip is available. *Values:* JFK to Midtown roughly 38-64 min by hour (occupied-trip benchmark) [corrected by fact-check: 2023 Midtown core to JFK weekday medians were 29-75 min by hour, LGA 21-44 min (P*)]. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: Schneider airport trip times [S]; TLC 2023 yellow trips [P*] · Data: TLC trip records · Model: Same engine; the agent chooses between the airport queue (R06) and deadheading back. · [R10-056]</sub>
   * **Empty-vehicle speed bias**: Empty and repositioning vehicles may move at different speeds than occupied trips, but trip data only observe occupied legs. *Values:* empty speed 0.8-1.0 x occupied speed [design]. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] TLC data record only paid trips, so deadhead speeds are unobserved · Data: none found · Model: Apply occupied-trip calibration to empty moves with an optional factor. · [R10-058]</sub>
* **Travel-time engine and calibration**
   * **Router default-speed bias**: Out-of-the-box router speeds assume free flow and badly underestimate dense-city travel times unless calibrated. *Values:* OSRM car.lua: primary 65 km/h, secondary 55, tertiary 40, residential 25 on roads without a maxspeed tag; where OSM has maxspeed it uses 0.8 x maxspeed (25 mph gives ~32 km/h) and adds 2 s per signal; turn penalty up to 7.5 s, U-turn 20 s [P]; Valhalla uses the maxspeed tag directly as routing speed [P]; vs real Midtown ~7-9 km/h daytime, so a 2-5x underestimate [corrected by fact-check: about 3.5x on 25-mph-tagged streets and up to ~7x on untagged primaries, derived]. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: OSRM car.lua defaults [P] vs Midtown 4.8 mph [S] and TLC 2023 Midtown core 5.4-6.0 mph [P*] · Data: https://github.com/Project-OSRM/osrm-backend/blob/master/profiles/car.lua ; TLC for calibration · Model: Never ship raw router ETAs; replace speeds with calibrated profiles and validate. · [R10-061]</sub>
   * **Travel-time engine architecture**: How the simulator computes travel time from A to B: zone-to-zone lookup, road-graph routing with time-dependent speeds, or a hybrid. *Values:* (a) 263-zone OD x hour table (lookup IDs 264-265 are Unknown and outside NYC) [P*]; (b) OSRM or Valhalla on OSM with speed profiles; (c) graph geometry for visuals plus OD-calibrated times. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: TLC trip files carry only taxi-zone IDs since July 2016, no coordinates [P*] · Data: TLC trip records; taxi zone shapefile; OpenStreetMap · Model: Recommend (c): Valhalla or OSRM path for geometry and the live map, edge times scaled so zone-pair x hour medians match TLC. · [R10-059]</sub>
   * **Routed vs recorded distance ratio**: The gap between the router's shortest-path distance and the distance real trips record for the same OD pair and hour. It scales fares, the per-mile pay floor and energy cost. *Values:* HVFHV Jan 2023, OD x 4-hour cells (>= 200 trips, median >= 3 mi; 7,190 cells): median trip_miles is 1.20x the 10th percentile and 1.27x the 5th; p90/p10 = 1.52 (includes address spread within zones). Yellow trips are about 4% shorter than app trips on the same OD: median ratio 0.96 (IQR 0.93-0.99) over 2,199 OD pairs; trip-weighted 0.98. The router-vs-record ratio still needs to be measured with an OSM router.. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden · all (separate factors for taxi and app)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own calc on HVFHV and yellow Jan 2023 (g1/a6.py), re-run in this fact-check. Part of the taxi-app gap may come from different odometers (taximeter vs app GPS) [U]. Router comparison [design]. · Data: NYC TLC trip records (trip_miles, trip_distance); OpenStreetMap with OSRM or Valhalla · Model: Detour factor per OD-hour = recorded median / routed distance, applied to paid distance and energy, separately for taxi and app. Validate on a held-out month. · [G1-030]</sub>
   * **Same travel-time source for replayed and simulated trips**: Whether replayed background trips use recorded durations while the agent's trips use the engine's travel times, and how the two are reconciled. *Values:* Engine times for all trips (default) / recorded times for all replayed trips / a hybrid that scales engine times to match recorded ones per origin-destination pair and hour [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] Mixing sources gives the agent a built-in speed edge or handicap over the bots, and breaks the displacement rule (R06-067) when the agent takes a replayed trip. R06-066 does not say which source to use. · Data: NYC TLC trip records (trip_time); NYC DOT Traffic Speeds https://data.cityofnewyork.us/d/i4gi-tjb9 · Model: Every vehicle uses one travel-time function; replayed durations are used only for calibration (R10-062). · [G2-029]</sub>
   * **Time resolution of speed profiles**: Bucket width for time-dependent speeds and how the chosen router supports it. *Values:* Valhalla: 2016 five-minute speeds per edge per week (DCT-II compressed) plus free-flow (night) and constrained (7 AM-7 PM) speeds; OSRM: no native time dependence, reload a segment-speed CSV with osrm-customize (MLD) per bucket; options 5 / 15 / 60 min. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Valhalla historical-traffic and speeds docs [P]; OSRM command-line docs [S], and docs/tools.md read on GitHub [P] (osrm-customize applies --segment-speed-file and --turn-penalty-file to an MLD graph; the release line is 26.x, package.json 26.10.0) · Data: https://github.com/valhalla/valhalla/blob/master/docs/docs/concepts/historical-traffic.md ; https://project-osrm.org/docs/v26.5.0/tools ; https://github.com/Project-OSRM/osrm-backend/blob/master/docs/tools.md · Model: Valhalla predicted traffic with depart_at times, or 168 hourly OSRM datasets switched by the simulation clock. · [R10-060]</sub>
   * **Time-dependent routing consistency (FIFO)**: Long trips cross speed buckets, so travel time must be integrated over time and a later departure must never arrive earlier. *Values:* buckets 5-60 min; trips up to 90+ min. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] standard requirement for time-dependent shortest paths · Data: none · Model: Time-dependent Dijkstra evaluating each edge at its entry time, with smoothed bucket boundaries. · [R10-065]</sub>

### 7. Riders and demand (65)
*Who wants a ride, from where to where, how they react to price and wait, and how they behave at pickup.*

* **Trip volume and randomness**
   * **Base request rate by zone and hour**: Expected number of ride requests starting in each taxi zone in each hour, split by day type, which is the main input that drives all earnings. *Values:* Citywide ~18M HVFHV trips in Aug 2024 [corrected by fact-check: 19.13M raw HVFHV rows in Aug 2024, about 617k/day rather than ~580k/day; TLC's '>18M' is consistent] plus ~3M yellow (~97k/day; confirmed, 2.98M rows = 96k/day); Jan 2023: 18.48M HVFHV trips (~596k/day). Per zone-hour (HVFHV Jan 2023): median 64, p99 ~560, max 1,625 pickups/hour [corrected by fact-check: the busiest zone-hours are East Village (zone 79) at 00:00-02:00 on weekend nights and Midtown Center (zone 161) at 19:00-21:00, not generic 'Midtown peaks']; 263 taxi zones plus code 264 (Unknown) and 265 (Outside of NYC). `High · World input · public/forecast · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] TLC testimony 27 Sep 2024: HV sector >18M trips in Aug 2024 (~90% of 2019), yellow ~3M (~50% of 2019); [P*] monthly row counts from a SHA-256 catalog of the TLC files (R02 data folder) and own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (HVFHV Jan 2023) · Data: NYC TLC trip records (yellow, green, HVFHV; PULocationID x pickup hour) https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Non-homogeneous Poisson rate per zone x 15-min slot x day type, fitted from served trips, then lifted for unserved demand (R05-066) · [R05-001]</sub>
   * **Modifier composition and saturation rule**: How base demand and the hour, day-type, weather, event, holiday and transit-disruption effects are combined (multiplied, added or fitted jointly), and where combined demand is capped. *Values:* Modes: (1) product of separately fitted multipliers; (2) one joint negative-binomial GLM on zone x 15-min counts, with main effects plus selected interactions (rain x hour, event x weather, holiday x hour); (3) additive event residual on a multiplicative base. Cap per zone-hour at an observed ceiling (max 1,625 HVFHV pickups per zone-hour, R05-001) or a surge-implied ceiling.. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden · all; single car and fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design guess] Stacked days (rain + event + holiday) are likely the most profitable scenarios, so the stacking rule sets the biggest payoffs. [P, from list] R04-002 plans a Poisson regression with hour-of-week fixed effects; its only fitted result so far is a daily wet-day regression (Uber +6.3%). [corrected: it was described as already fitted hourly]. · Data: NYC TLC trip records; NOAA hourly weather (LCD); event calendar (R03) · Model: Fit one log-link GLM jointly and validate it on held-out stacked days. Apply the ceiling as a soft cap, and record the composition rule used in each scenario. · [G1-019]</sub>
   * **Platform share of city demand**: The fraction of all city ride demand that reaches the platform the simulated driver or fleet works on. *Values:* Uber 73.5%, Lyft 26.5% of HVFHV trips in Jan 2023 [fact-check: confirmed from data, HV0003 13.58M vs HV0005 4.90M trips; earlier '~70-75% [U]' holds]; yellow and green taxis are separate channels; robotaxi operator share is a scenario knob 0.01-0.5. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies, field hvfhs_license_num (HV0003 Uber, HV0005 Lyft), HVFHV Jan 2023 · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, field hvfhs_license_num · Model: Thin total demand by platform share before matching; cross-link R06 for competitor capture · [R05-004]</sub>
   * **Bursty, clustered request arrivals**: Requests arriving in bursts within minutes when crowds leave a venue, a flight bank lands or a train line stops, rather than spread evenly. *Values:* Bursts of 5-500 requests within 10-30 min at one zone [design guess]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] arrivals cluster at event end times; cross-link R03 Events · Data: Event schedules (R03), flight schedules (BTS), MTA service alerts · Model: Batch or Hawkes-process arrivals triggered by scheduled event end, flight arrival and outage times · [R05-003]</sub>
   * **Day-to-day demand randomness (overdispersion)**: How much realised demand in a zone-hour scatters around its average, beyond pure Poisson noise, because of shared daily shocks. *Values:* Coefficient of variation 10-30% per zone-hour on comparable days [fact-check: confirmed. HVFHV Jan 2023, Tue-Thu, zone-hours with >=50 trips: median CV 15% (IQR 12-20%) vs 9% from Poisson noise alone, variance about 2.6x the mean; citywide daily total CV ~11%]; one citywide daily shock plus zone-level shocks. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (HVFHV Jan 2023): variance ~2.6x Poisson, so Poisson alone understates spread; matters for robustness across 100 runs · Data: NYC TLC trip records (daily counts by zone) · Model: Gamma-Poisson (negative binomial) mixture: daily city multiplier x zone multiplier x Poisson arrivals, seeded per run · [R05-002]</sub>
   * **Induced demand and rider retention**: Better service (short ETAs, few cancels) brings more requests later, and bad trips drive riders away. *Values:* Zone demand multiplier 0.9-1.1 responding to recent ETA [design guess]. `Med · World input · hidden · fleet, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; [corrected by fact-check: Frechette et al. 2019 show increasing returns to scale in taxi matching, which supports density effects but says nothing about rider retention] · Data: none found · Model: Zone demand multiplier tied to moving-average served ETA; rider churn after bad trips · [R05-068]</sub>
   * **Re-requests and duplicate requests**: Whether a rider who is not matched, or whose driver cancels, requests again on the same or another app, and whether one person can hold requests on two apps at once. *Values:* After a failed match or a driver cancellation: 40-70% request again on the same app within 5 min, 10-30% switch app, the rest leave [design]. Requests open on two apps at once: 5-15% at peaks [design].. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] HVFHV files hold completed trips only, with no failed or cancelled requests (R05-066); [S] rider multi-homing (R05-023). Without this, the censoring uplift counts some people twice [design]. · Data: none found · Model: Riders persist after a failure, with probabilities of requesting again or switching app. When a duplicate request is matched on one app, the other app's request is cancelled, which shows up there as a rider cancellation. · [G2-019]</sub>
   * **Within-day persistence and spatial correlation of demand shocks**: How much a zone's demand deviation carries over from hour to hour within a day and is shared with nearby zones, on top of the citywide day factor. *Values:* HVFHV Jan 2023 non-holiday weekdays, zone-hours averaging 20+ trips: a date x hour factor explains 41% of the log-residual variance (0.050 to 0.030), and borough x date x hour explains 48%. The remaining zone residual has autocorrelation 0.35-0.36 at 1 h, 0.28 at 2 h, 0.22-0.23 at 3 h and 0.11-0.13 at 6 h [P, replicated by fact-check]. Poisson noise is about 58-62% of that residual variance, so the underlying shock persists more: about 0.85-0.9 at 1 h, about 0.55 at 3 h and about 0.3 at 6 h [design inference]. Morning (7-10 h) and evening (16-19 h) residuals of the same zone-day correlate 0.22-0.23 [P].. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own re-check on the local HVFHV Jan 2023 file (the critic's method gave 0.40 at 1 h and 0.26 at 3 h; the fact-check's complete-grid version gave 0.35 and 0.22). Persistent shocks are what make watching the first hours worthwhile; R05-002 keeps one zone multiplier per day. · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records (pickups by zone, date and hour) · Model: Zone log-multiplier follows an hourly AR(1) process with rho about 0.8-0.85, optionally plus a small zone-day component, a borough x day x hour factor and the citywide day factor, with Poisson arrivals. [Corrected by fact-check: a pure AR(1) at 0.85-0.9 implies 0.61-0.73 at 3 h and 0.38-0.53 at 6 h, above the ~0.55 and ~0.3 implied by the data.] Validate against the autocorrelations above. · [G2-023]</sub>
* **Origin-destination and trip length**
   * **Destination choice matrix**: Probability that a request from a given zone at a given hour goes to each destination zone. *Values:* 263 x 263 zone matrix (~69k cells) per hour band and day type, very sparse outside Manhattan. `High · World input · hidden · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] PULocationID and DOLocationID confirmed in local HVFHV and yellow Parquet files; destination sets where the driver ends up · Data: NYC TLC trip records (PU/DO zone pairs) · Model: Empirical conditional OD with Bayesian smoothing; gravity-model fallback for sparse pairs · [R05-006]</sub>
   * **Directional imbalance by zone and hour**: Net flow of trips into versus out of each zone, such as morning inflow to Midtown and late-night outflow from bar districts. *Values:* Drop-off to pickup ratio 0.3-3 per zone-hour [corrected by fact-check: in HVFHV Jan 2023, weekday zone-hours averaging >=50 pickups/day have a ratio of 0.45-1.45 (5th-95th percentile, IQR 0.78-1.11); values near 0.3 or 3 occur only in small zones, at airports or in event hours]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (PU vs DO counts by zone and hour); imbalance drives deadhead and stranding · Data: TLC trip records · Model: Emerges from OD matrix; harness exposes it as a derived heatmap and tracks the agent's deadhead · [R05-009]</sub>
   * **Trip distance distribution**: Spread of trip lengths, from short Midtown hops to long airport and out-of-city runs. *Values:* Yellow Oct 2019: mean 3.05 mi, median 1.67 mi, 85% of trips 5 mi or less, 81% both 5 mi or less and fare $18 or less (confirms the '~80%' claim); yellow 2023: mean 3.5 mi, median 1.8 mi, 82% 5 mi or less; HVFHV Jan 2023: mean 4.8 mi, median 2.9 mi, 70% 5 mi or less, p99 ~26 mi [fact-check: all recomputed from TLC files; the earlier [U] means are confirmed]. `High · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (yellow Oct 2019 and 2023, HVFHV Jan 2023); [S] NYC Data Science 2019; trip length sets fare per trip versus time between trips · Data: TLC trip records (trip_distance, trip_miles) · Model: Emerges from OD plus routing; calibrate against empirical distance histograms by hour · [R05-007]</sub>
   * **Exact pickup and drop-off point within a zone**: Where in a taxi zone the rider actually stands or is dropped, since TLC data gives only zone IDs. *Values:* Zones ~0.3-2 mi across [U]; points weighted by buildings, foot traffic and historic GPS [corrected by fact-check: coordinates were dropped from July 2016 onward; yellow and green files up to June 2016 carry GPS points]. `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Schneider nyc-taxi-data README: 'Starting in July 2016, the TLC no longer provides pickup and dropoff coordinates' · Data: OSM buildings; NYC DOT pedestrian counts (NYC Open Data); pre-2016 yellow GPS points as prior · Model: Sample a point in the zone weighted by address density and historic pickup GPS; snap to curb (cross-link R01) · [R05-011]</sub>
   * **Return-trip coupling (tour chains)**: People who travel out for a concert, dinner or flight later make a predictable return trip. *Values:* P(return by car within 2-8 h) 0.3-0.6 for leisure trips [design guess]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] reverse wave after events; cross-link R03 · Data: none found (infer from paired OD flows in TLC data) · Model: Rider tours home-activity-home, or an event-linked return wave scheduled at event end · [R05-010]</sub>
* **Trip purpose and rider segments**
   * **Commute demand layer**: Home-to-work and work-to-home trips in AM and PM peaks, strongest where transit is poor. *Values:* Weekday peaks 7-10 and 16-19; larger share in outer-borough to Manhattan pairs with poor transit [design guess]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Hall, Palsson & Price 2018: Uber fills transit gaps and complements transit (+5% ridership after 2 years) · Data: LEHD LODES OD https://lehd.ces.census.gov/data/; US Census ACS journey to work · Model: Commute layer from LODES OD weighted by transit travel-time penalty · [R05-015]</sub>
   * **Nightlife demand layer**: Late-night demand from bar and club districts, peaking around closing time. *Values:* Fri/Sat 22:00-04:00; NYC last call 4 AM [U]; [corrected by fact-check: the earlier 'spike 01:00-04:00' is wrong. In nightlife zones (East Village 79, Lower East Side 148, West Village 249) Sat/Sun HVFHV pickups peak at 00:00-02:00 (~3,500/h for the three zones), are ~2,000 at 03:00 and ~950 at 04:00 (Jan 2023); there is no closing-time spike at 4 AM]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] NY on-premises alcohol sales end 4 AM; [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (HVFHV Jan 2023 hourly profile); also raises mess and incident risk (R05-054) · Data: NYS Liquor Authority active licenses (data.ny.gov); TLC trips · Model: POI-weighted layer from liquor-licence density, peaking 00:00-02:00 and tapering to 04:00 (fit the shape from TLC hourly counts) · [R05-016]</sub>
   * **Rider segment mix**: Mix of locals, tourists, business travellers, students, elderly and disabled riders, each with different habits. *Values:* US: 36% of adults used ride-hail (2018); 51% of 18-29 vs 24% of 50+; 45% urban; ~35% of New Yorkers use ride-hail [uncertain: NYC figure seen only via secondary coverage of the 2018 NYC DOT Mobility Report]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Pew Research Jan 2019; [S] NYC DOT Mobility Report 2018 coverage · Data: Pew Research; NYC DOT Citywide Mobility Survey; US Census ACS · Model: Segment attribute per request with zone and hour priors; segment sets price, wait, tip, luggage and group size parameters · [R05-013]</sub>
   * **Tourist and visitor demand layer**: Extra demand from visitors around hotels and attractions, varying by season. *Values:* 65M visitors in 2025 (52.4M domestic, ~12.3-12.6M international); 66.3M forecast 2026 (World Cup) [uncertain: report not re-opened; business.nyctourism.com unreachable]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NYC Tourism + Conventions annual report Mar 2026 · Data: NYC Tourism + Conventions reports https://business.nyctourism.com/press-media/press-releases/NYC-Tourism-Annual-Report-March-2026; OSM tourism=hotel · Model: Tourism layer scaled by month and hotel/attraction density; tourists less price-aware, more luggage · [R05-014]</sub>
   * **Trip purpose mix**: Share of requests that are commutes, airport, nightlife, shopping, medical, business or tourism trips, by hour and zone. *Values:* All-mode NYC: ~1/5 commute, ~1/7 social, ~1/8 shopping; hidden labels per request. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NYC DOT Citywide Mobility Survey 2017 [uncertain: shares not re-checked, nyc.gov blocked]; purpose drives OD, group size, tips and price sensitivity · Data: NYC DOT Citywide Mobility Survey reports and microdata https://www.nyc.gov/html/dot/downloads/pdf/2024-cms-report.pdf [uncertain: URL not opened]; OSM POIs · Model: Tag each request with purpose from P(purpose | origin and destination land use, hour) · [R05-012]</sub>
   * **Business traveller and premium demand**: Weekday travellers from Midtown and Financial District hotels and offices who often choose premium products and care less about price. *Values:* Higher premium-product share and lower price elasticity on weekdays [design guess]. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] expense-paid riders are less price sensitive · Data: none found · Model: Segment flag that lowers elasticity and raises premium product choice · [R05-019]</sub>
   * **Medical and paratransit trips**: Trips to and from hospitals and clinics, including MTA Access-A-Ride trips sent to app drivers. *Values:* access_a_ride_flag trips are a small share (<1%) [corrected by fact-check: no HVFHV trip is flagged in Jan 2023 (Uber leaves the field blank, Lyft reports 'N' on every trip), so the flag cannot measure paratransit volume]; daytime weekday peak [design guess]. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] access_a_ride_flag exists but is empty in Jan 2023 (own calc on local TLC Parquet copies); hospital-trip layer is [design] · Data: NYC Open Data hospital locations; MTA Access-A-Ride statistics [U]; TLC HVFHV access_a_ride_flag (empty in Jan 2023) · Model: Small daytime layer around hospitals; longer boarding times · [R05-017]</sub>
   * **Persistent rider population across days**: Whether riders are fresh random draws each day or a synthetic population with home and work anchors, repeat commutes and stable traits such as tipping, no-shows and rating. *Values:* Fresh draws (default) / a synthetic population in which 20-50% of weekday commute trips repeat [design]; a stable tipper type (R05-047).. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] It gives the 10-day learning protocol regular patterns to learn and makes rating or blocking riders (R09-065) meaningful. R05-047 already assumes a persistent tipper type. · Data: US Census LEHD LODES home-work flows https://lehd.ces.census.gov/data/ [U]; NYC Citywide Mobility Survey [U]; TLC records (no rider IDs) · Model: Generate a rider pool per zone with home and work anchors and traits. Each day's demand samples trips from the pool and matches the R05-001 totals. · [G2-022]</sub>
   * **Student and campus demand**: Demand around universities that follows the academic calendar (move-in, finals, breaks). *Values:* +/-10-30% in campus zones by term vs break [design guess]. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] cross-link R02 calendar · Data: OSM amenity=university; university academic calendars (public) · Model: Campus-zone multiplier keyed to academic calendar · [R05-018]</sub>
* **Price, wait and mode choice**
   * **Price elasticity to surge or fare level**: How much demand falls when the quoted price rises. *Values:* Elasticity about -0.5 near 1.2x surge and below 1 in magnitude at higher surges (UberX, 2015, Chicago, LA, NYC, SF) [uncertain: point value comes from course notes; the paper is usually summarised as inelastic demand of roughly -0.4 to -0.6 across surge levels [U]; NBER unreachable]. `High · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Cohen, Hahn, Hall, Levitt & Metcalfe 2016 (NBER w22627), number via Berkeley course notes; demand more inelastic than expected · Data: Published estimates only (no public NYC data) · Model: Conversion probability as constant-elasticity or logistic function of price multiplier, by segment · [R05-020]</sub>
   * **Sensitivity to quoted pickup ETA**: How much fewer people request a ride when the app shows a longer wait. *Values:* Price elasticities ~4x waiting-time elasticities (ride-hail auctions) [uncertain: ratio not re-read; Buchholz et al. use Prague (Liftago) data, not NYC]; one NYC study claims +1.5 min delay cuts demand ~37% (unverified). `High · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Buchholz, Doval, Kastl, Matejka & Salz (value of time); [S] Paithankar, Kockelman & Gurumurthy 2026 (not verified) · Data: Published estimates only · Model: Multiply conversion by exp(-beta x ETA) per segment; key for fleet positioning · [R05-021]</sub>
   * **Cross-platform price comparison (multi-homing)**: Riders who check several apps or hail a cab instead when one platform surges. *Values:* Share of riders comparing 2+ options 20-50% [design guess]. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver, taxi, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] guess; [corrected by fact-check: Frechette, Lizzeri & Salz 2019 model NYC taxi search frictions and find increasing returns to scale in matching; they do not estimate rider multi-homing or platform competition]; cross-link R06 · Data: none found · Model: Rider samples k options and picks lowest generalised cost (price + value of time x ETA) · [R05-023]</sub>
   * **Mode alternatives (subway, bus, Citi Bike, walking, own car)**: How good the other ways to make the same trip are, which shapes how many people choose a car. *Values:* Citi Bike faster than taxi for ~40% of taxi trips (2017) [uncertain: share not re-read; Schneider's comparison used Jul 2016-Jun 2017 trips between Citi Bike stations]; late-night subway headways ~20 min [U]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Todd Schneider 2017 taxi vs Citi Bike; [S] Hall et al. 2018 transit complement · Data: MTA GTFS and ridership (data.ny.gov); Citi Bike system data https://citibikenyc.com/system-data · Model: Nested logit or zone-hour multiplier from transit level of service · [R05-025]</sub>
   * **Pre-match abandonment and 'no cars available'**: How long a rider keeps waiting while the app is still searching for a car, and the platform rule that refuses or hides service when no car is within an ETA threshold. This is pre-match; post-match cancels are R05-022. *Values:* Search patience: median 1-3 min, then the rider abandons or switches app or mode [design]. 'No cars available' when the best ETA exceeds 15-25 min [design]. Abandonment 0-10% of requests citywide, far higher at night in outer zones [design].. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver; robotaxi; fleet; taxi e-hail`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]. [P, from list] Castillo, Knoepfle & Weyl: in thin hours and areas, riders cancel and waits jump (R06-039). TLC records hold completed trips only, so no public abandonment rate exists. · Data: none found (unmatched requests are not published) · Model: Each unmatched request draws a patience time. It leaves (to a rival, to a taxi hail, or lost) when patience runs out or the platform ETA threshold is exceeded. Feeds R06-022 and R06-039. · [G1-021]</sub>
   * **Ride product choice**: Which product the rider books (UberX, Comfort, XL, Black, WAV, Share, Pet, Car Seat; Lyft tiers), which decides which vehicles may take the trip. *Values:* Standard tier dominant (~80%+ [U]); XL/Black pay more per trip. `Med · World input · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] HVFHV Parquet columns confirmed: only shared and WAV flags, no product field, so the product split is not public · Data: none found for product split (only shared_request_flag, wav_request_flag) · Model: Multinomial logit over products using price ratio and party size; vehicle eligibility from R08 · [R05-024]</sub>
   * **Rider cancellation after match**: Chance that a rider cancels after a driver accepts, rising as the wait drags or the car moves the wrong way. *Values:* 5-15% of matched requests [design guess]; many cancels in the first minute; free window ~2 min then fee. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet, taxi e-hail`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Spare 2019: many cancels come very quickly; [S] Wang, Zhang & Zhang 2024 (Operations Research): long pickups cause cancels [uncertain: citation not located]; [corrected by fact-check: impact lowered from high to medium. At 5-15% of matches and ~2-5 min lost per cancel, partly repaid by fees, the loss is about 1-3% of driver time [design arithmetic]; kept core because cancels feed fleet ETA and conversion] · Data: none found (TLC data holds completed trips only) · Model: Hazard model over time since match with ETA-slip term; pays cancel fee per R07 rule · [R05-022]</sub>
   * **Riders who defer during surge and request again**: Some riders who balk at a surge price postpone the trip rather than drop it and check again 5-30 minutes later, so demand returns as the price falls. *Values:* Of riders who balk at a surge quote, 20-60% defer, and 40-80% of those request again within 5-30 min [design guess]. Lyft's CEO said Wait & Save (cheaper in exchange for a longer wait) was about 30% of Lyft rides in Q1 2023 [S]. Lyft said NYC averaged more than 150,000 Wait & Save rides a week in Q2 2023 [S], roughly 13% of Lyft's ~1.1M weekly NYC trips (4.90M Lyft trips in Jan 2023 HVFHV data) [design calc; added by fact-check]. So many riders trade time for price.. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet (own pricing, G2-026)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] FOX Business interview with Lyft CEO Risher (no primary filing found) and Lyft Q2 2023 earnings call. Deferral flattens and lengthens peaks, so arriving 'late' to a fading surge can still pay; R05-020 treats every lost request as gone [design]. · Data: none found (TLC records hold completed trips only) · Model: Balked requests go into a deferral pool and come back over 5-30 min, re-quoted at the price at that time. Calibrate together with R05-020 and R05-066. · [G2-018]</sub>
   * **Transit disruption substitution**: Extra car demand near stations when a subway line is delayed or closed. *Values:* Each subway disruption displaces >1,500 rides but adds <40 taxi, ride-hail and Citi Bike rides; station closures +2.8-3.3% ride-share use. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NYU Stern ridesharing and public transport study [uncertain: figures not re-read]; cross-link R03 · Data: MTA service alerts and incidents (data.ny.gov) · Model: On disruption, add ~2-5% of displaced riders as requests in affected station zones · [R05-026]</sub>
   * **Household car access by home zone**: Share of households without a car, which raises baseline taxi and app demand where car ownership is low. *Values:* Manhattan ~20-25% of households own a car vs ~45% citywide [U]. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] ACS vehicles available; already embedded in TLC baseline, matters for synthetic or non-NYC cities · Data: US Census ACS table B25044 https://data.census.gov · Model: Covariate in a gravity/generation model when generalising beyond NYC · [R05-028]</sub>
   * **Rider promotions and memberships on incumbent apps**: Promo codes, Uber One and Lyft Pink memberships and corporate vouchers lower a rider's price on one app without changing driver pay, which shifts demand between apps. *Values:* Uber One gives 6% back in Uber Cash on rides (it replaced a 5% discount in 2023); Lyft Pink gives about 5% off rides; both cost $9.99/month [S]. Uber One reportedly had about 50M members accounting for over half of Uber gross bookings in Q1 2026 [S, secondary summary]. Short promotions of 5-20% off on one app for 1-7 days [design].. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver (multi-app), fleet on a platform`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] TechCrunch (May 2023) on Uber One's switch to cash back; RideWise and Ridesharing Driver comparisons (2025-26); Briefing.com Q1 2026 summary. It matters for a multi-apping driver (R06-034) because it moves request share between apps while pay stays the same. · Data: none found (platform pages only) · Model: A time-limited multiplier on each app's demand share (R05-004) and on riders' price elasticity, with driver pay unchanged. Members get a standing 5-6% price cut on their home app. · [G2-011]</sub>
   * **Riders moving out of surge zones**: When surge or ETA is high, some riders walk to a cheaper or better-served neighbouring zone, or move the pickup pin, so demand leaks across surge boundaries. *Values:* 0-15% of surge-exposed requests move 100-400 m [design]; no NYC estimate found.. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver; robotaxi; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; [U] rider walking is widely reported, but no NYC estimate was found. · Data: none found · Model: For requests facing surge above a threshold, move the origin with probability p to an adjacent zone with lower surge or ETA, and add walk time. · [G1-022]</sub>
   * **Transit alternatives to the airports**: The price and reliability of rail and bus links to JFK, LGA and EWR, which cap how many travellers take a car and how sharply that share falls when surge is high. Generalises to any city with airport rail or bus links. *Values:* AirTrain JFK costs $8.50, plus subway or LIRR. It was half price ($4.25) from 30 Jun to Labor Day (1 Sep) 2025 at Jamaica and Howard Beach, as also in summer 2024. AirTrain JFK carried about 11.7M riders in 2025 (6.7M in 2023), about 30.7k per weekday in Q1 2026. The Q70 LaGuardia Link has been fare-free since 1 May 2022; its 2024 ridership was about 25% above 2019. EWR: AirTrain Newark plus NJ Transit or Amtrak. Outages are in R03-049.. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NY1, Frommers and 6sqft (Jun 2025) on the half-price AirTrain. [S] The Points Guy (1 May 2022) and an MTA service document on the fare-free Q70. [S] AirTrain ridership via Wikipedia only; the PANYNJ figure was not read. · Data: PANYNJ airport traffic reports https://www.panynj.gov/airports/en/statistics-general-info.html ; MTA ridership (data.ny.gov); MTA airport guides https://www.mta.info/guides/airports/laguardia · Model: Airport-trip mode choice (nested logit on fare, time, luggage and hour). Transit fares are rule inputs, with scenario toggles for fare changes or outages. · [G1-010]</sub>
* **Request attributes and special needs**
   * **Child car seat and pet requests**: Requests that need a car seat or allow a pet, which only some vehicles serve, sometimes at a surcharge. *Values:* Uber Car Seat in NYC: $10 surcharge, one seat per ride; pet requests <1% [U]. `Low · World input · agent sees · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] TechCrunch 2014 uberFAMILY; The Points Guy 2024 · Data: none found · Model: Rare request flags requiring equipped vehicle; surcharge added · [R05-035]</sub>
   * **Luggage and trunk need**: Bags that need trunk space, mostly on airport and hotel trips, adding loading time and vehicle needs. *Values:* Airport trips 1-3 bags; +1-2 min loading [design guess]. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; robotaxi has no driver to help load · Data: none found · Model: Bag count by purpose; capacity check vs vehicle (R08); adds boarding time · [R05-030]</sub>
   * **Mid-trip destination changes and repricing**: Riders change or mistype the destination or add stops after pickup, and the platform reprices the fare and driver pay on actual time and distance. Stops entered before the trip are R05-033. *Values:* 1-3% of app trips [design guess]. NYC-specific address traps include hyphenated Queens addresses and street names repeated across boroughs [U]. The 120% cap on the fare estimate does not apply when the passenger alters the route, including the destination, number of stops, vehicle type or a toll route (35 RCNY 59B-23, via R07-022) [P*, re-read by fact-check]. Taxi trips are metered, so they need no repricing.. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi (rider edits in the app), fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59B-23 rule text; [U] Uber/Lyft help pages on fare adjustment. This weakens decisions based on the upfront offer (R06-025). · Data: none found (TLC data records only the final drop-off) · Model: With a small probability per trip, redraw the destination mid-trip or add a stop. Recompute fare and pay on actual miles and minutes, and log the quote-vs-final gap. · [G2-010]</sub>
   * **Multi-stop trips**: Trips with extra stops on the way, each adding waiting time. *Values:* Up to 3 stops [U]; 2-8% of trips; +1-5 min per stop [design guess]. `Low · World input · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] · Data: none found · Model: Bernoulli extra stop with dwell time; fare adds time component · [R05-033]</sub>
   * **Party size**: Number of riders per request, which decides XL eligibility and shared-ride feasibility. *Values:* [corrected by fact-check: yellow 2023 passenger_count is 1 rider 75.2%, 2 riders 15.2%, 3 riders 3.8%, 4 riders 2.1%, 5-6 riders 2.2%, 0 (entry error) 1.6%; was '1 ~70%, 3-4 ~10%, 5+ ~2% [U]']; yellow counts are driver-entered; HVFHV files have no passenger count. `Low · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (yellow 2023, 37M trips; same result in R08 data folder); [S] CMU capstone · Data: TLC yellow and green passenger_count (none in HVFHV) · Model: Sample from empirical distribution by hour and purpose (nightlife groups larger) · [R05-029]</sub>
   * **Scheduled and reserved rides**: Rides booked ahead (for example early airport trips), known to the system before they start. *Values:* 1-3% of trips [design guess]; booking lead from hours to weeks. `Low · World input · public/forecast · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; gives the agent advance demand information · Data: none found · Model: Pre-announced requests released to the agent with lead time; can be accepted ahead · [R05-034]</sub>
   * **Service animals and other riders who must be carried**: Riders with service animals or folded mobility devices must be carried; refusing them counts as a service refusal, with fines and possible loss of the licence or app account. The ADA gives a similar duty across the US. *Values:* 0.1-0.5% of requests come with a service animal [design guess]. Refusing one is a service refusal. For taxis the penalty is $350 / $500 (pleaded / after hearing) for a first offence, rising to $750 / $1,000 plus licence revocation for a third within 36 months (35 RCNY 80-20, R07-042). App deactivation risk [U]. The animal is not revealed until pickup.. `Low · Rule/price · hidden · all (a robotaxi must carry them too)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] TLC Industry Notice 10-28 (29 Nov 2010, repeating 09-23): service animals must be admitted in all taxis and FHVs, secured or not; refusal is a service refusal, with penalties up to licence suspension or revocation. A Dec 2020 TLC amendment updated the service-animal wording [S]. [P*, from list] penalty table in R07-042. [U] ADA 49 CFR 37.167(d). [corrected: dated '$300-1,000' fines replaced by the current penalty table]. · Data: TLC Industry Notice 10-28 https://www.nyc.gov/assets/tlc/downloads/pdf/archived_industry_notices/industry_notice_10_28.pdf ; 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Request flag revealed at pickup. Refusal triggers a fine or deactivation draw (R07-042) and a compliance-panel entry (R12-059). · [G1-014]</sub>
   * **Shared-ride requests and match rate**: Requests for a shared ride and how often they actually get paired with another rider. *Values:* UberX Share relaunched in NYC June 2022 [uncertain: date not re-checked]; up to 20% off; designed to add ~8 min on average [S]; [fact-check: Jan 2023 shared requests were 2.6% of HVFHV trips (Uber 1.7%, Lyft 5.4%) and matched shared trips 1.0% (Uber 0.3%, Lyft 2.8%), so the '<5%' guess holds]. `Low · World input · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber newsroom 'Introducing UberX Share'; [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (shared_request_flag, shared_match_flag) · Data: TLC HVFHV shared_request_flag/shared_match_flag · Model: Share option in product choice; pooling handled in matching (R06) · [R05-032]</sub>
   * **Wheelchair-accessible (WAV) requests**: Requests that need a wheelchair-accessible vehicle, with service-time standards set by TLC. *Values:* [corrected by fact-check: the '90% within 10 min' standard is in force, not just proposed: 35 RCNY 59B-17(f)(3)(i), amended eff. 6 Mar 2025, requires approved Accessible Vehicle dispatchers (the route Uber and Lyft use [U]) to serve 90% of WAV requests in under 10 min; the alternative is sending 25% of all trips to WAVs (59B-17(c)(1)); the earlier 80%/10 min and 90%/15 min standard was not re-checked]; WAV fleet >7,600 (Oct 2024) [S]; 88% picked up within 10 min (2024) [S]; WAV requests are 0.15% of HVFHV trips and 6.6% of trips are served by a WAV (Jan 2023). `Low · World input · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59B-17 (verbatim RCNY copy, github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive); [S] TLC FHV annual accessibility report 2024; [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (wav_request_flag, wav_match_flag) · Data: TLC HVFHV wav_request_flag/wav_match_flag; TLC accessibility reports · Model: WAV requests only matchable to WAV vehicles; boarding +5-10 min; fleet must meet service standard (R07) · [R05-031]</sub>
* **Pickup, boarding and street hails**
   * **Street-hail encounters and hail rights**: Chance an empty taxi meets a hailing rider, given foot traffic and how many empty taxis pass first; plus who may street-hail where. *Values:* Yellow anywhere (only yellow in the Manhattan core and at airports); green only outside that zone; app cars and robotaxis never; 0-2 hails per block-minute [design].. `High · Rule/price · hidden · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: Medallions grant street-hail rights citywide [S]; green Hail Exclusionary Zone = Manhattan south of E 96th St and W 110th St plus the NYC airports [verified by fact-check: was 'zone rules [U]'; P*, 35 RCNY 51-03]. · Data: Yellow and green pickups by zone (TLC); foot traffic (R01) · Model: Hail arrivals per street segment; the first empty eligible taxi to pass gets it. · [R06-032]</sub>
   * **Rider lateness at pickup**: Time between the car arriving and the rider getting in. *Values:* [corrected by fact-check: for Uber completed trips in Jan 2023, on-scene to pickup is median 0.8 min, mean 1.0 min, p90 2.0 min; 12.8% wait over 2 min and 0.5% over 5 min; was 'median 1-2 min [design guess]']; wait-time fee after 2 min [S]. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, taxi e-hail`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] TechCrunch 2016 (2-min wait fee); [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (on_scene_datetime vs pickup_datetime; completed trips only, so no-shows are excluded) · Data: TLC HVFHV on_scene_datetime [corrected by fact-check: the dictionary says 'Accessible Vehicles-only', but in Jan 2023 Uber (HV0003) fills it on 100% of trips and Lyft (HV0005) on ~0.1%] · Model: Lognormal delay by segment (elderly, luggage, groups slower) · [R05-038]</sub>
   * **Rider no-show**: Rider never appears at the pickup, so the driver waits and then cancels for a fee. *Values:* 1-5% of matched trips [design guess]; wait 5 min before no-show fee (2016 NYC pilot); fee $5-$10+ [uncertain: 2026 NYC wait and fee amounts not confirmed; see R07-035]. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, taxi e-hail`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] TechCrunch Apr 2016 on Uber wait/no-show pilot in NYC · Data: none found · Model: Bernoulli no-show with night and nightlife uplift; agent chooses when to cancel; fee per R07 · [R05-037]</sub>
   * **Street hail vs e-hail channel share**: Split of taxi demand between people hailing on the street and people booking a taxi through an app. *Values:* Yellow e-hail share ~5-15% [U]; ~14,000 cabs in 2022 Uber-Curb-Arro beta [S]; green trip_type separates hail from dispatch [fact-check: green Oct 2019 is 79.4% street hail (1), 1.9% dispatch (2), 18.8% blank]. `Med · World input · hidden · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Al Jazeera 2022 on Uber listing NYC cabs; [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (green trip_type, Oct 2019) · Data: TLC green trip_type; yellow has no channel flag · Model: Split taxi demand into hail stream and dispatched stream · [R05-043]</sub>
   * **Designated pickup areas**: Places where app riders must walk to a set pickup zone, such as airports, stadiums and some venues. *Values:* Rider walk 3-15 min; dedicated lots at JFK and LGA [S]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber JFK driver information page (cell phone lot, virtual queue) · Data: Uber airport pages; Port Authority ground transport pages · Model: Map pickups at these POIs to fixed pickup nodes with rider walk delay (cross-link R01, R03) · [R05-041]</sub>
   * **Street hailers' direction bias (taxi)**: Street hailers mostly flag cabs already heading their way, so the avenue and direction a cab cruises on shapes where its riders want to go. *Values:* On one-way avenues, 60-85% of hailers have a destination ahead of the cab [design guess]. The effect is weaker on two-way streets and at taxi stands.. `Low · World input · hidden · taxi (street hail); not app cars or robotaxis`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] taxi-driver lore. TLC data is zone-level with no heading, so it can only partly test this. It matters most for steering toward home or the garage at shift end (R05-060) [design]. · Data: none found (TLC yellow records give pickup and drop-off zones only) · Model: Condition each hail's destination on the cab's heading by tilting the R05-006 destination weights toward zones ahead of it on its one-way street. · [G2-006]</sub>
   * **Wrong-rider pickups at crowded curbs**: At crowded let-outs and hubs a driver can pick up the wrong rider, or spend extra minutes finding the right one, unless PIN verification is used. *Values:* 0.1-1% of pickups at crowded curbs, costing 2-10 min and sometimes an unpaid wrong trip [design]. Uber's optional PIN verification was shown in Sep 2019, piloted in 7 cities and rolled out to all US and Canada riders in Jan 2020; when it is on, a trip cannot start until the driver enters the PIN [S; corrected by fact-check, was 'since 2019 [U]']. Robotaxi: the rider unlocks the car in the app, so wrong-rider risk is near zero, but finding the car can take longer [design].. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver; robotaxi (finding the car)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; [S] Inverse and local news (Jan 2020) on the PIN rollout. R10-039 and R05-040 cover pin error and R10-040 covers curb queues, but none covers wrong-rider events. · Data: none found · Model: A mismatch probability that rises with the number of cars and riders at the pickup node (R10-040), set to zero for riders with PIN on. Each event adds delay, or an unpaid trip plus a rider cancellation. · [G2-014]</sub>
* **Destination value, stranding and airport demand**
   * **Airport arrival demand**: Requests from arriving passengers, driven by flight arrival waves and how many choose a taxi or app car. *Values:* LGA 42% by cab + 14% black car (2012-14); 41% of JFK and 63% of LGA travellers use taxi/limo/e-hail [uncertain: survey shares not re-read]; [fact-check: Jan 2023 airport pickups per day: JFK ~11,170 app vs ~5,160 yellow (app 68%), LGA ~9,920 app vs ~2,880 yellow (app 78%); airports are 8.6% of yellow pickups but 3.5% of HVFHV pickups, consistent with ride-hail taking airport share]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Port Authority survey via PANYNJ indicators; [S] 2021 airport access study; [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies · Data: BTS arrivals; PANYNJ traffic reports; 'Taxis Dispatched at Port Authority of NY NJ Airports' dataset (data.gov) · Model: Arrivals x passengers per flight x mode share, released 20-60 min after landing at terminal pickup areas · [R05-062]</sub>
   * **Airport-bound demand (departures)**: Requests to JFK, LGA and EWR, tied to flight departure times. *Values:* Early-morning wave: airport-bound share of weekday HVFHV trips peaks at 24-28% at 04:00-06:00; volume peaks at 05:00 with a second bump at 13:00-16:00 [fact-check: own calc, Jan 2023]; JFK yellow flat fare $70 (Dec 2022 rule) [fact-check: confirmed in 2023 yellow records, RatecodeID 2 fare $70]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] TLC 2022 fare rule coverage: JFK flat fare $52 to $70; [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies; [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26 (via R07) · Data: BTS on-time and schedule data https://www.transtats.bts.gov/; TLC DOLocationID 132 JFK, 138 LGA, 1 EWR (confirmed in zone lookup) · Model: Departures by hour minus 2-3 h lead, x origin shares from TLC · [R05-061]</sub>
   * **Destination re-dispatch value**: Expected wait for the next paying trip after dropping off in a zone, which makes some fares worth more than their price. *Values:* Manhattan core 1-5 min vs outer Queens or Staten Island 10-30+ min [design guess]. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] computable from TLC pickups and drop-offs; central to accept/decline and positioning · Data: TLC trip records · Model: Emerges from demand plus supply; harness logs idle time after each drop · [R05-058]</sub>
   * **Airport passenger destination and fare mix**: Where airport passengers go (Manhattan, Brooklyn, Long Island, NJ) and so what the trip pays. *Values:* JFK to Manhattan flat $70 (yellow, confirmed); LGA metered; [corrected by fact-check: Jan 2023 yellow plus HVFHV airport pickups go JFK -> Manhattan 39%, Queens 24%, Brooklyn 21%, outside NYC 14%; LGA -> Manhattan 57%, Brooklyn 15%, Queens 15%, outside NYC 9%; half of yellow JFK pickups use the flat fare (RatecodeID 2)]. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies; [S] 2022 TLC rate rule coverage · Data: TLC trips with PULocationID 132, 138, 1 (zone IDs confirmed) · Model: Empirical destination distribution for airport pickups · [R05-064]</sub>
   * **Flight delays shifting airport demand**: Late or cancelled flights move or remove arrival waves. *Values:* Waves shift 15-180 min; storm days cancel banks [design guess]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; cross-link R03 and R04 · Data: BTS on-time performance https://www.transtats.bts.gov/ · Model: Replay real arrival times per scenario day · [R05-065]</sub>
   * **Landing-to-curb lag (deplaning, immigration, baggage)**: The delay between a flight reaching the gate and its passengers requesting or hailing a ride, which is short for domestic flights and long and variable for international ones. *Values:* Domestic about 15-35 min after gate arrival [U]. International about 40-90+ min, of which the CBP passport queue is about 15-60+ min [S]. JFK: FlightQueue's estimate from historical CBP data is about 26 min for US citizens and 37-48 min for non-US citizens [S; corrected by fact-check: a third-party estimate, not an official CBP average]. Peaks run well over 1 h (a U.S. Travel Association report cited 4.5 h at JFK in Dec 2012) [S]. LGA is almost all domestic, JFK heavily international and EWR mixed [U]. This refines the flat 20-60 min release window in R05-062.. `Med · World input · public/forecast · all (airport pickups); taxi via the holding lot; fleet airport allocation (R11-024)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] FlightQueue estimate (above).
