[S] Global Gateway Alliance analysis of CBP data, reported Dec 2013: kiosks at JFK Terminal 4 cut the average wait from 34.9 to 16.6 min and the maximum from 97 to 58 min within a month of installation (Oct to Nov 2013; the blog's '2012' baseline date looks like a typo). Joining the queue at the scheduled landing of an international bank is about an hour early [design]. · Data: CBP Airport Wait Times https://awt.cbp.gov (hourly average and maximum waits by airport and terminal; host not reachable from this session) [U]. BTS On-Time Performance https://www.transtats.bts.gov/ gives gate-in times for domestic nonstop flights of reporting US carriers ONLY, so it misses all international arrivals [S, corrected by fact-check]. For international arrival times: airport flight-status pages or OpenSky Network ADS-B landing data [U]. PANYNJ monthly airport traffic reports give the domestic/international passenger split [U]. · Model: For each arriving flight, release requests at gate time plus a lognormal lag set by domestic/international and terminal, with the CBP part drawn from that hour's published wait. This replaces the fixed 20-60 min offset in R05-062. Generalises to any airport through its international share. · [G2-001]</sub>
   * **Out-of-city trips and stranding**: Trips to Nassau, Westchester, New Jersey or Newark that pay well but leave the car far from demand. *Values:* Taxis must serve NYC, Nassau, Westchester and Newark Airport [fact-check: confirmed, 35 RCNY 80-20(a)(1); a driver who has operated more than 8 h may refuse EWR, Nassau and Westchester, 80-20(b)(8)]; [corrected by fact-check: 4.5% of HVFHV trips end outside the city (3.9% in zone 265 + 0.6% at EWR; avg 16.6 mi, avg driver pay $44.6, Jan 2023) and 0.7% of yellow trips (2023), not '1-3% [U]'; HVFHV pickups in zone 265 are ~0.005% of trips, so the return is almost always empty]; empty return 30-90 min [design guess]. `Med · World input · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-20 (verbatim RCNY copy); [P*] taxi zone lookup: 1 = Newark Airport, 265 = Outside of NYC; [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (also R08 data folder) · Data: TLC DOLocationID (1 = EWR, 265 = Outside of NYC; confirmed in zone lookup); yellow RatecodeID 3 Newark, 4 Nassau/Westchester, 5 negotiated · Model: External destination nodes; low return-match chance; tolls on return (R07) · [R05-059]</sub>
* **Ratings, incidents and disputes**
   * **Fare disputes, refunds and chargebacks**: Riders who complain about route or fees and get money back, sometimes clawed from the driver. *Values:* 0.5-2% of trips disputed [design guess]; complaints of false cleaning fees rising. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, taxi, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] BBB-cited 'vomit fraud' complaints in press · Data: none found · Model: Dispute chance rises with detour vs shortest path and ETA slip; payout rule from R07 · [R05-056]</sub>
   * **Lost items and return fees**: Items riders leave behind, which the driver can return for a fee. *Values:* $20 return fee to driver (Uber Canada 2023; older $15); 0.5-1% of trips [design guess]. `Low · World input · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber Canada 2023 Lost and Found Index; Tile 2024 analysis · Data: none found (frequency) · Model: Bernoulli lost item; agent decides return detour vs keep driving · [R05-055]</sub>
   * **Non-payment and fare evasion**: Taxi riders who run off without paying or whose card fails. *Values:* 0.1-0.5% of cash taxi trips [design guess]. `Low · World input · hidden · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] · Data: none found · Model: Bernoulli loss of fare on cash trips, higher late night · [R05-057]</sub>
   * **Ratings riders give the driver**: Star ratings riders leave, driven by wait, route, cleanliness and conduct, feeding the driver's rating. *Values:* Mostly 5 stars; 1-3 low ratings per 100 trips rising with delays [design guess]. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; matters through deactivation risk in R09 · Data: none found · Model: P(rating | service quality) updates driver rating state (R09) · [R05-051]</sub>
   * **Rider rating shown to driver**: The rider's own rating shown on the offer, a weak signal of no-show, mess or low tips. *Values:* Average rider ~4.89 (third party); NYC riders get the lowest ratings [uncertain: not re-checked]; low-rated riders can lose access (Uber, 2019). `Low · World input · agent sees · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber 2019 announcement (USA Today); [S] third-party FAQs · Data: none found · Model: Request attribute correlated with no-show, incident and tip propensity · [R05-052]</sub>
   * **Rider safety incidents**: Rare assaults, robberies or harassment involving riders or drivers. *Values:* 99.9% of Uber US trips had no reported safety incident (2017-18); 20 physical-assault deaths in 2019-20 (15 riders) [uncertain: rider/driver split not re-checked]. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, taxi, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber US Safety Reports (press coverage) · Data: Uber US Safety Report https://www.uber.com/us/en/about/reports/us-safety-report/ [U URL] · Model: Rare event with night and zone multipliers; trip lost, downtime, may end shift (R09) · [R05-053]</sub>
   * **Vomit, mess and cleaning fee**: A rider makes a mess, the car goes off duty for cleaning and the rider is charged a fee. *Values:* Uber tiers $20 small, $40 vomit, $80 large spill, $150 heavy bodily fluids; 1 per 500-2,000 late-weekend trips [design guess]. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber fee tiers via local news on 'vomit fraud' · Data: none found (frequency) · Model: P(mess | nightlife, hour) leads to 30-90 min downtime plus fee and dispute chance · [R05-054]</sub>

### 8. Competition and matching (53)
*The other cars on the road and how the platform decides who gets each rider.*

* **Competitor supply volume and mix**
   * **Competitor cars online by zone and hour**: Number of other app cars and taxis logged on (busy or idle) in each taxi zone in each 15-minute slot. *Values:* NYC app cars online: about 15k on average (15-17k if utilisation is nearer 53%), about 25-30k at the evening peak, about 4-6k before dawn [design calc: ~667k trips/day x ~19 min / ~0.58 utilisation]; plus about 3-5k yellow taxis on the road in a daytime hour [corrected by fact-check: was 5-8k [U]; ~130k yellow trips/day x ~16 min / ~0.5-0.6 occupancy gives ~2.5-3k on average, x ~1.5 at peak [design calc]; check against TLC monthly vehicles_per_day and avg_hours_per_day_per_vehicle]; 0 to ~1,500 per zone.. `High · World input · hidden · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: Matching collapses into long pickups when idle supply thins (Castillo, Knoepfle & Weyl 2017) [P, re-read by fact-check]; 243M app trips in 2025 (TLC budget testimony, Mar 2026) [S] [corrected by fact-check: a count of the 2025 HVFHV records gives 243,589,684 trips, about 667k/day; S, FerrisSearch analysis on GitHub]; online totals are [design] arithmetic. · Data: TLC monthly report CSV (vehicles_per_day, avg_hours_per_day_per_vehicle, unique_drivers) https://www.nyc.gov/assets/tlc/downloads/csv/data_reports_monthly.csv ; HVFHV trip records https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Supply field per zone x 15 min, scaled so citywide vehicle-hours match the TLC monthly report and local pickup times match HVFHV request-to-on-scene times [corrected by fact-check: Uber rows only; Lyft (HV0005) leaves on_scene_datetime empty on ~99.7% of trips, S, fleetsim notes on GitHub]. · [R06-001]</sub>
   * **Idle cars per open request (local balance)**: Ratio of idle competing cars to unserved requests in a zone at a moment; it sets how fast any one driver gets a ping and whether surge fires. *Values:* 0.2 (shortage: surge, long pickups) to >10 (glut: long idle); about 1/3 of taxi and app cars in the Manhattan core are empty at any moment (2013-2017 data).. `High · Changes during run · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Schaller, Empty Seats, Full Streets (2017): about one-third of vehicles in the Manhattan CBD empty [S]; thin idle supply triggers wild goose chases [P, re-read by fact-check]. · Data: Derived in simulation; Schaller report https://schallerconsult.com/rideservices/emptyseats.htm · Model: Computed each tick from idle background cars and pending requests per zone; feeds ping rate, the surge model (R07) and pickup ETAs. · [R06-002]</sub>
   * **Active yellow taxi fleet**: How many of the 13,587 medallions are actually on the road, competing for street hails and taxi e-hails. *Values:* About 10,520 active and about 3,069 in storage (Sep 2025) [uncertain: these sum to 13,589, not 13,587; source not re-checked]; over 1,000 medallions back on the road since Sep 2024; about 3-5k on the road in a daytime hour [corrected by fact-check: was 5-8k [U], which ~130k trips/day cannot keep busy; design calc as in R06-001].. `Med · World input · public/forecast · taxi, human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: AutoMarketplace analysis of TLC data, Sep 2025 [S]; 13,587 medallion total [U]. · Data: TLC monthly report (Yellow rows); AutoMarketplace https://automarketplace.substack.com/p/nyc-taxi-medallions-continue-gaining · Model: Scale the yellow bot fleet to the scenario date; each taxi bot has a shift schedule and a cruising policy. · [R06-004]</sub>
   * **Competitor product and vehicle-class mix**: Share of competing cars eligible for each product (standard, XL, Comfort, Black/Lux, wheelchair-accessible, Green/EV), since dispatch only matches within eligible classes. *Values:* Mostly standard; XL ~10-15%, Black/Lux ~5% [U]; wheelchair-accessible cars served about 9% of 2025 app trips (21.4M of 243.6M had wav_match_flag = Y) [corrected by fact-check: was ~5-10% of cars [U]; S, FerrisSearch analysis on GitHub]; EV share rising (Green Rides target for 2030).. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Public app trip data has no product field, only wheelchair request/match flags [S, schema read on GitHub]; class shares [U]. · Data: None found for the XL/Comfort/Black mix; HVFHV wav_match_flag gives the WAV share of trips [S]; NYC Open Data active FHV vehicle list (make, model, WAV) [U] · Model: Give each bot a vehicle class and keep separate supply pools per product; the agent's own class (R08) decides which pool it competes in. · [R06-007]</sub>
   * **Licensed pool versus active supply**: Headroom between licensed cars and drivers and those actually working, which limits how many extra cars can flood in for surge or events. *Values:* 82,225 licensed app (HVFHV) vehicles; 179,670 licensed taxi and FHV drivers (TLC 2025); a fraction works on any one day (~40-60% [U]).. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: TLC annual report 2025 [S]; daily share from TLC monthly vehicles_per_day vs unique_vehicles [U values]. · Data: TLC annual report https://www.nyc.gov/assets/tlc/downloads/pdf/annual_report_2025.pdf ; TLC monthly report · Model: Cap on total background drivers who can log on; the entry model draws from the inactive pool. · [R06-006]</sub>
   * **Trip market shares by service**: Share of trips served by Uber, Lyft, yellow taxis, green taxis and other bases, which sets how many requests flow through each channel the agent can use. *Values:* 2025: app (HVFHV) about 667k trips/day (243.6M), Uber ~72% (176.0M) and Lyft ~28% (67.6M) [corrected by fact-check: full-year counts of the 2025 HVFHV records, S, FerrisSearch analysis on GitHub] (Lyft 27.7% in Sep 2025); yellow about 130k/day (+16% 2024 to 2025, 57% of the 2019 level); green ~1-2k/day [U]; other bases (black car, livery, luxury) are small [corrected by fact-check: the ~1,059 'other' entries are a cumulative base-number list kept in a GitHub repo, not a count of bases active in 2025].. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: TLC testimony 17 Mar 2026 (243M app trips; taxi trips +16%, 57% of 2019) [S]; Uber/Lyft split from third-party analysis of TLC data [S]; a base map lists 30 Uber, 2 Lyft, 2 Via, 4 Juno and 1,059 other base numbers [S, re-read on GitHub by fact-check]; licence codes HV0002 Juno, HV0003 Uber, HV0004 Via, HV0005 Lyft [S, re-read]. · Data: TLC trip records (hvfhs_license_num: HV0003 Uber, HV0005 Lyft); FHV Base Aggregate Report https://data.cityofnewyork.us/Transportation/FHV-Base-Aggregate-Report/2v9c-2k7f ; https://github.com/toddwschneider/nyc-taxi-data · Model: Split generated requests into channels by zone-hour shares from trip records; each channel has its own matcher and background fleet. · [R06-003]</sub>
   * **Green taxi fleet and hail zone**: Size of the green (boro taxi) fleet, which may street-hail only outside Manhattan south of E 96th / W 110th St and outside the airports. *Values:* Small: ~1-2k trips/day and a few hundred to ~1,000 active cars [U]; the hail-exclusion zone is fixed: Manhattan south of E 96th St and W 110th St plus the NYC airports; green cars also may not start pre-arranged (base or app) trips in that Manhattan zone, though they may at airports [P*, 35 RCNY 51-03 and 82-13, verified by fact-check].. `Low · World input · public/forecast · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: Green volume shrank as app trips grew; no 2025-26 vehicle count found [U]. · Data: TLC green trip records; TLC monthly report (Green rows) · Model: Small bot fleet restricted to outer-borough and upper-Manhattan hail polygons; matters only if the agent plays a green taxi. · [R06-005]</sub>
   * **Informal and unlicensed competition**: Dollar vans, unlicensed livery cars and illegal street pickups by app cars, which take cheap short trips and hails in some neighbourhoods and at let-outs. Informal transit exists in other cities too, but its size is local. *Values:* TLC chair (Oct 2024): 35 TLC-licensed commuter vans vs 215 in 2019. Other counts: 31 in service (THE CITY); 38 licensed vehicles at 12 bases (City Council, 2024); 584 licensed in 2015. A state insurance-subsidy programme from Sep 2024 was credited with a 50% rise in licensed vans. Many more unlicensed vans run on the Flatbush Ave, Jamaica and Chinatown corridors [U]. Ridership is about 120,000/day (King & Goldwyn 2014 estimate; about 125,000 in a recent Economist piece) [S, dated]. Demand lost: 0-10% of short trips in affected zones [design].. `Low · World input · hidden · taxi; human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] City Limits and Next City (TLC chair, Oct 2024); amNY (City Council 2024 counts); ridership via an ASU study citing King & Goldwyn 2014. No current official ridership count exists. [corrected: ridership '~100k+ [U]' replaced by dated estimates]. · Data: TLC commuter-van licensing (counts via press); none found for unlicensed activity · Model: Remove a zone-hour share of short-trip and hail demand before generation. Illegal hail competitors at let-outs reduce taxi hail encounters (R05-042). · [G1-013]</sub>
* **Competitor driver behaviour**
   * **Shift start (log-on) times**: When competing drivers start work, by hour and weekday, mixing full-time and part-time drivers. *Values:* Weekday start peaks around 5-8 am and 3-6 pm [U]; full-time drivers gave ~80% of app rides (2018); 60-65% of yellow drivers are full-time (2026).. `High · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Parrott & Reich 2018 report for TLC [S]; TLC testimony 17 Mar 2026 [S]. · Data: TLC monthly report (avg_hours_per_day_per_driver, avg_days_drivers_on_road); 2010-2013 yellow trips with hashed driver IDs https://databank.illinois.edu/datasets/IDB-9610843 [U] · Model: Draw each bot's start time from an hour x weekday distribution fitted to supply curves; full-time (10-12 h) and part-time (2-5 h) types. · [R06-008]</sub>
   * **Short-run surge entry**: How strongly offline drivers log on, or online drivers stay longer, when surge or earnings rise. *Values:* Supply elasticity to hourly earnings of 0.2-1.0 within 15-60 min; lag 5-30 min [design].. `High · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Chen & Sheldon (Uber, US cities 2014-15): surge raises driver supply when surge is high [S]. · Data: None public for NYC; calibrate to how fast HVFHV pickup times recover after demand spikes · Model: Entry rate = base x (1 + e x (surge - 1)) with a lag; lower log-off hazard during surge. · [R06-010]</sub>
   * **Competitor acceptance and cherry-picking**: How often competitors decline offers (short, low pay, long pickup, unwanted destination), pushing those offers down the line to the agent. *Values:* Acceptance 50-95% by driver type [U]; top Uber Pro tiers need 90%+ acceptance in some markets.. `Med · Rule/price · hidden · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber Pro terms (Hong Kong) require 90%+ acceptance for top tiers [S]; NYC values [U]; NYC lets platforms restrict a driver's access without the usual 72 h notice for excessive cancellation or non-acceptance [P*, 35 RCNY 59D-22(c)(3), added by fact-check]. · Data: None found · Model: Bot accept probability = logistic(pay per minute, pickup ETA, destination); declined offers go back to the matcher. · [R06-016]</sub>
   * **Competitor information level as its own setting**: How much public event, weather and calendar information the background bots act on (share of informed bots and their lead time), set separately from the agent's information arm. *Values:* Share of informed bots 0 / 25 / 50 / 100%; lead time 0-120 min; crossed with the agent's arm (R03-060, R04-049) in a 2x2 or 3x2 design [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden · all; single car and fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P, from list] R06-052 ties bot event knowledge to the agent's treatment arm, which mixes two effects into one; [design]. · Data: none needed · Model: The bot policy reads the same info feeds through a per-bot access flag. The experiment matrix crosses bot and agent information levels. · [G1-028]</sub>
   * **Competitor multi-apping share**: Share of competitors logged into Uber and Lyft at once, so one car counts as supply on both and vanishes from both when matched. *Values:* US, Q1 2024: ~55% of Lyft drivers also on Uber, ~25% of Uber drivers also on Lyft; NYC share unknown.. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Gridwise driver-panel data [S]; Chicago's TNP driver data flags drivers reported by more than one company (MULTIPLE_TNPS) [S]; NYC's cruising rule already splits a car's idle time across platforms when it is available to more than one HVFHS [P*, 35 RCNY 59D-21(b)(1), added by fact-check]. · Data: Gridwise https://gridwise.io/blog/revealing-rideshare-trends-multi-apping-and-airport-insights ; Chicago TNP Drivers https://catalog.data.gov/dataset/transportation-network-providers-drivers · Model: Flag bots single- or dual-app; dual-app bots sit in both platforms' idle pools and take the better offer. · [R06-015]</sub>
   * **Competitor strategy mix**: Mix of background driver policies (cruiser, hotspot parker, airport queuer, surge chaser, home-anchored commuter, cherry-picker), with skill differences. *Values:* e.g. 30% hotspot parkers, 20% cruisers, 15% airport queuers, 15% surge chasers, 10% commuters, 10% random [design]; earnings-per-hour spread of +/-20-30% [U].. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Experience and where/when choices explain earnings gaps among Uber drivers (Cook et al. 2021) [U]; mix [design]. · Data: None found · Model: Bot population with archetype policies; mix tuned so zone supply and airport queue lengths match data. · [R06-017]</sub>
   * **Cruising versus parking when idle**: Whether idle competitors cruise (taxis hunting hails) or park and wait for pings (app drivers), which changes hail odds and congestion. *Values:* Empty time between passengers in the Manhattan core: 8 min for yellow vs 11 min for app cars (2017); app cars empty 41% of the time south of 96th St (2019).. `Med · Rule/price · hidden · taxi, human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: Schaller 2017 [S]; TLC data cited in the 2019 cruising-cap ruling [S]. · Data: Schaller report; TLC trip records · Model: Taxi bots cruise high-hail routes; app bots park near hotspots or drift; both feed the traffic model (R10). · [R06-019]</sub>
   * **Home anchoring of competitors**: Competitors start and end shifts near home (mostly Queens, Brooklyn, the Bronx, NJ, Long Island), so supply is rich in the outer boroughs early and drains from Manhattan late. *Values:* Home mix e.g. Queens 35-45%, Brooklyn 20-25%, Bronx 15-20%, other 15-25% [U]; 10-20% of late-evening idle cars heading home [design].. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: NYC app drivers are mostly immigrants living in the outer boroughs (Parrott & Reich 2018) [U]; mix [U]. · Data: None public for app drivers' homes; first and last pickups per shift in 2010-2013 taxi data [U]; Census ACS commute data [U] · Model: Each bot has a home zone; shifts start there and the last hour is biased toward home (uses the destination filter). · [R06-013]</sub>
   * **Incumbent platform response to the fleet**: How Uber, Lyft and their drivers react when a fleet takes share, with targeted rider discounts, driver incentives or price cuts in the fleet's zones. *Values:* Competitor price cut of 0-5% per 10 points of share the fleet takes [design]. In San Francisco, Waymo's premium over Uber narrowed from about 31% (Mar-Apr 2025) to about 12.7% (Thanksgiving to New Year 2025, ~100k rides), cause unknown (R07-061) [S].. `Med · World input · public/forecast · robotaxi fleet, human fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Obi price studies via TechCrunch (Jun 2025), Singularity Hub (Feb 2026) and Business Insider; primary Obi report not read. Holding competitor prices fixed overstates what a new entrant can win; R06-012 and R06-068 cover driver supply, not platform pricing. · Data: Obi price reports [S]; none official · Model: A competitor price multiplier in the fleet's zones responds, with a lag of days, to the fleet's recent share; off in single-car mode. · [G2-045]</sub>
   * **Long-run re-equilibration**: After fares or earnings stay higher for weeks, drivers add hours until utilisation falls and hourly pay returns near its old level. *Values:* Hourly earnings back near baseline about 2 months after an Uber fare rise.. `Med · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Hall, Horton & Knoepfle, NBER w30883, Ride-Sharing Markets Re-Equilibrate (2023) [S]. · Data: NBER w30883 https://www.nber.org/papers/w30883 · Model: For multi-day and learning runs (10 days then day 11), drift background supply day by day so competitor hourly pay tends to a target; off in single-day runs. · [R06-012]</sub>
   * **Shift end (log-off) rule**: What makes competitors stop: hours worked, a daily income target, fatigue, or a legal cap on hours. *Values:* Stop after 8-12 h, or at an income target of $200-350/day [design]; TLC fatigue rule: at most 10 h of passenger time in any 24 h (the count resets after 8 consecutive hours with no passengers) and 60 h per Monday-Sunday week; time between trips does not count [corrected by fact-check: P*, 35 RCNY 80-14(f)].. `Med · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Camerer et al. 1997 found negative wage elasticity (income targeting) among NYC cab drivers; Uber data shows supply rising with surge (Chen & Sheldon) [S]; later NYC cab work (Farber 2015, QJE) finds little income targeting, so the evidence is mixed [U, added by fact-check]. · Data: 2010-2013 yellow trips with driver IDs (Illinois Data Bank) [U] · Model: Log-off hazard = f(hours online, earnings vs target, time of day), fitted to shift-length distributions. · [R06-009]</sub>
   * **Surge chasing**: Share of idle competitors who drive toward surge or busy zones, and how fast, which wears surge away before late arrivals get there. *Values:* 10-40% of idle drivers within 2-3 km respond; reaction lag 2-10 min [design]; surge recomputed every ~1-5 min [U].. `Med · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Driver interviews report surge often gone on arrival (Lee et al. 2015, CHI) [U]; ranges [design]. · Data: None found · Model: Bot policy: with probability p move toward the zone with the best expected earnings within a radius; produces herding. · [R06-011]</sub>
   * **Taxi 4-5 pm shift change**: Yellow taxis go off duty to hand over at garages (mostly in Queens) in the late afternoon, cutting taxi supply just as demand rises. *Values:* Taxis on the road fell from 6,688 to 5,452 between 4 and 5 pm (2011), an 18.5% drop vs the hour before [corrected by fact-check: arithmetic; was 'about 20%'].. `Med · World input · public/forecast · taxi, human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: TLC 2011 study via DNAinfo; later city GPS review [S]. · Data: Yellow trip records (pickups by hour); https://www.dnainfo.com/new-york/20110112/manhattan/fewer-taxis-out-during-evening-rush-hour-study-shows · Model: Two-shift lease bots hand over at 4-5 pm with a deadhead to garage zones; check today's dip in 2024-26 yellow pickups by hour. · [R06-018]</sub>
   * **Competitor breaks**: Short supply dips when many drivers pause at once for meals, prayer, bathrooms or rest. *Values:* Lunch 12-2 pm; dinner or iftar at sunset; Friday midday prayers; 10-45 min break per 4-6 h [U].. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design guess]; an evening dip in Ramadan was reported by Uber in Paris (2017) [S]. · Data: None found · Model: Break hazard by time of day per bot type; break spots near food and parking clusters (R01). · [R06-014]</sub>
* **Platform dispatch and matching**
   * **Matching algorithm**: How the platform pairs requests and drivers: send the nearest idle driver at once, or optimise over a short batch. *Values:* First-dispatch nearest | batched matching every 2-10 s (Uber: a few seconds) | global minimum total ETA.. `High · Rule/price · public/forecast · core MVP · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber's marketplace page describes batched matching over a few seconds [S]; first-dispatch causes wild goose chases when prices are low (Castillo et al.) [P, re-read by fact-check]. · Data: Uber matching page https://www.uber.com/us/en/marketplace/matching/ · Model: Pluggable matcher; default batched assignment over idle drivers within the radius every 5 s; sensitivity-test the window. · [R06-020]</sub>
   * **Wild goose chase threshold**: Point where idle supply is so thin that matches go to far-away drivers, pickups lengthen, cancellations spike and pay falls. *Values:* Appears when idle density falls below the level where pickup ETA climbs steeply (city-specific) [design].. `High · Changes during run · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Castillo, Knoepfle & Weyl: in thin hours and areas of Manhattan many riders and drivers cancel and waits jump [P, re-read by fact-check]. · Data: MSR abstract https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/ECabstract.pdf · Model: Emerges from matcher, r_max and rider patience; include a capped-surge scenario to test it. · [R06-039]</sub>
   * **Equal dispatch treatment of agent and background cars**: Whether the simulated matcher, offer timeout, dispatch radius, tier perks and penalties treat the agent's cars exactly like the background bots. *Values:* Parity (default) / documented deviations. Checks: the agent's share of offers vs bots at equal ETA, decline-cascade timing, and tier perks (R09-041) applied by state, not by identity [design].. `Med · Rule/price · hidden · human app driver; robotaxi; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]. [S, from list] Real apps have priority tiers (Uber Pro, R09-041), so any perk must be applied the same way to both. · Data: none needed · Model: The matcher sees only vehicle state, not identity. A parity test runs a scripted clone of a bot as the 'agent' and checks that its offer rates match the bots'. · [G1-027]</sub>
   * **Forward dispatch**: The platform offers the next trip to a driver who is about to drop off, competing with nearby idle drivers. *Values:* On/off; look-ahead 0-5 min before drop-off [U].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Uber and Lyft send next-trip offers before drop-off. · Data: None found · Model: Busy drivers with <= L minutes left join the matching pool; cost = time left + ETA from the drop-off. · [R06-023]</sub>
   * **Matching cost metric**: What 'closest' means: straight-line distance, road travel time, or travel time with penalties for wrong heading, U-turns and bridges. *Values:* Straight-line | road ETA (R10) | road ETA + heading penalty.. `Med · Rule/price · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; Uber says batching lowers average wait [S]; a Manhattan HVFHV replay found batched min-cost assignment cut rider wait 5.3% and pickup deadhead 8.2% vs greedy nearest-car dispatch (fleetsim, GitHub) [S, added by fact-check]. · Data: OpenStreetMap road graph; NYC DOT speeds (R10) · Model: Use road ETA from the traffic model; penalise drivers pointed the wrong way on one-way avenues. · [R06-021]</sub>
   * **Maximum dispatch radius**: Furthest a driver can be sent for a pickup; beyond it the request waits or fails. *Values:* 8-15 min pickup cap typical [U]; a small radius avoids wild goose chases but leaves riders queued.. `Med · Rule/price · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Castillo et al.: a small maximum dispatch radius avoids wild goose chases but creates passenger queues [P, re-read by fact-check]. · Data: None (platform internal); infer from the tail of HVFHV request-to-on-scene times · Model: Parameter r_max in minutes; unmatched requests retry each batch, then cancel by rider patience (R05). · [R06-022]</sub>
   * **Offer timeout and decline cascade**: How long a driver has to accept, and how a declined or expired offer moves to the next driver. *Values:* About 10 s traditionally, 15 s in an Uber pilot [uncertain: not re-checked; driver guides often give ~15 s as Uber's standing window]; cascade to the next-best driver 3-5 times [U].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: The Rideshare Guy on Uber extending ping time [S]. · Data: https://therideshareguy.com/uber-extends-offer-acceptance-time-ping-for-drivers/ · Model: Offer queue per request; simulated time pauses for the agent's decision (latency handled as a harness setting, R12). · [R06-024]</sub>
   * **Product eligibility and opt-ins**: Which request types a car can receive (X, XL, Comfort, Black, Green/EV, wheelchair), including higher classes opting into lower ones. *Values:* Yes/no per product; Black and XL drivers can opt into X [U].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber-Waymo: robotaxis matched to UberX, Green, Comfort and Comfort Electric requests [S]; opt-in rules [U]. · Data: None found (product rules) · Model: Each request carries a product; the matcher filters eligible cars; the agent can toggle opt-ins. · [R06-029]</sub>
   * **Taxi e-hail channel**: Taxis also get app dispatches through Uber, Curb or Arro, a second request stream. *Values:* 5-25% of yellow trips via e-hail [U]; rising since Uber and Curb began dispatching cabs.. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: AutoMarketplace: yellow trips +17-20% year on year (Aug-Sep 2025), driven by returning cabs and Uber/Curb e-hail [S]. · Data: Partial: yellow and green payment_type = 0 marks Flex Fare (upfront-priced e-hail) trips, a lower bound on e-hail [corrected by fact-check: was 'none found'; P* via R07 dictionary excerpt]; metered e-hail trips are not flagged · Model: Route a share of app requests to taxis through a taxi e-hail matcher. · [R06-033]</sub>
   * **Broadcast offers (Trip Radar)**: Some requests are shown to several drivers at once and go to the first to accept, which rewards speed. *Values:* 0-20% of requests broadcast [design]; first to accept wins.. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber Trip Radar blog and driver reports of first-come matching [S]. · Data: https://www.uber.com/blog/introducing-trip-radar · Model: Broadcast unmatched requests to k drivers; winner is first accept in simulated time, so LLM wall-clock latency does not count. · [R06-026]</sub>
   * **Shared-ride matching**: Pooling riders into one car adds detours and changes per-trip pay. *Values:* shared_request_flag and shared_match_flag in the data; 2025: about 6.8M shared requests (2.8% of app trips, all Uber), of which 3.9M matched (1.6%) [corrected by fact-check: was 'small share [U]'; S, FerrisSearch analysis on GitHub].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: HVFHV columns [S]; pooling makes wild goose chases worse (Castillo et al.) [P]. · Data: HVFHV trip records · Model: Optional insertion heuristic; off in v1. · [R06-031]</sub>
   * **Wheelchair and Access-A-Ride dispatch**: Wheelchair-accessible requests and paratransit trips go only to accessible cars, under service-level targets. *Values:* Flags wav_request_flag, wav_match_flag and access_a_ride_flag in the app trip data; WAV-requested and WAV-matched trips about 699k of 243.6M in 2025 (~0.3%) [corrected by fact-check: column names re-read on GitHub; share from FerrisSearch analysis [S]]; accessible taxis must accept dispatches from the Accessible Taxi Dispatcher [P*, 35 RCNY 53-08].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: HVFHV columns [S, read on GitHub]; accessible licences are the only new FHV licences in 2026 [S]. · Data: HVFHV trip records · Model: Separate accessible request stream; accessible cars get priority dispatch. · [R06-030]</sub>
* **Airport and stand queues**
   * **Airport queue length and position**: First-in-first-out virtual queue in airport waiting lots; the app shows your position and how many drivers are ahead. *Values:* 0 to 500+ cars; position visible; JFK Cell Phone Lot and Limo Lot.. `High · Changes during run · agent sees · core MVP · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber JFK driver page [S]; Gridwise crowdsourced queue counts [S]. · Data: Uber JFK page; HVFHV pickups in airport zones · Model: One FIFO per airport and platform; inflow from drop-offs and arrivals, outflow = airport requests. · [R06-040]</sub>
   * **Airport queue rules**: Rules for keeping your place: stay inside the zone, stay online, do not decline or cancel repeatedly; lot capacity. *Values:* Lose place if offline, outside the zone, several declines or cancels in a row; a rider cancel does not cost the place.. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber JFK driver page [S]. · Data: Uber JFK driver page · Model: Geofence plus queue state machine; overflow turned away when the lot is full [design]. · [R06-042]</sub>
   * **Airport queue service rate**: How fast airport queues drain, set by arriving passengers' ride requests on each platform. *Values:* Depends on flight arrivals; 2025 app pickups: LGA 4.92M (~13.5k/day) and JFK 4.07M (~11.2k/day), ~25k/day combined, and the two top app pickup zones by revenue [corrected by fact-check: was ~10-25k/day [U]; S, FerrisSearch analysis of 2025 HVFHV records].. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] 2025 HVFHV counts; flight data is a cross-link to R03 and R05. · Data: HVFHV pickups in airport zones; BTS on-time data https://www.transtats.bts.gov ; Port Authority traffic reports · Model: Airport request process tied to the arrivals schedule, split by platform share. · [R06-045]</sub>
   * **Product-specific airport queues**: App airport queues are kept per product tier, so an XL, Black or WAV car can wait in a much shorter line than an X car at the same airport. Extends R06-040 and R06-042 (one queue per airport and platform). Lot rules are airport-specific; generalise with a per-airport rule table. *Values:* A separate virtual queue per airport x platform x product (X/Comfort, XL, Black/SUV, WAV). At LGA, UberX and XL wait in the FHV Hold Lot (46-10 Berrian Blvd). Black and Black SUV wait in the Cell Phone Lot on 94th St between Astoria Blvd and 23rd Ave. Both lots are open 06:00-01:00. Where a car parks inside the lot does not change its place. Per-product queue lengths: none found (design default: XL queue 0.2-0.5x and Black 0.1-0.3x the X queue). WAV queue handling: none found.. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver (XL/Black/WAV vs X); fleet vehicle-class mix; robotaxi only if airports are in the ODD`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber LaGuardia driver page via search snippets (uber.com unreachable from here): separate lots for X/XL and Black/Black SUV, open 6 am-1 am; a driver loses their place if offline, outside the zone or after repeated cancels. [uncertain: 2020-21 guides describe different lot layouts, so check the current page]. [U] The app shows queue position per product. Queue sizes are [design]. · Data: Uber airport driver pages https://www.uber.com/drive/new-york/airports/laguardia-airport/ ; HVFHV airport-zone pickups (zones 132 JFK, 138 LGA; product not recorded); none found for per-product queue lengths · Model: One FIFO queue per airport x platform x product. Service rate = airport requests for that product (product mix from R05-024). A car joins every queue it is eligible for, including opt-ins (R06-029); its first offer removes it from all of them. · [G1-002]</sub>
   * **Taxi hold lot and short-return ticket**: Yellow taxis queue in JFK and LGA holding lots; a short trip to nearby areas earns a ticket to an expedited line if they return in time. *Values:* 2-hour return window (since 2012); JFK short-return area = Queens, Brooklyn, Five Towns [uncertain: window length and area not re-checked; the notice is on nyc.gov, which was blocked].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: TLC industry notice 12-33 [S]. · Data: https://www.nyc.gov/assets/tlc/downloads/pdf/archived_industry_notices/industry_notice_12_33.pdf · Model: Ticket state with expiry; priority lane in the taxi queue. · [R06-043]</sub>
   * **Airport rematch and priority tiers**: A driver dropping off at the airport may get a pickup before joining the queue; top-tier drivers get priority. *Values:* Uber Pro Diamond/Platinum priority rematch (Australia, Hong Kong); NYC unknown.. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber Australia blog; Uber Hong Kong Pro terms [S]. · Data: https://www.uber.com/en-AU/blog/priority-airport-rematches-with-uber-pro · Model: At drop-off, probability p of a rematch from the queue head, higher for top tiers. · [R06-044]</sub>
* **Supply shocks and calendar dips**
   * **Driver strikes and protests**: Organised refusals to work or to serve a place, which cut competitor supply for a while. *Values:* e.g. LGA pickup strike 26 Feb 2023, noon to midnight; Uber claimed 36% more LGA trips that day; 0-3 a year.. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Gothamist and Fox Business reports [S]. · Data: News; NYTWA announcements · Model: Scenario event removing 10-60% of bots from a zone and window; announced in advance in event-aware runs. · [R06-047]</sub>
   * **Event let-out convergence**: Competitors flock to stadium, concert and venue exits, so a known event draws extra supply and shortens surge. *Values:* 0.5-3x normal supply within 1 km in the 30 min before let-out [design].. `Med · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Supply rises with surge (Chen & Sheldon) [S]; size [design]. · Data: None found · Model: Share of bots that know the event schedule; tie to the with/without event-info treatment so rivals and agent are matched. · [R06-052]</sub>
   * **Seasonal and holiday driver supply**: How many drivers are working changes with season and holidays (vacations abroad, Ramadan, Christmas), changing competition. *Values:* Unknown magnitude; proxy: rider waits by month and holiday (NYD 01h median wait 8.6 vs 4.8 min). `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver, taxi, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023 (wait proxy); monthly active-driver counts not read [U] · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page; TLC monthly industry reports (driver counts) · Model: Driver supply multiplier by month and holiday in R06, calibrated so waits match · [R02-032]</sub>
   * **Religious and cultural supply dips**: Competitor supply drops around iftar in Ramadan and Friday midday prayers, since many NYC drivers are Muslim. *Values:* -5% to -25% supply for 30-60 min at sunset in Ramadan [design]; Friday 12-2 pm dip [U].. `Low · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber France reported an evening driver drop in Ramadan 2017 (Paris) [S]; NYC not measured. · Data: None for NYC; test in HVFHV on-scene times · Model: Calendar-driven break hazard for a share of bots. · [R06-049]</sub>
* **Robotaxis, rival model fleets and incumbent response**
   * **Number of competing model fleets**: How many LLM agents, each running 1 or N cars, compete in the same market. *Values:* 1 (solo against bots) up to 5 models x N cars.. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet, all`
      * <sub>Evidence: Multi-agent rankings differ from solo ones in Vending-Bench Arena (team dossier D07) [S]. · Data: n/a · Model: Harness setting with seat rotation and mirrored runs (R12). · [R06-060]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi background fleet**: Number of driverless cars competing in the scenario city. *Values:* NYC 2026: none carrying passengers (test permit Aug 2025 for 8 cars with safety drivers, expired 31 Mar 2026; state law allows testing only); scenarios 0-10% of supply; Waymo US ~3,000 cars in early 2026 [S] [uncertain: the 3,800 upper figure from the May 2026 recall was not re-checked].. `Med · Benchmark setting · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: TechCrunch; West Side Spirit; fleet count from a May 2026 NHTSA recall [S]; NYC facts (Aug 2025 approval, 8 cars, specialist behind the wheel, Manhattan south of 112th St and Downtown Brooklyn, permits expired 31 Mar 2026, Hochul's Feb 2026 proposal withdrawn, ~3,000 US cars) match Vital City's Waymo map methodology on GitHub [S, re-checked by fact-check]. · Data: News; California CPUC AV data [U] · Model: Scenario parameter; robotaxi bots never log off and charge at depots. · [R06-056]</sub>
   * **Robotaxis on a human platform**: When a platform mixes robotaxis and human drivers, which riders get which, and at what price. *Values:* Uber-Waymo in Austin and Atlanta: UberX, Green, Comfort and Comfort Electric requests can go to a Waymo at the same price, with no tip prompt; ~100 robotaxis in Austin (2025) and ~200 by 2026 [corrected by fact-check: these are analyst estimates, not Waymo disclosures, per Vital City's methodology on GitHub [S]].. `Med · Rule/price · public/forecast · human app driver, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber investor release (2024); Reuters via StreetInsider [S]. · Data: https://investor.uber.com/news-events/news/press-release-details/2024/Uber-and-Waymo-Expand-Partnership-to-Bring-Autonomous-Ride-Hailing-to-Austin-and-Atlanta/default.aspx · Model: Matcher includes robotaxis for eligible products inside the geofence, with a preference weight and a rider opt-out rate [U]. · [R06-059]</sub>
   * **Tacit collusion between model fleets**: In a shared city, model fleets that set prices or supply can drift to matching high prices or withheld supply (or into price wars) without ever messaging each other. Explicit messaging is R06-065. *Values:* Metrics: a price index between a competitive (Nash) benchmark and the monopoly price; supply withdrawal in contested zones. Rule: allowed but reported / penalised above a threshold [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden · fleet; robotaxi (arena mode)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Fish, Gonczarowski & Shorrer, 'Algorithmic Collusion by Large Language Models' (arXiv 2404.00806, v5 5 Mar 2026): LLM pricing agents quickly and autonomously reach supracompetitive prices, and prompt wording changes how far. [S] Calvano, Calzolari, Denicolo & Pastorello (AER 110(10), 2020): Q-learning pricing agents collude without communicating. [P*, from list] Vending-Bench Arena cartels (R12-049). · Data: none needed · Model: An arena-mode monitor computes price and supply indices per zone-hour, anchored by competitive and monopoly reference policies, and reports a collusion index beside the scores. · [G1-047]</sub>
   * **Coordination channel between rival fleets**: Whether competing agents can message each other, with a risk of zone-splitting collusion. *Values:* None | public | private.. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: LLM agents formed cartels when given email in Vending-Bench Arena (team dossier D07) [S]. · Data: n/a · Model: Harness toggle; log messages and detect zone partitioning. · [R06-065]</sub>
* **How background supply is generated**
   * **Background supply generation mode**: How the simulator creates competitor cars: replay historical trips as ghost drivers, simulate rule-based bots, or a hybrid. *Values:* Replay | agent-based bots | hybrid (replayed demand, bot supply).. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; public app trip data has no driver or vehicle ID, so driver-level replay is impossible [S, columns re-read on GitHub by fact-check]; the TLC may collect driver and vehicle licence numbers per trip (Admin Code 19-550(d)) but does not publish them [P*]. · Data: TLC trip records · Model: Default hybrid: demand resampled from trips; supply from a calibrated bot population. · [R06-066]</sub>
   * **Double-counting guard for replayed days**: Weather, event, holiday and disruption modifiers apply only to a baseline with those effects removed, never on top of a replayed real day that already contains them. *Values:* Rule: a replayed day gets no modifiers, or only the difference between the scenario and what actually happened. A synthetic day gets a baseline cleaned of weather, events, holidays and outages, plus modifiers [design]. Reference days used to build baselines must be screened for data gaps (R02-065).. `High · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own re-check on local yellow 2023 files. Replayed days already contain their shocks: on the 29 Sep 2023 flood day, yellow trips were +10% to +21% from 05:00-09:00 and -17% to -28% from 11:00-15:00 against 8 and 15 Sep and 6 and 13 Oct (day total -12%). [Corrected by fact-check: the +46-63% copied from R04-003 is very likely an artefact of using 22 Sep 2023 as a reference Friday. That day holds only 4,633 trips because of the TLC file gap (R02-065); with it in the reference set the morning shows +44-59%.] This is also a live example of a contaminated baseline. R03-066 removes only events from the baseline [design]. · Data: NYC TLC trip records; NOAA weather; event calendars (R03); MTA alerts · Model: Fit a de-shocked baseline by regressing zone-hour demand on weather, event, holiday, outage and data-gap indicators, and add scenario modifiers only to that baseline. A unit test checks that a modifier is never applied on top of a replay that already contains it. · [G2-020]</sub>
   * **Supply elasticity setting**: Whether background supply is fixed or reacts to surge, events, agent behaviour and earnings. *Values:* Fixed | short-run elastic | short- and long-run elastic.. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Chen & Sheldon; Hall et al. [S]. · Data: n/a · Model: Toggle; report scores under both to separate skill from equilibrium effects. · [R06-068]</sub>
   * **Displacement rule for replayed trips**: When the agent serves a request that a background driver served in history, what happens to that driver. *Values:* Remove the trip from the background driver (it idles) | ignore (additive) | full re-simulation.. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]. · Data: n/a · Model: Background bots re-plan after losing a trip, so each request is served once. · [R06-067]</sub>

### 9. Fares and pay (48)
*What riders pay and what the driver actually earns per trip.*

* **Taxi meter, surcharges and flat fares**
   * **JFK-Manhattan flat fare**: Fixed taxi fare between JFK and any Manhattan address in either direction; trips from JFK to other boroughs stay metered. *Values:* $70.00 + $5 weekday 16:00-20:00 + MTA tax, improvement, congestion, CBD and airport fees and tolls [uncertain: whether the airport access fee applies on RatecodeID 2 trips]; multi-stop: flat to first stop then meter. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(b); [S] 2025 HVFHV: average base fare $73.18 for JFK pickups and $59.72 for LGA [corrected by fact-check: '$60-73' mixed the two], LGA/JFK the top revenue zones; driver bears the traffic risk on a fixed fare · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; RatecodeID = 2 in yellow records · Model: If (JFK, Manhattan zone) pair, replace meter with $70; keep surcharges; time cost from R10-010 · [R07-011]</sub>
   * **Taxi metered rate of fare**: The yellow and green taxi meter charges an initial unit plus one unit per fifth of a mile when moving at 12 mph or more, or per 60 seconds when slower or stopped. *Values:* $3.00 initial + $0.70 per unit = $3.50/mile above 12 mph, $0.70/min (=$42/h) below 12 mph; unchanged since 18 Dec 2022 and still in force Aug 2026. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(a) and 82-26(a), rules current through 13 Aug 2026 (re-read in fact-check) · Data: 35 RCNY via nyc_code_archive (verbatim export, current to 13 Aug 2026) https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; validate on yellow/green fare_amount in NYC TLC trip records https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Meter integrates the per-second speed trace from the travel-time engine: distance units above 12 mph, time units below (cross-link R10-043) · [R07-001]</sub>
   * **Taxi revenue split by fare component**: Which parts of a taxi fare the driver keeps and which pass to the owner, the state, the MTA, the City fund or the airport. *Values:* Driver keeps meter fare, rush/night/LGA extras and tips; MTA tax, congestion surcharge, CBD fee and airport fee are pass-through; improvement surcharge goes via the owner to the City's Taxicab Improvement Fund [corrected by fact-check]; owner may collect the MTA tax from the driver (58-21(c)(5)(vii)); tolls reimburse the E-ZPass holder. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-17(g)-(i), 58-21(c)(5), 51-03; scoring on rider totals would overstate driver revenue by roughly $5-7 per Manhattan trip [design calc] · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; yellow/green itemised fare fields · Model: Ledger with one row per fare component and a recipient (driver, owner, state, MTA, City fund, airport) · [R07-015]</sub>
   * **Airport fare and fee triggers**: Fare rules switched on by airport geography (amounts owned by R07). *Values:* Taxi: JFK to/from Manhattan flat $70 (+$5 weekdays 4-8pm); LGA +$5 to and from; airport access fee on JFK/LGA taxi pickups $1.25 until Mar 2023 and $1.75 from Apr 2023 (trip records), possibly $2.00 in 2026 [uncertain: 2026 amount from a code comment only]. HVFHV: $2.50 airport fee on pickups and drop-offs at JFK/LGA/EWR, already in Jan 2023 records [corrected by fact-check: the original said 'since Apr 2023'; the April 2023 change was the taxi access fee rising to $1.75]. Newark taxi trips: meter + $20 surcharge + tolls both ways [corrected by fact-check: $20 is the rule in force, not a proposal]. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(a)(2)(iii), (b), (c)(3); [P*] airport_fee values in yellow 2023-24 and HVFHV Jan 2023 records; [S] 2026 fare config quoting the TLC rate card; VisaVerge / Fox5 on the FHV fee · Data: TLC fare page; airport_fee fields in trip records · Model: Airport polygons trigger fare-engine branches · [R01-054]</sub>
   * **Flex Fare (taxi e-hail upfront price)**: TLC-approved e-hail apps can offer yellow and green taxi riders a binding upfront fare instead of the meter, using a filed rate schedule. *Values:* Optional per e-hail trip; recorded as payment_type 0; driver sees pickup, drop-off and quote before accepting; WAV requests may not cost more; apps include Curb, Arro and Uber's taxi integration [S]. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 78-15(b), 78-21(a)(3), 80-17(a)(1), 51-03; [P*] yellow dictionary payment_type 0 = Flex Fare trip; shifts traffic risk to the driver · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; yellow payment_type = 0 · Model: E-hail offers carry a fixed quote from an app pricing model (like R07-019); accepted Flex trips ignore the meter · [R07-014]</sub>
   * **LaGuardia surcharge**: Flat surcharge on yellow taxi trips to and from LaGuardia (green taxis: trips to LaGuardia). *Values:* $5.00 per trip; recorded with fare extras and treated as driver revenue [U] (listed with rush and night surcharges in 58-26(a)(2); owners may not take surcharges above the lease, 58-21(c)(5)). `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(a)(2)(iii) and 82-26(a)(2)(iii); makes LGA runs about $5 more valuable than an equal metered trip · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Add when origin or destination is zone 138 (yellow) or destination is 138 (green) · [R07-010]</sub>
   * **Newark Airport taxi trips**: Taxi trips to Newark use the meter plus a surcharge, and the passenger pays tolls both ways; drivers may refuse EWR after 8 hours of operation in a 24-hour period. *Values:* Meter + $20.00 Newark surcharge + tolls out and back; NYC taxis may not solicit or cruise for fares at Newark Airport (80-19(a)(4)(i), upgraded from [U] by fact-check; prearranged EWR pickups not checked), so model an empty return. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(c)(3), 80-17(f)(3), 80-20(b)(8), 80-19(a)(4)(i) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; RatecodeID = 3 · Model: Add $20 and double tolls; force an empty return leg; allow refusal flag after 8 h · [R07-012]</sub>
   * **Weekday rush-hour surcharge**: Flat surcharge on taxi trips that start in the weekday afternoon peak, except on legal holidays. *Values:* $2.50 for trips starting Mon-Fri after 16:00 and before 20:00, not on legal holidays; $5.00 on JFK-Manhattan flat-fare trips. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(a)(2)(i) and (b)(1)(i); about 12% on a $20 meter fare [design calc] · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; yellow 'extra' field; holiday calendar from R02 · Model: Add by trip start timestamp and holiday flag; driver keeps it (part of fare) · [R07-003]</sub>
   * **Airport access fee on taxi pickups**: Fee added to yellow taxi trips that pick up at JFK or LaGuardia, passed through to the airport operator. *Values:* $1.25 (older dictionary), then $1.75 in 2023-2025 records; one 2026 code source says the current TLC rate card shows $2.00 [S, unverified]; may change [uncertain: 2026 amount unresolved; rate card and 2026 trip files unreachable during fact-check]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] yellow dictionary (Mar 2025): 'For pick up only at LaGuardia and John F. Kennedy Airports'; [S] amounts from 2025 trip-data notebooks and a 2026 fare config · Data: NYC TLC trip records (Airport_fee) · Model: Add on pickups in zones 132 (JFK) and 138 (LGA); pass-through · [R07-009]</sub>
   * **Final-unit pre-assessment (round-up)**: The meter charges the full unit that is currently accruing, so every metered fare is rounded up to the next $0.70. *Values:* +$0.00 to +$0.70 per trip, about +$0.35 on average [design calc]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(a)(1)(i)-(v): initial unit $3.00, each additional unit $0.70, current unit pre-assessed · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; calibrate on yellow fare_amount vs trip_distance and duration · Model: Fare = $3.00 + 0.70 x floor(accrued units) [corrected by fact-check: the $3.00 prices the initial unit (first 1/5 mi or 60 s) and $0.70 each additional unit, so the earlier $3.00 + 0.70 x ceil(units) charges one unit too many] [uncertain: which convention meters implement; calibrate on yellow fare_amount] · [R07-002]</sub>
   * **MTA State surcharge on taxi trips**: State tax added to taxi trips that start in NYC and end in NYC or one of seven nearby counties; the driver collects it and the owner or e-hail app remits it. *Values:* $0.50 per trip; applies to destinations in NYC, Dutchess, Nassau, Orange, Putnam, Rockland, Suffolk, Westchester. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(a)(3) and 80-17(g) list the counties; [P*] 35 RCNY 51-03 defines the MTA Tax as the 50-cent tax under NYS Tax Law Art. 29-A (amount upgraded from [S] by fact-check) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; yellow/green mta_tax field · Model: Add to rider total; exclude from driver revenue · [R07-005]</sub>
   * **Out-of-city taxi fares**: Rules for taxi trips leaving NYC: Westchester and Nassau use double meter beyond the city line; other places use a flat dollar fare agreed before departure. *Values:* Westchester/Nassau: in-city meter + 2x meter outside + tolls both ways; other counties: negotiated flat (RatecodeID 5); service to Westchester/Nassau is mandatory. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(c), 80-17(f), 80-20(a)(1); rare trips but single fares of $100+ [U] · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; RatecodeID 4 and 5 · Model: Split route at city boundary; double outside units; negotiated fare drawn from historical RatecodeID 5 distribution · [R07-013]</sub>
   * **Overnight surcharge**: Flat surcharge on taxi trips that start at night. *Values:* $1.00 for trips starting after 20:00 and before 06:00 (not on JFK flat-fare trips [S]). `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(a)(2)(ii); [S] JFK flat-fare page lists no overnight charge (code comment citing TLC fare page) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Add by trip start time; driver keeps it · [R07-004]</sub>
   * **Taxi improvement surcharge**: Per-trip surcharge that the driver remits through the medallion (or green licence) owner to the City's Taxicab Improvement Fund, which pays for accessible taxis. *Values:* $1.00 per taxi trip and per green hail trip (levied since 1 Jan 2015; $0.30 before Dec 2022 [U]). `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(a)(1)(i) and (h), 80-17(h)-(i), 51-03 (deposited into the Taxicab Improvement Fund) [corrected by fact-check: fund money, not owner revenue] · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; improvement_surcharge field · Model: Add to rider total; exclude from driver and owner revenue; optional WAV-taxi driver payments from the fund · [R07-006]</sub>
* **App rider fares and surge**
   * **App rider fare formula (upfront price)**: The price an app rider is quoted before booking, built from a base charge, per-mile and per-minute rates, a booking fee and a minimum fare. *Values:* NYC 2025 average base_passenger_fare $27.66 (Uber), $25.09 (Lyft) [uncertain: average trip length not in the source; '~5-mile trips' unconfirmed]; mean fare per mile $7.81 / $7.14; short trips (<=3 mi) avg $14.46, long (10+ mi) $68.17. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] FerrisSearch analysis of 243.6M 2025 HVFHV trips; rate cards are filed with TLC (35 RCNY 59D-17(a)) but not published [P*] · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records (base_passenger_fare, trip_miles, trip_time) https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Fit fare = a + b*miles + c*minutes + zone x hour effects, times surge (R07-020), on HVFHV records · [R07-019]</sub>
   * **Surge (dynamic pricing)**: Price multiplier or add-on that rises when local requests outrun available drivers; drivers see a surge map and an added amount per trip. *Values:* Rider side 1.0-2.0x typical, rare spikes above 3x [U]; driver side +$1 to +$10 per trip [U]; NYC receipts must itemise any price multiplier. `High · Changes during run · agent sees · core MVP · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-20(d)(6) receipts itemise the multiplier; [S] Cohen et al. 2016 elasticity about -0.5 near 1.2x (via R05-020) · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records (fare residuals by zone-time); no public surge feed · Model: Surge = f(open requests / idle supply in zone over last 5-15 min), capped and smoothed; feeds rider conversion (R05) and driver offers · [R07-020]</sub>
   * **Ride product price levels**: Rider price and driver pay multipliers for app products such as standard, Comfort, XL, Black, WAV and Share. *Values:* Comfort ~1.2-1.3x, XL ~1.5x, Black ~2-2.5x, Share up to 20% off [U]; WAV must not cost more than standard. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] platform rate cards; [P*] 35 RCNY 78-15(b)(1)(ii) (no WAV premium on Flex); [P*] CNYCA: premium services average 40-60% higher fares; product mix from R05-024 · Data: none found (HVFHV data flags only shared and WAV) · Model: Product multiplier table; vehicle eligibility from R08; driver opt-ins in R09-066 · [R07-021]</sub>
   * **Rider-side taxes and fees on app trips**: Taxes and fees added on top of the app base fare that the rider pays but the driver does not receive. *Values:* Sales tax ~8.875% (2025 avg $2.32 on $27.66); Black Car Fund ~2.5% (avg $0.69); airport fee $2.50 per LGA/JFK/EWR pickup or drop-off; congestion $2.75; CBD $1.50; tolls. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] HVFHS dictionary (Mar 2025) fields and $2.50 airport fee; [S] 2025 fee averages (FerrisSearch); [S] 8.875% and 2.5-3% rates from GitHub analyses · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records (sales_tax, bcf, airport_fee, congestion_surcharge, cbd_congestion_fee) · Model: Add to rider price before the conversion model; exclude from driver pay · [R07-023]</sub>
   * **Emergency surge cap**: During New York state-declared emergencies Uber agreed to cap surge pricing, and state price-gouging law applies. *Values:* July 2014 Uber agreement with the NY Attorney General to cap surge in state-declared emergencies [S] (fact-check: a news sentence in the cited public dataset confirms an agreement 'to put a ceiling on surge pricing during state-declared emergencies' announced in July; the year 2014 is inferred from context); cap reported as the 4th-highest multiplier of the prior 60 days [U]; NY General Business Law 396-r [U]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: News text in a public GitHub corpus describing the 2014 agreement [S] · Data: NY Attorney General press releases https://ag.ny.gov/ (not fetched) · Model: When a declared-emergency flag is on, cap the surge multiplier at a configurable ceiling. · [R04-012]</sub>
   * **Fare estimate and quote rules**: Limits on how far an app's final charge may exceed the estimate it gave the rider. *Values:* Estimate ranges at most 150% high-to-low; charge at most 120% of the estimate (or of the top of the range) unless the rider changes the trip; livery bases must give binding quotes. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59B-23(b),(g) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Cap final charge at 1.2 x quote unless a destination change event occurs · [R07-022]</sub>
   * **Flexible-ETA and priority ride tiers**: Rider options to wait longer for a lower price, or to pay more for a faster pickup. *Values:* Wait-and-save: 10-25% off for a 10-20 min pickup window [design]. Lyft Wait & Save was about 30% of Lyft rides in Q1 2023 per the CEO, and NYC averaged 150k+ Wait & Save rides a week in Q2 2023 [S]. Uber Wait & Save was live in 10+ Indian cities (Jul 2025) [S]. Uber's priority option was tested at up to 20% above the basic fare in three Brazilian cities in 2021, and launched with a surcharge of unstated size in Buenos Aires, Mendoza and Córdoba in July 2023 [S; corrected by fact-check, was 'tested at up to 20% in Brazil and Argentina (2023)']. Waymo Premier ($29.99/month, Jun 2026) includes priority pickups [S].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi fleet (own app)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber India newsroom (11 Jul 2025), Bloomberg (2021, Brazil), Argentine press (Jul 2023), FOX Business and Lyft Q2 2023 call. These tiers let a fleet batch trips and smooth peaks, which lowers the fleet size needed for a given service level [design]. · Data: none found for NYC shares beyond Lyft's 150k/week · Model: Each request carries a tier (standard, wait or priority) with a pickup-time window and a price factor; the matcher may hold wait-tier requests for batching. · [G2-049]</sub>
* **Platform take and the driver pay floor**
   * **NYC minimum pay: per-mile component**: TLC floor on what Uber or Lyft must pay per passenger mile in the city: an expense factor divided by an applied utilization rate. *Values:* From 1 Aug 2025: $0.850/mi (WAV $1.061) / 68.5% = ~$1.24/mi (WAV ~$1.55); before: $0.789 / 58% = ~$1.36/mi (Mar 2024); 2019 factor $0.631; CPI-adjusted each 1 March [uncertain: Mar-Jul 2025 factor probably ~$0.820 (~$1.41/mi) [derived]; whether $0.850 is itself CPI-indexed from 1 Mar 2026, and the rates the TLC posted, could not be read]. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · core MVP · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-22(a)(1),(b) (amended eff. 1 Aug and 22 Sep 2025); [P*] CNYCA Feb 2025 expense model (proposed $0.871 non-WAV, $1.061 WAV; factor history); [S] 58% utilization in 2024 (Bloomberg via blog) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; TLC driver pay page https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/driver-pay.page (blocked; URL not verified) · Model: Floor_miles = factor_mile / 0.685 x in-city passenger miles; use the rate in force on the replay date (R07-065) · [R07-025]</sub>
   * **NYC minimum pay: per-minute component**: TLC floor on what Uber or Lyft must pay per passenger minute in the city, also scaled up by a utilization rate. *Values:* Base $0.287/min (2019) indexed to $0.338 by Mar 2024; / 53.3% from Aug 2025 = ~$0.63-0.69/min in 2025-26 [derived]; before Aug 2025 ~$0.58/min (58%, Mar 2024; ~$0.61 after the Mar 2025 CPI step [derived]); example 5 mi + 20 min trip floor ~ $19 [uncertain: the Aug 2025 rule was reported as a ~5% pay raise [S], which these derived rates (~+1% on that trip) do not reproduce; check posted rates]. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · core MVP · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-22(a)(2),(b); [P*] CNYCA factor table (Exhibit 2); [S] 58% utilization for 2024 (Bloomberg via blog) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; TLC driver pay page (CPI-adjusted rates; blocked here) · Model: Floor_minutes = factor_min / 0.533 x in-city passenger minutes; slow traffic raises floor pay per trip (cross-link R10) · [R07-026]</sub>
   * **Per-offer pay dispersion and how predictable it is**: How much the driver's upfront pay varies between offers that look alike, both as a share of the rider fare and per mile or minute, and how much of that spread a driver could predict. NYC-specific through the pay floor; elsewhere the share is the platform's free choice. *Values:* Sample: HVFHV Jan 2023, non-shared, non-WAV, fare >= $5, trip >= 0.3 mi and >= 2 min, pay > 0 (17.86M trips). Driver_pay / base fare: p10 0.63, median 0.77, p90 0.99 (mean 0.80 Uber, 0.77 Lyft). Pay exceeds the fare on 10.3% of Uber and 5.9% of Lyft trips, mostly where the NYC per-mile and per-minute floor binds [design interpretation]. Variance of the ratio: platform alone explains <1%; adding the OD pair explains about 8%; within platform x OD x 4-hour band (cells >= 20 trips), 90% remains. Within those cells (>= 30 trips, median 2-6 mi), per-mile pay p90/p10 = 1.60, median $3.88/mi. Surge proxy (fare vs OD median), weekdays only, normal-duration trips (<= 1.1x OD median time): adding it leaves 73% of the variance. Median share is 0.79 when the fare is < 0.9x the OD median, 0.72 at 0.9-1.1x, and 0.67-0.68 at >= 1.1x. Settings: residual spread 0 / calibrated (default) / 2x.. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver; fleet of app cars (single car and N cars); robotaxi only when dispatched through a partner platform`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own calc (g1/a1.py, a2.py), re-run in this fact-check on the local HVFHV Jan 2023 file: every figure reproduced to two decimals. The higher share in the low-fare bucket partly reflects the pay floor rather than surge [design interpretation]. [S] Oxford / Worker Info Exchange study, June 2025 (UK, 258 drivers, 1.5M trips 2016-2024): Uber's median take rate per driver rose from 25% to 29% after dynamic pricing, and exceeded 50% on some trips. Uber disputes the figures. Read via search snippets only (ox.ac.uk unreachable from here). · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records (base_passenger_fare, driver_pay, trip_miles, trip_time) https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Offer pay = max(NYC floor from R07-025/026, fare x share). Share = fitted f(platform, surge level, distance, duration) x a lognormal residual whose spread is fitted per cell. A harness knob scales the residual to test accept/decline skill. Base level: R07-024. · [G1-001]</sub>
   * **Platform take rate (driver share of fare)**: The share of the rider's base fare that the platform pays the driver for a trip. *Values:* NYC 2025 mean driver_pay / base fare 79% for both Uber and Lyft; 71-77% on Uber airport corridors (Lyft 83-84%); above 100% on some shared or WAV trips (shared unmatched 103%, Lyft WAV 204%); ~100% in 2019 vs ~80% in 2025 [S]. `High · Rule/price · hidden · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] FerrisSearch analysis of 243.6M 2025 HVFHV trips; [S] student analysis of HVFHV 2019-2025 · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records (driver_pay vs base_passenger_fare) · Model: Driver pay = max(NYC floor from R07-025/026, platform offer); offer = share(distance, zone, time) x fare + noise, fitted on HVFHV · [R07-024]</sub>
   * **Annual CPI indexation of pay factors**: Each 1 March the TLC raises the per-mile and per-minute factors by the NY-NJ-PA CPI-W change; it also resets them by rule from time to time. *Values:* Time factor +1.4% (2020), +5.5% (2022), +6.5% (Feb 2023), +3.5% (Mar 2024); distance factor +1.4%, +5.2%, +6.1%, then a distance-only reset of +6.7% to $0.762 on 13 Mar 2023 (transport CPI) and +3.5% to $0.789 (Mar 2024) [added by fact-check]; no step in 2021 (Covid); 2024 CPI-W 3.9%; changes every year. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-22(a)(4); [P*] CNYCA Exhibit 2 (factor history) · Data: TLC driver pay page; BLS CPI-W NY-NJ-PA (bls.gov blocked here) · Model: Rate table keyed by effective date; select by replay date · [R07-028]</sub>
   * **Time-versus-distance pay crossover speed**: The speed below which a trip earns by time rather than distance, which ties traffic speed directly to revenue. *Values:* yellow and green taxi meter: 70 cents per 1/5 mile at 12 mph or more, or per 60 s below 12 mph (35 RCNY 58-26(a)) [P*] [corrected by fact-check: the threshold is 'at 12 mph or more', not 'above']; HVFHV driver pay has per-mile and per-minute parts (R07). `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi, human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: 35 RCNY 58-26 via nyc_code_archive [P*]; TLC taxi fare page [S] · Data: https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/passengers/taxi-fare.page · Model: Meter integrates per-segment speed along the realised route, so the engine must emit a sub-trip speed trace, not only total time. · [R10-043]</sub>
   * **Hourly pay compliance option**: A platform may instead pay drivers per hour of accepting dispatches, as long as each hour pays at least the per-mile and per-minute floor for that hour's passenger miles and minutes. *Values:* On/off (per platform). `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-22(a)(5); NYC Admin Code 19-549(b) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Alternative pay mode: hourly wage w, topped up to the floor sum per hour · [R07-029]</sub>
   * **Out-of-city pay rates**: Higher per-mile and per-minute floor for the part of a trip that runs outside NYC, to cover the empty return. *Values:* $1.700/mi (WAV $2.122) and $0.574/min outside the city, not divided by utilization; applies to the outside part of trips that start in NYC and end outside it. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-22(a)(1)-(2) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Split the route at the city boundary; apply out-of-city rates to the outside part · [R07-027]</sub>
* **Bonuses, fees and pass-throughs**
   * **Cancellation and no-show fees**: Fee a rider pays for cancelling late or not showing up, mostly passed to the driver; taxi e-hail apps may charge them only if listed in a filed fee schedule. *Values:* Free window ~2 min after match; no-show after ~5 min wait; rider fee ~$5-10 in NYC [U]; driver share 75-100% [design]. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, taxi e-hail`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 78-15(c)-(d) (e-hail fee schedules); [U] app amounts; frequencies from R05-022 and R05-037 · Data: none found (TLC records contain no cancelled trips) · Model: On rider cancel after free window or no-show timer, credit the driver the fee and free the car · [R07-035]</sub>
   * **Quests (trip-count bonuses)**: Platform bonus for completing a set number of trips in a time window, often in tiers. *Values:* e.g. $30 for 15 trips on a weekday, $100 for 40 trips over a weekend [design]; NYC amounts not public. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Uber and Lyft run quests; progress tracked in R09-025; can make a marginal trip worth far more than its fare · Data: none found · Model: Offer objects (window, thresholds, payout) issued per driver; pay on threshold crossing · [R07-033]</sub>
   * **WAV trip pay premium**: Extra pay for trips done in wheelchair-accessible vehicles, from the higher floor factor and platform bonuses. *Values:* Floor factor $1.061 vs $0.850 per mile; 2025 Lyft WAV driver pay ~204% of fare (flat ~$15/ride), Uber ~101%; taxis in the accessible programme must accept dispatches. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-22(a)(1), 53-08; [S] FerrisSearch 2025 WAV analysis (~699K WAV trips); matters for a WAV persona, low overall · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records (wav_request_flag, wav_match_flag, driver_pay) · Model: WAV vehicle flag raises floor and adds a flat bonus per platform; WAV demand from R05-031 · [R07-039]</sub>
   * **Driver-side pay errors and support claims**: Small pay errors that go against the driver, such as a missing toll reimbursement, unpaid wait time or cancellation fee, or distance cut short by GPS drift. A driver recovers them only partly, by filing support tickets. This is distinct from rider disputes (R05-056) and the toll rule itself (R07-038). *Values:* Error rate 0.5-3% of trips; $2-15 per error; 30-80% recovered if claimed; 5-15 min of unpaid time per claim [design guess].. `Low · World input · agent sees · human app driver (taxi meter errors are rare)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Driver-forum reports; [design]. Not checkable with public data. · Data: none found · Model: Bernoulli error per trip on the toll, wait-fee and cancel-fee components. The agent can file a claim, which costs time and succeeds with a recovery probability. · [G1-012]</sub>
   * **Shared-ride pay treatment**: How drivers are paid when a rider books a discounted shared ride. *Values:* Uber 2025: shared matched avg fare $21.04 (payout 94%), unmatched $16.34 (payout 103%) vs $28.02 standard (78%); drivers paid as normal trips [S]; drivers may not force sharing. `Low · Rule/price · hidden · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] FerrisSearch 2025; [P*] 35 RCNY 80-19(c)(3) ($100 fine) · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records (shared_request_flag, shared_match_flag) · Model: Rider gets discount; driver pay computed as a normal trip on actual miles and minutes · [R07-040]</sub>
   * **Streaks, consecutive-trip and zone boosts**: Small add-ons for taking trips back to back or starting trips in a boosted zone or time. *Values:* $1-$5 per trip or a multiplier on surge zones [design]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] platform driver apps · Data: none found · Model: Per-trip add-on if conditions hold (consecutive acceptances, zone, time) · [R07-034]</sub>
   * **Taxi toll pass-through rules**: Passengers pay the actual E-ZPass toll; on some bridges the passenger pays no toll one way and double the other way to cover the driver's return. *Values:* Actual E-ZPass amount; none on Cross Bay, Marine Parkway and Verrazzano trips toward Brooklyn/Queens; 2x in the opposite direction; out-of-city: tolls both ways. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(e)-(f), 80-15 · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; tolls_amount field; toll rates in R10-021 · Model: Toll table by crossing and direction with passenger multiplier 0, 1 or 2; driver pays unreimbursed tolls · [R07-018]</sub>
   * **Toll reimbursement on app trips**: Platforms must pass the full toll paid on a dispatched trip back to the driver; tolls on empty legs are the driver's cost. *Values:* 100% of on-trip tolls; driver_pay excludes tolls and tips; empty-return tolls unreimbursed [U]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-17(c); [S] HVFHS dictionary: driver_pay 'not including tolls or tips' · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records (tolls) · Model: Credit on-trip tolls; charge deadhead tolls to the driver (toll table in R10-021) · [R07-038]</sub>
   * **Wait-time (late rider) fee**: Per-minute charge when the rider keeps the driver waiting past a grace period at pickup. *Values:* Grace ~2 min, then ~$0.30-0.60/min [U]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] platform help pages; lateness distribution in R05-038 · Data: none found · Model: Accrue fee per minute after grace; pass to driver · [R07-036]</sub>
* **Tips and payment**
