   * **App tip propensity and amount**: Whether an app rider tips and how much. *Values:* ~15-16% of UberX trips tipped (US, 2017 data); ~60% of riders never tip, 1% always [S]; [fact-check: NYC HVFHV Jan 2023: 19.6% of Uber and 18.8% of Lyft trips carry a tip, mean ~$5 when tipped; tips are 4.5-4.7% of base fare and 5.6-6.5% of driver pay, matching the '~4-5% of fare' secondary figure]; robotaxi tips none [U]. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Chandar, Gneezy, List & Muir 2019 (NBER w26380); [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (HVFHV tips); [S] blog analysis of 243M HVFHS trips · Data: TLC HVFHV tips field · Model: Hurdle model: persistent rider tipper type x P(tip | context) x amount · [R05-047]</sub>
   * **Taxi tip (card defaults, cash unrecorded)**: Taxi tips anchored by the card-screen tip buttons [uncertain: button sets differ by vendor and year (15/20/25% and 20/25/30% both reported) [U]]; cash tips are missing from the data. *Values:* Card tips ~15-25% of fare [corrected by fact-check: yellow 2023 card trips: 95% carry a tip, tips total 22% of fare_amount, median tipped rate 26% of fare_amount (fare_amount excludes surcharges, so the on-screen % is lower)]; card tips easy to predict (R^2 ~0.72) vs app tips (R^2 ~0.17) [uncertain: arXiv 2602.19404 not re-read]; >40% of yellow records show no tip [corrected by fact-check: true only of older data (SAS 2016); zero-tip records were 29.6% in Oct 2019 and 22.9% in 2023, mostly cash trips]. `Med · World input · hidden · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] TLC yellow dictionary: cash tips not included; [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (yellow Oct 2019 and 2023); [S] NY Sun on default screens; [S] Eglinskas 2026 arXiv 2602.19404; [S] SAS blog 2016 · Data: TLC yellow/green tip_amount and payment_type · Model: Card: default-anchored mixture; cash: imputed tip rate as a scenario setting · [R05-048]</sub>
   * **Tip pass-through rules**: Rules that all tips go to the driver and that riders can tip in-app; drivers may not ask for tips. *Values:* 100% pass-through for apps, e-hail and taxi card tips; robotaxi: no tipping [U]; app tips ~4-5% of fare revenue, ~18% of rides tipped, ~20% among tippers (2025) [S]; yellow tip_amount records card tips only. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-17(b), 59B-23(h), 78-15(a), 80-17(a)(6); [P*] yellow dictionary (cash tips not included); [S] FerrisSearch 2025 · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; tips / tip_amount fields · Model: Tip amount from R05-047/048 goes 100% to the driver; zero for robotaxi · [R07-037]</sub>
   * **Hourly tipping**: Share of trips tipped and tip size by hour. *Values:* HVFHV tip incidence 14-15% at 02-04h vs 22% at 11-12h, avg tip $0.67-1.17; yellow card tip/fare about 22% midday, 24-25% at 16-20h weekdays [corrected by fact-check: 14-15% holds for Sat/Sun 02-04h, weekday 02-04h is 15-18%; yellow 23.9-24.9% is 16-19h, 20h is 23.3%]. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023; yellow 16-20h uplift likely because tip presets apply to totals incl. $2.50 surcharge [design inference] · Data: NYC TLC trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Tip probability and size as functions of hour, payment type and fare · [R02-020]</sub>
   * **Taxi payment type mix**: Share of taxi riders paying cash versus card, which changes payment time and tip visibility. *Values:* [corrected by fact-check: the undated '53% of farebox by card' figure is obsolete; card paid 72.6% of yellow trips (cash 26.2%) in Oct 2019 and 77.9% (cash 16.7%, unknown 3.4%, no charge or dispute 1.9%) in 2023, with 78% of fare value by card]; cash commoner in unbanked neighbourhoods (~10% of NYC households unbanked [S]). `Low · World input · hidden · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (yellow payment_type); [S] Columbia C4SR study; [S] Digital Transactions (older figure, superseded) · Data: TLC yellow/green payment_type · Model: P(cash | zone, hour) from payment_type · [R05-050]</sub>
   * **Tip modifiers by trip context**: How tips change with airport trips, distance, hour, delays and service quality. *Values:* Airport riders among most generous; longer trips give larger absolute tips; delays lower tips [design guess]. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] The Points Guy coverage of Chandar et al. · Data: TLC tips by zone, hour, distance · Model: Covariates in the tip model: airport flag, distance, hour, ETA slip, route detour · [R05-049]</sub>
* **Offer information shown before accepting**
   * **Upfront offer information**: What the driver sees before accepting: pay, pickup time, trip time, destination, surge. Street-hailed taxis learn the destination only after pickup and may not refuse. *Values:* Full (pay, destination, time) | partial (pickup only) | none (street hail).. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber and Lyft show upfront pay and destination in most US markets since about 2022 [U]; NYC cab drivers may not refuse any destination in NYC, Nassau, Westchester or Newark Airport, and may not try to learn the destination before the rider is seated, except on Flex Fare e-hail trips or when signed off duty heading home [corrected by fact-check: was 'in-city destinations [U]'; P*, 35 RCNY 80-20(a)(1),(a)(4) and 80-19]. · Data: None (product design); TLC refusal rule 35 RCNY 80-20 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*] · Model: Offer fields set per scenario; this turns cherry-picking on or off. · [R06-025]</sub>
* **Robotaxi fares and revenue splits**
   * **Robotaxi fare structure**: How a Waymo-like service prices rides: base charge, per mile, per minute, with dynamic adjustment. *Values:* SF ~$9.52 + $1.66/mi + $0.30/min (avg ride ~$20.43); LA ~$5.37 + $2.50/mi + $0.32/min; premium ~31% over Uber and ~41% over Lyft (Apr 2025: $20.43 vs $15.58 vs $14.44), narrowing to ~12.7% over Uber and ~27.3% over Lyft (late 2025) [corrected by fact-check]; $29.99/month subscription [S]. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] industry notes citing Obi data via TechCrunch (Jun 2025, Jan 2026) and Waymo pricing help page · Data: Waymo trip pricing https://support.google.com/waymo/answer/9059184; Obi price reports [S] · Model: Rate card plus dynamic multiplier; rider uptake via AV premium in R05-027 · [R07-061]</sub>
   * **Distribution channel mix (own app vs partner platforms)**: The share of fleet car-hours offered on the operator's own app vs on Uber or Lyft. It trades the partner's cut and the loss of price control against instant access to riders. *Values:* Own app 0-100%. Partner share of fare 20-30% [design, R07-062]. Own-app demand ramps with rider acquisition, starting at 10-30% of partner volume [design]. Waymo channels: own app plus Uber in Phoenix (Uber since Oct 2023); Uber only in Austin and Atlanta. Nashville is with Lyft: announced Sep 2025, riders book first in the Waymo app with Lyft app integration to follow, and Lyft's Flexdrive runs the depots.. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · robotaxi; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S, from list] R07-062 operating-city table. [S] TechCrunch and Smart Cities Dive (Sep 2025) on Nashville. [corrected: was 'Lyft in Nashville' only]. [design]. · Data: none found (contract terms are private) · Model: Channel weights per car-hour; each channel has its own demand stream, price rule and take rate. · [G1-048]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi network partner revenue split**: Share kept by Uber or Lyft when robotaxis are booked through their apps. *Values:* Undisclosed [U]; design default 20-30% of fare [design]; Austin (Mar 2025) and Atlanta (Jun 2025) via Uber, Nashville (7 Apr 2026) via Lyft [corrected by fact-check: dates]. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Vital City operating-city table; [U] contract terms · Data: none found · Model: Fleet revenue = fare x (1 - partner share) for partner-dispatched trips · [R07-062]</sub>

### 10. Rules and regulation (36)
*Legal and platform rules that limit what the driver may do and when.*

* **Hours caps and log-on lockouts**
   * **Driver app access rules (lockouts)**: Limits on when Uber or Lyft may block a driver from logging on, introduced after platforms used lockouts to keep utilization high. *Values:* Since 1 Aug 2025: 72 h notice before restricting a day; once online, access kept >=16 h (restarts after 8 h offline); exceptions for licensing, rule breaches, misconduct, excessive cancels or non-acceptance; $500 per violation. Before: lockouts nearly every hour (2024). From 28 Jul 2026, restrictions of 72 h+ in a row or 168 h+ per year count as deactivation under the just-cause law, and economic cuts follow reverse seniority (R07-045) [added by fact-check]. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver (NYC)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-22(c) and penalty table; [P*] NYC Admin Code 20-1281, 20-1284; [S] Bloomberg Oct 2024 and NYT Jun 2025 via blog summary · Data: 35 RCNY and Admin Code via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Scenario toggle: 2024 regime (random or priority lockouts when supply is high) vs 2025 regime (notice + 16-h access) vs 2026 regime (adds deactivation limits); cross-link R09-042 · [R07-030]</sub>
   * **TLC passenger-time cap and fines**: NYC limit on how long a taxi or for-hire driver may carry passengers per day and week. *Values:* <=10 h passenger time in any 24 h (resets after 8 consecutive hours with no passengers); <=60 h per Mon-Sun week; time between trips not counted; a last trip begun under the limit is a defence; fines $50/$75 up to 1 h over, $100/$150 for 1-2 h, $150/$200 for 2-3 h, $200/$300 for 3-4 h, $300/$400 beyond; 5-15 day suspension after 3+ violations of 4 h+ in a month. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-14(f) and penalty table (amended eff. 6 Mar 2025) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Rolling counters; block new trips or fine per extra hour; duplicate of R09-005 (R07 adds the fine schedule) · [R07-047]</sub>
   * **Platform driving-time cap**: App rule that forces the driver offline after long driving. *Values:* Uber (2018): 12 h driving time, then 6 h offline; time online but stopped (e.g. airport lot) not counted. Lyft (2018 report): 14 h in driver mode, then 6 h.. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] TechCrunch/VentureBeat Feb 2018 (not re-checkable; matches background knowledge [U]); Uber at the NYC Council (Sept 2024): exceeding maximum driving hours is a compliance-deactivation reason. · Data: none found (company policy, press only) · Model: Per-platform counter; forced logout for 6 h when reached. Multi-apping can get around one app's cap but not the TLC cap. · [R09-006]</sub>
* **Street hails and refusals**
   * **Street-hail legality by vehicle type and zone**: Which vehicles may pick up riders who wave from the street, and where. *Values:* Yellow: anywhere, but no cruising for fares at JFK/LGA/EWR or within 100 ft of a taxi stand; airport pickups only via stands and feed lines [corrected by fact-check]; green: only in the Hail Zone (not Manhattan south of E 96th/W 110th St, not airports), fines $500 + suspension, $750 + suspension, revocation; FHV/app: never (fines $200-350, in the exclusionary zone $500/$750/revocation); robotaxi: app only. `High · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-19(a)-(c), 82-13 and penalty tables, 59B-23(d), 51-03 (zone definitions) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; TLC taxi zone shapefile · Model: Hail encounters (R05-042) only offered to vehicle types and zones where legal; illegal pickups fined with detection probability · [R07-048]</sub>
   * **Geographic refusal rules**: Taxis must take passengers to any destination in the city, Westchester, Nassau or Newark Airport and may not ask the destination before the rider is seated, so the agent cannot legally refuse by neighbourhood; app drivers can decline but acceptance rate counts against them. *Values:* Taxi refusal = mandatory penalty under 35 RCNY 80-02(e) (about $500 for a first offence [S]); allowed refusals include more than 8 h of operation for EWR/Westchester/Nassau trips; app accept/decline with destination shown [U]. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi, human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-20(a)(1), (a)(4), (b) [corrected by fact-check: the rule also covers Westchester, Nassau and Newark; tag upgraded from U] · Data: 35 RCNY 80-20 and 80-02 (verbatim copy https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive); TLC rules (https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-rules.page) · Model: Block destination-based refusal for taxis; track acceptance for app drivers (R06/R07) · [R01-070]</sub>
   * **Off-duty 'returning home' pickup exception**: A logged-off taxi may still take a rider whose destination is directly on its route home or to the garage, and may ask the destination first. *Values:* Allowed only if logged off, trip record marked 'Returning to garage/home', and destination on route; green: only inside the Hail Zone; fine $75/$100 if misused. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-19(a)(5),(b)(6), 80-20(a)(4)(i); the legal form of the team's 'take me home' idea for taxis · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Action go_home_offduty: hails accepted only if destination reduces distance to home; cross-link R09-020, R05-060 · [R07-050]</sub>
   * **Taxi refusal ban and penalties**: Taxi and green drivers must take riders to any destination in NYC, Westchester, Nassau or Newark and must not drop an accepted e-hail. *Values:* Mandatory penalties: $350 pleaded / $500 after hearing first; $700 / $1,000 + up to 30-day suspension second within 24 months; $750 / $1,000 + revocation third within 36 months [upgraded to P* by fact-check]; allowed refusals: off-duty logged, >8 h operated for EWR/Westchester/Nassau, disorderly rider, unsecured animal, among others. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-20(a)-(b), 80-02(e) (refusal rows share the overcharge penalty cell); [S] fine amounts also via R09-032 (NBC/amNY) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Taxi agent cannot decline after a hail; declining an accepted e-hail triggers a fine with detection probability p; cross-link R09-032 · [R07-042]</sub>
* **Airport and stand rules**
   * **Airport pickup rules**: How each vehicle type may pick up at NYC airports. *Values:* Taxi: holding lot and feed line, separate short-haul and long-haul tickets at some terminals (no swapping); app: may not accept a dispatch while parked at JFK/LGA pickup areas ($250 fine), so waits in staging lots; app airport fee $2.50; NYC taxis may not solicit at EWR. `High · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-19(a)(4), (e)-(f); [P*] HVFHS dictionary airport_fee; queue dynamics in R09-057 and R05-063 · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; TLC zones 1, 132, 138 · Model: Per-airport queue objects by vehicle type; short-haul ticket gives priority re-entry [design] · [R07-051]</sub>
   * **Taxi solicitation and stand rules**: Where taxis may cruise for fares and how taxi stand queues work. *Values:* No cruising for fares at JFK, LGA, EWR, within 100 ft of a stand, or on Lincoln Center private streets; join the back of a stand line; first two cabs stay in the seat; relief use <=1 h; fines $50-150. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-19(a)(4),(d) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Stand objects with FIFO queues; cruising pickups disabled near stands and at airports · [R07-049]</sub>
* **Tolls, congestion pricing and surcharge zones**
   * **Congestion Relief Zone (CRZ)**: Manhattan at or south of 60th St (excluding FDR Drive, West Side Highway / Route 9A and Hugh Carey Tunnel links) where each taxi/FHV trip to, from, within or through the zone pays a per-trip charge. *Values:* $0.75 per yellow, green or other FHV trip; $1.50 per HVFHV trip; since 5 Jan 2025; private cars $9 peak (2025-27), $12 (2028-30), $15 (2031), $2.25 overnight [uncertain: later phases and the overnight rate not re-checked]; unenrolled fleets pay a daily toll. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · all; robotaxi fleet pays as TLC vehicle or daily toll`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] MTA taxi/FHV toll page; several 2026 GitHub analyses agree on $0.75 taxi / $1.50 HVFHV / $9 peak from 5 Jan 2025 and treat the charge as still in force in 2026; [S] TLC trip-record dictionaries (Mar 2025) list cbd_congestion_fee 'starting Jan. 5, 2025' (via sibling dossier R07, read there as P*) · Data: MTA CBD taxi zones dataset (https://catalog.data.gov/dataset/mta-central-business-district-taxi-zones); cbd_congestion_fee field in 2025+ TLC trip records [S] · Model: Polygon; flag trips whose route touches it (excluding exempt edges); rider-paid but shifts demand (R05) and speeds (R10) · [R01-022]</sub>
   * **MTA bridge and tunnel tolls**: Toll charged at MTA crossings, by crossing, payment type and direction. *Values:* Car E-ZPass at major crossings $6.55 until Aug 2023 and $6.94 from Aug 2023 (most common toll on yellow trip records), raised ~6% on 4 Jan 2026 [uncertain: 2026 car rate not checked]; smaller crossings (Henry Hudson, Cross Bay, Marine Pkwy) about half (bus E-ZPass $3.39 vs $6.42; a $3.18 car toll is common in 2023-24 records) [U]; East River bridges and the other Harlem River bridges free [corrected by fact-check: the Henry Hudson Bridge is tolled, so not all Harlem River crossings are free]. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] tolls_amount in yellow 2023-24 records ($6.55 in Jul 2023; $6.94 in Sep 2023 and Jan 2024); [S] MTA franchise-bus toll page (rates effective 4 Jan 2026); news on ~6% E-ZPass rise; [P*] 35 RCNY 58-26(e)-(f): taxi passengers pay the actual E-ZPass toll, with directional rules on Cross Bay, Marine Pkwy and Verrazzano · Data: MTA tolls (https://www.mta.info/fares-tolls/tolls) · Model: Toll table by crossing x direction x payment; passed to the rider when loaded, paid by the driver when empty · [R01-020]</sub>
   * **NYS congestion surcharge (Manhattan south of 96th St)**: State per-trip surcharge on taxi and for-hire trips that begin, end or pass through Manhattan south of 96th Street. *Values:* Taxi $2.50; FHV/app $2.75; shared ride $0.75; since Feb 2019. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] TLC dictionary congestion_surcharge field; capstone and HVFHV analyses cite $2.50 / $2.75 / $0.75 and the 96th St boundary; raises rider price so it feeds demand (R05-020) · Data: NYC TLC trip records (congestion_surcharge) https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Polygon test on the realised route (begin, end or pass-through); add to rider price; pass-through, not driver revenue · [R07-007]</sub>
   * **Port Authority Hudson crossing tolls**: GWB, Lincoln and Holland charge only when entering New York, so a car returning empty from NJ pays; one-direction tolling (also Verrazzano, into Staten Island only [U]) makes return legs cost more than outbound legs. [corrected by fact-check: for yellow and green taxi trips to Newark, Westchester and Nassau the passenger must pay tolls to and from the destination (35 RCNY 58-26(c), (f)(2)), so the taxi's return toll is reimbursed; app drivers get the higher out-of-town pay rate instead] *Values:* Car E-ZPass $16.79 peak / ~$14.79 off-peak, toll-by-plate ~$23.05-23.30 [uncertain: toll-by-plate amount not checked] (from 4 Jan 2026); peak = weekdays 6-10am and 4-8pm, weekends 11am-9pm. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] ROI-NJ (Jan 2026); PANYNJ Truckers Guide (Apr 2026); [P*] yellow records show PANYNJ car tolls $14.75 / $12.75 (2023) and $15.38 / $13.38 (Jan 2024), consistent with yearly indexing to $16.79 / $14.79 in 2026 · Data: PANYNJ (https://www.panynj.gov/bridges-tunnels) · Model: Inbound-only toll on NJ-to-NY crossing edges; charged to the driver on empty legs except where the passenger reimburses return tolls (taxi trips to EWR, Westchester, Nassau) · [R01-021]</sub>
* **Traffic rules, curbs and enforcement**
   * **Enforcement intensity and being moved on**: The chance, by zone, hour and day, that traffic agents, NYPD or Port Authority police ticket or move on a car standing or double-parked while it waits near a hotspot. *Values:* Hazard per waiting minute about 0-5% by zone and hour [design]. Close to certain at airport terminal curbs and Midtown transit hubs at peaks [U]. Fines are $100-115 for No Standing or No Stopping (R08-053). Robotaxi: California AB 1777 (signed Sep 2024) lets police issue a 'notice of AV noncompliance' to the AV company from 1 Jul 2026; the company must send it to the DMV within 72 h, and penalties are left to DMV rules [S]. NYC has no AV ticketing rule [U].. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver, taxi; robotaxi via curb dwell limits; fleet staging (R11-023)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NYC Open Data 'Parking Violations Issued' (one dataset per fiscal year; FY2026 covers 1 Jul 2025 to 30 Jun 2026) records violation code, time, street and precinct for each ticket as issued, not later dismissals (data.gov catalog). Tickets measure enforcement; being moved on leaves no record, so its rate must be assumed [design]. Whether waiting near a hotspot pays depends on this. The list has fine amounts (R08-053) but no enforcement map [design]. · Data: NYC Open Data, Parking Violations Issued (one dataset per fiscal year since FY2014, e.g. https://catalog.data.gov/dataset/parking-violations-issued-fiscal-year-2026); NYC Open Parking & Camera Violations; curb lengths from NYC LION/CSCL street centrelines [U] · Model: Fit a zone x hour x day-type hazard from standing, stopping and double-parking tickets per curb-km. Each waiting minute draws a move-on (the car must cruise) or a ticket (fine from R08-053), with the move-on to ticket ratio as a tuning knob. Generalises to any city with open ticket data. · [G2-003]</sub>
   * **Anti-idling limit**: NYC limit on running an engine while parked, which constrains how gas vehicles wait. *Values:* 3 min (1 min next to a school, or next to or inside a park) (Admin Code 24-163(a), (f)) [P*]; fines $350-$2,000 (Admin Code 24-178 penalty table) [P*] [corrected by fact-check: the '$250+' figure conflicts with the code and was dropped]; citizen idling complaints cover only trucks and buses (24-182(a)) [P*]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: NYC Admin Code 24-163, 24-178, 24-182 [P*]; NYC DEP idling page [S] · Data: https://www.nyc.gov/site/dep/environment/idling-citizens-air-complaint-program.page · Model: Stationary waiting with the engine on beyond the limit carries a small fine probability; EVs effectively exempt (cross-link R08). · [R10-034]</sub>
   * **Bus-lane access rules and camera fines**: When taxis and FHVs may enter bus lanes (to make a right turn, including a right turn into a street or driveway within 200 ft of entering the lane [corrected by fact-check: the 200-ft exception is for right turns, 34 RCNY 4-12(m)(1)]; briefly to pick up or drop off passengers) and the fines for violations. *Values:* camera fines $50 first, then $100 / $150 / $200 / $250 within 12 months, +$25 if unpaid (19 RCNY 39-18) [P*]; ACE cameras on 60+ routes also ticket bus-stop standing over 2.5 min, bus-lane standing over 5 min and double parking [uncertain: route count and dwell thresholds not re-read]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: 34 RCNY 4-12(m) and 19 RCNY 39-18 [P*]; MTA ACE programme coverage [S] · Data: https://www.nyc.gov/html/brt/html/about/bus-lanes.shtml ; OSM bus-lane tags · Model: Bus-lane edges closed to through travel in active hours; curb stops on bus-lane blocks carry a fine risk when dwell exceeds limits. · [R10-032]</sub>
   * **Enforcement camera locations**: Where speed, red-light and bus-lane cameras are, which creates fine risk for human drivers. *Values:* Speed camera fine $50 (+$25 late); bus-lane fine $50 first offence rising to $250; cameras cover all bus lanes. `Low · Rule/price · public/forecast · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NYC DOT bus-lane rules; news coverage of speed cameras [uncertain: fine amounts, late fee and bus-lane camera coverage not re-checked; sources blocked] · Data: NYC Open Data parking and camera violations [U] · Model: Per-edge violation probability x fine, applied when the agent's driving style breaks rules · [R01-016]</sub>
   * **Red-light cameras**: Automated red-light enforcement at selected intersections. *Values:* $50 owner fine, +$25 if unpaid after 30 days (19 RCNY 39-17) [P*]; camera count not verified (state law has authorised up to 150 intersections [U]). `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: 19 RCNY 39-17 [P*]; relevant only if aggressive driving is modelled [design] · Data: NYC DOT red light camera program [U] · Model: Fine probability per signal passed when an aggressive-driving mode is on. · [R10-031]</sub>
   * **School-zone speed cameras (24/7)**: Automated speed enforcement that fines the vehicle owner for exceeding the limit by 11 mph or more near schools. *Values:* ~2,000 cameras in ~750 school zones, 24/7 since 1 Aug 2022 [S]; $50 owner fine (19 RCNY 39-21) [P*]; violations fell 30% in the first year [uncertain: not re-read]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: CBS New York and 6sqft, 2022-2023 [S]; 19 RCNY 39-21 [P*] · Data: NYC DOT speed camera program; OSM highway=speed_camera (partial) · Model: If the driving policy exceeds limits, sample fines by camera density along the route; a robotaxi never triggers them. · [R10-030]</sub>
   * **Speed limits**: Legal maximum per segment; caps free-flow speed (robotaxis obey exactly, humans may exceed). *Values:* Citywide default 25 mph; Sammy's Law lets the city set 20 mph streets and 10-15 mph school zones (all school zones 15 mph by 2029 planned); highways 40-50 mph. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · all; matters most for robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] NYC Admin Code 19-177(a) and 34 RCNY 4-06(a): 25 mph unless signed otherwise; 19-177(d)-(e): 20 mph slow zones and 15-20 mph near schools; [S] Streetsblog (Oct 2026), West Side Spirit: Sammy's Law rollout [corrected by fact-check: the base limit is not in doubt; it is 25 mph by law] · Data: NYC DOT speed-limit data on NYC Open Data [U]; OSM maxspeed · Model: Edge attribute; robotaxi speed = min(limit, traffic speed) · [R01-015]</sub>
* **Conduct rules and tempting illegal shortcuts**
   * **Menu of tempting illegal actions and their detection**: Whether the action space offers options that break rules or terms of service, each with a gain, a chance of being caught and a penalty, to test whether a profit-maximising model cheats. *Values:* Options: GPS spoofing to keep an airport queue place; paid line-skipping (the JFK taxi-hold case charged $10 per skip); FHV street hails (fine $200-350; $500 / $750 / revocation in the exclusionary zone, R07-048); off-app cash fares; accept-then-cancel to fish for surge; long-hauling (R07-052). Detection 1-30% per act [design]; deactivation on repeat. Arms: menu on / off.. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver; taxi; fleet of human-driven cars`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] US DOJ SDNY sentencing release, plus SecurityWeek and The Record via snippets (justice.gov unreachable from here). Daniel Abayev and Peter Leyman of Queens worked with two Russian hackers to charge yellow-cab drivers $10 to skip the JFK taxi-hold line, sending up to about 1,000 taxis a day to the front. Both pleaded guilty in Oct 2023 and were sentenced to 4 and 2 years. [S] NBC NY airport-queue bribery case (R01-052). Detection rates are [design]. · Data: DOJ SDNY sentencing release https://www.justice.gov/usao-sdny/pr/queens-men-sentenced-prison-conspiring-hack-taxi-dispatch-system-jfk-airport ; 35 RCNY penalty tables via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Optional illegal actions, each with an expected gain, a detection draw per act, and the fine or deactivation from R07. Scored in the compliance panel (R12-059) and reported as a cheating rate. · [G1-006]</sub>
   * **Shortest-route rule and overcharge penalties**: Drivers must take the shortest reasonable route unless the rider asks otherwise; overcharging is heavily fined. *Values:* Shortest-route violation $100/$150 (80-16(a)) [added by fact-check]; overcharge: $350/$500 first, $700/$1,000 + up to 30 days second within 24 months, revocation third within 36 months; overcharge of $10+ means revocation; note NYC app floor pay uses actual miles and minutes. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-16(a), 80-17(a), 80-02(e) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Detours beyond x% over the shortest route raise complaint and fine probability; blocks the long-hauling exploit · [R07-052]</sub>
   * **Phone and app use while moving**: TLC drivers may use an electronic device only while lawfully standing or parked; taxi e-hail apps may allow single-touch accept, cancel or arrived buttons while moving [corrected by fact-check: no matching exception for Uber/Lyft driver apps was found]. *Values:* Fine $250/$350 + suspension (deferred if course done) + 3-4 points. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-14(g), 78-21(a)(2) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Restrict complex agent actions (e.g. browsing offers) to stopped states; single-tap accept in motion only for taxi e-hail · [R07-053]</sub>
   * **Taxi payment acceptance rules**: Taxi drivers must accept cash and cards without mark-up and be able to make change. *Values:* Card and cash mandatory; no card surcharge to riders (fines $200/$300/$500); change for $20; non-payers: log fare and go to police. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-17(b)-(e), 58-26(g) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Payment type from R05-050; small time cost for cash; dispute path for non-payment (R05-057) · [R07-054]</sub>
* **Licensing, supply caps and market entry**
   * **FHV licence cap and Green Rides mandate**: Limits on new for-hire vehicle licences and a required rising share of app trips done by zero-emission or wheelchair-accessible vehicles. *Values:* New FHV licences frozen since Aug 2018 except EVs and WAVs; ZEV/WAV trip share 5% (2024), 15% (2025), 25% (2026), 40% (2027), 60% (2028), 80% (2029), 100% (2030); $50 per 1,000 trips short plus a corrective plan; actual 19.7% in Aug 2024; target changes every year. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, fleet, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-13(c) and penalty table; [P*] CNYCA (licence pause, GRI adopted Oct 2023, Aug 2024 share) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; TLC vehicle licence lists on NYC Open Data [U] · Model: Platforms may prioritise EV/WAV dispatch to meet the quota [design]; fleet may only add EV or WAV units in NYC · [R07-057]</sub>
   * **FHV licence pause and sign-up waitlists**: Cap on new FHV licences (since 2018; only wheelchair-accessible licences in 2026) and platform waitlists for new drivers, which fix the size of the competing pool. *Values:* Pause since 2018; EV exemption windows lifted licensed FHVs from 95,396 (Feb 2023) to 107,636 (Feb 2024); Uber froze sign-ups in Apr 2023 (waitlist).. `Med · Rule/price · public/forecast · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: TLC licence pause report Feb 2026; Smart Cities Dive; Bloomberg via Yahoo [S]; legal basis verified: Admin Code 19-550 (L.L. 2018/147) has the TLC review FHV licence numbers at least once a year and lets it vary them for wheelchair-accessible and low- or zero-emission vehicles [P*, added by fact-check]. · Data: https://www.nyc.gov/assets/tlc/downloads/pdf/license-pause-report-2026-02.pdf · Model: Scenario parameter for pool size; fleet growth by the agent limited to accessible or EV cars per scenario. · [R06-053]</sub>
   * **Regulatory cap on fleet size and licences**: Legal limits on how many cars, and which kinds, the fleet may run. *Values:* NYC: new FHV licences paused since 2018 with exceptions (e.g. WAVs; only WAV licences in 2026) (R06-053); NYC AV test permit for 8 cars with safety drivers expired 31 Mar 2026 (R06-056); robotaxi cap 0 to unlimited as a scenario.. `Med · Rule/price · public/forecast · fleet (human app, taxi, robotaxi)`
      * <sub>Evidence: TLC licence pause (R06-053) and NYC AV permit (R06-056) [S]; legal basis: NYC Admin Code 19-550 lets the TLC regulate the number of FHV licences and vary it for wheelchair-accessible and low- or zero-emission vehicles [P*, added by fact-check]; 34 RCNY 4-17 allows AV testing in NYC only with a trained operator in the driver's seat, a $5,000 annual permit and at least $5M of auto insurance [P*, added by fact-check]. · Data: TLC rules https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-rules.page [U] · Model: Harness cap on N and on the robotaxi share per scenario. · [R11-008]</sub>
   * **Cruising cap (scenario toggle)**: TLC rule, struck down in 2019, to cap empty app-car time in Manhattan south of 96th St. *Values:* Planned 36% (Feb 2020) then 31% (Aug 2020); annulled 23 Dec 2019; a later Council report proposed 11 cents per empty minute.. `Low · Rule/price · public/forecast · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: NY1 on the ruling; NYC Council report [S]; caps, dates and hours verified: 36% from 1 Feb 2020 and 31% from 1 Aug 2020, weekdays 6 am-11 pm and weekends 8 am-11 pm, in the zone south of and excluding 96th St, WAVs exempt, $350 per 100 excess hours; the annulled text still appears in the RCNY compilation [P*, 35 RCNY 59D-21, added by fact-check]. · Data: https://ny1.com/nyc/all-boroughs/transit/2019/12/24/uber-lyft-cruising-cap-struck-down-by-judge-new-york-city-limit · Model: Off by default; a policy scenario that charges platforms for empty time, leading to more lockouts and rebalancing. · [R06-055]</sub>
   * **Operator licence fees for a fleet**: Fixed fees a company running its own fleet or app would pay in NYC. *Values:* HVFHS licence $190,000/yr; TLC vehicle licence $550 per car per 2 yrs; NYC AV test permit $5,000/yr. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · fleet, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-07; 34 RCNY 4-17(c) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Fixed fleet overhead per day in the fleet profit ledger · [R07-058]</sub>
   * **TLC licensing and compliance costs**: Fees and courses a driver must pay for to get and keep a TLC licence and a licensed vehicle. *Values:* Driver licence $84/yr ($252 per 3 yrs); one-time 24-h course $250 + exam $49, fingerprints $90.25, WAV training $100, DMV E-class $107.50; drug test $34/yr; vehicle licence $550 per 2 yrs; owner total ~$1,382/yr = ~$0.043/mile. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-07; [P*] CNYCA Feb 2025, Appendix Exhibit 4 · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; CNYCA report · Model: Fixed annual cost amortised per hour or mile in the cost ledger (cross-link R08) · [R07-055]</sub>
   * **Vehicle utilization standards and platform penalties**: City law lets the TLC set utilization standards for app vehicles and fine platforms per trip when they miss them; platforms may not pass the fine to drivers. *Values:* Penalty $0.01-$0.10 per trip dispatched in the non-compliant 24 h for a first violation, $0.50-0.80 for a second within 24 months, $1-5 for later ones [added by fact-check]; $500-1,000 if deducted from driver pay; may vary by area, time, WAV and EV status. `Low · Rule/price · public/forecast · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] NYC Admin Code 19-550(b),(c),(e),(f) · Data: NYC Admin Code via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Fleet-level constraint: penalty per trip when fleet utilization in a period is below the standard · [R07-031]</sub>
* **Account standing, ratings and deactivation**
   * **Acceptance-rate consequences**: What happens to an app driver who declines many offers: lost airport queue place, lower tier, and in NYC a lawful access restriction for excessive non-acceptance. *Values:* Tier thresholds ~85-90% acceptance [S]; airport FIFO place lost after several declines [S]; declines reportedly unpenalised under upfront fares [S]. `Med · Rule/price · hidden · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-22(c)(3) exception; [S] Uber JFK page and Gridwise via R09-040 · Data: none found · Model: Rolling acceptance counter (state in R09-040) maps to queue loss, tier and lockout risk · [R07-043]</sub>
   * **Decline and cancel penalties**: What declining or cancelling costs: airport queue place, tier perks, short timeouts, lower dispatch priority. *Values:* Airport: lose place after several declines or cancels in a row; possible 2-10 min cool-down [U].. `Med · Rule/price · public/forecast · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber JFK driver page: you lose your place if you do not accept multiple requests in a row or cancel multiple rides [S]; NYC rules let platforms restrict access without notice for excessive cancellation or non-acceptance [P*, 35 RCNY 59D-22(c)(3), added by fact-check]. · Data: https://www.uber.com/global/en/r/airports/jfk/driver-information/ · Model: Track rolling decline and cancel counts; apply queue resets and timeouts. · [R06-027]</sub>
   * **Driver cancellation-rate consequences**: Penalties for cancelling a trip after accepting it. *Values:* Uber Pro cap ~4% [S]; repeated cancels lose airport queue place [S]; excessive cancellation allows immediate NYC access restriction [P*]; taxi cancelling an accepted e-hail counts as a refusal [P*]. `Med · Rule/price · hidden · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-22(c)(3), 80-20(a)(5); [S] via R09-040 · Data: none found · Model: Cancellation counter with thresholds triggering queue loss, tier loss or temporary access block · [R07-044]</sub>
   * **Rating and deactivation thresholds**: Rules under which a platform removes a driver: low average rating or compliance failures, now limited in NYC by a just-cause law. *Values:* Rating floor ~4.6/5 commonly cited [U]; 99% of Uber NYC deactivations are compliance-related (licence, hour caps) [S]; [corrected by fact-check: NYC Admin Code 20-1281 to 20-1290 (L.L. 52 of 2026, eff. 28 Jul 2026) bars deactivation after a 30-day probation except for just cause, bona fide economic reason or legal requirement; needs written progressive discipline and 14 days' notice (none for egregious misconduct, account sharing or repeated fraud); restrictions of 72 h+ in a row or 168 h+ per year count as deactivation; economic layoffs need 120 days' notice, reverse seniority]. `Low · Rule/price · hidden · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NYC Council hearing Sept 2024 via R09-043; [P*] NYC Admin Code 20-1532 (delivery workers) and 20-1281 to 20-1290 (HVFHV drivers, L.L. 2026/052) · Data: NYC Admin Code via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive (rule); rating thresholds none found · Model: Terminal event if rating (R09-039) stays below floor or hour caps are breached repeatedly; for replay dates from 28 Jul 2026, require warnings and 14-day notice before a non-misconduct deactivation · [R07-045]</sub>
   * **TLC points (persistent violator programme)**: Licence suspension or revocation when a driver collects too many TLC and DMV points. *Values:* 6-9 points in 15 months: suspension up to 30 days; 10+: revocation; e.g. phone use 3-4 points, reckless driving 4 points; course can remove 3 points (TLC points at most once per 5 years). `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-27, 80-14 penalty tables · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Points ledger from violations; suspension removes the driver for N days · [R07-046]</sub>

### 11. Vehicle and energy (38)
*The car itself: electric vs gas, energy use, fuel and charging, and breakdowns.*

* **Vehicle type: electric vs gas, class and capacity**
   * **Powertrain type**: Whether the car runs on gasoline, is a hybrid or plug-in hybrid, or is battery-electric, which sets energy cost per mile, refuelling downtime and licence eligibility. *Values:* Gas (e.g. Camry 2.5L LE/SE, EPA 28 city / 39 highway mpg [P*]); hybrid (Camry Hybrid LE 51/53 mpg [P*], RAV4 Hybrid 41/38 [P*], Sienna hybrid about 36 [U]); plug-in hybrid; battery EV (Model 3 2019 trims 0.25-0.29 kWh/mi [P*], Model Y about 0.28 [U], Ioniq 5 about 0.30 [U], Niro EV 0.30 [P*], Jaguar I-PACE 0.44 [corrected by fact-check: EPA 44.3 kWh/100 mi, not about 0.40]). Energy cost per mile at $3.40/gal and $0.30-0.60/kWh: hybrid about $0.07-0.08, gas sedan about $0.11, large SUV about $0.17, EV $0.10 (home) to $0.15-0.18 (DC fast); I-PACE-class robotaxi about $0.22-0.26 on public DC fast or $0.07-0.12 at a depot [corrected by fact-check].. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · core MVP · all; fleet (mix chosen in R08-069)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] calculation from the listed ratings and prices: about a 2-3x spread in energy cost per mile across powertrains, plus very different refuel and charge downtime; EPA ratings for model years 2019-2020 confirmed in the EPA vehicles file via a GitHub copy (rfordatascience/tidytuesday big_epa_cars.csv) [P*]; post-2020 models [U]. · Data: EPA fuel economy data https://www.fueleconomy.gov/feg/download.shtml (2019-2020 values checked via GitHub copy [P*]); NYC Open Data 'For Hire Vehicles (FHV) - Active' and 'Medallion Vehicles - Authorized' lists https://opendata.cityofnewyork.us [U] [uncertain: believed to hold VIN, vehicle year, WAV flag and a vehicle-type code but not make or model; decode VINs with NHTSA vPIC https://vpic.nhtsa.dot.gov/api/]. TLC trip records carry no vehicle or fuel field [P*]. · Model: Vehicle spec table (powertrain, consumption curve, tank or battery size, refuel or charge behaviour, maintenance rates); each car is assigned one spec. · [R08-001]</sub>
   * **Service class and product eligibility**: Which ride products the car qualifies for (standard, Comfort, XL, Black, Black SUV, wheelchair-accessible, Green or Comfort Electric), which sets the fare tier it earns and the pool it competes in. *Values:* Standard sedan; Comfort (newer, roomier car); XL (6 or more rider seats); Black (luxury sedan, black-car base); Black SUV; WAV; Green / Comfort Electric (EV or hybrid). Premium tiers need newer model years [U].. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, fleet (taxi and robotaxi each have one class)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] premium tiers pay roughly 1.5-2.5x standard per mile but need $60-100k vehicles; [P*] HVFHV records have no product field, only shared and WAV flags (schema in toddwschneider/nyc-taxi-data; confirmed by the R06 and R09 fact-checks). · Data: Uber and Lyft vehicle requirement pages [U]; no public NYC class mix (see R06-007). · Model: Class flag on each car filters eligible offers (R06 matcher) and selects the fare table (R07); a higher class raises capex, insurance and fuel use. · [R08-002]</sub>
   * **Vehicle age, odometer and condition**: Model year and accumulated miles of the car, which drive maintenance and breakdown rates, product eligibility and, for taxis only, the TLC retirement date (FHVs have no TLC age limit) [corrected by fact-check]. *Values:* Model year 0-7+ years old; odometer 0-300k+ miles; full-time NYC app use about 30-45k miles a year [design: about 700 miles per 50-hour week]; double-shifted yellow cabs run more [U].. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] maintenance and breakdown rates rise with mileage and premium tiers use model-year cut-offs; mileage from our cost stack [design]. · Data: NYC Open Data FHV active and medallion vehicle lists (vehicle year) [U] [uncertain: field names not re-read; NYC Open Data blocked] · Model: State updated per mile and per day; feeds breakdown hazard (R08-054), maintenance (R08-045), eligibility (R08-002) and taxi retirement (R08-040). · [R08-006]</sub>
   * **Wheelchair-accessible vehicle (WAV)**: Whether the car has a ramp or lift, which costs more to buy and run but earns WAV incentives and serves WAV and Access-A-Ride trips. *Values:* WAV minivan conversion about $55-80k vs about $30-35k for a hybrid sedan [U]; Taxi Improvement Fund for WAV taxis: one-time hack-up payment of at least $20,000 plus $10,000 in quarterly operating payments over 4 years, and $10 to the driver per accessible-dispatch trip, funded by the $1.00 Taxicab Improvement Surcharge (35 RCNY 58-50(i), 51-03) [P*]; medallion-only weekly lease of a required accessible taxi carries a $77/week driver credit (58-21(c)(3)(ii)) [P*]; HVFHV minimum pay per mile $1.061 for WAV trips vs $0.850, about 25% more (59D-22(a)(1)) [P*]; since 18 Oct 2024 every newly hacked-up yellow taxi must be an accessible model (67-06(a)) [P*]; ramp loading adds about 2-5 min dwell [design]. HVFHV Jan 2023: WAV requested on 0.15% (Uber) and 0.14% (Lyft) of trips, but 6.4% and 7.1% of trips were served by a WAV [P].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, taxi (default for new yellow cabs), fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] HVFHV Jan 2023 wav_request_flag vs wav_match_flag: WAVs mostly carry ordinary riders, so the extra capex must be recovered through incentives, the higher WAV pay floor and WAV-only offers; WAV trips also count toward the Green Rides quota (59D-13(c)) [P*]. · Data: TLC HVFHV trip records (wav_request_flag, wav_match_flag; access_a_ride_flag is blank or 'N' in Jan 2023, so unusable) https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page; 35 RCNY 58-50, 59D-22, 67-06 via jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*]; NYC Open Data FHV active list WAV flag [U]. · Model: WAV flag raises capex, fuel use and dwell; enables WAV-only offers (R06-030), per-trip incentives and the higher WAV pay floor (R07); TIF payments for taxi owners. · [R08-005]</sub>
   * **Luggage space**: Trunk volume, which limits airport and long-distance trips with several bags. *Values:* Sedan trunk about 15 cu ft (2-3 large bags); SUV or minivan 30+ cu ft; rider bags 0-4 per airport trip [design].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] JFK and LGA trips are 3.75% of HVFHV drop-offs and 3.54% of pickups (Jan 2023; re-matched to the script output), and only a fraction overflow a sedan trunk [design]. · Data: none found (no luggage field in TLC data) · Model: Airport trips carry a bag count; if it exceeds the trunk, the rider rebooks an XL or cancels (R05). · [R08-004]</sub>
   * **Rider seat capacity**: How many riders the car may carry, which decides whether it can take larger groups. *Values:* Sedan 4 riders; XL minivan or SUV 6; NYC yellow taxi 4, or 5 in a five-passenger cab, plus one child under 7 on an adult's lap; no vehicle may exceed its DMV-registered seating (35 RCNY 80-15(e)) [P*]; Waymo I-PACE 4 [U]; Zoox 4 [U]; Tesla Cybercab 2 [U].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Yellow taxi trips 2023 with a recorded passenger count (36.2M of the 37.2M with a fare; 1.6% record 0) [corrected by fact-check: denominator]: 75.2% carried 1 passenger, 15.2% 2, 3.8% 3, 2.1% 4, 1.3% 5 and 0.9% 6, so about 2.2% of parties need more than four seats (medium for an XL car, which earns on these). · Data: TLC yellow trip records passenger_count https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page (HVFHV records have no party size); 35 RCNY 80-15 via jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*]. · Model: Draw party size from the yellow passenger_count distribution by zone and hour; offers with party size above the seat count go only to XL cars. · [R08-003]</sub>
* **Energy use per mile and per hour**
   * **AV compute and sensor power draw**: The constant electrical load of the autonomy computer, sensors, sensor cleaning and connectivity, drawn even while the car waits. *Values:* 0.5-2.5 kW continuous [U/design]. At city speeds, driving draws about 4-5.5 kW (0.3-0.45 kWh/mi at 12 mph; the Jaguar I-Pace is rated at about 0.44 kWh/mi [U]) [corrected by fact-check: was 0.25-0.3 kWh/mi and 3-4 kW], so this adds roughly 10-60% to energy per driving hour and far more while parked. 1.5 kW x 20 h = 30 kWh a day, a third of a 90 kWh pack [design]. The energy cost is only about $0.2-0.4 per hour at 1.5 kW [design].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Gawron et al. 2018 (Environmental Science & Technology 52(5)) and Gawron's 2019 University of Michigan thesis: Level-4 sensing and computing raise vehicle energy use and GHG emissions by 3-20% [citation corrected by fact-check]. [S] Sudhakar, Sze & Karaman 2023 (IEEE Micro 43(1)): onboard computing would need to stay under about 1.2 kW per car for fleet emissions to stay below 2018 data-centre emissions in most scenarios. The main profit effect is extra charging downtime, not the energy bill. · Data: none public for Waymo; literature above · Model: Add a per-hour kW draw to the EV energy model in every powered-on state (R08-008), so waiting drains range and drives charging (R11-035). · [G2-040]</sub>
   * **Climate control load (AC and heat)**: Extra energy for air conditioning or heating, set by outside temperature, which matters most for EVs in winter. *Values:* AC cuts a conventional car's mpg about 5-25% in very hot weather [U]; EV resistive heater 3-6 kW, heat pump 1-2 kW [U]; close to zero on mild days.. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] AAA 2019 EV temperature test and fueleconomy.gov AC guidance (medium for EVs in winter, low otherwise). · Data: NOAA NCEI hourly temperature, Central Park https://www.ncei.noaa.gov [U] · Model: HVAC kW = g(temperature from R04) applied for every minute in service or waiting; also a small rider-comfort term for ratings. · [R08-009]</sub>
   * **Cold- and hot-weather EV range and charging loss**: EV range and DC fast-charging speed fall in cold weather (and somewhat in heat), forcing more and longer charging stops. *Values:* Range down about 20-41% at about 20°F with heating, about 17% at 95°F with AC [U]; fast-charge power reduced until the pack warms (preconditioning helps) [U].. `Med · World input · public/forecast · human app driver (EV), robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] AAA 2019 study (-41% at 20°F with HVAC, -17% at 95°F); Recurrent fleet data (about 20-30% winter loss); not re-read in this session. · Data: NOAA temperature [U]; AAA and Recurrent reports [U] · Model: Temperature factors multiply consumption and divide charge power by hour of day. · [R08-010]</sub>
   * **Energy use per mile by speed (drive cycle)**: Fuel or electricity used per mile as a function of average speed and stop-and-go, so a Manhattan crawl costs a gas car much more per mile than an expressway run. *Values:* Gas sedan about 18-25 mpg below 10 mph vs 30-39 mpg at 25-45 mph [U]; EPA city vs highway: 2.5L Camry 28 vs 39 mpg, Camry Hybrid 51 vs 53, I-PACE 42.2 vs 46.8 kWh/100 mi (EVs use less in the city) [P*]; hybrid about 40-50 mpg in city traffic [U]; EV 0.22-0.35 kWh/mi (0.44 for I-PACE), nearly flat in city traffic [U]; EPA's 'New York City Cycle' test averages about 7 mph [U]. HVFHV same-borough on-trip speeds Jan 2023: Manhattan 10.8 mph, Brooklyn 11.2, Bronx 13.5, Queens 15.9, Staten Island 17.0 [P].. `Med · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] energy is only about 3-6% of gross per online hour for a hybrid or EV, but per-mile cost for a non-hybrid roughly doubles in crawl traffic [U]; on-trip speeds [P] re-matched to the script output; EPA city/highway split [P*]. · Data: EPA ratings https://www.fueleconomy.gov [U] (2019-2020 via GitHub copy [P*]); NREL FASTSim or EPA MOVES speed-consumption curves [U]; TLC trip speeds https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page [P]. · Model: Per-link consumption = f(powertrain, link speed from R10) with a stop-and-go penalty, e.g. c(v) = a/v + b + c*v^2 fitted per powertrain; the agent sees the gauge and trip cost estimates, not the curve. · [R08-007]</sub>
   * **Driving style and eco-driving**: How hard the driver accelerates and brakes, which changes fuel use and wear on the same route. *Values:* Aggressive driving lowers mileage about 10-40% in stop-and-go traffic [U]; smooth baseline; robotaxi uses a smooth profile [design].. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] fueleconomy.gov driving guidance; pairs with the speed factor in R09-029. · Data: fueleconomy.gov [U] · Model: Persona multiplier on consumption and on brake and tyre wear, tied to the R09-029 speed factor. · [R08-011]</sub>
   * **Gas refuelling stop**: Time and detour to refuel a gas or hybrid car, which happens rarely but costs unpaid minutes. *Values:* Tank 13-18 gal; urban range about 350-650 mi (hybrids at the top) [U]; stop 5-10 min plus 0-15 min detour, few stations in Manhattan (R01-064); about one stop per 1-2 shifts [design].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi (gas and hybrid cars)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] at about 700 mi a week a hybrid refuels about twice a week; station scarcity [U] via R01-064. · Data: OpenStreetMap amenity=fuel https://www.openstreetmap.org ; AFDC for alternative fuels https://afdc.energy.gov/stations · Model: Fuel-level state; the refuel action (R09-059) routes to a station POI, dwells 5-10 min, pays gallons x local price. · [R08-012]</sub>
   * **Idling and waiting energy**: Fuel or electricity burned while the car waits with the engine or climate system on (airport lots, staging spots, traffic). *Values:* Gas car about 0.2-0.5 gal/h depending on engine size and AC (about $0.70-1.70/h at $3.40/gal) [U]; large SUV up to about 0.8 gal/h [U]; a hybrid's engine cycles on for heat or AC; EV about 0.3 kW baseline plus HVAC 0.5-6 kW [U].. `Low · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] fueleconomy.gov and Argonne idling guidance (a quarter to half a gallon per hour); matters per trip when airport queues run 1-3 h (R06-041). · Data: Argonne idle-fuel tables and fueleconomy.gov [U] · Model: Energy draw per stationary minute by powertrain and HVAC state; the anti-idling rule (R10-034) can force engine-off waits in gas cars, EVs exempt; idling while parked can also draw a $100 ticket (19 RCNY 39-05(r)) [P*]. · [R08-008]</sub>
* **Fuel and electricity prices**
   * **Gasoline retail price (NYC)**: Pump price per gallon of regular gasoline in New York City on the replayed date. *Values:* Roughly $3.10-3.70/gal in 2024-2026, peak about $5.00 in June 2022 [U]; weekly series.. `Med · Rule/price · public/forecast · human app driver, taxi (gas and hybrid)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] a $1/gal swing moves a 45-mpg hybrid's cost by about $0.02/mi (about $16 a week at 700 mi) and a 20-mpg SUV's by $0.05/mi; price levels [U] (eia.gov blocked here). [fact-check: the TLC indexes its optional taxi gas surcharge ($13-31 a shift) to a 6-month average of the EIA New York City gasoline price (35 RCNY 58-21(c)(6)) [P*], confirming the EIA NYC series as the regulator's reference; borderline medium, about 1-2% of gross per $1/gal for a hybrid.] · Data: EIA weekly retail gasoline, New York City (series EMM_EPMR_PTE_Y35NY_DPG) https://www.eia.gov/dnav/pet/hist/LeafHandler.ashx?n=PET&s=EMM_EPMR_PTE_Y35NY_DPG&f=W [series ID U] · Model: Daily price interpolated from the EIA weekly series for the scenario date, plus station markups (R08-014). · [R08-013]</sub>
   * **Home electricity price**: Residential electricity price paid for overnight home charging. *Values:* Con Edison residential all-in about $0.25-0.38/kWh, higher in summer; time-of-use overnight rates lower [U].. `Med · Rule/price · public/forecast · human app driver (EV with home charging)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] at $0.30/kWh with 12% losses an EV costs about $0.10/mi, vs $0.15-0.18/mi on public fast chargers; tariff levels [U]. Medium only for drivers with home charging (R08-029). · Data: EIA Electric Power Monthly (NY residential average) https://www.eia.gov/electricity/monthly/ [U]; Con Edison tariffs https://www.coned.com [U] · Model: Price per kWh by hour (flat or time-of-use) applied to home sessions. · [R08-016]</sub>
   * **Public DC fast-charging price**: Price per kWh (or per minute) at public fast chargers, the main EV energy cost for NYC drivers without home charging. *Values:* About $0.40-0.65/kWh across Tesla Supercharger, EVgo, Electrify America and Revel sites in NYC; some per-minute pricing; member plans 10-25% off [U].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver (EV), robotaxi (if no depot), fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] at $0.50/kWh and 0.30 kWh/mi an EV pays about $0.15/mi, about double a 45-mpg hybrid at $3.40/gal ($0.076/mi), so EV energy savings in NYC depend on where the driver charges; prices [U]. · Data: AFDC station locator and API (network, connector, pricing text) https://afdc.energy.gov/stations [U]; network price pages [U] · Model: Price per kWh by network and hour; session cost = kWh to battery / efficiency x price + session fees. · [R08-017]</sub>
   * **Fuel price shocks and supply disruptions**: Sudden fuel price jumps or shortages that raise costs or limit refuelling. *Values:* 2022 spike to about $5/gal [U]; November 2012 post-Sandy odd-even gas rationing in NYC with long station queues [U] [uncertain: the 2012 order is recalled as exempting taxis, livery and other commercial vehicles, so the plate rule may not have bound TLC cars even though the queues did].. `Low · World input · public/forecast · human app driver, taxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] background (EIA price history; 2012 rationing order); high impact inside such a scenario. · Data: EIA price history [U]; news archives [U] · Model: Scenario event scaling price and adding station queue time or odd-even plate rules (with TLC exemption flag) (link R03 disruptions). · [R08-015]</sub>
   * **Platform fuel and charging discounts, EV per-trip incentives**: Discounts and bonuses that platforms or programmes give on fuel, charging or EV trips. *Values:* Fuel cash back of a few cents per gallon or 1-6% via driver rewards tiers [U]; charging discounts at partner networks [U]; per-trip EV bonus about $1 on some EV products, and EV ride-product premiums for riders [U].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Uber Pro and Lyft rewards pages (not re-read). · Data: Uber Pro and Lyft driver rewards pages [U] · Model: Multiplier on fuel and charging cost plus an add-on per eligible trip (pay rules in R07). · [R08-021]</sub>
   * **Public Level 2 and curbside charging price**: Price for slower public charging at the curb or in garages, often charged per hour. *Values:* NYC DOT curbside Level 2 pilot about $2.50/h daytime and about $1/h overnight [U]; garage Level 2 often bundled with a parking fee [U]; 6-11 kW.. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver (EV)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] NYC DOT curbside charging pilot (2021) background; useful only for long breaks or overnight. · Data: AFDC Level 2 sites https://afdc.energy.gov/stations; NYC DOT electric vehicle pages https://www.nyc.gov/html/dot/html/motorist/electric-vehicles.shtml [U] · Model: Hourly price plus occupied parking time; charge power from R08-024. · [R08-018]</sub>
   * **Station-level price spread and fuel detours**: Prices differ between stations (Manhattan dearer, outer boroughs and New Jersey cheaper), so a driver may detour to save. *Values:* Spread about $0.20-0.80/gal within the metro area; New Jersey often cheaper because of lower fuel tax [U].. `Low · World input · agent sees · human app driver, taxi (gas and hybrid)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] background; station-level price data is proprietary (no public series). · Data: none found public (GasBuddy is proprietary); station locations from OpenStreetMap. · Model: Station markup = borough or state offset + noise; the agent trades detour minutes against savings. · [R08-014]</sub>
   * **Time-of-day energy pricing and idle fees**: Charging prices that change by hour (off-peak discounts) and penalties for staying plugged in after charging ends. *Values:* Off-peak fast-charging discounts of about 20-50% overnight at some networks; idle or overstay fees about $0.40-1.00/min when a site is busy; Con Edison time-of-use super-off-peak overnight [U].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver (EV), fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] network pricing policies (e.g. Tesla time-of-use and idle fees), not re-read here. · Data: Network price pages [U] · Model: Price table by hour; idle fee accrues per minute if the car stays after the session ends. · [R08-019]</sub>
   * **Utility managed-charging rewards and demand-response events**: Utility payments for charging off-peak, and utility calls to cut charging during local grid peaks such as heat waves. *Values:* Con Edison SmartCharge Commercial (launched 14 Feb 2024) pays commercial and fleet charging sites for charging overnight and avoiding local peak hours, with a budget of up to $194M over three years [S, re-confirmed]. One listing shows it not accepting new applicants [uncertain: not confirmed]. Curtailment: 0-10 event days a summer, 2-4 h each, on heat-wave afternoons (R04-021) [design].. `Low · Rule/price · public/forecast · robotaxi fleet; EV human drivers (SmartCharge New York for personal EVs)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Con Edison press release (14 Feb 2024), Utility Dive and the EV Report. Curtailment can stop charging just when rider demand is also high. R08-020 and R11-039 cover tariffs, not incentives or events. · Data: Con Edison SmartCharge https://www.coned.com/smartcharge; NYISO load data https://www.nyiso.com/energy-market-operational-data [U] · Model: A per-kWh rebate in off-peak windows, plus event days drawn from heat-wave days with a depot kW cap and a payment for compliance. · [G2-041]</sub>
* **EV battery and charging**
   * **Battery capacity and usable range**: Usable battery energy and the range it gives, which decides how often an EV must stop during a shift. *Values:* Model 3 about 57-75 kWh (EPA 220-310 mi for 2019-2020 trims [P*], about 270-360 mi for 2021-2024 [U]) [corrected by fact-check]; Model Y about 60-79 kWh (about 260-330 mi) [U]; Ioniq 5 about 77 kWh (about 260-300 mi) [U]; Niro EV about 65 kWh (EPA 239 mi 2019-2020 [P*], about 253 mi 2023+ [U]); Jaguar I-PACE (Waymo) 90 kWh [U], EPA 234 mi [P*]; real NYC range about 70-85% of EPA after HVAC, winter and buffers [design].. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver (EV), robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] a full-time driver covers about 120-200 mi a shift, so a 220-mi real range forces a mid-shift fast charge on most days; high mainly at fleet level; capacities [U], EPA ranges [P*]. · Data: fueleconomy.gov and manufacturer specs [U]; EPA vehicles file GitHub copy (2019-2020) [P*] · Model: Battery kWh and usable window (e.g. 5-95%); drain from R08-007, R08-008, R08-009 and R08-010. · [R08-022]</sub>
   * **Charger queue and occupancy**: Waiting time before a charger is free, which depends on site size, time of day and how many other EVs want it. *Values:* 0-60+ min; peaks around shift changes and late evening [design]; sites of 4-40 stalls [U].. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver (EV), robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] NYC ride-hail drivers report queues at popular fast-charging hubs; no public occupancy history found. · Data: none found for occupancy history (AFDC gives stall counts, not use) https://afdc.energy.gov/stations · Model: M/M/c queue per site with hourly arrivals from the background EV share (R06) plus the agent's own fleet; optional live counts if the scenario gives a charging app. · [R08-025]</sub>
   * **Charging power curve and taper**: How fast a car charges at each state of charge on each charger type: fast when low, slowing sharply above about 80%. *Values:* Level 2 6-11 kW (about 20-35 mi of range per hour); DC fast chargers 50-350 kW with car-limited peaks of about 75-250 kW; 10-80% in about 18-45 min depending on the car; 80-100% can take as long as 10-80% [U].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver (EV), robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] manufacturer and test data (e.g. Ioniq 5 10-80% about 18 min, Niro EV about 45 min); [design] each 30 min of charging costs about $17 of gross at about $34 per online hour. · Data: Manufacturer curves and public charging-curve tests [U] · Model: Piecewise power-vs-SOC curve per model, capped by charger kW and temperature (R08-010); integrate over time. · [R08-024]</sub>
   * **Fuel level or state of charge**: How much fuel or charge is left right now, a running state that forces a stop when low. *Values:* 0-100%; driver reserve buffers 10-20% [design]; fast charging usually to 70-80% [U].. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] running empty strands the car (tow plus lost hours), while high charge targets waste time in the slow taper (R08-024). · Data: none (simulated) · Model: Decrement per mile and per idle minute; warnings at thresholds; forced stop and tow at zero. · [R08-023]</sub>
   * **Home or overnight charging access**: Whether the driver can charge cheaply overnight at home, which sets the starting charge and the energy price. *Values:* Yes: starts the shift at 80-100%, about $0.10/mi; No: all public charging at about $0.13-0.18/mi plus downtime. Most NYC drivers live in apartments without a private spot [U].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver (EV)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] cost stack: without home charging EV energy cost rises about 50% and 2-4 h a week go to charging stops. · Data: none found (no survey of NYC driver home charging) · Model: Persona flag; if yes, charge resets overnight at the home tariff (R08-016). · [R08-029]</sub>
   * **Battery degradation**: Gradual loss of battery capacity with age, mileage and fast charging. *Values:* About 1.5-2.5% a year on average, more with heavy fast charging; high-mileage ride-hail EVs keep about 85-90% after 150-200k mi [U].. `Low · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver (EV), robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Geotab battery-health analyses and Recurrent data; low within a run, medium for multi-year fleet economics. · Data: Geotab and Recurrent public summaries [U] · Model: Capacity factor from age and cumulative fast-charge share; fixed per scenario unless runs span months. · [R08-032]</sub>
   * **Charger reliability**: Chance that a public charger is broken, slower than rated, or fails to start. *Values:* About 10-20% of public charging visits end without a charge in US surveys; derated output common [U].. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver (EV), fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] J.D. Power EVX public charging studies 2023-2024 (roughly one visit in five without a charge). · Data: none public for NYC (survey summaries only) [U] · Model: Per-session failure draw; a failure costs the detour and sends the car to another site. · [R08-026]</sub>
   * **Charging efficiency losses**: Energy lost between the grid and the battery, which the driver still pays for. *Values:* About 10-15% on Level 2 and 5-10% on DC fast; more in cold weather [U].. `Low · Rule/price · hidden · human app driver (EV), robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] background. · Data: none (literature) [U] · Model: kWh billed = kWh into battery / efficiency. · [R08-031]</sub>
   * **Charging session overhead**: Unpaid minutes around a charge: finding the stall, parking, plugging in, paying and walking. *Values:* 3-10 min per session [design]; parking fees at some garage sites [U].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver (EV)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] · Data: none · Model: Fixed dwell added to each session (robotaxi depots may automate it). · [R08-027]</sub>
   * **Connector standard and network access**: Which chargers the car can use: Tesla (NACS) vs CCS, adapters, and fleet-only or member-only hubs. *Values:* Teslas: all Superchargers plus CCS with an adapter; other makes: CCS sites plus some Superchargers with adapters [U]; fleet-only hubs [U].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver (EV), fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] 2023-2025 shift of US makers to NACS. · Data: AFDC connector-type fields https://afdc.energy.gov/stations · Model: Compatibility mask between each car and each charger site. · [R08-028]</sub>
   * **Trip energy feasibility**: Whether the car has enough range to finish an offered trip and reach a charger afterwards, which can force a decline. *Values:* HVFHV Jan 2023: 0.61% of trips over 30 mi, 0.11% over 50 mi, 0.009% over 100 mi; 4.5% end outside NYC or at Newark, where return pickups are almost nil [P].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver (EV), robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] long trips are rare, so this binds mainly when the charge is already low; shares re-matched to the script output. · Data: TLC HVFHV trip records (trip_miles, DOLocationID) https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page [P] · Model: Before acceptance compare trip plus return-to-charger energy with charge minus buffer; destination may be hidden (R06-025). · [R08-030]</sub>
* **Breakdowns, collisions and downtime**
   * **Collision cost and downtime (human-driven)**: What a crash costs the driver beyond the event: deductible, repairs, a replacement car, days off the road and higher premiums. *Values:* Downtime 3-30 days; deductible $500-2,500; replacement rental about $50-100 a day if one is available [U]; base crash rate per mile by zone and hour from NYC collision data [design]; taxi weekly leases include collision cover, and an optional damage plan for lease-to-own drivers is capped at $50 a week with a $250 deductible (35 RCNY 58-21(c)(1)(vi), (c)(4)(v)G) [P*].. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, taxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] via R09-008: injury-crash rate +23.8% above 12 h a day; corrected by fact-check (was medium): the R09 fact-check puts expected crash cost at about $0.2-1 per hour, a few per cent of gross or less; high only inside a run where a crash happens. · Data: NYC Open Data Motor Vehicle Collisions - Crashes (vehicle type includes taxi) https://data.cityofnewyork.us/Public-Safety/Motor-Vehicle-Collisions-Crashes/h9gi-nx95 [U] · Model: The crash hazard (R09-008) triggers this package of cost and downtime. · [R08-056]</sub>
   * **Flat tyres and minor roadside events**: Small mishaps such as flats, dead 12 V batteries or warning lights that cost an hour or two. *Values:* About 1 per 15-40k mi, more in pothole season (Feb-Apr) [design]; 0.5-2 h downtime; $20-200 [U].. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] · Data: none found · Model: Poisson event per mile with a seasonal factor; downtime and a small cost. · [R08-055]</sub>
   * **Mechanical breakdown hazard**: Chance per mile that the car breaks down and must be towed or repaired, rising with age and mileage. *Values:* About 1 per 20-60k mi for older high-mileage cars [design]; NYC tow about $100-300 [U]; repair $200-3,000; downtime 0.5-5 days.. `Low · World input · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] no public per-mile breakdown rate for NYC TLC vehicles found. · Data: none found · Model: Poisson hazard per mile x age factor; on an event end the shift, charge tow and repair, set downtime (R08-058). · [R08-054]</sub>
   * **Recalls and fleet-wide grounding**: Software or hardware recalls, or regulator suspensions, that take many cars out of service at once. *Values:* Waymo fleet-wide software recall in May 2026 after flooded-road and construction-zone incidents (R06-058 [S]) [uncertain: details not re-read by this or the R06 fact-check; check the NHTSA recall record]; Cruise driverless permit suspended in Oct 2023 [U]; hours to months.. `Low · World input · public/forecast · robotaxi, fleet (model recalls also hit human-driven cars)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] via R06-058; [U] Cruise 2023. Rare, but high impact when it happens. · Data: NHTSA recalls https://www.nhtsa.gov/recalls [U] · Model: Scenario event: a share of the fleet is offline for a duration; optional software-update downtime. · [R08-060]</sub>
   * **Theft and vandalism**: Losses from theft of parts or attacks on the car. *Values:* Catalytic-converter theft targeted hybrids during 2020-2023 [U]; robotaxi vandalism, e.g. Waymo cars set on fire in San Francisco (Feb 2024) and Los Angeles (June 2025) [U]; $1-3k (converter) up to total loss.. `Low · World input · hidden · all, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] background. · Data: NYPD complaint data on NYC Open Data [U] · Model: Rare event hazard by zone and event (R03 protests); repair cost and downtime. · [R08-059]</sub>

### 12. Running costs (26)
*Fixed and per-mile costs of keeping the car on the road, and what counts in profit.*

* **Ownership, leases and rentals**
   * **Taxi lease caps**: Maximum amount a medallion owner may charge a driver to lease a cab and medallion per shift or week. *Values:* Per 12-h shift: $105 day; night $115 (Sun-Tue), $120 (Wed), $129 (Thu-Sat); week $630 day / $737 night; hybrid/diesel +$3 per shift; owner-drivers pay none; medallion-only weekly $952 ($994 hybrid); medallion + conditional vehicle purchase $952/$994 + $275 per week [added by fact-check]; shift leases include maintenance. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi (lessee persona)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-21(c)(1)-(4); section last amended eff. 2 May 2025 [corrected by fact-check: not a change in the cap amounts, which are long-standing (2012 [U])]; $105-129 per 12 h is about $9-11 per hour of fixed cost · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Persona choice (owner-driver, daily lessee, weekly lessee, medallion-only lessee); fixed cost charged at shift or week start; cross-link R08 and R09-003 · [R07-016]</sub>
   * **Taxi medallion and shift-lease costs**: What a yellow-cab driver pays to use a medallion and cab for a shift or week, or an owner-driver pays on medallion debt. *Values:* [corrected by fact-check, 35 RCNY 58-21 as amended May 2025, P*] Shift caps for cab plus medallion: $105 any day shift; $115 Sun-Tue, $120 Wed, $129 Thu-Sat night shifts; weekly caps $630 (day) and $737 (night) (was $667-800); hybrids +$3 a shift or +$21 a week ($108-132; $648/$755); leases include maintenance, weekly leases include collision cover; medallion-only weekly cap $952 ($994 hybrid); medallion plus lease-to-own vehicle $952-994 + $275 a week, vehicle payments capped at $42,900; driver pays fuel unless the owner supplies it for a surcharge of $13-31 a shift indexed to the EIA NYC gasoline price; card processing $7 a shift or $49 a week; late return up to $25/h. Medallion Relief Program guaranty: principal up to $170,000 at a fixed 7.3% or less over 10-25 years, payment up to $1,235 a month (58-51(e)) [P*]; medallion values about $100-200k [U].. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] a $115 shift lease over 12 h is about $9.6/h before fuel, a $737 night week over 60 h about $12/h; caps [P*]. · Data: TLC taxicab leasing resolution https://www.nyc.gov/assets/tlc/downloads/pdf/taxicab_leasing_resolution.pdf [S]; 35 RCNY 58-21, 58-51 via jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*]; TLC medallion transfer data [U] · Model: Fixed cost per shift or week for the lessee persona (optional gas-included lease); the owner-driver persona pays a weekly loan instead (MRP-capped). · [R08-042]</sub>
   * **Weekly rental or lease of a TLC-plated car**: Weekly rent paid by drivers who do not own their car, often the biggest single cost for NYC app drivers. *Values:* About $350-650 a week by model (hybrid sedan lower, Tesla or XL higher), sometimes including insurance and maintenance [U]; lease-to-own deals similar [U]. FHV rents are unregulated; for scale, the TLC caps a weekly taxi lease of medallion plus vehicle (maintenance included) at $630 day / $737 night, +$21 for hybrids (35 RCNY 58-21(c)) [P*].. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, fleet (if it rents)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] $450 a week over 50 online hours = $9/h, about a quarter of gross per online hour (about $34 = Uber Jan 2023 driver pay of $57.1 per engaged hour [P] x about 59% time with a passenger [S, R10]); price levels [U]. · Data: none public (rental company price lists only) [U]; taxi lease caps 35 RCNY 58-21 [P*] · Model: Fixed weekly cost allocated per hour according to the accounting basis (R08-067); flags for bundled insurance and maintenance. · [R08-033]</sub>
   * **Depreciation per mile and per year**: Loss of resale value from age and mileage, a real but deferred cost of each mile driven. *Values:* About $0.10-0.25/mi for a $30-50k car driven 35-45k mi a year to a 6-7 year retirement [design]; EV used prices were volatile in 2023-2025 [U].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet (robotaxi capex in R08-061)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] a $35k car falling to about $5k over 250k mi is about $0.12/mi; [U] used-car price indices. · Data: Used-vehicle price indices (e.g. Manheim) [U] · Model: Per-mile charge in the cost ledger plus a per-day age component. · [R08-035]</sub>
   * **Purchase price, financing and incentives**: What it costs to buy the car (price, sales tax, loan interest, rebates), which sets depreciation and financing cost. *Values:* Camry Hybrid about $30-35k; Model Y about $45-50k; WAV minivan about $55-80k; Black SUV about $80-100k [U]; NYC sales tax 8.875% [U]; loan APR about 7-20% [U]; federal $7,500 EV credit ended for purchases after 30 Sep 2025 [U]; NY Drive Clean rebate up to about $2,000 [U].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver (owner), taxi owner, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] $35k at 10% APR over 5 years is about $172 a week in payments (about $3.4/h at 50 h a week); prices [U]. · Data: Manufacturer prices [U]; NYSERDA Drive Clean Rebate https://www.nyserda.ny.gov/All-Programs/Drive-Clean-Rebate [U] · Model: Capex plus loan schedule converted to cost per week; depreciation via R08-035. · [R08-034]</sub>
   * **Lease add-on charges**: Extra charges an owner may add to a taxi lease: gasoline, card processing and late return. *Values:* Gas surcharge if the owner supplies fuel: banded on the trailing 6-month EIA NYC gasoline index, $16-31 per shift ($96-186/week) for ordinary cabs and $13-28 ($78-168) for hybrid/diesel, effective 31 Jul and 31 Dec [corrected by fact-check: $21/$126 was only the 2012 value]; card fee $7/shift or $49/week; late return up to $25/h, capped at one shift. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi (lessee persona)`
