      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-21(c)(5)-(6) · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; EIA NYC gasoline prices https://www.eia.gov/petroleum/gasdiesel/ · Model: Optional persona options; gas surcharge looked up from the index band on the replay date; late fee accrues per hour after shift end (cross-link R09-019) · [R07-017]</sub>
   * **Taxi equipment and card-processing fees**: Costs for the taxi technology system (meter, screen, card reader), roof light and partition, and fees fleets charge drivers on card payments. *Values:* [corrected by fact-check, P*] Card processing is a flat $7 per daily shift or $49 per week (35 RCNY 58-21(c)(5)(xi)), not about 5% of card fares, and no other card pass-along is allowed (58-26(g)); medallion-only and lease-to-own caps already include card fees (58-21(c)(3)(iv), (c)(4)(v)); the $1.00 Taxicab Improvement Surcharge is deducted from card receipts but is a rider surcharge paid to the WAV fund (58-16(f), 58-21(f)); technology-system charges only as set by TLC rule (58-21(c)(5)(viii)), size [U].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] $49 a week is about 1-1.5% of a taxi driver's gross; card share can be computed from payment_type in TLC records [P] (fare-side rules in R07). · Data: TLC yellow trip records payment_type https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page; 35 RCNY 58-21, 58-26 via jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*] · Model: Flat card-processing charge per shift or week (not a percentage) plus any TLC-set technology fee. · [R08-043]</sub>
   * **Vehicle-borne advertising income**: Small extra income from roof-top or in-car screen advertising that offsets vehicle costs. *Values:* About $0-300 a month where programmes exist [U]. [corrected by fact-check, P*] Only taxis may carry rooftop ads in NYC, on a TLC-authorised fixture with a $50 a year permit (35 RCNY 67-16, 58-07(g)), and the income goes to the medallion owner, not usually the lessee [U]; FHVs, including Uber and Lyft cars, may not carry rooftop ads, and interior ads need a TLC-licensed interior advertising provider (59A-29(e)); robotaxis outside TLC licensing unregulated here [U].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi (medallion owner), robotaxi; human app driver interior ads only`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] background; [P*] advertising rules. · Data: none found for income; 35 RCNY 67-16, 59A-29 via jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*] · Model: Fixed monthly credit, or credit per online hour in ad zones; taxi-owner and robotaxi personas only (FHV rooftop ads banned). · [R08-044]</sub>
* **Insurance, licences and inspections**
   * **Commercial (TLC) insurance premium**: Annual cost of the commercial liability policy that every TLC-licensed vehicle must carry at all times. *Values:* About $5,000-12,000+ a year for an FHV, higher for new drivers or after claims [U]; TLC minimums [P*]: $100,000 per person no-fault basic economic loss, $100,000/$300,000 bodily injury, $10,000 property damage for FHVs under 8 seats (35 RCNY 59A-12(c)(1)) and taxis (58-13(d)(1)); cover must run 24/7 (59A-12(c)(5)); taxis run more than one shift a day need double-shift cover (58-13(d)(2)); 8-15 seat FHVs $1.5M combined; the NYC FHV market is dominated by one insurer (American Transit) [U].. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] $8,000 a year = about $154 a week = about $3/h at 50 h a week, about 9% of gross per online hour; premium levels [U]; coverage minimums verified [P*]. · Data: TLC insurance requirements https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/vehicles/insurance.page [U]; 35 RCNY 58-13, 59A-12 via jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*]; NY Department of Financial Services [U] · Model: Fixed weekly cost, scaled by driver experience and claim history (R08-037). · [R08-036]</sub>
   * **Licence rules for adding vehicles (EV and WAV)**: Rules on which new vehicles may be licensed and what share of trips must be zero-emission or accessible, which constrain fleet growth and vehicle choice. *Values:* FHV licence pause since 2018 with EV exemption windows (licensed FHVs 95,396 in Feb 2023 to 107,636 in Feb 2024) (R06-053 [S]); issuance reviewed every six months from Feb 2023, WAV licences always issued, EV-only licences (individual, or up to 25 per applicant) released at TLC discretion (35 RCNY 59A-06(a)) [P*] [uncertain: whether any EV-only licences were released in 2026; 'only WAV licences in 2026' rests on a snippet]; Green Rides [P*]: each high-volume service must dispatch at least 5% (2024), 15% (2025), 25% (2026), 40% (2027), 60% (2028), 80% (2029) and 100% (2030) of trips to zero-emission or accessible vehicles, fine $50 per 1,000 trips short (59D-13(c)).. `Med · Rule/price · public/forecast · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Green Rides schedule and licensing rules from rule text; licence counts [S] via R06-053. High for fleet composition. · Data: TLC licence pause report https://www.nyc.gov/assets/tlc/downloads/pdf/license-pause-report-2026-02.pdf [S]; 35 RCNY 59A-06, 59D-13 via jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*] · Model: Fleet expansion only allows licence-eligible types; scenario year sets the required ZEV-or-WAV trip share (25% in 2026). · [R08-041]</sub>
   * **Inspection frequency and downtime**: How often the car must be inspected, and the hours lost each time. *Values:* [corrected by fact-check, P*] Taxis every four months at the TLC facility (35 RCNY 58-29(b)); FHVs also three times a year, at least once every four months (59A-26(a)(1)) (was 'about once a year'); only new-licence and renewal FHV inspections must be at the TLC Safety and Emissions facility (Woodside, Queens [U]), the others at any DMV-registered station (59A-04(e)-(f)); late taxi inspection means summary suspension; about 1-4 h each with travel and waiting [design].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi, human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] frequency from rule text; three visits of 1-4 h a year are under 0.5% of online time. · Data: TLC inspection pages https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc [U]; 35 RCNY 58-29, 59A-26, 59A-04 via jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*] · Model: Scheduled off-duty block on a set date in multi-week runs (three a year). · [R08-039]</sub>
   * **Insurance deductible and claim surcharge**: Out-of-pocket cost per claim and the premium rise after an at-fault crash. *Values:* Deductible $500-2,500 [design, as in R09-008]; at-fault surcharge about +10-40% for about 3 years [U].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] background. · Data: NY Department of Financial Services consumer guides [U] · Model: On a crash (R08-056) charge the deductible and raise the future premium. · [R08-037]</sub>
   * **TLC licence, registration and inspection fees**: Government fees to keep the driver and car licensed (TLC driver licence, vehicle licence, DMV registration, inspections, drug test). *Values:* [corrected by fact-check from rule text, P*] TLC driver licence $84 a year on a 3-year term, i.e. $252 (35 RCNY 80-07, 80-06); FHV vehicle licence $275 a year on a 2-year term, i.e. $550 (59A-07(a), 59A-06); Commercial Motor Vehicle Tax $800 per 2-year FHV licence period if not paid to DMV (59A-07(g)); TLC inspection fee $75 for FHVs (59A-04(e)(7)) and $90 for taxis (58-07(d)); taxicab licence $550 a year (58-07(a)); DMV registration about $100-300 [U]; together about $15-20 a week for an FHV owner-driver [design] (was $5-15).. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet (robotaxi licensing in NYC undefined)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] about $0.3-0.4 per online hour; fee values now [P*]. · Data: TLC licensing pages https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/drivers/get-a-tlc-drivers-license.page [U]; 35 RCNY 80-07, 59A-07, 59A-04, 58-07 via jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*] · Model: Fixed annual costs prorated per week. · [R08-038]</sub>
   * **Vehicle age limits and retirement**: Maximum vehicle age allowed under TLC rules, which caps service life and the resale value at retirement. *Values:* [corrected by fact-check, P*] Taxis retire at the first scheduled inspection after 84 months (7 years) from hack-up, less the vehicle's age if hacked up used (35 RCNY 67-18(a)-(b)); one-time hardship extension up to 6 months (67-18(d)); an accessible taxi may stay in service past the date while it passes inspection (67-18(c)); since 18 Oct 2024 only Accessible Taxicab Models may be hacked up, new (under 500 miles, two latest model years) or converted (67-06), and Alternative Fuel Medallions need a hybrid or CNG model (67-05(b)). FHVs and green cars have no TLC scheduled retirement (59A-28(d) repealed; 82-61 reserved); platform model-year limits apply [U]. (Was 'roughly 5-8 model years for taxis and FHVs'.). `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi (TLC rule), human app driver (platform limits only), fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] rule text; medium for multi-year fleet planning. · Data: TLC rules https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-rules.page [U]; 35 RCNY 67-05, 67-06, 67-18, 59A-28 via jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*] · Model: Retirement date from hack-up date (taxis) or platform model-year cut-off (FHVs) sets the depreciation horizon (R08-035) and forces replacement in fleet runs. · [R08-040]</sub>
* **Maintenance, tyres and cleaning**
   * **Simplified all-in per-mile cost (calibration)**: One cost per mile that bundles energy, maintenance, tyres and depreciation, for simple modes and as a sanity check. *Values:* IRS business standard mileage rate 70 cents/mi for 2025 [U] [uncertain: the 2026 rate is recalled as 72.5 cents/mi; irs.gov blocked, not re-read]; AAA average new-car total about $0.75-0.85/mi at 15k mi a year [U]; our NYC full-time stack about $0.61/mi (owner, hybrid) to $0.81/mi (renter), fixed costs included [design].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] cost stack for 700 mi per 50-hour week (see dossier; arithmetic re-checked); IRS and AAA values [U]. · Data: IRS standard mileage rates https://www.irs.gov/tax-professionals/standard-mileage-rates [U]; AAA Your Driving Costs [U] · Model: 'Simple cost mode' uses one per-mile and one per-hour rate; detailed mode uses the itemised variables. · [R08-049]</sub>
   * **Brake wear**: Cost of brake pads and rotors, high in stop-and-go traffic for gas cars and much lower with regenerative braking. *Values:* Gas-car pads about 20-40k mi in NYC traffic; hybrids and EVs often 2-3x longer; pads and rotors about $300-700 per axle [U].. `Low · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] background. · Data: none found · Model: Per-mile accrual scaled by stops per mile (from R10 speeds) and powertrain. · [R08-047]</sub>
   * **Car wash and interior cleaning (human-driven)**: Routine washing and cleaning to keep the car presentable for ratings and TLC rules. *Values:* $15-40 per wash, or about $30-60 a month for unlimited plans; every 1-7 days, more often in winter salt season [U]; 20-40 min per visit [design].. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] background; cleanliness feeds ratings (R09-039) and mess events (R09-046, robotaxi cleaning in R09-047). · Data: none found · Model: Cleanliness state decays per trip and with weather; a wash action costs money and time. · [R08-048]</sub>
   * **Routine maintenance per mile**: Scheduled servicing (oil and filters, fluids, small repairs), charged per mile. *Values:* Gas and hybrid about $0.03-0.06/mi; EV about $0.02-0.04/mi (no oil); oil change every 5-10k mi at about $50-100 [U].. `Low · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] maintenance plus tyres at about $0.06/mi is about $0.84/h (about 2.5% of gross); [U] AAA maintenance estimates. · Data: AAA Your Driving Costs https://newsroom.aaa.com [U] · Model: Per-mile accrual, with service events every N miles that take the car off the road for 1-2 h (taxi lessees: owner pays, 35 RCNY 58-21(b)). · [R08-045]</sub>
   * **Tyre wear and replacement**: Cost of tyres worn by city driving, potholes and heavy EVs. *Values:* Set of four about $600-1,200; life about 30-60k mi; EVs wear about 10-30% faster [U].. `Low · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] background. · Data: none found (AAA bundles tyres with maintenance) [U] · Model: Per-mile accrual, faster for EVs and aggressive personas (R08-011). · [R08-046]</sub>
* **Tolls, parking and fines paid by the driver**
   * **Driver-paid tolls (loaded vs empty legs)**: Tolls are passed to the rider on paid trips, including the return toll on trips that leave the city; the driver pays only tolls on empty repositioning legs not tied to a trip (e.g. a tolled crossing to an airport queue or home). [corrected by fact-check: was 'paid by the driver on empty legs such as returning from New Jersey, Newark or Staten Island'.] *Values:* Rule [P*]: on trips beyond NYC the rider pays 'all necessary tolls to and from the destination' (35 RCNY 58-26(f)(2) taxis, 59A-23(b)(2) FHVs); apps must pass to the driver any toll paid on a dispatched trip (59D-17(c)); taxi riders pay double going into and nothing coming out of the one-way-tolled Verrazzano, Cross Bay and Marine Parkway crossings (58-26(f)(1)). Data [P]: yellow 2023: 8.2% of trips had tolls (mean $7.34, about 3.0% of fares); HVFHV Jan 2023: Uber 10.8% (mean $10.11), Lyft 16.7% (mean $5.31), about 4.3-5.0% of base fares; 100% of app drop-offs at Newark carry a toll (median $21) and 56-62% of other out-of-city drop-offs (median $20), although westbound Hudson crossings are free; yellow 2023: 98% of Newark drop-offs (median $17.75). MTA E-ZPass car toll at major crossings $6.55 until 5 Aug 2023 and $6.94 from 6 Aug 2023 [P], about 6% higher from Jan 2026 (R01-020 [S]); Hudson crossings $14.79-16.79 inbound only in 2026 (R01-021 [S]), $12.75/$14.75 in 2023 records [P]. 3.9% of HVFHV trips end outside NYC and 0.6% at Newark, with almost no pickups there [P].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*][P] corrected by fact-check (was medium): the return toll is rider-paid by rule and in the data, so the driver keeps only the return time and energy (deadhead, R01-059 and R10) plus tolls on voluntary empty repositioning, which can mostly be routed around; likely well under 1% of gross [design]. · Data: TLC trip records (tolls, tolls_amount) https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page [P] (fc_tolls.py); 35 RCNY 58-26, 59A-23, 59D-17 via jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*]; MTA tolls https://www.mta.info/fares-tolls/tolls ; PANYNJ https://www.panynj.gov/bridges-tunnels · Model: Toll on crossing edges by direction (R01); charged to the rider when loaded, plus the return toll on out-of-city trips; charged to the driver only for empty repositioning crossings not tied to a trip. · [R08-050]</sub>
   * **Parking costs (overnight and during the shift)**: What it costs to keep the car overnight and to park during breaks or waits. *Values:* Overnight: free street parking with alternate-side moves, or a garage at about $200-600 a month in the outer boroughs and $500-1,000+ in Manhattan [U]; short-term Manhattan garage about $20-50 for 1-2 h [U]; relief stands free for up to 1 h (R01-031; 34 RCNY 4-08, re-read by the R10 fact-check [P*]); airport FHV lots free [U].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] background; relief-stand limit [P*]. · Data: none public for prices; relief stands on NYC Open Data https://catalog.data.gov/dataset/taxi-and-for-hire-vehicle-fhv-relief-stands (R01-031) · Model: Per-night or per-hour parking price by zone; break actions choose paid or free spots. · [R08-052]</sub>
   * **Ticket and fine ledger**: Money lost to parking, standing and camera tickets and TLC summonses, whichever domain models the triggering event. *Values:* [verified with detail, P*] 19 RCNY 39-05 base fines: no stopping or no standing $100; no parking $30 and meters $20, both $50 in Manhattan south of 96th St; street cleaning $50; idling $100; plus a state mandatory surcharge [U], so about $35-115 as posted; bus-lane and bus-route cameras $50, $100, $150, $200, then $250 within 12 months (39-18, 39-24); speed and red-light cameras $50 (39-21, 39-17 via the R10 fact-check); DEP anti-idling $350-2,000 (R10 fact-check); TLC fines in 35 RCNY about $25-1,000 plus suspensions; taxi owners may deduct parking and red-light camera tickets from the driver's deposit (58-21(e)(2)); rental fleets add admin fees [U].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi (robotaxi: owner liability)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] fine schedules; events come from R09-014, R10-030 to R10-032 and R10-034. · Data: NYC Open Data 'Open Parking and Camera Violations' https://data.cityofnewyork.us/City-Government/Open-Parking-and-Camera-Violations/nc67-uf89 [U]; 19 RCNY 39-05, 39-18, 39-24 via jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*] · Model: Each violation event posts a fine to the ledger; camera tickets go to the registered owner and are passed on with a fee. · [R08-053]</sub>
   * **Toll payment method**: Whether the car pays by E-ZPass or by plate and mail, and the admin fees rental fleets add. *Values:* [corrected by fact-check, P*] Every taxi and FHV owner must hold an MTA E-ZPass account and tag, and drivers may not operate for hire without one and must use it at all MTA crossings (35 RCNY 58-27, 59A-24, 59D-18, 80-15(d)); toll-by-mail (about 1.4-1.5x E-ZPass, PANYNJ about $23 vs $16.79, R01-021 [S]) applies to TLC cars only on tag failure, otherwise to non-TLC cars such as a robotaxi; taxi owners holding the tag may require a replenishment deposit of up to $10 a shift, capped at $100 (58-27(d)(2)); rental-fleet admin fee per toll $0-25 [U].. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees · all (toll-by-mail mainly non-TLC robotaxi)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] E-ZPass mandate; [S] via R01-021; [U] rental fees. · Data: MTA and PANYNJ toll schedules (as R08-050); 35 RCNY 58-27, 59A-24, 80-15 via jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*] · Model: Toll multiplier and fixed fee by payment type; default E-ZPass for TLC personas. · [R08-051]</sub>
   * **Towing, impound and hearing time**: Rare, costly events that cost both money and shift hours: a tow from a no-standing, hydrant or rush-hour lane spot, getting the car back from the impound lot, and time spent contesting summonses. Fees are NYC-specific; generalise with a city fee table. *Values:* NYPD tow for a parking or standing violation: $185 regular ($370 heavy duty) plus $20 per night of storage, on top of the ticket. Booted cars follow a separate Department of Finance schedule: a car is booted once it owes >= $350 in judgment tickets, and towed if not paid within 48 h. For cars booted on or after 1 Nov 2025, the boot fee rises from $142 to $185 and the tow (< 8,500 lb) from $140 to $220. Storage rises from $10/day (first 3 days) then $15, to $30/day then $50. A 5% poundage fee is added. Reaching the pound and paying takes 2-4 h [design]. Each summons hearing takes 1-4 h (OATH for TLC; DOF for parking; DMV TVB for moving violations) [design]. Tow hazard: 0-0.5 per 1,000 h parked at illegal Midtown curbs [design].. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver; taxi (robotaxi depots rarely street-park)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NYPD towed-vehicles page, NYC DOF towed-vehicle FAQ and nyc.gov/booting via search snippets; the fee tables were not read directly. [corrected: '$140-220 tow, $30/$50 storage' are the old and new DOF boot-and-tow fees, not a range, and do not apply to NYPD tows]. Time losses are [design]. · Data: NYPD towed vehicles https://www.nyc.gov/site/nypd/services/vehicles-property/towed-vehicles.page ; NYC DOF FAQ https://www.nyc.gov/site/finance/vehicles/services-towed-vehicles-faq.page ; DOF booting https://www.nyc.gov/booting ; NYC Open Data parking violations (hazard by street) · Model: When the agent waits or parks at an illegal curb (R01-025), draw a tow with a hazard by street class and hour. A tow costs fee + storage + 2-4 h off-shift. Each summons can be contested through an optional hearing action that costs time and may cut the fine (ledger in R08-053). · [G1-007]</sub>
* **Personal costs and profit accounting**
   * **Cost accounting basis for profit**: Which costs count in the profit score: only marginal costs (energy, wear, tolls, fines) or also fixed costs (rental, insurance, licences, capex) per hour. *Values:* Marginal only; marginal plus time-based fixed costs; full economic cost including depreciation and financing [design].. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] in our cost stack, fixed costs are about $4.6/h for an owner and $9.5/h for a renter vs marginal costs of about $2-4/h for a hybrid (energy and wear, plus depreciation for owners), so the basis moves profit per hour by about 14-28% of gross and changes incentives (fixed costs reward longer shifts); arithmetic re-checked. · Data: none (design choice) · Model: Ledger tags each cost as marginal or fixed; scoring sums by the chosen basis; report all three. · [R08-067]</sub>
   * **Personal running costs**: The driver's own costs that are not tied to the car: phone, data, mount, food and drinks. *Values:* Phone and data $40-100/month; food $10-25/shift [design/U].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] no public NYC survey found; IRS mileage rates cover the car only [U]. · Data: none found · Model: Fixed per-shift and per-meal costs deducted from profit. · [R09-037]</sub>

### 13. Driver as a person (39)
*The human behind the wheel: hours, fatigue, breaks, home, motivation, skill and standing.*

* **Shift structure and working hours**
   * **Driver work profile (persona type)**: The type of driver, which sets default availability, hours, costs and constraints. *Values:* Full-time app driver (only job); part-time app driver (other job); taxi lessee day or night; owner-driver; robotaxi (no driver).. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Parrott (presentation drawing on the 2018 TLC study): 60-65% of NYC drivers full-time; Uber 2014 survey (Hall & Krueger): 52% part-time and 75% of part-timers have other jobs [uncertain: survey figures not re-checkable this session]. · Data: TLC driver survey summary (nyc.gov/assets/tlc/downloads/pdf/driver_survey_summary.pdf) [S]; Hall & Krueger 2018 ILR Review 71(3). · Model: Scenario config picks a profile that loads availability windows, home zone, hour caps and cost items. · [R09-004]</sub>
   * **Taxi lease shift window and handover**: Drivers who lease a yellow cab work a fixed 12-hour shift and must hand the cab back at a set time. *Values:* Two 12 h shifts per cab; handover usually 16:00-17:00; 35 RCNY 58-21(i)(1)(ii) requires 12 consecutive hours ([P*]; the pilot waiver is [S]); late-return charge at most $25 per hour, capped at one shift's lease (35 RCNY 58-21(c)(5)(iv)) [corrected by fact-check: was 'per hour [U]'].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 58-21 (verbatim, nyc_code_archive); [S] 2011 city GPS study and TLC counts (6,688 to 5,452 cabs between 4 and 5 pm); TLC pilot press release 2015 on moving shift change outside 4-8 pm. · Data: 35 RCNY 58-21 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*]; TLC taxicab leasing resolution (nyc.gov/assets/tlc/downloads/pdf/taxicab_leasing_resolution.pdf) [S]; 2009-2013 FOIL trips for shift timing. · Model: Taxi persona gets a hard shift end and a garage location; late return costs up to $25/h, capped at one shift's lease. The same handover also creates a supply dip in the competitor model (R06). · [R09-003]</sub>
* **Fatigue and safety risk**
   * **Crash and incident risk from fatigue and hours**: Chance per hour of a collision that ends the shift, rising with fatigue and night driving. *Values:* Base hazard by zone and hour; x1.24 when over 12 h/day; cost = rest of shift lost + deductible $500-2,500 + repair days [design].. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, taxi (robotaxi uses an AV incident rate, R08)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [corrected by fact-check: was medium; design calc: at roughly one crash per 5,000-10,000 driving hours [U] and $2,000-5,000 total cost, expected cost is about $0.2-1 per hour even with the x1.24 multiplier] [S] TLC: +23.8% injury-crash rate above 12 h/day; Dalziel & Job 1997 (Accident Analysis & Prevention, Sydney taxi drivers) [U]: more total break time, fewer accidents. · Data: NYC Open Data Motor Vehicle Collisions (opendata.cityofnewyork.us, h9gi-nx95) [U, blocked here] · Model: Bernoulli draw each tick with hazard = base(zone, hour) x fatigue multiplier; on a crash end the shift and charge costs. · [R09-008]</sub>
   * **Fatigue effect on driving performance**: Tired drivers drive a little slower and make more navigation errors. *Values:* Speed -0 to -5%; route-error rate +0 to +10% at high fatigue [design].. `Low · Changes during run · hidden · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] no driver-level public evidence found. · Data: none found · Model: Multiply travel time by (1 + k x fatigue); add an occasional detour. · [R09-009]</sub>
   * **Fatigue level**: Hidden driver tiredness that grows with hours on duty, time awake and night hours, and falls with breaks and sleep. *Values:* 0-1 scale; about +0.06-0.10 per hour driven; x1.5 between 02:00 and 06:00; a 30 min break recovers about 0.1-0.2 [design].. `Low · Changes during run · hidden · human app driver, taxi, fleet (per driver)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [corrected by fact-check: was medium; its profit effect runs only through crash risk (R09-008) and speed (R09-009), both small, and the legal cap (R09-005) binds first] [S] TLC fatigue-rule presentation: taxi drivers working over 12 h/day had a 23.8% higher injury-crash rate (2014-15 data); Singapore survey links fatigue to more than 10 h/day. · Data: TLC presentation (nyc.gov/assets/tlc/downloads/pdf/presentation_fatigued_driving_rule.pdf) [S]; no public dose-response curve found. · Model: Two-process score (time awake plus circadian dip) updated each tick; feeds crash risk and a small speed penalty. The agent sees only hours worked. · [R09-007]</sub>
* **Breaks, meals and bodily needs**
   * **Breaks taken while waiting in an airport queue**: Meals, prayers and restroom stops taken inside an airport holding lot overlap with queue time instead of adding to it, within the limits of each queue's place-keeping rules. *Values:* Share of queue time usable as break time 0-80% [design]. A 12 h driver needs about 60-90 min of breaks (R09-010). With one 90-min airport queue, overlapping 30-60 min of breaks cuts the queue's opportunity cost by a third or more [design]. App drivers keep their virtual-queue place only while online and inside the lot geofence (R06-042) [S], so walking to facilities outside the lot risks the place. Taxis: a driver may occupy a taxi stand for no more than 1 h on relief time (35 RCNY 80-19(d)(5)). At terminals with holding areas the driver must park in the holding area before taking relief and join the END of the Feed Line on return (80-19(e)(2)) [P*; added by fact-check], so a taxi break can cost queue position.. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi (not robotaxi: no breaks, R09-044); human-driven fleet (break scheduling)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] arithmetic above, mainly for drivers who use airport-queue strategies. [P*] 35 RCNY 80-19(d)(5) and (e)(2) (rule text re-read in a local copy of the jehiah/nyc_code_archive). [S] amNY and NBC NY (2016-17): the Port Authority said app drivers could use restrooms at the JFK central taxi hold, but drivers avoided leaving their lot for fear of losing their place in line; portable toilets were later added to the FHV lot. Food trucks and prayer space at the lots [U]. · Data: none found for usage shares; 35 RCNY 80-19 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive; lot amenities from PANYNJ and the Uber JFK driver page [U] · Model: While the car holds a queue place in a lot with amenities, the agent may take a break that reduces hunger, bathroom and fatigue needs. App queue: the place is kept while the car stays online inside the geofence; a walk to off-lot facilities carries a small chance of a missed dispatch. Taxi queue: per 80-19(e)(2), a break taken from the holding area sends the cab to the end of the feed line if its turn came. Break minutes count toward R09-010 needs. · [G2-002]</sub>
   * **Bathroom need interval**: How often the driver must stop for a toilet. *Values:* Every 2-4 h [design]; shorter with more fluids.. `Low · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Columbia News Service 2023: many NYC drivers urinate in bottles for lack of access; worse for women drivers. · Data: none found · Model: Timer that forces a 5-15 min stop plus search time (R09-012) when it runs out; any break resets it. · [R09-011]</sub>
   * **Parking and ticket cost during breaks**: Cost and risk of tickets when stopping for meals, the bathroom or prayer where parking is scarce. *Values:* NYC parking or standing fines about $65-115 [U]; ticket chance per stop 0-30% by zone [design].. `Low · Rule/price · public/forecast · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Jamiat and RNS reports: parking tickets are routine for NYC drivers stopping to pray; Fortune 2025: drivers ask for placards for brief stops. · Data: NYC Open Data Open Parking and Camera Violations [U]; NYC Department of Finance fine schedule [U] · Model: Ticket probability per break minute by zone x fine; zero at relief stands and airport lots. · [R09-014]</sub>
   * **Time to find a bathroom and a legal place to stop**: Extra minutes to reach a usable restroom and stop legally, which depends on the zone. *Values:* 5-30 min detour [design]; NYC has 109 relief stands but only 42 are open to app drivers (2023) [S]; airport lots have facilities; taxi relief time at a taxi stand at most 1 h, and posted vehicle-type and time limits apply at relief stands (35 RCNY 80-19(d)(5), (7)) [P*].. `Low · World input · public/forecast · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Columbia News Service 2023 (109 and 42); Documented (33 of 74 in an earlier count); Fortune 2025: TLC seeking new relief-stand sites; [P*] 35 RCNY 80-19(d). · Data: TLC/DOT relief-stand list [U]; NYC Parks public restrooms on NYC Open Data [U]; OpenStreetMap amenity=toilets https://www.openstreetmap.org · Model: Per-zone restroom access-time table; breaks in the Manhattan core cost more minutes than at airports or outer-borough gas stations. · [R09-012]</sub>
* **Home, commute and 'take me home'**
   * **Commute deadhead at shift start and end**: Unpaid miles and minutes from home to the first pickup and from the last drop-off back home. *Values:* 0-60 min each way in NYC [design]; idle miles about 30% of all miles (Gridwise 2025); nearly half of miles without a passenger (UCS) [uncertain: neither re-checkable; both national or multi-city, not NYC].. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Gridwise 2025; UCS; TLC voted to cut allowed Manhattan cruising time from 41% to 31% [P*: 35 RCNY 59D-21 sets 36% from 1 Feb 2020 and 31% from 1 Aug 2020 for HVFHV cars in the Congestion Zone, 6 am-11 pm, measured per company; the 41% baseline is S; (U) a New York court annulled the cap in Dec 2019, so enforcement is uncertain although it remains in the rule text]. · Data: TLC trip records (HVFHV trip_miles and trip_time, passenger miles only) https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page; none at driver level · Model: Emerges from routing home to first zone and last zone to home; costed per mile (R08) and per hour of time (R09-023). · [R09-016]</sub>
   * **Destination filter quota and mechanics**: App feature that only offers trips heading toward a chosen destination, with a daily limit; taxis have a legal on-route-home equivalent. *Values:* Uber: 2 uses/day, resets at midnight, a use counts when you arrive [uncertain: when a use is counted]; Lyft: 2-6/day in pilots [uncertain: sources conflict; R06 cites 6/day]; matching is slower while on. Taxi [corrected by fact-check: was 'no filter']: a logged-off driver returning to the garage or home may pick up a passenger whose destination is directly on the route, after recording 'Returning to garage (or home)', and may then ask the destination first (35 RCNY 80-19(a)(5), 80-20(a)(4)(i)) [P*].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver, taxi (garage/home return rule)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Rideshare Guy and RidesharingDriver blogs (Uber 2/day; Lyft cut from 6 to 2 in pilots); drivers report longer waits with the filter on; [P*] 35 RCNY 80-19(a)(5). · Data: 35 RCNY 80-19 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*]; platform help pages only for apps · Model: While active, offer only trips whose drop-off cuts the ETA to the destination by at least X%; decrement the quota on arrival; lower offer rate. Taxi: an on-route-home flag limits street hails to riders going toward the garage or home. · [R09-017]</sub>
   * **Distance and time to home**: Live network distance from the car to home; near shift end it decides whether to accept a trip or use a destination filter. *Values:* 0-40 km; destination-filter uses per day (Uber historically 2/day) [U]; taxi 'returning to garage or home' rule. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Background knowledge of platform destination filters; [P*] 35 RCNY 80-19(a)(5): an off-duty taxi driver heading home or to the garage may take a passenger only if the destination is directly on the route and the trip record says 'Returning to garage (or home)' [corrected by fact-check: added the taxi go-home rule] · Data: Derived from road graph · Model: Recompute each step; 'take me home' mode biases matching toward trips ending near home · [R01-062]</sub>
   * **Driver home location**: Where the driver lives, which sets start and end deadhead and the 'take me home' target. *Values:* NYC app drivers: Queens about 35%, Brooklyn about 24% (TLC, 2018); most of the rest in the Bronx [uncertain: Bronx share and share living outside NYC (NJ, Long Island, Westchester) not checked]; few in Manhattan.. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · core MVP · human app driver, taxi owner-driver, fleet (each car its own home)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Brooklyn Eagle 2018 citing TLC: 35% live in Queens and 24% in Brooklyn. · Data: ACS PUMS (taxi drivers and chauffeurs by residence PUMA) https://www.census.gov/programs-surveys/acs/microdata.html [U]; TLC figures [S] · Model: Assign each driver a home taxi zone drawn from a borough/PUMA distribution; store it as a persona attribute. · [R09-015]</sub>
   * **Hard stop or return deadline**: A time by which the driver must be home or finished (childcare, other job, garage return). *Values:* None, or a fixed time such as 15:00 school pickup, 17:00 taxi handover or 23:00 [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Lyft destination mode has an 'arrive on time' deadline option; taxi day drivers leave Manhattan early to avoid late fees (capped at $25/h, 35 RCNY 58-21 [P*]). · Data: none found · Model: Persona constraint with a per-minute late penalty or forced logout; the agent must plan its last trips toward home. · [R09-018]</sub>
   * **Taxi garage location and late-return fee**: Leased yellow cabs start and end at a garage, usually outside Manhattan. *Values:* Garages mostly in Queens and Brooklyn (R11 notes a cluster in western Queens [U]); late fee at most $25 per hour, capped at one shift's lease (35 RCNY 58-21(c)(5)(iv)) [P*] [corrected by fact-check: was 'per hour [U]'].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Shift-change reporting: most trips are in Manhattan but most garages are in Brooklyn and Queens, so day drivers leave Manhattan early; [P*] 35 RCNY 58-21 late charge and return-to-premises term. · Data: TLC licensed agent and fleet addresses on NYC Open Data [U]; 35 RCNY 58-21 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*] · Model: For the lessee persona the garage zone replaces home; the shift ends at the garage by the handover time; late return accrues up to $25/h. · [R09-019]</sub>
   * **Trips heading toward the driver's home**: How many requests go toward the driver's home area at shift end, for the 'take me home' feature. *Values:* P(match toward home within 15 min) 0.1-0.6 by home zone and hour [design guess]. `Med · World input · public/forecast · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] from team notes; destination filter decision in R09 · Data: TLC OD matrix · Model: Filter demand by heading; harness measures end-of-shift deadhead saved · [R05-060]</sub>
   * **Value of ending near home ('take me home')**: How much the score rewards finishing the shift close to home. *Values:* 0 (pure profit) up to an explicit cost of the final trip home, e.g. home deadhead time valued at $20-30/h plus per-mile cost [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] team notes ask for a 'take me home' feature; Onde vendor page says drivers go offline early to avoid ending far from home [S]. · Data: none (design choice) · Model: Score = profit minus the cost of the final drive home (time at reservation wage + per-mile cost); reported separately. · [R09-020]</sub>
* **Motivation, targets and value of time**
   * **Reservation wage (value of the driver's time)**: What an hour of the driver's time is worth, used to judge whether one more hour is worth working. *Values:* $0 (pure revenue) or $15-35/h varying by hour (higher late night and weekends) [design]; NYC 2018 pay standard was $17.22/h after expenses.. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · all human drivers; fleet scoring`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Chen et al. 2019: reservation wages vary hour to hour and flexibility more than doubles driver surplus; Parrott & Reich 2018: $17.22/h standard (matches background knowledge [U]). · Data: Parrott & Reich 2018 https://irle.berkeley.edu/an-earnings-standard-for-new-york-citys-app-based-drivers [S] · Model: Objective = revenue - costs - sum of hours x w(t). This makes 'when to stop' a real trade-off instead of 'work 24 h'. · [R09-023]</sub>
   * **Daily hours target**: A planned number of hours after which the driver stops, whatever the earnings. *Values:* 4-12 h [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, competitor drivers`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] University of Washington Seattle ride-hail study: time targets affect stopping more than earnings targets; Farber 2005 (JPE 113(1)) tied stopping mainly to hours worked, not income [U; matches background knowledge, not re-checked]. · Data: none at driver level for NYC today (no IDs); 2009-13 FOIL trips; Chicago taxi IDs · Model: Stopping hazard that rises with hours worked; used for competitor supply and as a persona constraint. · [R09-022]</sub>
   * **Daily income target (reference point)**: A daily income goal at which a human-like driver tends to stop. *Values:* None, or $150-350/day, adapting to recent earnings [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, competitor drivers (R06)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Camerer et al. 1997 QJE: wage elasticity of -0.5 to -1 (target earning); Farber 2015 QJE 130(4) on all 2009-13 NYC trips: elasticities mostly positive, only about 1/8 of wage variation unanticipated [uncertain: the 1/8 figure not re-checkable]; Thakral & To 2021 AER: stopping rises with recent earnings. · Data: TLC 2009-2013 trip data (used by Farber and Thakral & To) · Model: Optional prompt variant, or a stopping rule for simulated competitors; also a probe: does the LLM itself stop early on good days? · [R09-021]</sub>
   * **Incentive and quest progress**: Progress toward platform bonuses such as trip-count quests and consecutive-trip bonuses. *Values:* e.g. $X for N trips per day or week (amounts in R07).. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Uber and Lyft run quest and streak bonuses; amounts belong to R07. · Data: none found · Model: Counters that raise the effective pay per trip near thresholds; tests whether the agent works longer to hit a quest. · [R09-025]</sub>
   * **Cumulative earnings and hours today**: Running totals the agent sees during the shift. *Values:* $0-600; 0-14 h.. `Low · Changes during run · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Thakral & To 2021: recent cumulative earnings drive human stopping decisions. · Data: simulator output · Model: Dashboard state in every observation; also input to competitor stopping rules. · [R09-024]</sub>
   * **Payout timing and cash-flow pressure**: Weekly payouts versus paid instant cash-outs, weekly rental due dates and ticket deductions from deposits put pressure on a driver's cash on hand. *Values:* Lyft Express Pay: $0.50 per transfer at launch, raised to $1.25 per The Rideshare Guy (year of change not shown) [S]. An unofficial 2026 fintech guide lists $1.75 and says the fee is waived in New York State [uncertain: not confirmed by Lyft or the TLC]. Uber Instant Pay fee [U]. Weekly payouts are free. Rental is due weekly on a fixed day (R08-033). Taxi owners may deduct parking and red-light camera tickets from the driver's deposit (35 RCNY 58-21(e)(2), via R08-053) [P*].. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, taxi (lease drivers)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] The Rideshare Guy on the $1.25 Lyft Express Pay fee; [S] Joingerald guide (2026, unofficial). The fee itself is small and may be zero in NY, but a persona short of cash works longer and accepts worse trips [design]. · Data: none found (platform help pages only) · Model: An optional persona with a cash balance, due dates and a penalty for missing rent, plus an instant cash-out action with a configurable fee (default 0 for NY, $1.25-1.75 elsewhere). · [G2-012]</sub>
* **Skill, experience and local knowledge**
   * **Experience level**: Lifetime trips and shifts, which raise earnings per hour through better search and timing. *Values:* New (<100 trips) to veteran (>2,500 trips); 0-120+ shifts.. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, competitor drivers`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Cook et al. 2021 REStud: drivers with >2,500 trips earn about 14% more per hour than those with <100 (secondary summary; matches background knowledge [U]); Haggag, McManus & Paci 2017 (AEJ: Applied 9(1)): NYC taxi novices earn about 5% less per hour and catch up in about 100-120 shifts (AEA summary, not re-checked). · Data: Haggag et al. replication data https://openicpsr.org/openicpsr/project/113636/version/V1/view [S, project id not checked]; Cook et al. NBER w24732 · Model: For the LLM, experience is what it learned in the training days (R09-030); for simulated competitors, a search-efficiency multiplier that rises with trips. · [R09-026]</sub>
   * **Zone familiarity (local knowledge)**: Knowledge, per neighbourhood, of where the next fare is likely to be after a drop-off. *Values:* 0-1 per taxi zone (263 zones [P*, zone lookup]) [design].. `Med · Changes during run · hidden · human app driver, taxi, competitor drivers`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Haggag et al. 2017: neighbourhood-specific experience improves search; novices lose most after drop-offs in difficult areas and at slow times. · Data: Haggag et al. replication; TLC taxi zone shapefile · Model: For bots, a zone-level search-time multiplier; for the LLM, it emerges from its memory notes. · [R09-027]</sub>
   * **Driving speed and aggressiveness**: How fast the driver drives relative to traffic, trading time against ticket and crash risk. *Values:* -5% to +5% versus flow speed [design]; NYC speed-camera fine about $50 [U].. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [corrected by fact-check: was medium; the 2.2% speed difference explains about half of a 7% gap, i.e. about 3-4% of hourly earnings, similar to R09-028 (low)] [S] Cook et al. 2021: men drive about 2.2% faster, which explains about half of the 7% gender gap in hourly earnings. · Data: NYC Open Data camera violations [U] · Model: Persona speed factor on link travel times; adds ticket and crash hazards. · [R09-029]</sub>
   * **Navigation skill and route efficiency**: How close the driver's routes are to the fastest route. *Values:* 0.95-1.10 x optimal travel time [design]; app navigation by default.. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Ashkrof et al. 2020 focus groups: drivers name an 'unreliable navigation algorithm' as a drawback. · Data: NYC DOT traffic speeds (R10) [U] · Model: Multiplier on travel time when the agent does not choose a route; the route-choice action (R09-061) overrides it. · [R09-028]</sub>
* **Preferences, life constraints and personal safety**
   * **Avoided areas and hours, and the legality of refusing**: Areas or times a driver persona will not serve, and the penalty where refusing is illegal. *Values:* A list of zones and hours. NYC taxi refusing a destination in NYC, Westchester, Nassau or Newark Airport [corrected by fact-check, 35 RCNY 80-02(e), 80-20, P*]: first offence $350 on a guilty plea or $500 after a hearing; second within 24 months $700 or $1,000 plus possible suspension up to 30 days; third within 36 months licence revocation. After more than 8 h of operation in 24 h a taxi may refuse Newark, Nassau and Westchester (80-20(b)(8)). App drivers may decline before accepting (but see R09-040).. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-20, 80-02(e); [S] NBC New York / amNY: outer-borough refusals are illegal; enforcement by complaints, secret shoppers and GPS checks. · Data: 35 RCNY 80-20 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*]; NYC 311 taxi complaints [U] · Model: Persona blacklist limits repositioning and acceptance; a taxi refusal after a hail draws the tiered fine with complaint probability p. · [R09-032]</sub>
   * **Other job and availability windows**: Times when the driver cannot drive because of another job or commitment. *Values:* e.g. weekdays 09:00-17:00 blocked, weekends free [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber 2014 survey: 75% of part-time drivers had other jobs [uncertain: not re-checked]; Chen et al. 2019: the value of the work lies in flexible timing. · Data: Hall & Krueger 2018 ILR Review [S] · Model: Blocked time windows in the persona; the agent must choose the best remaining hours. · [R09-035]</sub>
   * **Trip-length preference**: Whether the driver favours long trips (airport, out of town) or short quick ones. *Values:* Prefers trips over 45 min and airport runs, or short Manhattan hops [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Seattle stated-preference study: trips over 45 min, surge and higher rider ratings raise acceptance; Ashkrof et al. 2022: pickup time is the strongest negative. · Data: HVFHV trip_miles and trip_time distribution (TLC) [P*, fields verified] · Model: Persona prior for simulated drivers; for the agent it is revealed by accept and decline choices (R09-054). · [R09-033]</sub>
   * **Childcare and family constraints**: Fixed family duties, such as school runs, that split or end the shift. *Values:* e.g. 07:30-08:30 and 15:00-16:00 blocked and the driver must be at home [design].. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Cook et al. 2021: part of the earnings gap comes from where and when people can drive (interpretation). · Data: none found · Model: Like R09-035 but with a location: be in the home zone at time t. · [R09-036]</sub>
   * **Demographic persona traits (age, gender)**: Traits that correlate with when and where people drive and how they handle safety. *Values:* Not used directly; vary behaviour through experience, speed and time-place choices instead.. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden · competitor drivers, fleet personas`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Cook et al. 2021: the 7% male-female hourly gap is explained by experience, speed and where/when people drive; no discrimination by Uber or riders found. · Data: Cook et al. NBER w24732 [S] · Model: Generate heterogeneity among simulated drivers through behaviour parameters, not protected traits (fairness). · [R09-038]</sub>
   * **Language and communication**: The driver's English and other languages, which affect rapport, ratings, tips and special requests. *Values:* English fluent or limited, plus 1-3 other languages; about 92% of NYC licensed drivers born abroad, over 100 languages [uncertain].. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] TLC filings: about 92% of licensees foreign-born; licensing tests in English, Spanish, Bengali and Urdu [uncertain: neither re-checked; 'over 90% immigrants' is widely reported (U)]. Effect on tips is a [design] guess. · Data: TLC language access annual report 2019 (nyc.gov) [S] · Model: Small modifier on rating and tip draws when the rider's language matches. · [R09-034]</sub>
   * **Personal safety risk by zone and time**: Chance of robbery, assault or a dangerous rider, which varies by area, hour and cash handling. *Values:* Rare, about 1e-5 to 1e-4 events per hour [design]; taxi and limo drivers' violent work deaths 18 per 100,000 per year (2003-13) [uncertain].. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver, taxi (cash raises risk)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NIOSH via ISHN: 18 per 100,000 taxi and limo drivers (2003-2013); risk factors are cash, working alone and late night; cameras cut homicide rates about sevenfold [uncertain: rate and camera effect from a trade-press summary, not re-checked against NIOSH papers]. · Data: NYPD Complaint Data on NYC Open Data [U]; BLS Census of Fatal Occupational Injuries https://www.bls.gov/iif/ [S] · Model: Low-probability incident hazard by zone x hour x cash share; an incident ends the shift. It matters mostly through the persona's avoidance choices. · [R09-031]</sub>
   * **Safety tools and suspensions after unfounded complaints**: Dashcams, in-app audio recording and rider ID checks, weighed against the risk that an unfounded rider complaint suspends the account pending review. *Values:* 0-0.5 suspensions per 1,000 trips, each lasting 1-14 days [design]; a recording shortens them [U]. NYC L.L. 52 of 2026 (from 28 Jul 2026) treats restrictions of 72 h or more in a row as deactivations, which need just cause (via R07-045) [P*].. `Low · World input · hidden · human app driver; taxi (TLC complaint process)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] NYC Admin Code 20-1281 to 20-1290 (via R07-045); [U] Uber/Lyft safety feature pages; [S] R09-043: 99% of Uber NYC deactivations are compliance-related. · Data: none found for complaint rates · Model: A rare per-trip suspension event, more likely at night [design], that removes days in multi-day runs; from 28 Jul 2026 NYC suspensions over 72 h follow the just-cause process. A dashcam option costs about $100-300 once [U] and halves the suspension length [design]. · [G2-015]</sub>
* **Reputation and platform standing**
   * **Acceptance and cancellation rates**: Share of offers accepted, and of accepted trips the driver cancels, used for tiers, queues and access. *Values:* Acceptance 0-100% (Uber Pro historically 85% or more); cancellation 0-100% (4% or less for Pro; keep under 10%).. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-22(c)(3): an NYC platform may restrict a driver's access without the usual 72 h notice for excessive cancellation or non-acceptance; [S] Uber JFK driver page: declining several requests in a row or cancelling several rides loses your FIFO queue place; under upfront fares, declines reportedly no longer count (Gridwise). · Data: 35 RCNY 59D-22 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*]; platform thresholds none found · Model: Rolling counters; thresholds change tier, airport queue place and (if enabled) access restriction. · [R09-040]</sub>
   * **Driver rating**: Average of rider ratings, which can trigger warnings, lost perks or deactivation. *Values:* 1-5 stars; average of the last 500 rated trips; city-specific minimum (not published); Uber Pro requires 4.85 or more.. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver (robotaxi: trip ratings for the fleet)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber help text: rating is the average of the 500 most recent rated trips, with warnings near the local minimum (matches background knowledge [U]); Uber Pro criteria (Gridwise, Uber UK). · Data: none found (platform internal) · Model: Each trip draws a rating from the rider model (R05) given wait, route, cleanliness and service; rolling 500-trip mean; thresholds trigger events. · [R09-039]</sub>
   * **Deactivation and compliance risk**: Risk of losing app access through compliance problems, a low rating or rule breaches. *Values:* Uber NYC: 99% of deactivations are compliance-related (e.g. not on the TLC active list, over maximum hours).. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NYC Council transportation committee hearing, 27 Sept 2024 (citymeetings.nyc summary). Rare but ends earnings entirely. · Data: NYC Council hearing record [S] · Model: Terminal event if hour caps or the rating minimum are breached repeatedly; ends the run with a large penalty. · [R09-043]</sub>
   * **Loyalty tier (e.g. Uber Pro)**: Status level earned from points and quality metrics that unlocks perks. *Values:* Blue, Gold, Platinum, Diamond; point thresholds vary by city (UK example: 600, 1,100, 1,600).. `Low · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber Pro UK page; Uber blog: the new Uber Pro lowers Gold criteria and adds a Driving Insights score. · Data: Uber Pro programme terms (uber.com/legal) [S] · Model: Points per trip with peak multipliers; perks modelled as small pay or priority bonuses. · [R09-041]</sub>

### 14. Agent decisions (31)
*Everything the model can choose, in one action space.*

* **Where to be: wait, cruise, park or reposition**
   * **Reposition to a target zone**: Drive empty to another zone expected to be busier. *Values:* Any of 263 taxi zones [P*]; 5-45 min of travel.. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · core MVP · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Lu, Frazier & Kislev 2018 (ACM EC): seeing the surge heat map changes where drivers go and what they earn; Ashkrof et al. 2022 relocation study. · Data: HVFHV request_datetime by zone (demand) [P*, field verified]; TLC taxi zone shapefile · Model: Action reposition(zone); travel time from R10; offers can arrive on the way. · [R09-053]</sub>
   * **Wait, cruise or park**: After a drop-off, stay put, cruise for street hails, or park at a staging spot to wait for app offers. *Values:* Wait in place; cruise a loop; park at a chosen spot.. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · core MVP · all (cruising matters most for taxis)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Buchholz 2022 REStud 89(2): NYC taxis compete by choosing where to search; search frictions leave about 53,000 customers per weekday unserved [uncertain: the 53,000 figure not re-checkable]. · Data: TLC yellow and green trip records (pickup density by zone and hour) · Model: Action on each idle tick; cruising costs fuel and raises the street-hail encounter rate; parking costs time and may risk a ticket. · [R09-052]</sub>
   * **Chase surge or not**: Drive toward an area showing surge pricing. *Values:* Chase or ignore; surges often last under 10 min (2015).. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Chen, Mislove & Wilson 2015 (SF and Manhattan): most surges last under 10 min and often end before drivers arrive; Uber says surge does attract drivers. · Data: none public for surge maps (R07) · Model: Surge map in the observation with a short half-life; arriving after it ends pays normal fares. · [R09-062]</sub>
   * **Cruising route for street hails**: For a taxi, which streets to drive while empty, trading distance (fuel, time) against passing more pedestrians. *Values:* Route through high-hail edges vs waiting at a stand; cruise speed in traffic. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · taxi; robotaxi with street hail (none today)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; Schaller (2017): yellow taxis average 8 min empty between trips in the Manhattan CBD [S] [uncertain: figure not found in the checked excerpts]; [P*] 34 RCNY 4-11(b): only taxis may cruise for passengers · Data: Pre-2016 TLC yellow coordinates for calibration [U] · Model: Agent picks the next edge or a loop; hails generated only on traversed edges · [R01-041]</sub>
   * **Hail-search cruising speed (taxi)**: Speed and pattern at which a taxi cruises empty looking for street hails, trading hail encounters against fuel and time. *Values:* 5-15 mph [design]. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] NYC street-hail supply and demand studies exist (arXiv 1909.12861) [S]; 34 RCNY 4-11(b) bars vehicles other than taxis from cruising for passengers [P*] · Data: TLC yellow and green trip records (pickup zones as hail density) · Model: Hail arrivals per edge-minute (R05) times time spent on each edge; slower cruising raises exposure per block. · [R10-052]</sub>
   * **Pre-positioning ahead of forecast weather**: Moving one car or a fleet before rain or a storm arrives toward zones where demand will jump, or home before a ban. *Values:* Lead 15-120 min; share of a fleet staged near hubs and nightlife before forecast rain [design]. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · all; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; value depends on onset dynamics (R04-003) and information level (R04-049) · Data: none (agent decision) · Model: Ordinary repositioning actions; the benefit comes from forecast information and onset dynamics. · [R04-053]</sub>
* **Which work: offers, ride types, apps and delivery**
   * **Accept or decline an offer**: Take or skip an app trip offer given pickup time, pay, destination and rider rating. *Values:* Accept or decline within about 15 s [U]; offer shows pickup ETA, pay and destination (upfront fares).. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · core MVP · human app driver, taxi e-hail, robotaxi dispatch`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Ashkrof et al. 2022: pickup time is the strongest negative factor; surge is valued at $0.50-0.71 per minute of pickup [uncertain: not re-checked]; Uber's 2022 upfront fares show pay and destination before accepting. · Data: HVFHV request-to-pickup gaps (TLC; request_datetime, on_scene_datetime, pickup_datetime [P*]) · Model: Offer event with a timeout; a decline passes the offer to the next driver and updates the acceptance rate (R09-040). · [R09-054]</sub>
   * **Cancel after accepting, and no-show wait**: Cancel an accepted trip, or decide how long to wait for a rider who does not appear. *Values:* Wait 2-5+ min before a no-show cancel; fees per R07. Taxi: not serving an accepted e-hail counts as a refusal (35 RCNY 80-20(a)(5)) [P*].. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver, taxi e-hail, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber Pro cancellation limit of 4%; Uber JFK page: cancelling several rides loses your queue place; [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-22(c)(3) allows NYC access restriction for excessive cancellation. · Data: none found (public TLC files hold no cancellations) · Model: Actions cancel_trip and wait_for_rider(max_min); rider arrival time drawn from R05; no-show fee paid after the threshold. · [R09-055]</sub>
   * **Platform choice and multi-apping**: Which apps to be online on, and which offer to take when several arrive. *Values:* Uber, Lyft, both, or taxi with e-hail apps.. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver, taxi (e-hail), fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Tyler Technologies on TLC data: a rising share of drivers work for 2-3 platforms [uncertain: not re-checked; R06 uses Gridwise's national panel: about 55% of Lyft drivers also on Uber and 25% of Uber drivers also on Lyft, Q1 2024 (S)]; 2018 press: drivers switch apps to get around one app's hour cap. · Data: TLC HVFHV (hvfhs_license_num splits Uber HV0003 and Lyft HV0005) [P*, verified in schema] · Model: Agent toggles apps; each platform has its own offer stream, rules and counters; one trip at a time. · [R09-056]</sub>
   * **App controls: pause, last trip, auto-accept, filter to any place**: Driver-app controls beyond accept and decline: pausing or going offline, blocking back-to-back requests before the last trip, and auto-accept where offered. Aiming the destination filter at any place (for example JFK) is covered by R09-017 and R06-028. *Values:* Actions: pause/offline; a 'stop new requests' or 'last ride' flag; auto-accept on/off (availability varies by market [U]); destination-filter target = any zone, with Uber's 2 uses/day (R09-017). Back-to-back requests are on by default and cannot be switched off. Uber's pages show a stop-requests (hand) button in the delivery app, and for rides they say to tap 'Go Offline' in the Trip Planner.. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver; fleet of app cars`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber help pages on back-to-back trips via snippets (uber.com unreachable). Delivery variants are in English; the ride variant was seen only in Russian. [corrected: going offline is not the only documented way to stop requests]. Forum reports of a ride-side 'stop new requests' button [S, anecdotal]. Auto-accept is [U]. · Data: Uber back-to-back help pages: delivery https://www.uber.com/drive/delivery/basics/back-to-back-trips/ ; ride variant e.g. https://www.uber.com/lt/ru/drive/basics/back-to-back-trips/ [uncertain: a US English ride page was not confirmed]; none found for usage rates · Model: Action flags in the agent API. Forward dispatch (R06-023) skips agents with the last-trip flag set. The filter target accepts any zone (mechanics in R09-017). · [G1-011]</sub>
   * **Car-based delivery as a fallback**: Switching to food or package delivery apps during dead hours or lockouts, an outside option some NYC drivers use. *Values:* NYC minimum pay for app delivery workers (restaurant delivery since 2023, grocery added in 2026): $21.44/h from 1 Apr 2025 and $22.13/h from the first pay period on or after 1 Apr 2026 (a 3.2% inflation adjustment), before tips [S]. Whether the rate is per hour of trip time or of all logged-in time [uncertain: rule text not read]. The worker pays car costs [U]. Robotaxi analogue: Waymo with Uber Eats in Phoenix (earlier pilot) [U], and with DoorDash in Phoenix (multi-year pilot announced Oct 2025, about 315 sq mi, orders left in the trunk) [S].. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver; robotaxi fleet (idle cars); not taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] NYC DCWP minimum pay pages and the 2026 rate announcement; [S] TechCrunch (16 Oct 2025) on Waymo-DoorDash. Gives dead hours and lockouts (R06-054) a realistic outside option [design]. · Data: NYC DCWP Delivery Workers https://www.nyc.gov/site/dca/workers/workersrights/Delivery-Workers.page · Model: Action switch_to_delivery(hours) pays a fallback hourly rate times a utilisation factor, minus car costs. The car takes no rides while switched. · [G2-013]</sub>
   * **Ride-type opt-ins**: Opt in or out of shared rides, wheelchair-accessible requests, scheduled rides or larger and premium tiers. *Values:* On or off per product; WAV needs an accessible vehicle.. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] HVFHV files carry shared_request_flag, shared_match_flag, wav_request_flag, wav_match_flag and access_a_ride_flag (schema in toddwschneider/nyc-taxi-data; re-verified). · Data: TLC HVFHV trip records · Model: Product filters on the offer stream; opting out lowers the offer rate. · [R09-066]</sub>
   * **Street-hail choices and the off-duty light (taxi)**: Which hailing passengers to stop for and when to switch on the off-duty light, within anti-refusal rules. *Values:* Stop or pass; off-duty on or off. To refuse legally a taxi must be ending its shift or going for repairs, logged off with both rear doors locked (35 RCNY 80-20(b)(5)-(6)) [P*]; while returning to the garage or home it may take only riders going directly along its route (80-19(a)(5)) [P*].. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 80-20 and 80-02(e): refusing in-city destinations is illegal (fines $350-1,000, revocation on a third offence within 36 months); [S] enforcement through complaints and secret shoppers. · Data: 35 RCNY 80-19, 80-20 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*]; NYC 311 taxi complaints [U]; yellow and green trip records · Model: Hail encounters while cruising; passing a hailer while on duty may draw a refusal fine with low probability; an on-route-home mode filters hails by direction. · [R09-063]</sub>
* **Airports, stands and venues: join, stay or leave**
   * **Airport queue: join, stay or leave**: Whether to drive to JFK, LGA or EWR and wait in the queue for a long airport fare. *Values:* Queue wait 0-3+ h (JFK taxi hold lot historically 2-3 h); Uber runs a virtual FIFO queue in staging lots; lot caps vary by demand; short-trip priority passes. Taxis may not cruise for fares at JFK, LGA or EWR and must use the feed line; a taxi driver taking relief time must park in the holding area and rejoin the end of the line (35 RCNY 80-19(a)(4), (e)(2)) [P*].. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Queens DA case and press: JFK taxi hold-lot waits average 2-3 h; Uber JFK driver page: virtual FIFO, and you lose your place if you go offline, leave the zone, or decline or cancel repeatedly; [P*] 35 RCNY 80-19(e). · Data: TLC trip records airport_fee and zones 1 (EWR), 132 (JFK), 138 (LGA) [P*, verified in zone lookup and schema]; Uber JFK driver info https://www.uber.com/global/en/r/airports/jfk/driver-information/ [S] · Model: A queue per airport fed by flight arrivals (R03); the agent sees its position and expected wait (hidden in a hard mode); leaving for a break sends a taxi to the back of the line. · [R09-057]</sub>
   * **Stage at a venue or leave (agent decision)**: Whether to wait near a venue for the let-out, and when to arrive, given the dead time during the event. *Values:* Arrive 0-45 min before the expected end; idle cost = time + fuel; base demand at outer venues near zero (zone 93: ~12 yellow pickups a day without events). `High · Agent decision · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc: let-out pay per engaged hour +19-27% (HVFHV zones 186 and 230), but outer venue zones are empty before the end; fleet version is R11-025 · Data: n/a · Model: Move and wait actions; the payoff emerges from demand, surge and rivals · [R03-057]</sub>
   * **Join a venue taxi stand or work the hail blocks (taxi decision)**: Whether a taxi joins the stand queue at a venue or hub at let-out, cruises nearby blocks for street hails, or leaves; the fleet version (how many of N cars to stage) is R11-025. *Values:* Queue position 0-60; expected wait = position / hail rate; hail rate peaks 15-30 min after the end [design]. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · taxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc: zone 186 yellow pickups 22:00-24:00 range 142-581 a night, so the stand payoff varies about 2x between the 10th- and 90th-percentile nights and 4x from the quietest to the busiest [corrected by fact-check: originally '3-4x between nights']; [design] · Data: TLC yellow trip records · Model: Stand queue from R03-026; actions join, cruise or leave; reward emerges from the hail process · [R03-059]</sub>
* **When to work, rest, refuel and go home**
   * **Decision to work through severe weather**: The agent's choice to start, continue or stop driving when a storm hits, trading higher utilisation and surge against delay, crash and damage risk. *Values:* Work / pause / go home, at any time, triggered by alerts or observed conditions [design]; 29 Sep 2023: trips +17% but fare per engaged hour $55-57 vs $68 in the 08:00-09:00 peak [P]. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Our TLC event study shows the trade-off [P]; decision design [design] · Data: none (agent decision) · Model: Action space includes go-offline and return-home at any time; payoff emerges from the simulator. · [R04-047]</sub>
   * **Shift end and total hours (daily, weekly)**: How long the driver works each day and how many days each week. *Values:* App: 1-12 h/day within caps; NYC full-time about 40-60 h/week; taxi lease shift 12 h ([P*] 35 RCNY 58-21(i)(1)(ii); owners may offer pro-rated shorter shifts, 58-21(c)(1)(vii)); 4-7 days/week.. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet (per car)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Parrott (presentation drawing on the 2018 TLC earnings study): 60-65% of NYC app drivers are full-time with driving as their only job; Hall & Krueger 2015 draft [corrected by fact-check: about half (51%) of US Uber drivers drove 1-15 h/week in late 2014, pooled across markets, with NYC drivers working longer; not 'most outside NYC' (U)]. · Data: TLC Data Hub driver fare and earnings (added Jan 2023 [uncertain: date not checked], via nyc.gov/tlc); TLC 2009-2013 FOIL trips; Chicago taxi IDs. · Model: Agent calls go_offline; harness tracks daily and weekly hours and reports profit per hour and per day. · [R09-002]</sub>
   * **Shift start time and place**: When and where the driver goes online for the day. *Values:* App driver: any time 00:00-23:59, usually from the home zone; taxi lessee: fixed by lease (day shift about 05:00, night shift about 17:00).. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver, taxi (constrained by lease), fleet (per car)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Chen, Chevalier, Rossi & Oehlsen 2019 (JPE 127(6)): drivers' reservation wages vary hour by hour, and forced inflexible schedules would cut their hours by more than two-thirds [uncertain: not re-checkable; the paper's headline is that drivers earn more than twice the surplus they would under less flexible arrangements]; NYC 2011 GPS study (DNAinfo, TLC figures): active cabs fall about 20% at the 4-5 pm shift change. · Data: TLC 2009-2013 trip data with hashed hack licence (2013 FOIL release; archived TLC trips at ICPSR 37254, https://www.icpsr.umich.edu/web/ICPSR/studies/37254 [uncertain: whether ICPSR keeps the hashed IDs]; alternative [U]: Donovan & Work, NYC Taxi Trip Data 2010-2013, Illinois Data Bank, https://doi.org/10.13012/J8PN93H8, with anonymised medallion and hack-licence IDs); Chicago Taxi Trips with a stable Taxi ID (https://data.cityofchicago.org, wrvz-psew). Current TLC public files carry no driver ID (yellow, HVFHV [P*]; green [P]). · Model: Action go_online(time, zone); the taxi persona is restricted to its lease window; the start zone defaults to home. · [R09-001]</sub>
   * **Go home and use the destination filter**: Decide when to head home and whether to spend a destination-filter use (app) or go off duty on route home (taxi) to earn on the way. *Values:* 0-2 filter uses per day (Uber); start time of the home run; taxi on-route-home pickups (35 RCNY 80-19(a)(5)) [P*].. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · core MVP · human app driver, taxi, fleet (each car)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber allows 2 filter uses per day; drivers report slower matching with the filter on; [P*] taxi garage/home return rule. · Data: none found (apps); 35 RCNY 80-19 [P*] (taxi) · Model: Action set_destination(zone); the matcher restricts offers until home is reached. · [R09-060]</sub>
   * **Refuel or charge (when and where)**: Choose when and where to refuel or charge, trading downtime against range and price. *Values:* Gas 5-10 min; EV fast charge 20-45 min; home charging overnight (details R08).. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] E-taxi trajectory studies note drivers switch GPS off during breaks such as lunch near chargers; details in R08. · Data: AFDC stations https://afdc.energy.gov/stations ; EIA fuel prices (R08) · Model: Action refuel_or_charge(station); combining it with a meal break saves time. · [R09-059]</sub>
   * **Take a break (when, what, where)**: Choose the timing, type (meal, bathroom, prayer, rest) and place of breaks. *Values:* 5-60 min; any zone.. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, fleet (stagger breaks across cars)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Meal peaks and relief-stand scarcity (R09-010 to R09-014); [design] breaks placed in demand troughs cost little. · Data: see R09-010 to R09-014 · Model: Action take_break(type, minutes, zone); resets the matching timers. · [R09-058]</sub>
* **Route choice and driving**
   * **Avoid or work around closures (agent decision)**: Choosing to avoid parade, protest or closure areas, or to work their edges where displaced demand spills. *Values:* Per zone-hour choice; spill-over demand at closure edges [design]. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc: pickups fall 34-66% inside parade and UNGA zones while citywide trips fall only 5-11% · Data: n/a · Model: Closures appear in observations when the information level allows · [R03-058]</sub>
   * **Free vs tolled crossing choice**: Choice between toll-free East River bridges and tolled tunnels or bridges, trading time for money. *Values:* free: Queensboro, Williamsburg, Manhattan, Brooklyn bridges; tolled: Queens-Midtown and Hugh Carey tunnels, RFK, Verrazzano, Hudson crossings [U]; toll amounts in R07. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] whether the rider or driver pays the toll decides if avoidance pays; see R07 · Data: MTA Bridges & Tunnels and PANYNJ toll schedules (R07); OSM toll=yes · Model: Route options with generalised cost = time x value of time + toll x payer share; agent picks a route or a preference. · [R10-021]</sub>
   * **Routing objective**: What the route planner minimises: fastest time, shortest distance, toll-free, highway-free or a risk-adjusted time. *Values:* fastest / shortest / avoid tolls / avoid highways / P90 time. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: OSRM car profile supports routability, duration and distance weights [P] · Data: none needed · Model: Expose k alternative routes with time, distance and toll and let the agent choose, or fix a policy per prompt variant. · [R10-042]</sub>
   * **Route choice**: Pick the route for a trip or reposition: fastest, shortest, or avoiding tolls and the CBD charge. *Values:* App route or 2-3 alternatives; taxis must take the 'shortest reasonable route' unless the passenger asks otherwise (35 RCNY 80-16(a)) [P*], so detours on metered trips are illegal.. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] MIT study: metered taxi airport trips about 7.4% longer than optimal; Tang 2018: detours when paid by distance; 2010 TLC case of 1.8 million overcharged trips [corrected by fact-check: that case was meter-rate fraud (drivers set the higher out-of-city rate code), not route detours (U)]. · Data: NYC DOT traffic speeds; OpenStreetMap (R10); 35 RCNY 80-16 [P*] · Model: Offer 2-3 route options with ETA, tolls and distance; detours on metered trips raise the fare but risk complaints, fines and lower ratings. · [R09-061]</sub>
* **Information gathering and memory**
   * **Write and use memory notes**: What the agent records about hotspots, timing and mistakes for later days. *Values:* Notes per day within a size budget.. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] 10-day learning protocol; human analogue is learning by driving [S Haggag et al.]. High for the learning evaluation. · Data: none (design) · Model: write_note tool; notes are injected the next day; measure the gain on day 11. · [R09-068]</sub>
   * **Information gathering**: Whether to spend steps or tool calls checking events, weather, news and traffic before deciding. *Values:* None, event calendar, weather forecast, news feed; a tool-call budget.. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] team plan to test with and without public event information. · Data: Event calendars, NOAA forecasts (R03, R04, R12) · Model: Tools that cost simulated minutes or tokens; the harness logs their use. · [R09-067]</sub>
* **Platform nudges and rider service**
   * **Follow or ignore platform nudges**: Whether to follow app heat maps, 'go here' prompts or demand forecasts. *Values:* Follow, ignore, or follow in part.. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Ashkrof et al. 2020: drivers name 'misleading guidance' as a main drawback; Lu et al. 2018: the heat map changes relocation. · Data: none public · Model: Platform nudge in the observation with configurable accuracy and bias (the platform wants supply, not driver profit). · [R09-070]</sub>
   * **Rate or block riders**: Rate riders and optionally avoid being matched with them again. *Values:* 1-5 stars; a low rating blocks future matches [U].. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Uber lets drivers avoid re-matching with riders they rate low; CBS: Uber planned to ban low-rated riders [S]. · Data: none found · Model: Optional; matters mostly for repeat riders in long runs. · [R09-065]</sub>
   * **Rider interaction and service style**: How the agent responds to riders: greeting, small talk, help with bags, music, route requests, complaints. *Values:* Free-text or menu replies [design].. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees · all (robotaxi through remote support or in-car chat)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] tips and ratings depend on service (R05). · Data: none found · Model: Scripted rider messages; replies scored by a rubric that shifts rating and tip draws slightly. · [R09-064]</sub>

### 15. Fleet (60)
*Level 2: one model running N cars, including positioning strategy, charging, depots and market impact.*

* **Fleet size, mix and starting state**
   * **Fleet size N**: Number of cars the model controls in a fleet run. *Values:* 1 (single-car level), 5, 10, 25, 100, 500; stretch 1,000-5,000. Anchors: a Manhattan robotaxi sim needed ~3,000 cars to serve ~90% and ~5,000 to serve ~100% of ~150k Manhattan-internal weekday app requests (greedy dispatch); Waymo's whole US fleet is ~3,000-3,800 cars (R06-056) [uncertain: ~3,000 in early 2026 is Waymo's own figure (Vital City methodology, S); the 3,800 upper figure (Jalopnik, May 2026 recall) was not re-checked].. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: fleetsim greedy sweep of 1,500-6,000 cars on June 2024 HVFHV trips: unserved demand 47.7% to 0%, utilisation 57% to 28% [S, GitHub README read directly]. · Data: Harness setting; demand scale from NYC TLC HVFHV trip records https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Harness parameter; run a ladder of N on the same seed and demand; report profit per fleet and per car. · [R11-001]</sub>
