   * **Vehicle choice and fleet mix**: At fleet level, which vehicles to buy or rent (EV vs hybrid, WAV, XL, premium) and how many spares, within licence rules and budget. *Values:* Mix of vehicle specs; capex budget; licence-eligible types (R08-041); Green Rides ZEV-or-WAV trip share (25% in 2026, 100% by 2030, 35 RCNY 59D-13(c)) [P*]; taxi hack-ups accessible-only since Oct 2024 (67-06(a)) [P*]; spare ratio [design].. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] energy cost spread about $0.07-0.26/mi (corrected by fact-check from $0.08-0.18, using EPA's 0.44 kWh/mi for an I-PACE-class car and depot vs public DC fast prices) plus capex from about $30k to $200k per car. · Data: EPA specs [U] (2019-2020 via GitHub copy [P*]); TLC licence rules (R08-041) [P*] · Model: Procurement action at scenario start (or monthly) choosing specs; costs flow to the ledger (fleet decisions in R11). · [R08-069]</sub>
   * **Active fleet by hour (dynamic fleet sizing)**: How many of the N cars the model keeps on duty each hour, while the rest wait at a depot, charge or rest. *Values:* 0-N per hour; e.g. 100% at 7-10 am and 5-9 pm, 30-50% at 3-5 am [design].. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · fleet (robotaxi and human fleets)`
      * <sub>Evidence: FleetPy ships time-based and utilisation-based dynamic fleet sizing (target utilisation band, minimum active fleet; inactive cars go to the nearest depot and charge there) [P, code read on GitHub]. · Data: Hourly demand curve from TLC trip records (R02); FleetPy reference code https://github.com/TUM-VT/FleetPy · Model: Action set_active(k, hour); deactivated cars drive to a depot; on-duty costs (remote assistance, wear, wages) accrue only while active. · [R11-002]</sub>
   * **Driver-type mix in the fleet**: Whether fleet cars are driverless or driven by employees, contractors or lessees, each with its own rules and costs. *Values:* 100% robotaxi / 100% human / mixed, e.g. robotaxis inside a geofence plus human cars for airports and freeways.. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Waymo freeway and airport limits (R06-058, R10-023) and Uber dispatching Waymo cars beside human drivers in Austin and Atlanta (R06-059) [S]. · Data: none (scenario setting) · Model: Per-car flag selects a rule set: robotaxi (geofence, no breaks, remote assistance) or human (shifts, breaks, compliance R11-018). · [R11-004]</sub>
   * **Starting positions**: Where the fleet's cars are when the run starts. *Values:* all at one depot / spread over several depots / proportional to historical pickups at the start hour / random / all in one zone (stress test).. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; at about 1 empty mile per trip in Manhattan (fleetsim) a bad start costs each car a few empty trips; matters for the first 1-3 hours, little over several days. · Data: TLC trip records for the historical pickup distribution · Model: Harness option with a fixed seed; give every model the same start or report scores per start mode. · [R11-005]</sub>
   * **Fleet growth between days**: Whether the model may add or retire cars between days, with a lead time and a capital or lease cost. *Values:* fixed N (default) / plus or minus 10% per week, 1-7 day delivery, lease cost per car-day [design].. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; rival supply re-equilibrates over about two months after pay changes (Hall et al., R06-012) [S]. · Data: none · Model: Off in v1; in multi-day campaigns, a daily 'order or return cars' action with cost and delay. · [R11-007]</sub>
   * **Starting energy and condition**: Battery or fuel level, cleanliness and maintenance clock of each car at the start. *Values:* all 100%; uniform 40-100%; 'morning after an overnight charge' 90-100% [design].. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; energy binds within a single day: with no charging policy 4-12% of demand goes unserved (fleetsim) [S]. · Data: none · Model: Initial state vector per car drawn from the chosen profile with a fixed seed. · [R11-006]</sub>
* **Control architecture and dispatch authority**
   * **Control architecture**: How the model is wired to the cars: one central fleet brain, one model instance per car, a hierarchy where the model sets targets and an algorithm executes, or a policy the model writes as code. *Values:* centralised / decentralised per car / hierarchical (model every 5-15 min, algorithm every few seconds) / policy-as-code.. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; LLM-E-AMoD had ChatGPT and Gemini write MPC controllers for a 250-EV rebalancing and charging problem rather than decide each step [S, GitHub README, re-read by fact-check: a synthetic 20-node, 10 x 10 km MATLAB network, not NYC]; LLM4RD uses an LLM to design reward functions for multi-agent (MAPPO) EV ride-hailing scheduling, default 1,200 vehicles [S, README read directly by fact-check; was a GitHub listing]. · Data: n/a; precedent https://github.com/fviadero/LLM-E-AMoD · Model: Harness modes over one simulator API; v1 hierarchical so that model cost scales with time, not with N. · [R11-009]</sub>
   * **Dispatch authority and action set**: Who assigns requests to the fleet's cars, and so what the model may do: only reposition and accept, assign every request itself, or choose which own car takes a platform offer. *Values:* platform-dispatched (Uber/Lyft fleet owner under an NYC base) / self-dispatched (robotaxi operator app, taxi e-hail) / platform offers, operator assigns (Uber-Waymo Austin model).. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet (human app, taxi, robotaxi)`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber matches riders to Waymo cars in Austin and Atlanta while a partner runs fleet operations (R06-059) [S]; the matcher-side flag is R06-063. · Data: n/a · Model: Action schema per mode; in self-dispatch the model or its algorithm calls assign(request, car) inside the batch window (R11-011). · [R11-010]</sub>
   * **Fleet control interface**: How an LLM controls N cars: one call per car, one dispatcher call for all cars, or the model sets parameters that a solver executes. *Values:* Per-car agents / central dispatcher every 1-5 sim-min / policy-parameter mode (zone targets, acceptance thresholds) run by a matcher. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] LPSim README advertises 'closed-loop LLM-driven fleet optimization' over a structured state API (verified); [design] per-car calls multiply cost by N (cross-link R11). · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Fleet tool with batched assignments and rebalancing orders, validated by the fleet manager. · [R12-030]</sub>
   * **Strength of the fallback autopilot between model calls**: How good the built-in dispatcher, rebalancer and charging policy is that drives the cars between model decisions and after the token budget runs out. *Values:* Levels: none (cars idle) / naive (nearest dispatch, no rebalancing) / standard (batched dispatch, proportional rebalancing, threshold charging) / strong (MPC or LP baseline). Fixed, published, and scored as its own baseline [design].. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet; robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]. [S, from list] fleetsim: once a coded matcher dispatches, strategic repositioning moved median wait only about 8% (R11-012), so the default policy decides most of the outcome. · Data: FleetPy https://github.com/TUM-VT/FleetPy ; AMoDeus https://github.com/amodeus-science/amodeus (R11-027) · Model: A fixed default policy per level. Score = model fleet profit minus autopilot-only profit at the same level; run at least two levels. · [G1-046]</sub>
   * **Dispatch batching window**: How long requests are pooled before the fleet assigns them to cars. *Values:* 0 s (greedy, first come) / 10-60 s / up to 300 s.. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · fleet (self-dispatched robotaxi, taxi e-hail)`
      * <sub>Evidence: fleetsim at 3,000 cars: a 30 s batch cut deadhead 8.2% and median wait 5.3% vs greedy; 300 s cut deadhead 14.8% but raised wait [S]; Uber batches over a few seconds (R06-020) [S]. · Data: n/a · Model: Matcher parameter; min-cost bipartite assignment per window; under platform dispatch it is a fixed rule. · [R11-011]</sub>
   * **Human driver compliance with fleet instructions**: Chance that a human driver follows the fleet's repositioning, charging or break instruction. *Values:* robotaxi 100%; employee drivers 80-95%; contractors 20-60% [design].. `Med · World input · hidden · human app driver fleet, taxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] NYC app drivers are independent contractors who choose where to drive; [U] Revel ran an all-EV ride-hail fleet with employee drivers in NYC [uncertain: not re-checkable this session, including whether Revel still runs ride-hail in NYC]. · Data: none found · Model: Bernoulli draw per instruction and driver, lower late in the shift and for targets far from home (R09-015). · [R11-018]</sub>
   * **Information sharing between own cars**: In decentralised mode, what one car's agent can see of the others. *Values:* none / shared heat map / shared notes file / direct messages.. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: AMoDeus lists rebalancing policies 'without explicit communication' (Arsie et al. 2009) as their own class [P, README]; sharing between rival fleets is R06-065. · Data: n/a · Model: Harness toggle; log messages; measure clustering (R11-028) with and without sharing. · [R11-016]</sub>
   * **Intra-fleet dispatch rule**: Whether the platform matches each fleet car on its own (nearest wins) or lets the fleet operator pick which of its cars takes a request. *Values:* Independent | operator assigns | operator assigns with veto.. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; Uber dispatches the Waymo fleet on its platform [S]. · Data: n/a · Model: Matcher flag; operator-assign adds an internal batching step for the fleet. · [R06-063]</sub>
   * **Own-fleet state visibility**: What the model knows about its own cars at each call. *Values:* full telemetry (position, status, battery, ETA, cleanliness) for robotaxis / delayed or partial for human-driven cars / nothing in real time (classic taxi garage).. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; robotaxi fleets stream telemetry to remote operations (R09-048, R09-049) [S]; taxi garages see cars only at shift change [corrected by fact-check: NYC taxi technology systems must record each logged-on cab's position at least every 30 s (35 RCNY 66-03(h), P*), so the data exist; whether owners or garages can see it live was not confirmed]. · Data: n/a · Model: Observation builder with per-field delay and noise by driver type. · [R11-015]</sub>
   * **Strategic decision epoch**: How often the model is asked to re-plan repositioning, charging and the active fleet. *Values:* every 1, 5, 15, 30 or 60 sim minutes, plus event triggers (surge, failure, let-out).. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet (also single car)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [corrected by fact-check: impact was high; no evidence supports high, and fleetsim found the strategic repositioning layer moved median wait only about 8% once a coded matcher dispatches, so the epoch mainly sets token cost (R11-014)]; offpeak repositions idle cars every 15 min [S]; gnn-rl-for-amod runs rebalancing on a coarse time step (json_tstep, default 3; the command-line flag is spelled --json_tsetp in main.py) [P, code]; [design] shorter epochs react faster but cost more tokens. · Data: n/a · Model: Harness clock calls the model every k minutes and on flagged events; a default policy runs in between. · [R11-012]</sub>
   * **Fleet-wide learning of closures and hazards**: When one fleet car meets an unmapped closure or hazard, the map update reaches all cars, so only the first car pays the delay. *Values:* Propagation delay 0-30 min; 50-100% of later encounters avoided [design].. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees · robotaxi; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; [U] operators describe pushing map updates fleet-wide. · Data: none found · Model: Shared closure memory in the fleet map. The R03-055 delay applies only to cars that meet the closure before the update. Compare N=1 with N=100. · [G1-052]</sub>
* **Repositioning and rebalancing**
   * **Idle-car repositioning decision**: Where each idle fleet car goes after a drop-off: stay, cruise, or drive empty to a target zone. *Values:* any of 263 taxi zones; 0-30 min of empty driving; per car or as a zone quota.. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: fleetsim at 5,000 cars: forecast-driven repositioning cut median wait 8.0% and p90 wait 12.5% [S]; the single-car version is R09-052/053. · Data: TLC HVFHV request times by zone; TLC taxi zone shapefile · Model: Action reposition(car or k cars, zone); travel time from R10; cars can be matched en route. · [R11-019]</sub>
   * **Operator-chosen service area and request admission control**: Inside the legal operating area, which zones, hours and trip types the fleet chooses to serve, such as a smaller area that keeps cars dense, refusing trips that strand cars, or capping trip length. *Values:* Actions set_service_area(zones, hours) and reject_if(trip_km > x or destination value < y) [design], bounded by coverage rules (R11-058) and, on human platforms, refusal rules (R07-042).. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · robotaxi fleet; human fleet with its own base`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Cruise's first paid driverless rides in SF (June 2022) ran only from about 10 pm to 6 am on selected streets, with up to 30 cars, before expanding citywide in 2023 [upgraded by fact-check from U]. Waymo launches each city on a subset and then expands [U]. [S] Vazifeh et al. 2018 (Nature 557) on fleet size vs service level. · Data: NYC TLC trip records (to value zones); Waymo and Cruise service-area announcements [S] · Model: Fleet-level masks on which requests the fleet will quote. Unquoted requests go to competitors, and the score includes coverage penalties. · [G2-037]</sub>
   * **Airport allocation across the fleet**: How many fleet cars to commit to the JFK and LaGuardia queues at the same time. *Values:* 0-20% of the fleet [design]; own cars ahead in a first-in-first-out queue lengthen each other's wait.. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Airport queue waits of 1-3 h (R06-041) [S]; robotaxis may lack freeway access to airports (R10-023) [S]. · Data: TLC trip records (zones 132 JFK, 138 LaGuardia); flight schedules (R05-062) · Model: Queue per airport with own cars counted; marginal value of the k-th car in the queue. · [R11-024]</sub>
   * **Event pre-positioning**: How many fleet cars to stage near a venue before a let-out (Madison Square Garden, Barclays Center, Yankee Stadium, Citi Field, US Open). *Values:* 0-50 cars; arrive 0-30 min before the end [design].. `Med · Agent decision · public/forecast · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Event let-out convergence (R06-052) [S]; [U] Cruise robotaxis stalled near a 2023 San Francisco music festival when cellular links were congested [uncertain: news recollection (Outside Lands, Aug 2023), not re-checkable this session]. · Data: NYC permitted event calendar on NYC Open Data; venue schedules (R03) · Model: Event demand pulse; staging limited by R11-023; overcommitment shows up as cannibalisation. · [R11-025]</sub>
   * **Rebalancing objective and target distribution**: What the fleet balances idle cars against (request counts, fare value or unmet demand) and over what horizon. *Values:* proportional to requests / to fare value / to forecast shortfall; horizon 15-60 min.. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: gnn-rl-for-amod sets a desired distribution and then solves a min-cost rebalancing flow (reb_flow_solver) [P, code]; Pavone et al. 2012 fluid rebalancing is listed in AMoDeus [P, README]. · Data: TLC trip records (fares by zone and hour) · Model: Model outputs a target share per zone; an LP turns targets into car moves. · [R11-020]</sub>
   * **Repositioning reach cap**: Maximum empty-drive time the fleet allows for one repositioning move. *Values:* 5-30 min; fleetsim used 20 min.. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [corrected by fact-check: impact was low] fleetsim 20-minute reachability cap [S]; the README says the cap gated the whole policy: with a 10-min cap only 5.5% of zone pairs were reachable at midday and repositioning gave -1.2% wait for +6.6% empty miles, while 20 min gave -8.0% wait for +87% empty miles (empty-mile cost per 1% of wait rose from 5.5% to 10.9%) [S, re-read by fact-check]. · Data: n/a · Model: Constraint in the move LP or a validity check on each action. · [R11-021]</sub>
   * **Return to base with revenue**: Taking last trips that end near the depot or the driver's home before charging, cleaning or shift end: the fleet version of 'take me home'. *Values:* destination radius 1-5 km; start 30-90 min before the planned return [design].. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Destination filter mechanics (R06-028, R09-017) [S]; [design]. · Data: n/a · Model: A 'heading to base' flag biases matching toward trips that end near the base. · [R11-026]</sub>
* **Coordination inside the fleet**
   * **Anti-clustering rule**: A cap or penalty on how many own idle cars may sit in one zone. *Values:* none / soft penalty / hard cap per zone, e.g. at most 1.2 x forecast requests per 15 min [design].. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]. · Data: n/a · Model: Constraint in the move LP, or a harness guard that rejects moves above the cap. · [R11-029]</sub>
   * **Fleet ride pooling**: Combining riders from the fleet's own requests into one car. *Values:* off / up to 2 / up to 4 riders.. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · robotaxi fleet (own-app mode)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Alonso-Mora et al. 2017 (PNAS): about 3,000 four-seat cars could serve about 98% of NYC taxi demand [uncertain: paper not reachable this session]; the algorithm ships in AMoDeus ('High-Capacity Algorithm') and FleetPy (pooling/batch/AlonsoMora) [P, re-checked by fact-check]. · Data: HVFHV shared_request and shared_match flags (R06-031) · Model: Insertion heuristic; off in v1. · [R11-032]</sub>
   * **Earnings fairness across fleet drivers**: How evenly the fleet spreads good trips among its human drivers. *Values:* Gini of driver daily pay 0.1-0.4 [design].. `Low · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver fleet, taxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]. · Data: n/a · Model: Track pay per driver; optional attrition penalty when a driver's pay falls below a threshold. · [R11-033]</sub>
   * **Territory partition**: Whether cars are given home territories or float across the whole city. *Values:* free-floating / k territories / one borough each.. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]. · Data: TLC taxi zone shapefile · Model: Optional home-zone constraint per car. · [R11-031]</sub>
* **Charging at fleet scale**
   * **Charger-to-vehicle ratio**: Number of charging plugs the fleet can use per car. *Values:* 1:15 (36 x 150 kW for 550 cars; offpeak's cars serve a 20% sample of Manhattan yellow-taxi demand [added by fact-check]), 1:23 (24 chargers), 1:42 (120 chargers for 5,000 cars); a Waymo SF depot had 19 dual chargers (R09-045).. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · robotaxi fleet, EV fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: offpeak: 24 chargers with smart scheduling served 96.1% of riders vs 93.4% with 72 chargers and a threshold rule [S]; fleetsim used 120 chargers at 10 sites [S]. · Data: AFDC Station Locator https://afdc.energy.gov/stations [U] · Model: Charger nodes with plug count and power; cars queue when all plugs are busy. · [R11-034]</sub>
   * **Charging schedule policy**: When fleet cars charge and to what level: fixed thresholds, top-ups in demand troughs, joint with dispatch, or overnight only. *Values:* threshold 20 to 80%; 20 to 100%; trough top-ups; joint min-cost with dispatch.. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · EV fleet, robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: offpeak: orchestrated charging served 3.4 points more riders at 15% lower energy cost than a 20-80% threshold [S]; fleetsim: no charging policy leaves 4-12% of demand unserved [S]. · Data: n/a · Model: Charging as a per-car action or a policy parameter set; charge time from charger power and taper (R08). · [R11-035]</sub>
   * **Charging site count and placement**: How many charging sites the fleet uses and where they are. *Values:* 1-12 sites; mean drive to a charger 12.9 min (1 site) to 5.6 min (12 sites).. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · EV fleet, robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: offpeak siting study: 6 p-median sites cut mean drive to 6.5 min and charger-trip distance about 25% vs the 6 busiest drop-off zones, while riders served changed by under 0.5 points [S]. · Data: AFDC stations; NYC MapPLUTO zoning for candidate sites [U] · Model: Site list per scenario; optional p-median solver if the model chooses sites. · [R11-037]</sub>
   * **Depot electricity tariff and demand charges**: Commercial electricity price for a fleet depot, including monthly charges on peak kW that punish many cars fast-charging at once. *Values:* Energy about $0.12-0.25/kWh plus demand charges about $15-50 per kW-month on commercial tariffs [U]; NY utility EV fleet programmes reduce demand charges [U].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] 20 chargers x 150 kW peaking together = 3 MW, i.e. roughly $45k-150k a month in demand charges at those rates; tariff levels [U]. · Data: Con Edison commercial tariffs https://www.coned.com [U]; NY PSC / NYSERDA EV programmes [U] · Model: Depot bill = kWh x energy price + monthly peak kW x demand rate; the fleet agent can stagger charging to cut the peak. · [R08-020]</sub>
   * **Dispatch battery reserve**: Minimum charge a car must keep after a trip and the drive to a charger before it may accept a request. *Values:* 5-20%, or 'enough for the trip plus the nearest charger' [design].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · EV fleet, robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: offpeak uses a 5% reserve and an energy-sufficiency check [S]; fleetsim's joint policy plugged in at 25-30% vs 10-20% for the naive rule [S]. · Data: n/a · Model: Feasibility filter in matching; a stranding event if a battery reaches 0. · [R11-040]</sub>
   * **Fleet peak power, grid connection and demand charges**: The fleet's combined charging load, the depot's grid limit and the utility's charge on peak kW. *Values:* about 3.1-3.6 MW peak for 550 cars (about 6 kW per car) [corrected by fact-check: 2.2-3.6 MW across offpeak's policies (2.19 MW when charging to 100%), with 36 x 150 kW = 5.4 MW installed]; demand charge in $/kW-month [U].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · EV fleet, robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: offpeak peak loads of 3.12-3.61 MW; demand charges not modelled there [S]; [U] Con Edison commercial tariffs bill peak demand. · Data: Con Edison tariffs https://www.coned.com/en/rates-tariffs [U]; NYISO prices https://www.nyiso.com/energy-market-operational-data [U] · Model: Depot kW cap; monthly peak charge prorated per day. · [R11-039]</sub>
   * **Private depot vs public charging mix**: Share of charging done at the fleet's own depots versus public fast chargers shared with other EVs. *Values:* private only / public only / mix; public price and occupancy vary by hour.. `Med · Benchmark setting · public/forecast · EV fleet, robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: offpeak: two busy Palo Alto public sites had no free port 20-25% of weekday working hours [S]; [U] Revel built its own fast-charging hubs in NYC. · Data: AFDC (public stations, network, power) https://afdc.energy.gov/stations [U] · Model: Public chargers with hourly background occupancy and a posted price; private chargers carry a fixed cost. · [R11-038]</sub>
* **Depots, maintenance and availability**
   * **Depot locations and capacity**: Where robotaxis go to charge, be cleaned and park, and how many each depot can serve at once. *Values:* 1-5 depots per city [design]; e.g. an SF site with 19 chargers serving 2 cars each; about 70 workers per depot.. `Med · World input · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Understanding AI (SF depot, 19 dual chargers); Jalopnik (fleet over 3,800 cars, depots of about 70 staff each). · Data: none public; AFDC stations as proxies https://afdc.energy.gov/stations [U] · Model: Depot nodes with charger and cleaning-bay queues; the deadhead to the depot is unpaid. · [R09-045]</sub>
   * **Depot throughput**: How many cars per hour a depot can clean, inspect, charge and park. *Values:* 2-10 cleaning bays; 10-30 min per clean (R09-047); about 70 staff per depot (R09-045).. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Depots of about 70 staff each (R09-045) [S]; cleaning dwell (R09-047) [design]. · Data: none found · Model: Multi-server queue per service at each depot. · [R11-044]</sub>
   * **Repair downtime and spare vehicles**: Days a car is off the road after a breakdown or crash, and whether a spare or rental replaces it. *Values:* Human driver earns nothing unless a TLC-plated replacement is found [U]; taxi weekly leases must be pro-rated when the car is unavailable through no fault of the lessee (35 RCNY 58-21(c)(1)(v)) [P*]; fleet spare ratio 0-15% [design].. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] a 10-day repair at about $34 per online hour x 50 h a week is about $2,400 of lost gross; medium mainly via the fleet spare-ratio decision. · Data: none · Model: Car state 'in repair' with a countdown; the fleet agent can keep spare cars (R08-069). · [R08-058]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi fleet-ops decisions**: Send a car to the depot, clean it, charge it, take it out of service or hold it in reserve. *Values:* Per car, per tick.. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Depot operations reporting (Waymo-Lyft Nashville; SF depot). · Data: none public · Model: Fleet actions mapped to depot queues; time out of service earns nothing. · [R09-069]</sub>
   * **Depot siting constraints: zoning, permits, lead time, community friction**: Where a depot may legally go, how long its chargers take to be energised, and neighbour complaints that can cap depot size or hours. Extends R11-043 (depot siting and cost). *Values:* Large garages and vehicle storage are limited to manufacturing and some commercial districts [U]. Utility connection takes 6-36 months [U]. Neighbour complaints happen: Waymo cars honked at night in a SoMa, San Francisco lot (Aug 2024), and Waymo fixed this with a software update. A size or hours cap is [design]. Scenario input: a list of feasible sites with lead times.. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] NYC Zoning Resolution use rules. [S] ABC7 and SF Standard (Aug 2024) on the honking lot. [corrected: it was fixed by software, not capped]. [design]. · Data: NYC MapPLUTO (zoning by lot) https://www.nyc.gov/site/planning/data-maps/open-data/dwn-pluto-mappluto.page ; Con Edison hosting-capacity maps [U] · Model: Depot candidates filtered by zoning, each with an available-from date and a capacity cap. A complaint hazard reduces night operations. · [G1-043]</sub>
   * **Mileage balancing and vehicle retirement**: Spreading miles evenly across cars and retiring cars at age or mileage limits. *Values:* 50,000-100,000 miles a year for a 24/7 robotaxi [design]; TLC vehicle age limits [verified by fact-check, P*, was U: taxis must be retired at the first inspection 84 months after hack-up, and accessible cabs may stay on while they pass inspection (35 RCNY 67-18); the FHV retirement provision (35 RCNY 59A-28(d)) is repealed, so no TLC age cap for FHVs was found].. `Low · Changes during run · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 67-18 and 59A-28; [design]. · Data: 35 RCNY 67-18 and 59A-28 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*] · Model: Odometer per car; depreciation per mile (R08); multi-week runs only. · [R11-048]</sub>
   * **Preventive maintenance and inspection rotation**: Scheduled downtime per car for service, tyres, sensor calibration and regulatory inspection, and how the fleet staggers it. *Values:* 2-6 h per 5,000-10,000 miles [design]; NYC taxis must be inspected every four months (35 RCNY 58-29(b)) and FHVs three times a year (35 RCNY 59A-26(a)) [verified by fact-check, P*, was U]; the TLC Safety and Emissions facility is in Woodside [U].. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] TLC inspection schedule (35 RCNY 58-29, 59A-26); [design]. · Data: 35 RCNY 58-29 and 59A-26 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*] · Model: Mileage and calendar counters; maintenance slots booked in low-demand hours. · [R11-045]</sub>
   * **Spare vehicle ratio**: Share of the fleet held back to replace cars in repair or maintenance. *Values:* 0-20% [design].. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] US transit bus fleets use a 20% spare-ratio guideline (FTA) [uncertain: recalled FTA guidance for fleets of 50 or more buses; not re-checkable this session]. · Data: none · Model: Spares sit at the depot at low cost and are swapped in on failure. · [R11-047]</sub>
* **Operating model, staffing and fixed costs**
   * **Fleet fixed-cost stack per car-day**: Costs the fleet pays per car per day whether or not it drives: insurance, licences, financing or depreciation, depot stall, software and base fees. *Values:* about $40-150 per car-day [design]; TLC commercial insurance is several thousand dollars a year per car [U]; fixed licence items include the TLC vehicle licence of $550 per car per 2 years and, for an own app, the HVFHS licence of $190,000 a year (R07-058) [P*, added by fact-check].. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; per-vehicle cost items in R08. · Data: TLC licence fees [U]; insurance filings with NY DFS [U] · Model: Daily fixed charge per car on the fleet's books; idle and spare cars still pay it. · [R11-054]</sub>
   * **Human fleet shift roster**: When each human-driven fleet car is on the road (shift starts, lengths, handovers), compared with a robotaxi running 24/7. *Values:* 8-12 h shifts; staggered starts to cover 7-10 am and 5-9 pm peaks; double shift of two 12 h shifts per car [verified by fact-check, P*, was U].. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · human app driver fleet, taxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: NYC taxi day and night lease shifts of 12 consecutive hours (35 RCNY 58-21, via R09-003) [P*] with the 4-5 pm change (R02-044, R06-018) [S]; single-driver hours in R09-001/002. · Data: TLC trip records (hourly demand curve, R02) · Model: Roster action per day; each handover at the depot costs deadhead and 10-30 min. · [R11-050]</sub>
   * **Labour and operating model**: Who drives the fleet's cars and how they are paid, which sets the fleet's cost per hour. *Values:* robotaxi (remote assistance plus field staff) / employees on an hourly wage (NYC minimum about $16.50-17 an hour in 2025-26 [U] [uncertain: not re-checkable this session]) / contractors on TLC per-trip minimum pay (R07) / lessees paying a shift lease / outsourced fleet partner [corrected by fact-check: Waymo's fleet partners are Moove (Phoenix, Miami) and Avomo (Austin, Atlanta, Uber-dispatched rides) per industry notes on GitHub [S], and Lyft in Nashville (R09-044) [S]; Avis was not confirmed by any source read].. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] NYC minimum wage schedule; Waymo's partner fleet operators [S]; TLC driver pay rules (R07). · Data: NY State minimum wage https://dol.ny.gov/minimum-wage-0 [U]; TLC driver pay rules (R07) · Model: Cost function per model: wage x paid hours, per-trip pay, or lease income. · [R11-049]</sub>
   * **Double-shifting and lease or rental income**: For taxi and rental fleets, earning a fixed lease per shift while drivers keep the fares, and running each car for two shifts. *Values:* taxi lease caps about $100-130 per 12 h shift [corrected by fact-check: $105-129 per 12 h shift for cab plus medallion ($108-132 for hybrids) and $630-737 a week, 35 RCNY 58-21(c), P*]; weekly TLC car rental about $400-600 [U] [uncertain: FHV rentals have no rate cap, only disclosure rules (Admin Code 19-553), and the range was not checked]; single vs double shift.. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · taxi fleet, human app driver (rental) fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] TLC lease caps (35 RCNY 58-21); lease shift structure in R02-045 and R09-003. · Data: TLC rules (lease caps) https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-rules.page [U]; verbatim 35 RCNY 58-21 at https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*] · Model: Alternative fleet business model: fleet profit = leases minus fixed costs, with drivers as R06 bots. · [R11-051]</sub>
   * **Field and depot staff**: Cleaners, technicians, charger handlers and recovery drivers the fleet needs per car-day. *Values:* about 70 staff per depot in three 24/7 shifts [S]; 0.1-0.3 staff per car [design].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: WSMV on the Nashville depot (R09-044) and Jalopnik on depot staffing (R09-045) [S]. · Data: none found · Model: Staff per depot x wage; recovery response time depends on field staff on shift. · [R11-053]</sub>
   * **Fleet-level overhead and break-even fleet size**: Costs that do not grow per car, such as an operations centre, a minimum 24/7 remote-assistance and field crew, licences, mapping and software, and the smallest fleet size that covers them. *Values:* HVFHS licence $190,000 a year (35 RCNY 59D-07(a)) and NYC AV test permit $5,000 a year (34 RCNY 4-17(c)) [P*, rule text re-read by fact-check]. One remote seat staffed 24/7 needs about 4.2-5 FTE (168 h / 40 h plus leave), so 5 seats need about 25 FTE [design]. Report break-even N on the scale ladder (R11-067).. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · fleet (robotaxi and human)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59D-07 and 34 RCNY 4-17(c) via R07-058. Charging fixed costs per car-day (R11-054) makes every fleet size look equally efficient [design]. · Data: 35 RCNY and 34 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: A lump-sum daily overhead plus step costs (per remote seat, per depot) that do not depend on N. The score reports profit vs N and the break-even N. · [G2-047]</sub>
   * **Rider acquisition cost and promotions**: Spending on first-ride credits, referral bonuses, promo codes and marketing to grow the fleet's own rider base. *Values:* $10-50 per rider acquired, plus a demand response to promotions [design]. Links to the adoption ramp (G2-042) and retention (R05-068).. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · robotaxi fleet (own app), fleet with its own base`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Uber's FY2025 Form 10-K risk factors say driver incentives, consumer discounts and promotions have hurt and will keep hurting its financial performance. That they are booked as a reduction of revenue worth billions a year [uncertain: revenue note not read]. Leaving this cost out makes own-app profit look better than any real launch [design]. · Data: Uber Form 10-K on SEC EDGAR https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1543151/000154315126000015/uber-20251231.htm · Model: Actions set_promo_budget(per day) or set_first_ride_credit($). Spending grows the registered-rider base and short-run demand, and the cost is booked against fleet profit. · [G2-046]</sub>
   * **Base affiliation and licensing overhead**: In NYC every for-hire car must belong to a licensed base, which brings fees and reporting duties. *Values:* own base ($500 a year base licence fee, 35 RCNY 59B-07) vs affiliation with an Uber or Lyft base; each FHV must affiliate with one base, and only one at a time (35 RCNY 59A-04(h)-(i)) [verified by fact-check, P*, was U]; other cities use TNC or AV permits instead.. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver fleet, robotaxi fleet (NYC-specific)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59A-04 and 59B-07. · Data: NYC Open Data FHV Base Aggregate Report [U] · Model: Fixed fee and data-reporting duty per scenario. · [R11-055]</sub>
   * **Experience-curve cost decline**: The remote-assistance rate, incident rate and mapping cost fall as the fleet's total miles in a city grow. *Values:* Cost = c0 x (cumulative miles / m0)^-b, with b = 0.1-0.3 [design]. Reference: Waymo's California miles per disengagement rose from about 5,100-5,600 (2016-17) to 11,017 (2018), 13,219 (2019) and 29,945 (2020) [S, added by fact-check].. `Low · Changes during run · hidden · robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] California DMV annual disengagement reports via Waymo's blog, VentureBeat and The Robot Report. These cover testing with safety drivers, not driverless remote-assistance rates, so they only roughly proxy the curve. Matters for multi-day and fleet-growth runs. · Data: California DMV Disengagement Reports https://www.dmv.ca.gov/portal/vehicle-industry-services/autonomous-vehicles/disengagement-reports/ · Model: Track the fleet's cumulative miles per city and scale the R09-048 request rate and the R08-057 incident rate along the curve. · [G2-048]</sub>
   * **Extra revenue from idle cars (delivery)**: Using idle cars in demand troughs for food or parcel delivery, which raises utilisation. *Values:* Delivery jobs $5-12 each, 15-30 min including handoff, with the customer collecting from the trunk. Trough hours 02-06h and 14-16h [design]. Precedents: Waymo with Uber Eats in metro Phoenix (launched 3 Apr 2024, select merchants); a Waymo-DoorDash Phoenix service announced 16 Oct 2025, starting with DashMart orders.. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees · robotaxi; fleet (human drivers can also multi-app with delivery)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Waymo blog (Apr 2024) and azfamily on Uber Eats; [S] DoorDash release, azfamily and KTAR (16 Oct 2025): the customer opens the trunk in the app. Values are [design]. · Data: none found (delivery demand is not public) · Model: Delivery request stream by zone-hour with pay per job; a car on a delivery job is unavailable for rides. · [G1-049]</sub>
* **Market impact, pricing and service obligations**
   * **Agent fleet share of total supply**: The agent's cars as a share of all supply where they work, which decides whether they take the market as given or move it. *Values:* 1 car ~0.007% of NYC app supply; N=100 ~0.7%; N=1,000 ~7% [design, using ~15k cars online].. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; added supply cuts utilisation (Hall et al.) [S]. · Data: n/a · Model: Either shrink background supply by the fleet size (displacement) or keep it fixed (additive); report both. · [R06-061]</sub>
   * **Fleet effect on surge and fares**: A large fleet fills shortages, so surge fires less and fares fall for everyone, the fleet included. *Values:* negligible below 100 cars in NYC (under 1% of supply, R06-061); material at 5-10% or more of local supply.. `High · Changes during run · hidden · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Fleet share (R06-061) and Hall et al. re-equilibration (R06-012) [S]; [design]. · Data: n/a · Model: The surge engine (R07) counts fleet cars in local idle supply. · [R11-056]</sub>
   * **Fleet own-app pricing policy**: In own-app robotaxi or fleet mode, whether and how the model sets its own fares, surge rules, caps, discounts and subscription price instead of accepting platform prices. *Values:* Off (default: platform or fixed rate card) / zone-hour multiplier of 0.7-2.0x with emergency caps (R04-012) / full rate card (base, per mile, per minute) / subscription price [design]. Reference: Waymo SF about $9.52 + $1.66/mi + $0.30/min (R07-061) [S]. Waymo Premier is an invite-only $29.99/month membership (launched Jun 2026 in SF, LA and Phoenix) with priority pickups and 10% back in Waymo Cash [S, detail added by fact-check]. NYC: HVFHS fares may not exceed the rate schedule filed with the TLC (35 RCNY 59D-17(a)), and final charges are capped at 1.2x the estimate (59B-23) [P*, re-read].. `High · Agent decision · agent sees · robotaxi fleet (own app), fleet with its own base; not single app drivers or metered taxis`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Castillo, Knoepfle & Weyl, 'Surge pricing solves the wild goose chase' (ACM EC 2017; tested on Uber Manhattan data); [S via list] Waymo rate card (R07-061). In the Uber-Waymo Austin/Atlanta set-up the operator has no price lever (R06-059), and Waymo Premier is not offered there [S]. · Data: Waymo pricing help page https://support.google.com/waymo/answer/9059184; 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Actions set_price(zone, hour, multiplier), set_rate_card(...) or set_membership(price, perks), bounded by the rule caps. Riders respond via R05-020 and R05-027, deferral via G2-018, and competitors via G2-045. · [G2-026]</sub>
   * **Accessibility compliance for a fleet with no accessible AVs**: How a robotaxi fleet without wheelchair-accessible AVs meets NYC's WAV rules: by paying an approved accessible dispatcher, running a human WAV sub-fleet, or paying per trip. Extends R11-059, which states the rule; this is the compliance-mode choice. *Values:* (a) 25% of trips in WAVs: infeasible without WAVs. (b) Join an accessible dispatcher that serves 90% of WAV requests within 10 min (35 RCNY 59B-17), for a fee per WAV request [design]. (c) A human WAV sub-fleet of k cars. WAV requests are about 0.15% of HVFHV trips (Jan 2023, R05-031).. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*, from list] 35 RCNY 59B-17(c)(1)(v) and (f)(3) (R11-059); [U] current Waymo vehicles are not wheelchair-accessible. · Data: 35 RCNY via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive ; HVFHV wav_request_flag · Model: Compliance-mode setting. Option (b) adds a per-trip or membership cost to the fleet ledger; option (c) adds a WAV sub-fleet with human labour cost (R11-049). · [G1-045]</sub>
   * **Service coverage and equity obligations**: Rules or permit conditions that make the fleet serve all boroughs, night hours or minimum response times. *Values:* none / outer-borough share of at least X% / maximum p90 wait / green-cab-style hail zone [design].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; green taxi hail zone (R01-006). · Data: TLC rules [U] · Model: Constraint with penalties in the score. · [R11-058]</sub>
   * **Wheelchair-accessible service obligation**: NYC rules on accessible vehicles and response times, which fix part of the fleet mix and its positioning. *Values:* HVFHV bases must answer most WAV requests within 10-15 min [corrected by fact-check: each FHV base must either send at least 25% of its trips to accessible vehicles (since July 2022) or join an approved accessible-vehicle dispatcher that serves at least 90% of WAV requests in under 10 min (35 RCNY 59B-17(c)(1)(v) and (f)(3)), and every HVFHS app must let riders request a WAV (59D-13(b)), P*]; yellow fleet goal of half accessible cabs [uncertain: the conversion schedule in 35 RCNY 58-50(a)-(d) now reads 'Reserved'; accessible cabs get at least $20,000 at hack-up plus $10,000 over 4 years from the Taxi Improvement Fund, and drivers $10 per accessible-dispatch trip (58-50(i)), P*].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · human app driver fleet, taxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 35 RCNY 59B-17, 59D-13 and 58-50; HVFHV wav_request_flag and wav_match_flag columns (R05-031) [S]; WAVs did about 9% of 2025 app trips (R06) [S]. · Data: HVFHV trip records (WAV flags) · Model: WAV request stream; penalty if response targets are missed; WAV cars cost more (R08). · [R11-059]</sub>

### 16. Robotaxi-specific (46)
*What changes when there is no driver: the operating domain, AV behaviour, remote help, cabin and costs.*

* **Legal status, permits and the operating domain (ODD)**
   * **Geofence endpoint rule and boundary-trip leakage**: Whether a robotaxi trip must start and end inside the service area, whether drop-offs just outside are allowed, and what happens to requests that cross the boundary. *Values:* Rules: both ends inside / pickup inside with drop-off up to X km outside / airport exceptions [design]. Proxy geofence: the TLC 'Yellow Zone' (55 Manhattan zones south of about E 96th and W 110th St, excluding Roosevelt Island) [P]. HVFHV Jan 2023: 30.5% of core pickups end outside the core. Trips with both ends inside are 24.5% of trips but 19.2% of base fare, vs 50.7% of fare for trips touching the core, so the strict rule keeps about 38% of core-touching fare. Yellow Oct 2023: 54.2% vs 87.2%, so it keeps about 62%, and only 10.7% of yellow core pickups end outside the core. Airport legs (JFK, LGA, EWR) are 16.7% (HVFHV) and 24.3% (yellow) of core-touching fare [P, all re-verified by fact-check].. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own re-check on local TLC files with the critic's geofence logic. [U] Waymo help pages and rider reports say pickup and drop-off must both be inside the service area. R01-008 and R07-060 never define the endpoint rule. · Data: NYC TLC trip records; taxi zone lookup (service_zone field) https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Filter requests on (pickup zone, drop-off zone) by the rule. Rejected quotes lower rider retention (R05-068). Report the share of demand and fare lost. · [G2-036]</sub>
   * **NYC robotaxi fee mapping (counterfactual)**: Design choice of which NYC rule set a hypothetical robotaxi follows, since no commercial rule exists. *Values:* Option A (FHV-like): upfront fare, $2.75 congestion, $1.50 CBD, $2.50 airport fee, no hails, no pay floor; Option B (taxi-like): meter, hails, medallion needed; plus whether empty robotaxis pay the $9 daily CRZ car toll (peak; 75% less overnight [S]). `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] no NYC commercial pathway exists (R07-059); choice shifts fees by several dollars per trip · Data: none (design choice) · Model: Rule-pack switch read by pricing and fee modules · [R07-064]</sub>
   * **Operating design domain (where and when a robotaxi may drive)**: The approved service polygon, hours, road types and conditions for a robotaxi; requests outside it cannot be served. *Values:* NYC permit: only DOT-approved areas, dates and hours; Waymo Nashville launch 60 sq mi; airports and freeways added city by city [U]; pauses in severe weather [U]. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 34 RCNY 4-17(f)(3)-(4); [S] Vital City (Nashville area); freeway access in R10-023 · Data: Waymo service-area maps [U]; path-avmap av-map-data boundaries https://github.com/path-avmap/av-map-data · Model: Polygon + time windows + weather thresholds; out-of-ODD requests rejected or handed to human drivers · [R07-060]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi freeway access and route restrictions**: Whether the robotaxi may use expressways and which streets or maneuvers its operating domain excludes, which can make trips much longer. *Values:* Waymo freeway rides began 12 Nov 2025 in SF, LA, Phoenix for some riders, only when 'meaningfully faster', up to 50% shorter; 2024 LA test: Waymo 33 min 58 s vs 15 min 20 s for Uber/Lyft (~2.2x) [uncertain: a single-trip media test, not a representative ratio]. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: TechCrunch and Axios, Nov 2025 [S]; Forbes LA comparison via secondary coverage [S] · Data: Waymo announcements; none for NYC (Aug 2025 permit was testing only: up to 8 cars, safety driver, no passengers [S]); 34 RCNY 4-17 requires an NYC DOT test permit ($5,000 a year) with a trained test operator in the driver's seat and approved areas and hours [P*] · Model: Per-vehicle-type edge mask (no motorway, excluded zones) and maneuver penalties in the routing profile. · [R10-023]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi legal status by jurisdiction**: Whether paid driverless rides are allowed in the simulated city, which decides if the robotaxi track is real or counterfactual. *Values:* NYC: testing only with a trained safety driver (8 Waymo cars, Manhattan south of 112th St and Downtown Brooklyn); state permits expired 31 Mar 2026; Feb 2026 state proposal (excluding NYC) withdrawn; NY law requires a hand on the wheel. Permissive: 28 states per Vital City, including AZ, CA, FL, GA, TN, TX where Waymo operates (11 Waymo cities, ~3,000 cars, May 2026) [corrected by fact-check]; ambiguous: WA (Seattle human-driver rule), IL. Effectively barred: NY, MA, NJ. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Vital City Waymo map methodology (6 May 2026) citing THE CITY, CNBC, TechCrunch; [P*] 34 RCNY 4-17 · Data: Vital City waymo-map https://github.com/vitalcity-nyc/waymo-map; NCSL AV legislation tracker [U] · Model: Scenario flag: NYC counterfactual (use R07-064 mapping) or a permissive city rule pack (R07-067); if a safety driver is required, add wage and hour rules (R09-051) · [R07-059]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi operating geofence (ODD)**: The polygon and road types where an autonomous vehicle is allowed and able to drive and serve riders. *Values:* Example: Waymo NYC test permit (Aug 2025 to 31 Mar 2026) covered Manhattan south of 112th St + Downtown Brooklyn, 8 cars, safety driver, no paying riders. Scenario options: core-only / core + airports / citywide. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Vital City Waymo map methodology (GitHub, 6 May 2026, citing THE CITY, CNBC and the Aug 2025 Mayor's Office release): 8 cars with safety drivers in Manhattan south of 112th St and Downtown Brooklyn, state testing permits expired 31 Mar 2026, NY law effectively bars driverless commercial service; [P*] 34 RCNY 4-17: an NYC test permit needs NYS DMV approval, a test operator in the driver's seat, DOT-approved areas and hours and a $5,000 yearly fee [uncertain: 'passenger service would need a TLC licence' not re-checked] · Data: 34 RCNY 4-17; Vital City waymo-map (https://github.com/vitalcity-nyc/waymo-map); no machine-readable NYC geofence found · Model: Geofence polygon + road-class whitelist; requests outside are unservable or lost to competitors · [R01-008]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi weather operating envelope**: Weather limits beyond which robotaxis stop taking rides, return to base or restrict their area. *Values:* Thresholds e.g. rain >0.3 in/h, any snow accumulation, visibility <0.5 mi, flash-flood warning, high winds [design]; Aurora: weather constrained its driverless trucks in Texas ~40% of the time in 2025 until rain, fog and wind were validated [P*]; Waymo says its 6th-gen sensors handle rain, fog and snow and it is expanding to snowy cities [S]. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Aurora Q4 2025 earnings call transcript, 11 Feb 2026 [P*] (fact-check: re-read verbatim in the GitHub transcript copy, 'During 2025, inclement weather of all types constrained our driverless operations in Texas roughly 40% of the time'; tag raised from [S]); Waymo technical summaries [S] · Data: none public (operator internal); CPUC/DMV filings partial [U] · Model: ODD table: weather class -> normal / restricted zones or speeds / no new pickups / return to base; 'robotaxi generation' scenario knob. · [R04-039]</sub>
   * **Safety-operator requirement**: Whether local rules require a human operator on board, which brings back a wage and human limits. *Values:* None, or required. NYC [corrected by fact-check: was 'reportedly [U]']: 34 RCNY 4-17(d)(4) (added 7 Sep 2021) requires a trained test vehicle operator in the driver's seat of every AV tested in NYC, plus a $5,000/yr permit and $5M of insurance [P*]. Waymo's NYC test permit (Aug 2025, 8 cars, no paying riders) expired 31 Mar 2026 and there is no commercial pathway [S, via R01/R06/R07], so a NYC robotaxi is a counterfactual.. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi (NYC; most other cities allow driverless service under state rules)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [corrected by fact-check: was medium; when on (the NYC legal default) it adds a full driver wage plus shift and fatigue limits to every car-hour] [P*] 34 RCNY 4-17(d)(4); [S] TechCrunch Aug 2025 and West Side Spirit 2026 on the Waymo permit. · Data: 34 RCNY 4-17 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive [P*]; NYC DOT AV testing permit programme [U] · Model: If on, the robotaxi carries a driver wage plus the shift and fatigue rules from the human sections. · [R09-051]</sub>
   * **ODD connectivity: fleet islands across river crossings**: Whether the robotaxi may use the bridges and tunnels that join parts of its service area; if not, the fleet splits into sub-fleets that cannot rebalance into each other. Rivers make this acute in NYC; it generalises to any geofence with chokepoints. *Values:* Per-crossing whitelist (e.g. Brooklyn Bridge, Manhattan Bridge, Hugh Carey Tunnel). Waymo's 2025-26 NYC test area was two pieces: Manhattan south of 112th St plus Downtown Brooklyn. Testing ended when the permits lapsed on 31 Mar 2026. Options: separate islands / bridges only / all crossings. Tunnels cut GNSS and cellular signal [U].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · robotaxi; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S, from list] Test area in R01-008 and permit lapse in R07-059; [S] Streetsblog and Bloomberg (Apr 2026): testing halted. [design] Cross-river trip shares come from the TLC OD matrix. · Data: TLC OD matrix; Vital City waymo-map https://github.com/vitalcity-nyc/waymo-map ; OSM bridge and tunnel tags · Model: Edge mask on crossings per vehicle type, with zones split into connected components. Cross-component requests are rejected or handed to a partner, and each component needs its own depot and chargers. · [G1-038]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi operating-hours window**: Robotaxi services may be allowed only in certain hours (night-only launches, daytime-only testing), as part of their operating design domain. *Values:* 24/7, night-only (e.g. 22:00-05:30), or daytime-only; NYC testing with safety driver only [U] [corrected by fact-check: 34 RCNY 4-17(d)(4) requires a trained test operator in the driver's seat and the permit application must state hours of operation [P*]; Waymo's NYC test permit (Aug 2025, 8 cars, no riders) expired 31 Mar 2026 [S, via R06/R07], so NYC has no robotaxi service and its hours are a counterfactual setting]. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Cruise SF 2022 night-only launch, Waymo 24/7 in Phoenix/SF; not verified this session [uncertain: CPUC permit pages unreachable] · Data: CPUC AV deployment reports, https://www.cpuc.ca.gov/ ; NYC DOT AV permits · Model: Fleet availability mask by hour-of-week · [R02-061]</sub>
   * **AV permit allocation and scarcity value**: How a limited number of AV permits is handed out (fee, lottery, auction, phased growth) and the resulting cost per permit. Overlaps R11-008 (caps) and R07-064 (fee mapping). *Values:* Permit cost $0-50k per car-year [design]. The NYC AV test permit costs $5,000 a year (34 RCNY 4-17, from list). Taxi medallions peaked at about $1-1.3M around 2013-14, then collapsed (some sold for under $140k at a 2019 auction). FHV licence pause since 2018 (R06-053).. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Medallion price history via press snippets [upgraded from U; peak widened to $1-1.3M]. [P*, from list] AV test permit fee. · Data: 34 RCNY 4-17 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive ; TLC medallion transfer data [U] · Model: Permit cost per car-year in the fixed-cost stack, plus a growth cap per period for phased schemes. · [G1-051]</sub>
   * **AV-specific road-use levies (per empty mile, per trip, displacement fund)**: Fees a regulator could charge a robotaxi fleet per empty mile, per trip or per car, to curb empty cruising or to fund displaced drivers. *Values:* $0-0.50 per empty mile; $0-2.50 per AV trip; $0-5,000 per car-year [design]. A Jan 2021 report by Charles Komanoff for the NYC Council proposed charging app companies (not drivers) 11 cents per empty minute at peak, and half that at other times, in the zone south of 96th St [S]. The existing NYS congestion surcharge on FHV trips is $2.75 (R07-007) [S; tag downgraded by fact-check from P* to match R07-007]. The MTA congestion-zone charge of $1.50 per FHV trip (since Jan 2025) also applies [U, via R01-022].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi fleet; human app fleet (the 2021 proposal's target)`

This extends R06-055, which targets human app platforms. · Data: none (scenario rule); NYC Council report https://council.nyc.gov/curbing-fhv-stockpiling · Model: A scenario toggle that charges the fleet per empty mile or minute, per trip and per car-day; off by default. · [G2-043]</sub>
   * **Remote-operator location and licensing rules**: Rules on where remote assistants must be based, what licence or training they need, and how many cars each may supervise. *Values:* An onshore requirement multiplies wages by 1-3x; a cap of 1 agent per 10-50 cars [design].    * **AV capability transfer factor (Phoenix/SF to NYC)**: One reported scenario knob that scales every AV parameter borrowed from today's robotaxi cities to NYC density: speed penalty, remote-assistance rate, stuck and incident rates, pickup failures and weather limits. *Values:* Presets low / mid / high: remote-assist rate x1 / x2 / x4 the Phoenix/SF base; speed factor 0.95 / 0.85 / 0.70; stuck rate x1 / x3 / x10 [design]. Run all three presets and report how sensitive the human-vs-robotaxi gap is to them. · [G1-039]</sub>
   * **Driving-style speed factor (human vs robotaxi)**: How fast a vehicle drives relative to traffic and limits, from assertive human driving to conservative autonomous driving. *Values:* human 1.0-1.1 x traffic speed [design]; robotaxi 0.8-0.95 on surface streets [design]; LA test trip time ratio 2.2x including routing [uncertain: single trip]. `Med · Rule/price · hidden · robotaxi, human app driver, taxi`

   * **Pedestrians and drivers exploiting AV deference**: Pedestrians cross, and drivers cut in, more freely in front of a car they know will always yield, so robotaxis are slowed most where foot traffic is heaviest. *Values:* Extra AV delay per turn or curb pull = k x pedestrian volume x AV recognisability; 0-60 s per turn at Midtown peaks [design]. Grows with the AV share of traffic.. `Med · World input · hidden · robotaxi; fleet`

Planning Education and Research 38(1), doi 10.1177/0739456X16675674: a game-theory model of 'crosswalk chicken' in which pedestrians gain impunity against cautious AVs (eScholarship PDF not read). Driver cut-ins and all magnitudes are [design].    * **Robotaxi sensor degradation, cleaning and cautious driving**: Rain, snow and salt spray on sensors make robotaxis drive more cautiously, call remote help more often and stop to clean. *Values:* Extra speed penalty -5% to -15% beyond human drivers in rain or snow; more remote-assistance stops; cleaning stops [design guess]; Waymo simulates raindrops on sensors and glare [S]. `Med · Rule/price · hidden · robotaxi, fleet`

This adds a matching constraint and a fleet-mix choice (R11-003). · [G2-038]</sub>
* **Rider acceptance and pickup experience**
   * **Robotaxi acceptance and AV price premium**: How willing riders are to ride with no driver, and how much extra they will pay for it. *Values:* Waymo premium ~31% over Uber and ~41% over Lyft (Apr 2025) narrowing to ~12.7% over Uber and ~27% over Lyft (late 2025) [fact-check: late-2025 figures confirmed in a secondary GitHub industry note; Apr 2025 Uber gap derived from that note's price table]; 70% of Waymo users prefer driverless; 74% cite safety concern [uncertain: Obi survey figures and n=507 not re-checked]. `High · World input · hidden · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Obi data via TechCrunch Jan 2026 and Obi survey (n=507); [S] kvynlim/industry-research Waymo note on GitHub (Obi-based price table) · Data: Obi public reports (survey); none official · Model: AV-preference term in product/mode choice by segment, with novelty decaying over time · [R05-027]</sub>
   * **AV stopping policy: brief in-lane stop vs strict legal curb**: Whether the robotaxi may stop briefly in a travel lane or beside parked cars to load, as NYC drivers routinely do, or must find a legal curb gap (placement rule in R01-033). *Values:* Toggle: strict legal curb / brief in-lane stop of 60 s or less / human-like. Extra Midtown pickup time 0-3 min under strict vs about 0 human-like [design], with a fine and complaint hazard in the lax modes. Taxis and FHVs may stop briefly where standing is banned, within 12 in of the curb (34 RCNY 4-11(c)).. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · robotaxi; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*, from list] R01-025 and R10-037 curb rules. [S] In San Francisco, Waymo cars got 589 parking tickets in 2024 (74 for double parking, 124-134 for obstructing traffic) and about 4,200 in 2025, clustered at busy pickup spots (Jalopnik, Electrek, SF Gazetteer via snippets). [corrected: upgraded from U]. · Data: NYC DOT parking regulation signs (NYC Open Data); 34 RCNY 4-11 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Policy flag on the curb model. 'Strict' uses R01-033 snapping and R10-037 curb search. 'In-lane' adds a fine or complaint draw and blocks the lane briefly. · [G1-042]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi adoption ramp and registered-rider base**: How many riders have the app and will try a driverless car, growing over the weeks and months after launch through waitlists, word of mouth and novelty. *Values:* A logistic ramp with a launch-date parameter [design]. Waymo paid rides per week: about 50k (May 2024), 100k (Aug 2024), 200k (Feb 2025), 250k (Apr 2025), about 400k (Feb 2026) and 500k across 10 cities (Mar 2026), with a stated target of 1M by end-2026 [S; upgraded by fact-check from U]. Waymo opened SF to all riders in June 2024 after a waitlist [U].. `Med · World input · public/forecast · robotaxi, robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] TechCrunch (Feb 2025; 27 Mar 2026), Sherwood News, CNBC/NBC on Alphabet's April 2025 earnings. Much of the growth came from new cities, so it is not a single-city ramp. In a new market, the number of signed-up riders, not the fleet, caps demand; R05-027 covers willingness to ride and the price premium, not the rider base. · Data: Waymo company statements [S]; CPUC quarterly AV reports [U] · Model: The share of riders eligible for robotaxi follows a logistic curve in time since launch and scales R05-004's robotaxi share for each scenario date, so demand grows in multi-day runs. · [G2-042]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi pickup-point placement**: A robotaxi stops only at a legal, safe point, which may be up to a block away, adding rider walk time and cancellations. *Values:* Walk 0-200 m [design]; density of valid pickup points per km of curb. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] / [U] Operator practice not verified · Data: None found (operator-internal); OSM curbs · Model: Snap each request to the nearest legal curb node; walk time adds to rendezvous; cancel chance rises with walk · [R01-033]</sub>
* **Remote assistance, stuck cars and recovery**
   * **Remote-assistance pool sizing and queue**: How many remote agents the robotaxi fleet staffs, and how long cars wait for help when many ask at once. *Values:* about 1 agent per 15-45 cars (Cruise about 1:15-20, Waymo about 1:43, R09-049); correlated surges at events and outages.. `Med · Agent decision · agent sees · robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Waymo Senate testimony and reply to Sen. Markey; CNBC on Cruise (via R09-049) [S]. · Data: none public beyond testimony · Model: Multi-server queue with c agents on shift; request rate per car from R09-048; cost = agents x wage. · [R11-052]</sub>
   * **Remote-assistance request rate and latency**: How often a robotaxi asks a remote human for guidance, and how long it waits for an answer. *Values:* Cruise 2023: a session every 2.5-5 miles per the NYT, every 4-5 miles per Cruise, many brief; Waymo: not disclosed; wait 0-5 min [design].. `Low · World input · hidden · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] CNBC on the 2023 NYT report (Cruise) and Vogt's reply; TechCrunch Mar 2026: companies refuse to say how often their AVs need remote help. · Data: none found (not disclosed) · Model: Poisson events per mile, more in complex zones and at events; each adds a delay that grows when agents are busy (queue). · [R09-048]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi door left open by rider**: A rider leaves a door open so the driverless car cannot move until someone closes it. *Values:* 'Not too common' per Waymo; door-closing jobs paid $6.25 + $5 (DoorDash, Atlanta) or up to $24 (Honk, LA) [uncertain: press amounts not re-read]; 0.1-1% of trips [design guess]. `Low · World input · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Fortune and Euronews Feb 2026 on Waymo door-closing pilots · Data: none found (press only) · Model: Bernoulli per trip; car idle 10-40 min plus closing fee · [R05-045]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi rider help requests**: Riders who need remote help in a driverless car (wrong car, confusion, accessibility, luggage), which slows the trip. *Values:* 1-3% of trips need remote support; +1-5 min [design guess]. `Low · World input · hidden · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] · Data: none found · Model: Bernoulli support event adding delay and remote-operator cost · [R05-046]</sub>
   * **Stuck vehicle and field recovery**: Events where a robotaxi stops safely but cannot continue and needs a field team. *Values:* About 0.1-1 per 1,000 vehicle-hours; recovery 20-90 min; spikes during blackouts and big events [design].. `Low · World input · hidden · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; public incidents belong to R03. · Data: none found (California DMV and CPUC AV reports as a proxy [U]) · Model: Hazard per hour by zone; the car is out of service until a recovery team arrives. · [R09-050]</sub>
* **Cabin state and cleaning**
   * **Cleaning schedule and dwell time**: How often each car gets routine cleaning and how long it takes. *Values:* Every 8-24 h or every N trips; 10-30 min per clean [design].. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] no public cadence found; a 2020 report says Waymo was studying cleaning between rides [S]. · Data: none found · Model: Cleanliness decays per trip; cleaning jobs queue at the depot. · [R09-047]</sub>
   * **Interior cleanliness and mess events**: State of the cabin, and the chance a rider leaves a mess or item that takes the car out of service. *Values:* Clean, dirty or unusable; mess chance 0.1-1% per trip, higher late on weekend nights [design]; rider fee $50 if self-reported, $100 if not (Waymo) [uncertain: not re-checkable]; Tesla $50-150.. `Low · Changes during run · hidden · robotaxi (human drivers face the same via cleaning fees, R07)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Waymo One rider rules; Jalopnik on fleet handlers; Dec 2025 reports on Tesla robotaxi fees. · Data: Waymo rider rules https://support.google.com/waymo/answer/9197501 [S] · Model: Per-trip mess draw by hour and nearby events, revealed when a rider reports it; a severe mess forces a depot return; the fee partly offsets the loss. · [R09-046]</sub>
* **Failures, incidents, damage and obstruction**
   * **Correlated fleet-wide outage**: One cause grounding many or all fleet cars at once: a software fault or recall, lost connectivity, a power blackout or a permit suspension. *Values:* 0-2 events a year; lasting 1 h to weeks; 10-100% of the fleet [design].. `High · World input · hidden · robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Waymo fleet-wide software recall in May 2026 after flooded-road incidents (R06-058) [S]; [U] Cruise lost its California permit in Oct 2023 and halted its fleet; [U] Waymo cars stalled at dark signals in a Dec 2025 San Francisco blackout [uncertain: both are news recollections, not re-checkable this session]. · Data: NHTSA recalls https://www.nhtsa.gov/recalls [U]; NHTSA Standing General Order crash reports [U] · Model: Rare scenario shock applied to all cars of one type; used in stress-test scenarios. · [R11-060]</sub>
   * **Crash and liability tail**: Rare severe crashes whose claims, deductibles and suspensions fall on the fleet. *Values:* claim $10k to over $1M; probability per million miles by driver type [design].. `Med · World input · hidden · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Waymo reports fewer injury crashes per mile than human benchmarks; NHTSA SGO data [uncertain: not re-checkable this session]. · Data: NHTSA SGO https://www.nhtsa.gov/laws-regulations/standing-general-order-crash-reporting [U]; NYPD collisions [U] · Model: Per-mile crash draw with severity; deductible charged to profit; optional suspension. · [R11-062]</sub>
   * **Safety-incident trust shock and regulatory contagion**: A publicised robotaxi crash or failure, in the fleet or at a rival, that cuts rider demand for AVs and can bring regulator-ordered fleet caps or suspensions. The regulator side overlaps R11-060 and R08-060; the demand dip and contagion are new. *Values:* Demand multiplier on AV rides 0.7-0.95, recovering over 2-8 weeks [design]. Fleet cap at 50%: after the 17 Aug 2023 fire-truck crash, the CA DMV had Cruise cut from about 400 cars to at most 50 by day and 150 at night. Full suspension: the DMV suspended Cruise's deployment and driverless testing permits on 24 Oct 2023. Chance of contagion to other operators 0-50% [design].. `Med · World input · public/forecast · robotaxi; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] SF Standard and InsideEVs (Aug 2023) on the 50%/50/150 cap; [S] TechCrunch (24 Oct 2023) on the suspension [upgraded from U]; [U] GM ended Cruise robotaxi funding (Dec 2024). · Data: CA DMV press releases; NHTSA Standing General Order crash data https://www.nhtsa.gov/laws-regulations/standing-general-order-crash-reporting · Model: Rare event drawn from the crash hazard (R11-062) with a publicity probability. It triggers an AV demand dip (R05-027) and an optional fleet cap or grounding (R11-060). · [G1-044]</sub>
   * **Deliberate obstruction of robotaxis (coning, blocking, fake requests)**: Interference that does no damage, such as cones on hoods, people standing in front of the car or fake requests that lure cars, but stops a car until remote help or a field team clears it. *Values:* 0-0.5 events per 1,000 car-hours, higher near nightlife and protests, with 10-60 min of downtime each [design]. Safe Street Rebel's 'Week of Cone' in San Francisco was organised ahead of the 13 July 2023 CPUC meeting [S]; the group said it avoided bus routes and cars carrying passengers [S]. About a dozen cars coned [uncertain: count not found in sources checked].. `Low · World input · hidden · robotaxi, robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] SF Standard and TechCrunch (July 2023) and NPR (28 Aug 2023): a cone on the hood shuts the car down until it is removed or a technician resets the car; Waymo called it vandalism. The cost is downtime and remote-assistance load, not repairs as with vandalism (R08-059). · Data: none found (news only) · Model: A hazard by zone, hour and event, applied only to empty cars if following the group's stated rule. Each event triggers a remote-assistance request (R09-048) and possibly field recovery (R09-050). · [G2-039]</sub>
   * **Mid-trip failure handling**: What the fleet does when a car fails with a rider aboard: send another own car, refund, or leave the rider to rebook. *Values:* rescue ETA 5-20 min; refund 0-100%; rating hit [design].. `Low · Agent decision · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; stuck-vehicle recovery (R09-050). · Data: none found · Model: The event triggers a decision; a rescue trip gets priority; costs are logged. · [R11-061]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi (ADS) crash and incident rate**: How often a driverless car is in a crash or incident that stops it, and what it costs. *Values:* Large reductions vs human benchmarks reported by Waymo (e.g. Swiss Re 2024: about 88% fewer property-damage and 92% fewer bodily-injury claims over 25.3M miles) [U]; even minor contacts stop the car for inspection [design].. `Low · World input · hidden · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Swiss Re and Waymo 2024 study; corrected by fact-check (was medium): claim rates about 90% below human drivers make the expected crash cost per robotaxi hour smaller than the human case (R08-056, low); severe-incident costs sit in R08-060 and R08-066. ADS crashes are listed publicly under NHTSA's Standing General Order. · Data: NHTSA Standing General Order crash data https://www.nhtsa.gov/laws-regulations/standing-general-order-crash-reporting ; Waymo Safety Impact https://waymo.com/safety/impact/ ; California DMV AV collision reports [U] · Model: Hazard per mile below the human base; each event takes the car out for inspection and repair; rare severe events trigger R08-060. · [R08-057]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi incident reporting and enforcement**: Duties after crashes and rule breaches that can take robotaxis off the road. *Values:* NYC permit: crash notice within 4 h, periodic data (miles, takeovers, shift lengths, NHTSA SGO reports), stop within 24 h of suspension; NHTSA Standing General Order crash reports; California AB 1777 police notices to AV companies and emergency geofencing [uncertain: signed 2024, operative 1 Jul 2026 per background knowledge; not checked]. `Low · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] 34 RCNY 4-17(f)(10),(g),(h); [U] AB 1777 · Data: NHTSA SGO crash data https://www.nhtsa.gov/laws-regulations/standing-general-order-crash-reporting · Model: Incident event removes the vehicle for a review period; emergency geofences block zones temporarily · [R07-063]</sub>
   * **Vandalism and unrest pull-back**: Damage to fleet cars, or areas the fleet withdraws from, during protests or targeted vandalism. *Values:* 0-5 cars damaged per event; area closed 1-72 h [design].. `Low · World input · public/forecast · robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] several Waymo cars were set on fire during Los Angeles protests in June 2025 [uncertain: news recollection, not re-checkable this session]. · Data: none (news) · Model: Event-linked damage hazard and a zone closure flag. · [R11-063]</sub>
* **Robotaxi costs (capex, sensors, maps, insurance)**
   * **Robotaxi vehicle capex and amortisation**: Cost of a driverless car (base vehicle, sensors, compute) spread over its service life. *Values:* Current Waymo-type car about $100-200k all-in [U]; next-generation platforms (Zeekr, Ioniq 5) aimed lower [U]; Tesla Cybercab target under $30k [U]; amortised over about 300k mi: $0.10-0.67/mi [design].. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] $150k over 300k mi = $0.50/mi, vs about $0.12-0.15/mi depreciation for a human driver's hybrid; capex levels [U]. · Data: none public (company statements and analyst estimates) [U] · Model: Per-mile and per-day amortisation in the fleet ledger. · [R08-061]</sub>
   * **Depot real estate cost**: Rent for depot and parking space for each robotaxi in an expensive city. *Values:* NYC industrial rent about $20-40 per sq ft a year [U]; about 300-400 sq ft per car with aisles and chargers [design], so about $6k-16k per car a year.. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet (also taxi garages)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] calculation; rent levels [U]. · Data: none public (commercial real-estate reports) [U] · Model: Fixed cost per car slot; depot location trades rent against deadhead (R10-057). · [R08-064]</sub>
   * **Fleet operations overhead per vehicle**: Non-driving labour per car: depot staff who charge, clean and inspect, field teams who recover stuck cars, and fleet managers. *Values:* Depots of about 70 staff serving hundreds of cars (R09-045 [S]); 24/7 three-shift depots (R09-044 [S]); about $10-25 per vehicle-day [design]; remote assistance costed in R09-049.. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] via R09-044 and R09-045; per-car cost [design]. · Data: none public · Model: Fixed cost per car-day plus a per-incident field-recovery cost and delay. · [R08-063]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi capex split: wear vs obsolescence**: How much of a robotaxi's purchase cost is charged per mile driven (wear) and how much per day owned (hardware going out of date). This decides whether idle cars are cheap or expensive. *Values:* Time-based share of capex 0-100% (default 50%). $150k over 300k mi = $0.50/mi, or over 4-5 years = $82-103 per car-day. Sensor hardware generations about 4-5 years apart [U].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · robotaxi; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S, from list] R08-061: capex $100-200k over about 300k mi. [U] Waymo hardware generations. [design] The best fleet size and active-fleet policy flip between the two bases. · Data: none public · Model: Split amortisation in the ledger: the per-mile part counts as marginal cost and the per-day part as fixed cost (R08-067). Report the best fleet size under both. · [G1-050]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi insurance and liability**: Liability cover for a driverless fleet, often self-insured, priced per mile or per car. *Values:* About $0.03-0.15/mi [design]; fewer claims than human drivers (R08-057) but a heavy tail of severe events and legal exposure [U].. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Swiss Re and Waymo claims study; no public robotaxi premium found. · Data: none found · Model: Per-mile charge plus a rare catastrophic-event tail. · [R08-066]</sub>
   * **HD mapping and map upkeep**: Cost and time to map a new service area and keep it current, which limits where robotaxis may operate. *Values:* One-time mapping cost per km2 plus ongoing updates; lead time weeks to months; no published figures [U].. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] no public cost data. · Data: none found · Model: Geofence-expansion action with cost and delay (geofence in R01-008). · [R08-065]</sub>
   * **Sensor cleaning, calibration and replacement**: Keeping cameras and lidar clean and calibrated, including weather-driven cleaning and repairs after minor damage. *Values:* Onboard washer and air jets; depot cleaning each visit; recalibration after windshield or sensor replacement; 0-60 min per depot visit [design]; worse in snow, salt and rain [U].. `Low · Rule/price · hidden · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] AV sensor-cleaning design background; no public per-car cost. · Data: none found · Model: Sensor-dirt state grows with precipitation and road salt (R04); above a threshold the car returns to the depot. · [R08-062]</sub>
* **Responses to events and closures**
   * **Robotaxi handling of closures and police direction**: Robotaxis slow, re-route or stop at unmapped closures, cones or officers directing traffic. *Values:* Delay 1-10 min per encounter; remote-assistance call probability 0.1-0.5 [design]. `Med · World input · hidden · robotaxi, fleet`
