      * <sub>Evidence: [U]; cross-link R09-048 and R09-050 (remote assistance, stuck vehicles) · Data: none found · Model: When a route meets a fresh closure, add a delay or a remote-assistance event · [R03-055]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi service pauses and event exclusion zones**: Operator decision to geofence out or pause robotaxis around big events, protests or emergencies. *Values:* Zone exclusions for hours to days; e.g. Waymo paused downtown Los Angeles service during the June 2025 protests [U] [uncertain: news recollection, not re-checkable this session]. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] · Data: none found · Model: Service-area polygon toggled by event flags; requests inside are rejected · [R03-054]</sub>

### 17. Information and data (43)
*What the model is allowed to know (public only, no insider info) and the data that builds the world.*

* **Information ladder and the public vs insider line**
   * **Calendar awareness given to the agent**: Whether the agent is told the date, weekday, holiday name and known calendar effects, as an experiment arm like event awareness. *Values:* None / weekday+hour only / full calendar incl. holidays / calendar plus historical hourly profile. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] mirrors the team's with/without event-info test; holiday and weekday effects of 0.45-1.11x shown in TLC data · Data: none (benchmark setting) · Model: System-prompt variants; same world, different information · [R02-005]</sub>
   * **Information level ladder**: Which public information the agent can query, set as ordered levels so the value of each layer can be measured. *Values:* L0 own state and offers only; L1 + map and historical aggregates; L2 + calendar and weather forecast; L3 + public event calendar; L4 + in-app market signals; Oracle = true future demand (upper bound only, never scored). `High · Benchmark setting · public/forecast · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] own computation, HVFHV Jan 2023 (re-verified against the script output): zone-hour pay per engaged hour from the six prior non-holiday weekdays correlates 0.84 with the day-11 values (5,528 zone-hours), so L1 already carries most of the learnable spatial signal; event layers matter mainly on event days [design]. · Data: TLC trip records; NOAA/NWS; NYC Open Data events (see data sources) · Model: Each level is a set of enabled query tools; runs at several levels on the same seeds. · [R12-013]</sub>
   * **Public vs insider boundary rule**: The written rule for what counts as fair, public input at decision time, for each role. *Values:* Allowed: public sources, the driver-app view, own history (a robotaxi operator may also use its own fleet logs). Not allowed: future requests, unserved demand, rival platforms' internal data, the replayed day's own TLC records, realised weather. `High · Rule/price · agent sees · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] TLC monthly files arrive with about a 2-month lag (the team catalog lists files through Aug 2026 as of mid-Oct 2026; verified), so a real driver never has the day's trip records at the time. · Data: NYC TLC trip record page https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Every tool is tagged allowed or disallowed per role and checked by the harness. · [R12-021]</sub>
   * **Driver information networks**: Driver-run chat groups and crowd apps that share airport queue counts, police and tow activity, crashes and venue let-outs in near real time, separate from official alerts and the platform app. *Values:* Off, or a noisy community feed: delay 2-15 min, 10-30% of items wrong or stale, covering airports and big venues only [design]. The same feed can also go to 0-50% of rival bots [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · public/forecast · human app driver; taxi (sharing inside one's own fleet is R11-016)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Chris Blattman blog (Feb 2016), summarising a New Yorker piece: 'the Drivers Report', a WhatsApp group started by LaGuardia drivers to share airport traffic and demand. [S] The Outline on NYC cabbies' private group chats (title seen only). [S] Gridwise crowdsourced queue counts (R06-040). The size of the edge is a [design guess]. · Data: none found (private chats; Gridwise data proprietary). Generate the feed from sim state. · Model: Make feed items from true sim state (queue lengths, incidents, let-outs) with delay, dropout and error. Treat as allowed community information under the public-info rule (R12-021) [design]. Cross the agent's access with the bots' access. · [G1-005]</sub>
   * **Traffic information level**: How much traffic information the agent receives: none, typical map times, live speeds, or live speeds plus incidents and closures. *Values:* L0 none / L1 typical times / L2 live speeds / L3 live + incidents + gridlock alerts. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] mirrors the team's with/without public event information test · Data: none · Model: Harness flag selecting which traffic layers enter the observation. · [R10-045]</sub>
* **With vs without public info (events, weather, incidents)**
   * **Event information level (experiment arm)**: How much event information the model gets: none, a public calendar, calendar plus live feeds, or an oracle. *Values:* L0 none; L1 public calendar (venue, start, typical end, capacity); L2 = L1 + live alerts (MTA, DOT, 511NY, scores); L3 oracle (true end times and demand; upper bound only). `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] from the meeting notes; merge candidate with R12-008 (event-awareness arm) and R12-013 (information ladder), adding event-specific levels · Data: n/a · Model: Prompt variant plus tool availability; same random seeds and demand in every arm · [R03-060]</sub>
   * **Event-awareness prompt arm**: The team's planned A/B arm: the prompt either tells the model that public events (concerts, games, parades) move demand and gives it the event tool, or says nothing. *Values:* Off / mention only / mention plus event-calendar tool. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] team meeting plan; events create local demand surges and egress queues (cross-link R03); the effect is concentrated on event days (high there) and near zero otherwise. · Data: NYC Permitted Event Information https://data.cityofnewyork.us/d/tvpp-9vvx ; venue schedules for ticketed events · Model: Crossed with the information level (R12-013) on the same seeds so the difference isolates the prompt. · [R12-008]</sub>
   * **Public event calendar content**: Which event fields the agent gets and from which public sources. *Values:* None / NYC permitted events (name, type, borough, location, start and end, street closure; about one month of lead time) / + ticketed venues (MSG, Barclays, Yankee Stadium, Citi Field, USTA) / + pre-event attendance estimates. `High · Benchmark setting · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S via GitHub copies of the Socrata metadata] NYC Permitted Event Information (tvpp-9vvx) lists approved events 'that will occur within the next month', with 13 columns (event id, name, start and end, agency, type, borough, location, street side, closure type, community board, police precinct, CEMS id); ticketed events on private property are generally not street permits, so they need venue schedules [corrected by fact-check: scope and columns added]; the most frequent event types are youth and adult sports permits and farmers markets [S], so filtering for large events matters (cross-link R03). · Data: https://data.cityofnewyork.us/d/tvpp-9vvx (next month); https://data.cityofnewyork.us/d/bkfu-528j (historical); team and venue schedules · Model: Event tool returns events within a time window and radius; attendance only from figures public before the event. · [R12-016]</sub>
   * **Weather information level given to the agent (see R12-015)**: What weather the agent can see: none, current conditions only, a public forecast, or perfect foresight. R12-015 owns serving forecast vintages; this variable defines the weather view levels. *Values:* L0 none; L1 current conditions at own location; L2 L1 plus hourly forecast 0-48 h; L3 perfect future weather (oracle, ablation only) [design]. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] mirrors the team's with/without event-information test · Data: NWS API https://api.weather.gov ; Open-Meteo forecast and Previous Runs APIs · Model: Harness injects the chosen weather view into the observation each step. · [R04-049]</sub>
   * **Forecast error and false alarms**: Noise in published information: wrong end times, cancelled events still listed, unconfirmed incidents. *Values:* 5-20% of listings inaccurate [design]. `Med · Benchmark setting · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Electric Zoo day-1 cancellation; [design] · Data: n/a · Model: Perturb feed items; keep the truth hidden · [R03-063]</sub>
   * **Forecast error and lead time (feeds R12-015)**: How wrong the public forecast was, in timing and amount, at each lead time. Forecasts must be served as issued (R12-015). *Values:* Open-Meteo Previous Runs API stores forecasts at 1-7 day lead offsets, processed from early 2024; its Historical Forecast API (from 2021-22) stitches first forecast hours, so it is closer to observed weather than to a forecast [P*] (fact-check: confirmed in Open-Meteo's own website source on GitHub: lead offsets of 1-7 days, most models archived from January 2024, GFS 2 m temperature from March 2021, JMA GSM/MSM from 2018 [P*]); Jan 2015 blizzard forecast 20-30 in vs 9.8 in observed [U, P*]. `Med · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: Open-Meteo documentation copy [P*]; blizzard bust [U] · Data: Open-Meteo Previous Runs API https://open-meteo.com/en/docs/previous-runs-api ; IEM archived NWS text products https://mesonet.agron.iastate.edu/ ; NOAA NBM/NDFD archives [U] · Model: Give the agent the forecast actually issued before each time (from archives), or a synthetic forecast = truth plus calibrated timing and amount noise. · [R04-050]</sub>
   * **Information latency for unplanned events**: Delay between an incident starting and it appearing in public feeds or to the agent. *Values:* 0-15 min typical; MTA alerts 2-10 min [U]; social media faster but noisy. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; cross-link R10-028 · Data: n/a · Model: Publish each incident to the agent after a sampled delay · [R03-062]</sub>
   * **News, alerts and incident feed**: Time-stamped public alerts the agent may read: traffic incidents, subway outages, severe-weather warnings, news headlines. *Values:* None / official alerts only (511NY, MTA service alerts, NWS warnings) / + news headlines. `Med · Benchmark setting · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S, GitHub copies of the 511NY developer docs] the 511NY developer API gives incidents and events; it needs a developer key and is throttled to 10 calls per 60 s (verified); subway disruptions push riders into cars [U] (cross-link R03, R05). · Data: 511NY https://511ny.org/developers/doc ; MTA GTFS-realtime alerts https://new.mta.info/developers ; GDELT news archive [U] · Model: Replay archived alerts at their publish time; generated shocks get synthetic alerts. · [R12-017]</sub>
   * **Spatial weather awareness**: Whether the agent sees weather only where its car is, a citywide summary, or a radar-style zone map. *Values:* Own location / citywide / zone grid [design]. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] · Data: NOAA MRMS radar precipitation https://mrms.ncep.noaa.gov/ [U]; NEXRAD on AWS · Model: Observation granularity switch; matters only when the simulator models spatial weather (R04-057). · [R04-052]</sub>
   * **Weather forecast as issued**: The forecast the agent sees must be the one published before the decision, never the weather that actually happened. *Values:* None / text day-ahead forecast / hourly precipitation probability and temperature / perfect foresight (oracle only). `Med · Benchmark setting · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] serving observed weather as a 'forecast' leaks the future; [P] Open-Meteo data is CC BY 4.0 (free API non-commercial only); NWS forecasts and the NDFD forecast archive at NCEI are public [U]; the weather-to-demand link is R04. · Data: NWS API https://api.weather.gov (User-Agent header required) ; NOAA NCEI GHCN/LCD Central Park USW00094728 https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/ ; Open-Meteo https://open-meteo.com · Model: Store forecast vintages keyed by issue time and serve the latest vintage issued before the sim clock. · [R12-015]</sub>
* **What the agent sees: history, app signals and own state**
   * **Historical trip aggregates given**: Pre-computed public summaries of past trips that the agent can read, such as pickups and pay by zone and hour of week. *Values:* None / last 10 days / same season last year / full history; fields: pickups, pay per engaged hour, median wait, drop-off mix by zone x hour of week. `High · Benchmark setting · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] own computation, HVFHV Jan 2023: the mean of the six prior non-holiday weekdays predicts Wed 11 Jan zone-hour pickups with WAPE 0.134 (top-10 zones per hour overlap 82%), close to the 0.120 reached by a plain mean of 19 other weekdays [corrected by fact-check: 0.120 is a reference level that uses days after the target, not a proven floor]. · Data: NYC TLC trip records https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Pipeline computes aggregates only from data dated before the scenario window and serves them through a query tool. · [R12-014]</sub>
   * **In-app market signals**: What a real driver app would show: surge or bonus heatmap, busy areas, airport queue length. *Values:* Off / coarse heatmap refreshed every 5 min / fine heatmap every 1 min; taxis get none except via e-hail apps. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] Uber and Lyft driver apps show surge heatmaps and airport queues; the surge rules and queues are R06/R07, showing them is an information choice [design]; the high rating is a design judgement because the signal reveals current local demand. · Data: none found (platform data is not public; the simulator generates the signal from its own state) · Model: Render from the simulator's matching state with display lag and spatial coarsening. · [R12-019]</sub>
   * **Agent-visible history consistent with the sim's demand**: Whether the historical summaries shown to the agent are raw TLC numbers, or come from the same pipeline (censoring uplift, smoothing, era) that generates the sim's demand. *Values:* Options: raw TLC aggregates / pipeline-consistent aggregates from non-test days (default) / the generator's exact rates (unrealistically perfect, close to an oracle) [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]. [P, from list] R05-001 lifts demand for unserved trips (R05-066), while R12-014 serves summaries of past trips. · Data: NYC TLC trip records · Model: Compute the agent's history by running the sim's demand generator on held-out days, or apply the same uplift and smoothing to raw data. Log which version each arm uses. · [G1-034]</sub>
   * **Cost and vehicle-state information shown to the agent**: Which cost and vehicle data the agent can see: fuel or charge level, live fuel and charging prices, charger queues, maintenance warnings, cost per mile. *Values:* Minimal (gauge only); standard (gauge, prices, cost per mile); full (plus charger occupancy and breakdown risk) [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] mirrors the team's with/without event-information ablation. · Data: none · Model: Observation filter on the vehicle-state API. · [R08-068]</sub>
   * **Demand forecast given to the fleet controller**: The demand outlook supplied to the model at each call. *Values:* none / historical mean by zone and hour / noisy forecast / oracle (true future requests).. `Med · Benchmark setting · public/forecast · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: fleetsim: an oracle forecast gave the same wait as the historical mean but ordered 20-30% more moves and empty miles [S]. · Data: TLC trip records (historical zone-hour means); event calendars (R03) · Model: Forecast service with selectable quality; the event-information toggle is the with/without event awareness prompt test. · [R11-017]</sub>
   * **Map and geographic knowledge given**: How much map knowledge the harness supplies versus what the model must bring from pre-training. *Values:* None (world knowledge only) / zone list with names and adjacency / + travel-time matrix tool / + points of interest (airports, venues, hospitals, chargers). `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] NYC is heavily described on the web, so models start with strong priors [U]; a travel-time tool narrows the gap between models with and without NYC knowledge. · Data: TLC taxi zones (263 zones; IDs 264 Unknown, 265 Outside of NYC); OpenStreetMap NY extract https://download.geofabrik.de/north-america/us/new-york.html · Model: Map tools backed by the R01/R10 engine. · [R12-018]</sub>
   * **Own-history log access**: Whether the agent can query a structured log of its own past trips, earnings and idle spells, beyond its own notes. *Values:* None / current shift / all earlier days in the run. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S via R01] Haggag, McManus and Paci (2017): NYC taxi drivers learn by driving; [design] a log tool tests learning from data rather than from memory notes. · Data: none needed (simulator ledger) · Model: Query tool over the run's own ledger; on in learning-protocol runs. · [R12-020]</sub>
   * **Price-checking zones in the public rider app**: Drivers open the public rider app to see the quoted price, pickup ETA and nearby car icons for a zone before deciding whether to move there. *Values:* Settings: off / quote plus ETA for any zone, 1-3 min stale with +/-10-20% noise / capped at N checks per hour [design]. Car icons on rider apps are approximate [U].. `Med · Benchmark setting · public/forecast · human app driver; taxi (to read app competition); fleet operator (watching competitor prices)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] described in driver guides and forums. It is a public, legal reading of local surge and supply that R12-019 (driver-app signals) does not cover [design]. · Data: none (generated from the simulator's own matching state) · Model: Tool rider_quote(zone) returns a price multiplier and ETA from the sim state with delay, coarsening and noise. Each call costs simulated seconds and counts against the tool budget (R12-028). · [G2-004]</sub>
   * **Reliability information given to the agent**: Whether the agent sees only an expected travel time or also a spread (P10-P90) for candidate trips and moves. *Values:* none / mean only / mean + P90 / full distribution. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] lets the benchmark test risk-aware choices such as late-shift airport runs · Data: derived from the R10-008 noise model · Model: Harness flag controlling the ETA object returned in observations. · [R10-011]</sub>
   * **Rival visibility**: What the agent can see about other cars: nothing, a busy or surge heat map, airport queue counts, or live rival positions. *Values:* None | heat map (as in driver apps) | queue counts | full positions (unrealistic).. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: Uber app shows queue position and drivers ahead at JFK [S]; driver apps do not show rivals' cars [U]. · Data: n/a · Model: Observation levels in the API; realistic default = surge or busy heat map plus own queue position. · [R06-064]</sub>
* **Data era and replay date**
   * **Data era**: Which period's data calibrates demand, prices and speeds. *Values:* 2019 (pre-COVID) / 2022-2024 / 2025 onward (after congestion pricing started on 5 Jan 2025). `High · Benchmark setting · public/forecast · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] TLC catalog row counts (team R02 data, re-verified): yellow raw rows per day rose from 123,670 (Oct 2024) to 142,861 (Oct 2025); [S via R10] congestion pricing changed CBD speeds, so every module must share one era. · Data: NYC TLC trip records · Model: Era tag on every calibrated parameter; mixing eras fails validation. · [R12-043]</sub>
   * **Scenario replay date**: Which real calendar date (or synthetic typical day) an episode replays, chosen by the benchmark designer. *Values:* Ordinary weekday, Friday, Saturday, Sunday, holiday, event day; or synthetic median day. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023: Christmas 2023 had 45% of a normal Monday's yellow trips; NYE night pays +60% per engaged hour [P*] · Data: NYC TLC trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Harness picks dates from a stratified day-type list; same date and seed across models (common random numbers) · [R02-003]</sub>
   * **Rule-set vintage matched to the replay date**: The fares, fees, pay floors and caps used must match the date of the demand being replayed, or be a declared counterfactual. *Values:* Key dates: taxi fare 18 Dec 2022; HVFHV distance-factor reset 13 Mar 2023; CBD toll 5 Jan 2025; HVFHV floor and access rules 1 Aug 2025; CPI step each 1 March; Green Rides step each 1 Jan; HVFHV wrongful-deactivation law 28 Jul 2026; CRZ step-up 2028 [U] [corrected by fact-check: 'lease caps 2 May 2025' removed; that date is a later amendment to 58-21, not a change in the cap amounts]. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] amendment histories in the rules archive; mismatch can bias revenue by 5-15% [design guess]; cross-link R10-064 · Data: 35 RCNY and Admin Code via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive · Model: Versioned rule tables with effective dates; harness asserts data date and rule date agree · [R07-065]</sub>
* **Replay vs synthetic world (demand, weather)**
   * **Demand source mode**: Whether riders come from an exact historical day, a resample of it, a fitted generator, or a hybrid. *Values:* Exact replay / bootstrap resample / parametric synthetic / replay plus perturbation. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] TLC records hold completed trips only, so replay caps demand at served trips (cross-link R05-069, R05-066); [P] tabmemcheck README shows GPT-4 completing random rows of Iris and GPT-3.5 completing Titanic and UCI Wine rows, so exact replay of well-known data invites recall. · Data: NYC TLC trip records · Model: Generator fitted per zone x hour of week with day effects; exact replay kept for validation. · [R12-039]</sub>
   * **Weather replay vs synthetic generator, and seeds (see R03-064)**: Whether a scenario replays the real weather of a historical day (matched to its TLC demand) or uses generated weather, and how weather randomness is shared across runs. Pair with the R03-064 memorisation guard. *Values:* Replay (realistic; models may remember famous days such as Ida) / stochastic (Markov chain on hourly classes fitted to Central Park) / hybrid; same weather seed for all models in a comparison [design]. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; memorisation risk noted by R05 · Data: NOAA LCD; TLC trip records · Model: Weather module interface replay(date) or generate(month, seed); common random numbers across models. · [R04-055]</sub>
* **Data pipeline and calibration**
   * **Latent vs observed demand (censoring)**: Trip data shows only served trips, so real demand in busy, short-supplied places is higher than the records. *Values:* Unserved share 5-20% at peaks [design guess]; zero cancelled requests in TLC files. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] Gammelli et al. 2020 censored Gaussian processes; [P*] local HVFHV Parquet schema has no cancel or unserved-request field · Data: TLC trip records (censored); method arXiv 2001.07402 · Model: Lift demand where supply was tight (long waits, surge) using a censored model; expose uplift as a scenario knob · [R05-066]</sub>
   * **Population sampling fraction and density scaling**: Whether the simulator runs all riders and background cars in the agent's area or only a sample, and if a sample, how the matching radius, queues and road capacity are rescaled. *Values:* 100% / 25% / 10% sample [design]. With cars placed at random, the distance to the nearest idle car scales with 1/sqrt(density), so a 10% sample gives about 3.2x longer pickups unless rescaled. Real data are much flatter across places: across weekday zone-hours, the elasticity of the median Uber on-scene ETA to trips per hour is about -0.03 to -0.10 depending on controls (pooled -0.08, within hour of day -0.09, within borough x hour -0.03) [P]. The median of zone-hour medians is about 3.3-3.9 min on Staten Island vs 2.0-2.3 min in Manhattan, depending on the minimum-trips filter [P; corrected by fact-check, was 3.45 vs 2.12, which is one filter choice]. Within the same zone and hour of day, busier days have LONGER waits (elasticity about +0.2), because demand shocks drain idle cars [P, added by fact-check].. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden · all; fleet scale ladder (R11-067)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own calc on HVFHV Jan 2023 weekdays, Uber trips (on-scene time is filled for Uber rows only). [S] Frechette, Lizzeri & Salz 2019 (AER 109(8)): increasing returns in taxi matching (via R05-042). [U] MATSim rescales flow and storage capacity for sampled runs. R11-068 covers runtime only. The cross-sectional slope mixes density with supply response, so it is a calibration target, not a causal density law. · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records (request_datetime and on_scene_datetime; Uber rows only) · Model: Prefer full density in the agent's region. If sampling, shrink map distances by sqrt(f) or rescale the matcher. Require a calibration test showing that pickup ETA vs demand matches BOTH the cross-sectional TLC slope and the positive within-zone-hour slope. · [G2-024]</sub>
   * **As-of timestamp discipline**: Every piece of information carries an issue time no later than the sim clock, and the harness enforces it. *Values:* Strict (issue time <= now) / lagged (issue time <= now minus the real publication lag). `Med · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] look-ahead leakage is a standard failure of backtests and of LLM forecasting evaluations [U]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Data store keyed by (valid time, issue time); unit tests that no tool returns rows issued in the future. · [R12-022]</sub>
   * **Demand timestamp basis (request vs pickup time)**: Whether demand is fitted on request time (app trips only) or pickup time (all trips; yellow taxis have no request time), and how taxi demand is shifted back to request time. *Values:* HVFHV Jan 2023: request-to-pickup median 3.85 min and p90 7.92 min (Uber 3.72/7.42, Lyft 4.22/9.80). Binned by request time instead of pickup time, 30.6% of trips fall in a different 15-min bin and 7.7% in a different hour [P, re-verified exactly by fact-check]. on_scene_datetime is filled for 13.59M of 18.48M trips, essentially Uber only [P]. Yellow has no request time: street hails are close to pickup time, and e-hails need an imputed wait [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own re-check on the local HVFHV Jan 2023 file (18,479,031 trips, request_datetime filled for all). Fitting on pickup time moves let-out waves and rush peaks later by the wait. · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records (request_datetime, on_scene_datetime, pickup_datetime) https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Fit app demand on request_datetime; for yellow, use pickup time for hails. Check that simulated waits reproduce the observed pickup-time profile. · [G2-021]</sub>
   * **Driver-level behaviour data**: Datasets with persistent driver IDs, used to fit shift lengths, stopping rules and airport choices. *Values:* 2010-2013 NYC yellow trips with hashed medallion and licence IDs (FOIL); Chicago TNP Drivers monthly (trips per driver, MULTIPLE_TNPS).. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Chris Whong used the 2013 FOIL data for airport waits [S]; Chicago dataset documentation [S]. · Data: https://databank.illinois.edu/datasets/IDB-9610843 [U]; https://catalog.data.gov/dataset/transportation-network-providers-drivers · Model: Fit hazard models offline; carry the parameters to 2025 with a drift adjustment. · [R06-070]</sub>
   * **Event residual extraction (calibration)**: Method to learn event demand signatures from TLC data by subtracting a zone x hour x weekday baseline. *Values:* Residual per 15 min within ~2 km of the venue; templates per venue type. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] the calculations in this dossier (US Open, NYE, Broadway, MSG) · Data: TLC yellow and HVFHV records; Retrosheet; event calendars · Model: Robust baseline (median of the same weekday +/- 4 weeks) and residual templates per venue type · [R03-066]</sub>
   * **Sparse cells and smoothing of rates**: How rate tables estimated from few observations (outer-zone x 15-min demand, pay ratios, travel times) are smoothed, so that noise does not create fake hotspots that the agent learns. *Values:* HVFHV Jan 2023, 20 non-holiday weekdays: 11.3% of zone x hour cells average under 5 trips/h and 5.4% under 1 (counting all 263 zones; 10.6% and 4.7% over the 261 zones with any trip). 48.6% of the ~55.6k observed OD pairs average under 1 trip/day. Methods: empirical-Bayes pooling toward borough-hour means, neighbour averaging, gravity fallback. OD smoothing is already in R05-006; R10-062 uses OD neighbour averaging for travel times.. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Re-run in this fact-check on the local HVFHV file (55,618 OD pairs vs the critic's 55,974; shares agree within 0.1 point). [P, from list] R05-006 (Bayesian OD smoothing) and R10-062 (neighbour averaging of travel times). · Data: NYC TLC trip records https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Shrinkage estimator per table, with a documented prior and strength. Test that a model with memory gains nothing from cells that smoothing removes. · [G1-023]</sub>
   * **Supply-constrained bias in fitted weather and event effects**: Rain, event and holiday uplifts estimated from completed trips understate the real jump in requests, because extra requests in busy hours are capped by the cars available. This applies the censoring in R05-066 to effect estimates; merge candidate with R05-066. *Values:* Correction factor 1.0-1.5x on fitted uplifts in tight hours [design]. Diagnostic (HVFHV Jan 2023, 20 non-holiday weekdays): daily trips vs median request-to-pickup wait r = +0.62 raw and +0.81 after removing day-of-week means, so busier days are tighter.. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Re-run in this fact-check (g1/a7.py plus a de-meaned check): raw 0.62, de-meaned 0.81 (the gap critic had 0.80). Part of the correlation is the post-holiday ramp within January. [S] Brodeur & Nield, IZA DP 9986 (2016) and JEBO 152 (2018): Uber rides per hour about 25% higher in rain vs about 4% for taxis, explained by surge-induced supply. A 2017 working-paper version with Uber data to Dec 2016 reports about 18% vs 5%. [uncertain: which pair the JEBO article reports; snippets conflict and iza.org was unreachable]. · Data: HVFHV request, on-scene and pickup times; NOAA hourly weather · Model: Estimate uplifts with wait-adjusted or censored (Tobit-style) regressions, or back out requests by inverting the sim's matcher. Cross-link R05-066. · [G1-020]</sub>
   * **TLC data gaps that look like events**: Missing days in the trip files that look like demand collapses if replayed. *Values:* Yellow 2023: Sep 21 at 33% of normal, Sep 22-24 at ~4-5% of normal. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc: daily counts of the SHA-256-verified yellow 2023 files · Data: TLC trip records · Model: Flag and exclude or impute days below 60% of the same-weekday median unless a known event explains them · [R03-067]</sub>
   * **TLC timestamp and DST artefacts**: TLC timestamps are local time without zone, so DST nights create impossible or inflated durations that must be cleaned before calibration. *Values:* Fall-back night 2023: 795 of 8,435 trips at 01h with zero/negative duration; spring: 992 trips starting 01h with ~84 min mean duration inflated by the skipped hour [added by fact-check: the yellow 2023 file, whose SHA-256 matches TLC's, is missing about 95% of trips from about 13:00 on 21 Sep to 24 Sep 2023 (4,633-5,732 trips/day vs about 105,000); flag or drop these days]. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC yellow 2023 · Data: NYC TLC trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Localize with tz rules, flag ambiguous hour, drop or repair durations before fitting · [R02-065]</sub>
   * **Trip-record cleaning rules**: Filters applied to TLC records before fitting speeds (bad timestamps, zero distance, impossible speeds). *Values:* drop duration under 60 s or over 3 h, speed over 60 mph or under 1 mph, zero distance [design]; in 2023 yellow data these rules remove 1.15% (<60 s), 0.09% (>3 h), 2.02% (zero distance), 0.06% (>60 mph) and 0.18% (<1 mph) of trips [P*]. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Published NYC travel-time studies drop trips under 10 s or over 24 h [S] · Data: TLC trip records · Model: Documented filter step in the data pipeline with counts removed per rule. · [R10-063]</sub>
   * **Data licence filter**: Only data whose terms allow use, and ideally redistribution, in a public benchmark may feed the simulator. *Values:* Allowed: NYC and NY State open data, US federal data (NOAA, EIA, NLR, formerly NREL), OpenStreetMap (ODbL, share-alike on derived databases), Open-Meteo (data CC BY 4.0; free API non-commercial only). Excluded: Google-Maps-derived travel times, paid feeds (e.g. PredictHQ), scraped app data. `Low · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Open-Meteo README: data CC BY 4.0, APIs 'free for open-source developers and non-commercial use', code AGPLv3; [P*] NREL is now the National Laboratory of the Rockies and its developer API moved to developer.nlr.gov (developer.nrel.gov stopped resolving 29 May 2026) [corrected by fact-check]; the other terms are [U] and must be checked on portals that were blocked here. · Data: See the data sources table · Model: Licence field in the data catalog; the build fails on unknown licences; ship fitted parameters where raw redistribution is unclear. · [R12-068]</sub>
   * **Weather data join and cleaning rules**: Rules for turning raw weather files into simulator inputs aligned with trip records. *Values:* LCD 'T' = trace precipitation; report types FM-15 (routine hourly), FM-16 (special), SOD (daily summary); LCD exports in metric or imperial units [uncertain: not re-checked; NOAA host blocked]; align to America/New_York local time [P*]. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Public LCD cleaning notebooks [P*] · Data: NOAA LCD documentation https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/products/land-based-station/local-climatological-data · Model: Pipeline step with unit tests: hourly bins aligned to TLC pickup hour; trace treated as 0.001 in. · [R04-059]</sub>

### 18. Benchmark settings (86)
*How the evaluation is run, scored and shown: model, prompt, cadence, learning protocol, seeds, baselines, cost.*

* **Model under test and inference**
   * **Model under test**: The LLM or agent system whose decisions drive the car or fleet; the main independent variable of the benchmark. *Values:* 5 frontier models for the headline board (e.g. Claude Fable 5.1, Claude Opus 5.5, OpenAI and Google flagships) plus cheap development models; one model per run, or several per city in arena mode. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Vending-Bench 2 end balances spread from about $9.2k to $15.5k across the top 7 of 66 entries, and about $7.5k-$15.5k across the top 10 (29 Sep 2026 capture, D01/D11) [corrected by fact-check: was 'across top models']; Vending-Bench 1: 'All models exhibit very high variance across their five runs' (verified in the paper text). · Data: Provider model docs, e.g. https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models · Model: One provider adapter per model behind an identical tool schema, observation and budget. · [R12-001]</sub>
   * **Context-management policy**: How much run history the model sees at each step, and how older history is trimmed, summarised or moved into memory. *Values:* Sliding window of 10k / 30k / 60k tokens; server-side compaction (Claude default trigger 150k tokens, beta); memory tool on or off. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Vending-Bench 1: GPT-4o mini with a 60k-token memory did worse than with 30k, and collapse was not explained by the context filling up (Pearson r = 0.167 between days until sales stop and days until full memory) (verified in the paper text); compaction default 150k verified in the bundled Claude API reference. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Harness-level policy fixed in the frozen track; token counts logged per step. · [R12-004]</sub>
   * **Frozen harness vs bring-your-own agent**: Whether every model runs inside one fixed agent loop (measures the model) or teams may bring their own scaffold (measures the whole system and its cost). *Values:* Frozen: fixed tools, prompts, context policy and budgets / BYO: any scaffold, reaching the simulator only through the same metered tool API. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P via D10] SWE-bench bash-only and Terminal-Bench's separate --agent and --model flags exist because scaffolds move scores enough to need separate tracks. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Two leaderboard tracks; BYO tool calls metered by the tool server. · [R12-005]</sub>
   * **Model snapshot and serving provider**: The exact model version string and the API or host that serves it, pinned and recorded for every run. *Values:* Dated snapshot vs moving alias; first-party API vs third-party host vs self-hosted weights; quantisation level for open models. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S via team phase-1 dossier B, GitHub copy of the Vending-Bench 1 leaderboard] Kimi K2 Thinking averaged $1,296 net worth via Moonshot's own API vs $649 via a third-party API; on the leaderboard's minimum-net-worth metric the order flips ($418.54 third-party vs $354.00 Moonshot) [corrected by fact-check: source was misattributed to D11, and the flip was omitted]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Run manifest records model id, provider, region, quantisation and date; leaderboard refuses unpinned aliases. · [R12-002]</sub>
   * **Reasoning effort and sampling settings**: The thinking or effort level and sampling parameters used on every call. *Values:* Effort low / medium / high / xhigh / max (Claude Opus 5.5 defaults to medium); temperature only where the API accepts it; max output 1k-16k tokens per call. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Claude API reference (cached 6 Oct 2026): effort levels, Opus 5.5 default 'medium', non-default sampling rejected on current Claude models (verified); [P* via team phase-1 notes, GitHub copy of Andon Labs posts] Opus 4.8 scored much higher at High than at Max effort on Vending-Bench 2, which Andon attributes to less context compaction [corrected by fact-check: benchmark version specified]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Fixed per leaderboard track; an effort x model ablation on a subset of seeds. · [R12-003]</sub>
   * **Development model tier**: Cheap models used to build, debug and red-team the simulator and harness before paying for frontier runs. *Values:* Claude Haiku 5.5 ($0.10 in / $0.50 out per MTok for prompts of 100K tokens or fewer; $0.50 / $2.50 above), Sonnet 5.5 ($2 / $10), open-weight Qwen models self-hosted or hosted; also usable as bot drivers. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Claude price table (cached 6 Oct 2026): Haiku 5.5 costs 1/40 of Opus 5.5 per token, but its tokenizer counts the same text as about 30% more tokens, so it is about 1/31 per unit of text [corrected by fact-check: long-prompt tier and tokenizer added]; the team's '80-90% of the intelligence at 1/100 of the cost' for Qwen is unverified [U]. · Data: Claude pricing https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing; Qwen model cards (Hugging Face, not reachable here) · Model: Dev configuration profile; never mixed into headline results. · [R12-006]</sub>
* **System prompt and framing**
   * **Base system prompt and paraphrase set**: The fixed instructions that set the task, rules and tools, plus a few paraphrases to check that rankings do not hinge on wording. *Values:* Minimal / standard / detailed-rules prompt; 2-3 meaning-preserving paraphrases per variant. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Sclar, Choi, Tsvetkov and Suhr 2023 (arXiv 2310.11324), FormatSpread repo: LLM results shift with meaning-preserving prompt formatting; the README gives no magnitude, and the size of the effect for long agentic runs is [U]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Prompts versioned with the benchmark; headline uses the standard prompt; paraphrases run on a seed subset. · [R12-007]</sub>
   * **Objective statement and cost framing**: What the prompt says to maximise and which costs it makes salient. *Values:* Net profit per online hour / total daily net profit / gross revenue; costs itemised in the prompt vs only in the ledger; optional constraint text such as 'be home by 02:00'. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Vending-Bench 1: setting the $2 daily fee to zero did not raise sales; without cost pressure GPT-4o mini 'appears to get stuck in loops' (verified in the paper text). · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: One canonical objective for the headline (net profit per online hour after all costs); others as ablations. · [R12-009]</sub>
   * **Role framing**: Which operator the model plays, which fixes its rules, costs and allowed actions. *Values:* Human app driver (Uber/Lyft, NYC HVFHV) / yellow or green taxi (street hail plus e-hail) / robotaxi operator (no driver; usually fleet level). `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · human app driver, taxi, robotaxi`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] own computation, TLC Jan 2023 weekdays: HVFHV driver pay $54.04 per engaged hour vs yellow $109.84 gross per engaged hour (total_amount incl. surcharges, tolls and card tips only, not driver take-home; 'engaged' = passenger on board), so roles face different economics [corrected by fact-check: cash tips are not recorded in total_amount]. · Data: NYC TLC trip records https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Role selects a rule pack (fares, fees, hours rules, actions) from R07-R09; persona details are R09-004. · [R12-011]</sub>
   * **Strategy hints**: Whether the prompt includes driver know-how such as airport runs, the Manhattan core at rush hour, or avoiding drop-offs that strand the car. *Values:* None / generic tips / NYC-specific playbook. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P via D10] Project Vend 2: 'Among the most impactful changes we made was forcing Claudius to follow procedures'; [S via R01] Haggag, McManus and Paci (2017, AEJ: Applied): neighbourhood experience raises NYC taxi drivers' productivity. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Hints off for the headline so the benchmark measures the model's own strategy; hints as an ablation. · [R12-010]</sub>
   * **Simulation disclosure**: Whether the model is told it is in a benchmark simulation or framed as doing a real job, which can change its conduct. *Values:* Disclosed / neutral / framed as real. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P* via D01/D11] Andon Labs: Claude Fable 5 skipped paying a customer because 'customers are part of the simulation anyway'; effect on profit unmeasured [U]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Framing twins on a seed subset; conduct metrics reported by framing. · [R12-012]</sub>
* **Observation, actions and decision cadence**
   * **Action space and granularity**: The set of moves the agent can make and their spatial and time resolution. *Values:* Accept / decline offer; reposition to a zone (263 TLC zones) or a point; wait N minutes; cruise loop (taxi); go offline or take a break; go home; refuel or charge; set destination filter; optional route choice (R10-042). `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] MaaSSim models drivers who 'reject/accept incoming requests', 'strategically re-position while being idle' and 'opt-out of the system' (verified in README); [design]; the spatial unit itself is R01-009. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Typed tools with validation; the same set for every model within a role. · [R12-025]</sub>
   * **Decision cadence and wake-up rule**: When the model is called: on each event, on a fixed tick, or when it asks to be woken. *Values:* Event-driven (offer, drop-off, arrival, idle timer every 5 / 10 / 15 sim-min) / fixed tick of 1 / 5 / 15 min / agent-set wait(until). `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P via D10] YC-Bench jumps to the next event and Vending-Bench clones charge sim-minutes per call; [design] calls per sim-day set both how much skill can show and the run cost (R12-063). · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Discrete-event core (SimPy-style priority queue) wakes the agent on events plus an idle heartbeat. · [R12-026]</sub>
   * **Wall-clock coupling and model latency**: Whether simulated time stops while the model thinks, or slow answers cost sim time and missed offers. *Values:* Paused (turn-based) / latency-charged (sim time advances by measured latency x factor) / real time. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S via R06-024] real ride offers time out after about 10-15 s; long reasoning calls can exceed that [U], so latency modes would heavily penalise slow models. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Headline paused; latency-charged as a separate track; latency recorded on every call. · [R12-027]</sub>
   * **Invalid or malformed action policy**: What happens when the model returns an illegal, malformed or impossible action. *Values:* No-op with error message / up to K retries / default safe action (wait) / penalty per invalid call. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Vending-Bench 1 runs fell into 'meltdown' loops (verified in the paper text); [design] cheap models break format more often, so this policy can dominate their scores. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Validate against the tool schema, return the error, count invalid calls as a reported metric. · [R12-029]</sub>
   * **Observation and action format**: How the state is rendered to the model and how its actions come back. *Values:* JSON / natural-language summary / ASCII grid map / rendered map image (multimodal); actions via strict tool schemas vs parsed free text. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] the MIT-licensed LPSim (Large-scale Parallel Simulator, GitHub Xuan-1998) exposes get_state(), a to_prompt_context() natural-language view and a TOOL_DEFINITION for OpenAI/Anthropic function calling, for LLM fleet control [corrected by fact-check: '(MIT)' was the licence, not the institution]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Default JSON plus a short text summary with strict tool schemas; image map as an ablation. · [R12-024]</sub>
   * **Observation content per step**: The state fields shown to the agent each time it is asked to act. *Values:* Clock, zone and coordinates, status (idle / en route / occupied / charging / offline), fuel or battery, earnings and costs so far, hours online, the pending offer (fields per R05-036), nearby signals. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] tomslee/ridehail reports vehicle time as P1 (waiting for a trip), P2 (en route to pickup) and P3 (with passenger), the standard state split (verified in README); [design]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Fixed observation schema versioned with the benchmark. · [R12-023]</sub>
   * **Tool-call time cost and per-step budget**: How much sim time information queries take, and how many tool calls are allowed per decision. *Values:* 0 or 1 sim-min per query; 3-10 tool calls per step; optional daily query cap. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P via D10] ProsusAI vending-bench charges 5-75 sim-min per tool call inside a working day (5 for reads, prices and notes; 25 for email, search and payments; 75 for stocking and cash collection) [corrected by fact-check: was 5-45]; [design] without any cost an agent can query everything every step for free (cross-link R09-067). · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Per-tool time-cost table and a call counter enforced by the harness. · [R12-028]</sub>
   * **Within-step update order**: The fixed order in which request arrivals, surge updates, batch matching, bot moves, agent actions and state updates run when events share a timestamp. *Values:* A fixed, documented order, e.g. arrivals -> surge computed from the pre-match state -> matching -> bots -> agent -> state. Ties broken by a seeded random draw or by entity id. Sensitivity test with 2-3 alternative orders [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]. [P, from list] R12-026 specifies a SimPy-style priority queue but no ordering for events with the same timestamp. · Data: none needed · Model: Priority classes in the event queue; a unit test that results do not depend on container order; report the score change under alternative orders. · [G1-026]</sub>
* **Horizon, episodes and the learning protocol**
   * **Memory carry-over scope**: Which notes and learnings persist between days and between runs. *Values:* None / within one 10-day run / across runs (lifelong notes) / across models (not allowed). `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] the learning test only works if notes persist across days; [P] own computation: a 10-day window already holds most of the learnable weekday pattern (WAPE 0.134 vs 0.120 for a plain 19-weekday mean) [corrected by fact-check: 0.120 is a reference level, not a proven floor]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Memory store snapshotted at day boundaries and cleared per protocol arm. · [R12-033]</sub>
   * **End-of-episode valuation and go-home rule**: How the final position, charge and unfinished trips are valued, so the agent cannot game the last hour. *Values:* End at home required, or penalised by the deadhead cost home; leftover charge valued at energy cost; trip in progress completed or prorated. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Vending-Bench 1 valued inventory at cost and its highest-net-worth run prioritised storage over cash; [design] the team's 'take me home' feature needs a terminal rule (cross-link R09-020). · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Terminal settlement step in the ledger. · [R12-032]</sub>
   * **Episode horizon and shift window**: The length of one scored episode and when it may start and end. *Values:* One shift (8-12 h) / one day (24 h, agent picks its shift) / 10-day block / week; start at home at a fixed or chosen time; starting fuel or charge. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Vending-Bench 1: agents often stopped selling after some sim-days (mean days until sales stop 8-102 by model; verified in Table 1), so long horizons expose derailment that short ones hide. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Scenario config sets horizon and start state; shift start inside a day is an agent action. · [R12-031]</sub>
   * **Learning-gain control arms**: Comparison runs that turn 'score on day 11' into a measure of learning. *Values:* Day 11 with memory vs (a) the same model with empty memory, (b) with expert-written notes, (c) with notes written by another model. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Anthropic, 'A statistical approach to model evaluations' (read directly): report paired differences on shared items; [design]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Same seeds across arms; learning gain is the paired difference. · [R12-038]</sub>
   * **Memory format, size cap and reflection step**: The form and size of stored learnings, and whether the agent gets an end-of-day turn to write them. *Values:* Free-text notes capped at 2k / 8k / 20k tokens; key-value store; table; end-of-day reflection turn on or off. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Vending-Bench 1 gave a scratchpad, key-value store and vector database (verified); [P via D11] YC-Bench found scratchpad use 'the strongest predictor of success'. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Memory tool with a hard size cap; reflection prompt at the end of each sim-day. · [R12-034]</sub>
   * **Repetition design (the 'x100')**: How the team's '10 days x 100 runs' is read: 100 independent 10-day runs, or 100 passes over the same days with accumulating notes. *Values:* Independent replicates (fresh memory each) / repeated epochs with persistent memory / epochs with demand redrawn each pass. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] repeated exact replays let notes memorise specific requests, which is overfitting, not learning; [P] own computation: even a 19-weekday mean leaves zone-hour WAPE of about 0.12, so accuracy well beyond that on replayed days is a warning sign of memorisation [corrected by fact-check: was 'gains beyond that are memorisation'; covariate-aware learners could beat a plain mean somewhat]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Protocol config; demand redrawn from the generator on every epoch. · [R12-037]</sub>
   * **Test day choice (day 11)**: Which day the learned agent is tested on. *Values:* Next day / same weekday a week later / weekend / event day / shock day / different season. `Med · Benchmark setting · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] own computation (re-verified): in the previous 10 days a weekday target has 5-8 same-type days but a weekend target only 2-3; rolling day-type-aware zone-hour WAPE ran 0.11-0.28 for HVFHV (median 0.174) and 0.14-0.34 for yellow (median 0.198). · Data: NYC TLC trip records · Model: Test pool stratified by day type; learning gain reported per stratum. · [R12-036]</sub>
   * **Training window composition (days 1-10)**: Which calendar days make up the learning days. *Values:* 10 consecutive days including weekends and holidays / weekdays only / matched day types; same month vs mixed seasons. `Med · Benchmark setting · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] own computation, HVFHV Jan 2023 (re-verified): predicting 11 Jan from all of 1-10 Jan (including the New Year holidays) gives zone-hour WAPE 0.238 vs 0.134 from the six non-holiday weekdays, so the window decides what can be learned. · Data: NYC TLC trip records · Model: Scenario builder picks windows from a calendar table with holiday flags (R02). · [R12-035]</sub>
   * **What state carries over between days**: Which physical and account states persist from days 1-10 into day 11: car position, fuel or charge, rating, acceptance and cancellation rates, weekly quest progress, weekly lease, weekly hours used and fatigue. *Values:* Options: reset everything daily / carry position and energy / carry account standing / carry everything (default for the learning test) [design]. Weekly items: quests (R07-033), leases (R02-045), and the TLC passenger-time cap of 10 h per 24 h and 60 h per week (R09-005).. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · all; single car and fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S, from list] Weekly quests, leases and hour caps; [design]. R12-033 covers memory notes only. [corrected: the TLC cap counts passenger time only]. · Data: none needed · Model: State snapshot at each day boundary, with a carry flag per field. The learning protocol (R12-033) declares which flags are set. · [G1-032]</sub>
* **Scenarios, seeds, difficulty and leakage**
   * **Difficulty preset and noise scale**: Bundled settings from easy to hard, plus a multiplier on randomness. *Values:* Easy: Manhattan only, deterministic travel times, no rivals, full information / Medium: whole city, stochastic travel, bot drivers / Hard: shocks, EV range, fatigue, difficult riders; noise scale 0-1.5x calibrated. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P via D11] CEO-Bench exposes competitor strength as a difficulty knob; [P] tomslee/ridehail ships village / town / city presets (plus game and game-big); [P] own computation: real zone-hour day-to-day error is WAPE about 0.12 for a plain 19-weekday mean [corrected by fact-check: a reference level, not a proven floor]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Preset files over module switches; leaderboard per preset. · [R12-047]</sub>
   * **Memorisation guard for real events**: Controls that stop a model from using pretraining memory of famous real events in the no-information arm. *Values:* Date shift (present 2023 as an unnamed year), renamed venues and events, injected synthetic events, held-out dates after the model's cutoff. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] validity requirement; complements R12-046 (contamination probes) · Data: n/a · Model: Scenario obfuscation layer; check the no-information arm on days with events removed · [R03-064]</sub>
   * **Runs per model and statistical power**: How many seeds and repeats per model and scenario are needed to separate models. *Values:* 5-100 runs per model; report means, paired differences and clustered confidence intervals. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S via D11, inference] observed run-to-run CVs of 0.07-1.0 in business sims mean 5 unpaired runs detect only gaps of about 0.12-1.8x the mean (about 1.77 x CV at 80% power) [corrected by fact-check: was 0.35-1.8x, which is the CV 0.2 to 1.0 range]; [P] Anthropic: clustered standard errors 'can be over three times as large as naive standard errors'; use power analysis. Impact is on ranking reliability, not on expected profit. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Pilot on a dev model to estimate variance, then size the run count by power analysis. · [R12-041]</sub>
   * **Scenario day-type mix**: Weights of ordinary, weekend, holiday, rain or snow, event and shock days in the test pool. *Values:* e.g. 50% ordinary weekdays, 20% weekends, 10% holidays, 10% bad weather, 10% big-event days. `High · Benchmark setting · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] own computation (re-verified): HVFHV pay per engaged hour was $72.15 on 1 Jan 2023 (holiday, median wait 6.05 min) vs about $54 on ordinary weekdays, so the mix shifts absolute levels. · Data: TLC trip records; NOAA weather; NYC permitted events · Model: Stratified scenario sampler; scores reported per stratum and pooled with fixed weights. · [R12-042]</sub>
   * **Seeds and common random numbers**: Separate random streams per component so every model faces the same riders, traffic and shocks. *Values:* One seed per scenario with streams for demand, travel time, rider behaviour, rivals and shocks; optional antithetic pairs. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] own computation, HVFHV Jan 2023 weekdays (re-verified): daily trips vary with CV 12.4% but pay per engaged hour only 1.4%, so unpaired day draws would swamp model gaps of a few percent; [P* via D11] CEO-Bench uses independent RNGs per simulator component. Impact is on ranking reliability, not on expected profit. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Streams keyed by (component, entity, period); the same seed list for every model. · [R12-040]</sub>
   * **Solo vs shared-city arena mode**: Whether each model drives alone among scripted bots, or several models drive in one city at once and compete for riders. *Values:* Solo plus bots / arena with 2-5 models, seat rotation and mirrored seeds. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P* via D11] Vending-Bench Arena: agents sharing one location formed price cartels; [S via R06/D07] rankings differ from solo play (cross-link R06-060) [uncertain: D07's fact-check found mixed evidence; Andon says Claude models were historically worse in multiplayer, but its Arena page shows Claude winning rounds 2, 4 and 6 of the first six]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Arena scheduler rotates start zones and seeds; scores per seat and pooled. · [R12-049]</sub>
   * **Warm-up and starting state of the background world**: A burn-in period, or a start from replayed in-progress trips, so that bots, airport queues, open requests and surge are in a steady state when the agent starts. *Values:* Burn-in of 2-4 h of simulated time before the agent enters [design], or start from the trips in progress at t0 and historical airport queue lengths. JFK taxi holding-lot waits have been reported at about 2-3 h (R06-041, NBC New York) [S] [uncertain: R01-052 flags the figure as not re-checked].. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] Starting with empty queues and idle bots hands the agent free airport fares and easy offers in the first hours. R11-005 and R11-006 set only the agent's own fleet. · Data: NYC TLC trip records (trips in progress at t0) · Model: Run the world with bots only from t0 minus the burn-in, then insert the agent, checking queue lengths and idle shares against hour-of-day targets at that point. · [G2-030]</sub>
   * **City rule pack (generalisation beyond NYC)**: Swappable bundle of fare, fee, pay-floor and hailing rules so the same engine can run other cities. *Values:* NYC default; stubs for e.g. Seattle and Minnesota per-mile/per-minute floors, California Prop 22 engaged-time guarantee, Chicago ground-transport tax [U]; Seattle local rule requiring a human driver in AVs [S]. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; values for other cities not verified this session [U]; [S] Vital City (Seattle AV rule) · Data: City regulations (not collected this session) · Model: Config files per city with the same schema as the NYC tables · [R07-067]</sub>
   * **Counterfactual event injection**: Adding synthetic events to quiet replay days, or removing real ones, so the true event effect is known. *Values:* 0-5 injected events a day, 1k-80k attendance. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] · Data: n/a · Model: Add event-demand residual templates learned from TLC to baseline demand · [R03-065]</sub>
   * **Data release lag and contamination**: TLC posts data about two months after the month ends, and models may already know past dates, which matters for fair 'public info only' tests. *Values:* Lag ~2 months [U] [uncertain: TLC page unreachable; the checksum catalog lists files to Aug 2026, consistent with a lag of about two months]; replay dates before vs after model knowledge cutoff. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] lag per repo catalog note; [design] contamination concern · Data: NYC TLC trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Tag each replay date relative to each model's cutoff; optionally shift dates or perturb · [R02-066]</sub>
   * **Date and identity masking**: Hiding the real date, and optionally the real zone names, so the model cannot recall what happened on that day. *Values:* Real date / weekday and season only / shifted year / renamed or mirrored zones. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] tabmemcheck (Bordt et al., 'Elephants Never Forget', COLM 2024): GPT-3.5/4 completed rows of famous public tables; [S] NYC taxi data is heavily reused, e.g. the Kaggle 'New York City Taxi Trip Duration' competition data reappears in public code [fact-check: was U], so recall of specific days is plausible but untested. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Masking layer renames dates and, in an anonymised track, zones; a real-name track keeps credit for legitimate world knowledge. · [R12-044]</sub>
   * **Generalisation city**: Running the same agent in another city to test transfer beyond NYC knowledge. *Values:* NYC (default) / Chicago (TNP trips; taxi trips with anonymised taxi ID) / synthetic grid city. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S via GitHub copies] Chicago publishes TNP trips (m6dm-c72p, 2018-2022; n26f-ihde, 2023-2024) and taxi trips (wrvz-psew, 2013-2023; ajtu-isnz, 2024 onward) [corrected by fact-check: the cited tables are closed series; newer IDs added]; [S] the Chicago taxi table carries a taxi_id column (GitHub schema copies; was U), allowing the shift reconstruction NYC data no longer permits. · Data: Chicago Data Portal https://data.cityofchicago.org/d/m6dm-c72p ; https://data.cityofchicago.org/d/wrvz-psew ; https://data.cityofchicago.org/d/ajtu-isnz ; https://data.cityofchicago.org/d/n26f-ihde · Model: City pack (map, zones, demand, rules) behind the same interfaces. · [R12-048]</sub>
   * **Post-cutoff fresh test months**: Test scenarios built from TLC months published after each model's training cutoff. *Values:* Test months at least one month after the model cutoff; refreshed every season. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] the TLC Jan 2023 files used here are byte-identical (SHA-256 recomputed by fact-check) to the TLC originals in the team catalog, which lists months through Aug 2026 by Oct 2026, so new months keep arriving with about a 2-month lag. · Data: NYC TLC trip records · Model: Pipeline pulls each new month, refits the generator and adds a fresh test season. · [R12-045]</sub>
   * **Private hold-out pool, canary and contamination probes**: Keeping test seeds private, marking files so they are kept out of training data, and testing models for recall before scoring. *Values:* Public development seeds / private test seeds; canary GUID in every file; probes asking for the scenario day's weather, trip counts and event outcomes. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P via D10] AppWorld ships encrypted test bundles with a canary string; [S via D08] ARC Prize calls its API-evaluated set 'semi-private' because of leakage risk; [P] tabmemcheck provides header, row, feature completion and first-token tests. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Private seed list held by maintainers; probe suite run per model; flagged days dropped. · [R12-046]</sub>
   * **Shared day-level shock across modules**: One hidden day-and-area factor that moves demand, travel speed, rider waits and surge together, instead of separate random draws in each module. It couples R05-002 (demand overdispersion) and R10-009 (day-level slowdowns). *Values:* HVFHV Jan 2023, 20 non-holiday weekdays, day-of-week means removed: trips vs median wait r = +0.81; trips vs Manhattan-internal speed r = -0.86; wait vs speed r = -0.86 (raw: +0.62, -0.68, -0.79). Modes: independent draws / one shared factor with a loading per module / Gaussian copula.. `Med · World input · hidden · all; single car and fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Re-run in this fact-check: all six correlations reproduced within 0.01 (speed = Manhattan-internal miles / trip time). Only 20 days are used, and part of the correlation is the post-holiday ramp within January (trips rose from 478k on 3 Jan to 701k on 27 Jan); validate on more months. [corrected: impact high -> medium. R12-040 finds pay per engaged hour varies only 1.4% across these days, so the shock moves volume, waits and variance more than profit per hour. Its effect on profit per online hour is unmeasured]. · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV records; NYC DOT Traffic Speeds https://data.cityofnewyork.us/d/i4gi-tjb9 · Model: Draw a latent daily factor z ~ N(0,1) per area: demand x exp(a z), speed x exp(-b z), bot log-on x exp(c z). Fit the loadings on several months, and seed z in the common-random-number stream (R12-040). · [G1-024]</sub>
   * **Side-channel leakage audit**: Checks that hidden state does not leak through the harness: sequential request IDs, wake-up triggers, error messages, tool latency, offer order, log timestamps, or heat maps built from the true hidden rates. *Values:* Pass or fail for each channel: random opaque IDs, wake-ups only on events the public could see, uniform error text, constant tool latency, and heat maps built from observed rather than true demand [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] Even with strict as-of rules (R12-022), a request-ID counter can reveal total demand, and an event-triggered wake-up can reveal an incident before the public would know. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: An audit script compares what the agent sees across seeds that differ only in hidden state; any difference flags a leak. · [G2-034]</sub>
   * **Tie-breaking and deterministic execution**: A seeded rule for equal-ETA or simultaneous claims on one request, deterministic tie-breaks in batched assignment, and no dependence on thread order or floating-point quirks. *Values:* Tie rule: seeded random / rotate by seat / lowest car ID (to avoid) [design]. The same seed and the same actions must give an identical log [design].. `Med · Rule/price · hidden · all; arena and fleet modes`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] Without it, the same seed does not reproduce a run and arena seats (R12-049) get hidden advantages. It affects ranking reliability and common random numbers (R12-040) rather than expected profit. · Data: none needed (harness rule) · Model: Resolve ties with a seeded draw per request from a dedicated random stream, iterate in sorted order everywhere, and have a CI test re-run a seed and diff the event log. · [G2-025]</sub>
   * **Out-of-NYC event scenario (Coachella-style)**: A portable event module for a festival town with no public trip data, to test whether event skill generalises. *Values:* Coachella: Empire Polo Club, Indio CA, two April weekends, ~125k a day, rideshare lot with long waits [U]; alternative calibration: Chicago TNP trips plus Lollapalooza [U] [uncertain: Coachella site, dates and capacity not re-checkable this session]. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] · Data: Chicago Transportation Network Providers - Trips (data.cityofchicago.org m6dm-c72p; ID S via the City of Chicago developer blog on GitHub; later years may sit in a successor table, uncertain) · Model: Synthetic town graph plus event templates from NYC and Chicago residuals · [R03-069]</sub>
* **Baselines and scoring**
   * **Exploit and degenerate-strategy audit**: A red-team pass with scripted probes and the RL baseline to find strategies that profit from simulator artefacts rather than real skill. *Values:* Checklist: free or instant repositioning, zone-centroid shortcuts, zero-time trips within one zone, farming cancellation fees, resetting queues by logging off and on, gaming the displacement rule, bots that never adapt, and loopholes in end-of-day valuation. Target: no probe beats the best honest heuristic by more than a few percent [design].. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] specification-gaming examples (Krakovna et al., DeepMind blog 2020). R12-055 says RL shows 'how exploitable the world is', but there is no audit step. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Scripted exploit probes in CI plus periodic RL runs on development seeds; each exploit found becomes a regression test. · [G2-033]</sub>
   * **Fleet objective function**: What the fleet is scored on. *Values:* total profit / profit per car / profit with a service floor (e.g. served at least 95%, p90 wait at most 10 min) / weighted profit and service.. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: fleetsim: raising its fairness weight cut unserved requests from 9.66% to 8.17% but raised median wait from 9.0 to 16.9 min [S], so the best policy flips with the objective. · Data: n/a · Model: Harness scoring function; robotaxi own-app mode includes a service floor, platform-driver mode scores profit only. · [R11-065]</sub>
   * **Headline metric**: The single number used for ranking. *Values:* Net profit per online hour after all costs (fuel or energy, maintenance, depreciation, insurance, tolls, fees, commission); fleet: net profit per vehicle-hour and in total. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] own computation: citywide HVFHV pay per engaged hour is stable (CV 1.4%), so profit per online hour mostly measures utilisation and positioning, the skills under test; [design] team goal. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Ledger computes net profit; online hours come from the status log. · [R12-056]</sub>
   * **Hindsight oracle**: The best achievable profit on the realised day with perfect knowledge of every request, used as the score ceiling. *Values:* Exact longest path over a time-expanded graph (single car); ILP or min-cost flow (fleet); approximations for long horizons. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P via D11] Magentic Marketplace reports an 'Optimal (theoretical upper bound)' and Supply_Chain_Bench scores against reference policies including a best-found feasible one; [design]; impact is on the normalised score because the oracle is the ceiling. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Computed offline per seed after the run; never shown to agents. · [R12-053]</sub>
   * **Normalisation and aggregation**: How profits from different scenarios are combined into one score with uncertainty. *Values:* Per scenario (agent - passive) / (oracle - passive); aggregate with the interquartile mean and stratified-bootstrap CIs; per-family profiles. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P via D11] IQM with stratified-bootstrap CIs (rliable, Agarwal et al. NeurIPS 2021) recommended for business sims; [P] day levels differ ($72 holiday vs $54 weekday per engaged hour), so raw dollars are not comparable across days. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Scoring script runs after runs are sealed. · [R12-057]</sub>
   * **Passive and random floor baselines**: Simple policies that set the score floor. *Values:* Passive: stay where dropped and accept every offer; random repositioning; for taxis, random cruising. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden · core MVP`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P via D10, README re-read] ABC checklist: 'A trivial do-nothing agent scores 38% pass@k and pass^k' on tau-bench (a benchmark flaw since fixed), so floors must be measured before claiming skill; impact is on the normalised score because the passive run is the floor. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Scripted policies run on every seed; the passive score is the normalisation floor. · [R12-050]</sub>
   * **Uncertain parameters and ranking stability**: Run the leaderboard over a set of plausible draws of the guessed parameters and report whether model rankings hold, with each parameter tagged by where its value came from. *Values:* Parameters to vary: unserved demand 5-20% (R05-066), supply elasticity 0.2-1.0 (R06-010), price elasticity (R05-020), bot strategy mix (R06-017). Use 10-30 draws and report Kendall's tau of rankings across them [design]. Give wider ranges to values borrowed from SF, Chicago or pre-2019 studies.. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; [U] domain randomisation (Tobin et al. 2017, IROS) is standard sim-to-real practice. If rankings flip within these ranges, the benchmark measures the guess, not the model. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Latin-hypercube draws over the flagged parameters, run first with cheap development models (R12-006), and a rank-stability panel published next to the headline score. · [G2-032]</sub>
   * **Compliance, safety and service panel**: Penalties and side metrics for rule-breaking and for how riders are served. *Values:* TLC rule violations (taxi refusals, hours-of-service, illegal stopping), unsafe instructions, discriminatory declines; acceptance and cancellation rates; outer-borough service share; rider wait (fleet); for a fleet or robotaxi operator only, an optional Manhattan-core cruising-cap toggle. `Med · Rule/price · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P via D11] Business Arena logs fines and violations and conduct panels are standard in business sims; the rules themselves are R07. [corrected by fact-check: the HVFHS cruising cap (35 RCNY 59D-21, at most 31% of available time cruising below 96th St) binds app companies, not drivers, and was reported annulled by a state court in Dec 2019 [U]; the 2025 access rules (59D-22(c)) also bind platforms] · Data: Rules of the City of New York Title 35 via https://github.com/jehiah/nyc_code_archive (see R07) · Model: Rule engine scans the event log; severe violations disqualify, others deduct. · [R12-059]</sub>
   * **Efficiency diagnostics**: A breakdown of how time and miles were used. *Values:* P1 / P2 / P3 time shares, empty miles per paid mile, trips per hour, average wait for the next offer. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] tomslee/ridehail's P1/P2/P3 split; [S via R10] about 41% of FHV time in Manhattan below 96th Street was without a passenger in 2019; the TLC's cruising definition counts the drive to a pickup as cruising, so this is P1 + P2 [fact-check note]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Computed from the status log and shown on the dashboard. · [R12-060]</sub>
   * **Event-window scoring metrics**: Measures of how well the agent used event information. *Values:* Profit per hour overall; profit within +/- 2 h and 2 km of let-outs; value of information = L1 - L0; information-use efficiency = (L1 - L0)/(L3 - L0). `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; event-window version of R12-061 (information-value metric) · Data: n/a · Model: Tag each trip with its event window; report per arm with paired seeds · [R03-068]</sub>
   * **Fleet KPI panel**: Standard fleet statistics reported beside profit. *Values:* utilisation, trips per car-day, empty-mile share, riders served, wait p50 and p90, charging deadhead, availability, compute cost.. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: fleetsim and offpeak report these [S]; Waymo about 20 trips per car-day (R06-057) [S]. · Data: n/a · Model: Harness logs per car per minute and summarises per run. · [R11-066]</sub>
