   * **Heuristic baselines**: Rule-based strategies a competent human might use. *Values:* Heatmap follower (go to the best historical zone-hour); surge chaser; taxi rules (Manhattan core and airports, head back after outer-borough drop-offs); go home at the end. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] own computation: zone-hour pay ranks are stable day to day (r = 0.84), so a heatmap follower should be a strong baseline; [design]. [corrected by fact-check: impact was high; a reference baseline does not change the agent's profit or the normalised score unless used as the normalisation anchor] · Data: TLC aggregates (R12-014) · Model: Scripted policies that use only allowed information. · [R12-051]</sub>
   * **Human reference**: How people do, from players using the same interface and from real driver earnings. *Values:* Expert NYC drivers or trained players on the UI (5 or more); real-world anchor: HVFHV driver pay about $54 per engaged (passenger-on-board) hour (weekdays, Jan 2023) before idle time, the drive to pickups and costs. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Vending-Bench 1 had one human (5 h, $844.05) who beat every model's worst run (best model minimum $476; verified); [P] own computation of pay per engaged hour from TLC driver_pay and trip_time. · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV records (driver_pay, trip_time) · Model: Human UI over the same tool API at the same information level. · [R12-054]</sub>
   * **Information-value metric**: The profit difference caused by giving one more layer of information, on the same seeds. *Values:* profit(level k) - profit(level k-1) per model and day type; event-day subset for the event arm. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] team plan to test with vs without event information; paired design per Anthropic's evaluation guidance [P]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Paired runs across information levels, reported per model. · [R12-061]</sub>
   * **Jackpot-trip variance and luck-adjusted scoring**: How the score handles rare high-value trips that dominate a single day's profit, for example by subtracting a baseline run on the same seed (control variate) or by scoring the expected value of the trips offered. *Values:* HVFHV Jan 2023: the top 1% of trips by driver pay (>= $68.72) earn 5.5% of all pay; the top 5% (>= $43.04) earn 17.7%; 0.27% of trips pay >= $100; 91% of top-1% trips touch an airport or leave NYC. Mean pay is $17.05 per trip (median $12.81), so at ~20-25 trips a day one $100 trip is about 23-29% of daily pay. Methods: none / paired baseline on the same seed / control variate on offered-trip value / both.. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden · all; matters most for single-car runs (a fleet averages over N cars)`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own re-run on HVFHV Jan 2023 (18.19M trips with pay; g1/a7.py), repeated in this fact-check. [corrected: share of daily pay was 15-25%]. Trips per day [U]. [U] Control variates are standard (Law & Kelton). · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV records (driver_pay) · Model: Log the value of every offered trip. Score = profit - beta x (offered jackpot value - its expectation), with beta fitted across runs. Report raw and adjusted scores. · [G1-025]</sub>
   * **Operations-research planner baseline**: An optimisation policy that plans on expected demand without seeing the future. *Values:* Single car: dynamic programming over zone x 5-min time on expected earnings; fleet: min-cost flow or batched bipartite matching with rebalancing. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S via R01] Buchholz (2022, Review of Economic Studies) models NYC taxi search as a dynamic spatial equilibrium; [U] MDP methods for taxi driver revenue (e.g. Rong et al., CIKM 2016). [corrected by fact-check: impact was high; a reference baseline not used for normalisation does not change the measured profit] · Data: TLC aggregates (R12-014) · Model: Solver reads the generator's expected rates and re-plans every 5 sim-min. · [R12-052]</sub>
   * **Reference rebalancing baselines**: Non-LLM fleet policies run on the same scenarios to anchor model scores. *Values:* no repositioning; greedy nearest dispatch; proportional LP (Pavone-style); model predictive control; a published RL policy.. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: AMoDeus ships about ten unit-capacity dispatch and rebalancing algorithms plus four ride-sharing ones (GPL-2.0, LICENSE.txt checked) [P, README]; FleetPy ships Alonso-Mora, Pavone and linear-hailing repositioning modules (MIT) [P, code: src/misc/init_modules.py re-read by fact-check]; FleetPy's optimisation modules use Gurobi, free only under an academic licence [P, README, added by fact-check]. · Data: https://github.com/TUM-VT/FleetPy; https://github.com/amodeus-science/amodeus · Model: Port 2-3 baselines into the harness; report model profit as a % of the best baseline at the same N. · [R11-027]</sub>
   * **Risk and consistency panel**: Measures of bad days and reliability shown next to the mean. *Values:* CVaR of daily profit (worst 10-20% of seeds), worst run, SD across seeds, stranding rate (battery or fuel out), pass^k (all k runs beat the human median). `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P via D11] tau-bench's pass^k; [P*] Vending-Bench 1: Claude 3.5 Sonnet's worst run was $476 against a mean of $2,217.93 (verified). · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Computed from per-run ledgers. · [R12-058]</sub>
   * **Scale ladder and normalisation**: Running the same model at several fleet sizes and against baselines, to separate skill from scale. *Values:* N in {1, 10, 100}; score = profit / best-baseline profit at the same N.. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]. · Data: n/a · Model: Harness sweep; plot profit per car against N for each model. · [R11-067]</sub>
   * **Emissions and carbon accounting (optional)**: Optional score term for CO2 from fuel and electricity, to test green goals or Green Rides compliance. *Values:* Gasoline about 8.9 kg CO2 per gallon [P*: EPA tailpipe CO2 x combined mpg gives a median 8,880 g/gal across 2,960 gasoline models from 2015 on]; NYC grid about 0.25-0.35 kg CO2 per kWh [U] [uncertain: EPA eGRID's NYC/Westchester subregion (NYCW) is recalled at about 0.40 kg/kWh because in-city generation is mostly gas, while the NY state average is nearer 0.2-0.25; read eGRID before use]; carbon price $0-200 per tonne [design].. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] EPA emission factors; gasoline factor [P*]. · Data: EPA GHG Emission Factors Hub https://www.epa.gov/climateleadership/ghg-emission-factors-hub [U]; EPA eGRID https://www.epa.gov/egrid [U] · Model: Emissions per mile by powertrain; optional penalty in the score. · [R08-070]</sub>
   * **Learned RL baseline**: A policy trained by reinforcement learning on the simulator, which shows how hard and how exploitable the world is. *Values:* Tabular Q-learning over zone x time, or deep RL (PPO); trained on development seeds only. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] LPSim provides a 'Gymnasium environment wrapper for training dispatch/pricing policies' (verified); [U] RL dispatch is used in production ride-hailing. [corrected by fact-check: impact was medium; a stretch reference policy not used for scoring] · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Gymnasium wrapper around the simulator; evaluated on private seeds. · [R12-055]</sub>
   * **Real vs nominal dollars**: Whether fares, costs and profits from scenarios in different years are converted to one price year before scores are pooled. *Values:* Options: nominal / deflated to a base year with CPI-U (New York-Newark-Jersey City) / normalised per scenario only (R12-057). NY-area CPI-U rose about 20% from 2019 to 2024 (2019 annual 278.2; 2024 mean of monthly values 334.2). The US city average rose about 23% [U]. TLC pay factors are re-indexed each March by NY-NJ-PA CPI-W (R07-028).. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [S] BLS monthly NY-area CPI values for 2024 via search snippets. [uncertain: the 2019 annual average is from memory; bls.gov was not read]. [P*, from list] R07-028 indexation. [corrected: the ~23% figure is the US average, not the NY-area index]. · Data: BLS CPI-U New York-Newark-Jersey City, series CUURS12ASA0 https://www.bls.gov/regions/new-york-new-jersey/ (bls.gov not read from here) · Model: Deflator table by month. The ledger stores both nominal and real values; the headline uses real values whenever eras are pooled. · [G1-033]</sub>
* **Run cost, validity and versioning**
   * **Compute and token budget per run**: Cap on model calls, tokens or dollars for one evaluation run, which limits how many cars can get individual attention. *Values:* about $20 per run (team estimate); per-car control of 100 cars at one call per 5 min is 28,800 calls a day [design].. `High · Benchmark setting · agent sees · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Team cost estimate of $10K for 5 models x 100 runs x $20 (meeting notes) and arithmetic [design]. · Data: n/a · Model: Harness meter; when the budget runs out, cars fall back to a default policy; report profit per dollar of compute. · [R11-014]</sub>
   * **Benchmark version freeze and re-scoring rule**: Simulator code, calibrated parameters, data snapshot and rule tables are versioned together. Scores are compared only within a version, and older models are re-run after an upgrade. *Values:* A version number per release (v1.0, v1.1, ...). Major bump on any calibration or rule change. Re-run the top-k models on each major release. The leaderboard shows the version [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]. [P, from list] R12-072 records config hashes per run but has no release or re-scoring rule. · Data: none needed · Model: A release manifest hashes code, parameters, data and rules; the leaderboard is keyed by version. · [G1-037]</sub>
   * **Checkpoint, resume and fork**: Save the full simulator state, including the position of every random stream, so an interrupted run resumes identically and counterfactual branches can fork from the same mid-day state. *Values:* Checkpoint every 15-60 simulated minutes [design]. Fork arms such as with vs without event info, or alternative prompts, from one shared state.. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P* via list] Vending-Bench runs took 5-10 real hours (R12-065). Forking measures the value of information more precisely than rerunning whole days [design]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Serialise the world, agent memory, random-stream states and pending events. A resume test must give an identical log, and a fork API supports paired value-of-information tests (R12-061). · [G2-035]</sub>
   * **Compute-cost accounting**: Whether API spend is only reported or also charged inside the game. *Values:* Reported only ($ and tokens per run) / in-world charge (e.g. $100 per million output tokens, as in Vending-Bench 2) / cost-adjusted score. `Med · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P* via D11] Vending-Bench 2's prompt charges '$100 per million output tokens' weekly; [P via D11] HAL argues for 'accuracy and cost by default'. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Token meter per call; optional deduction in the ledger. · [R12-062]</sub>
   * **Handling API failures and run validity**: What happens on API timeouts, 5xx errors, rate limits, refusals or truncated outputs: how many retries, whether the sim clock keeps running, and when a run is voided rather than scored. *Values:* 3 retries with backoff. The sim clock is frozen during infrastructure errors (headline track) or charged (latency track). Refusals and truncations count as model behaviour (no-op). Void and re-run on the same seed if more than 2% of calls fail for infrastructure reasons [design].. `Med · Rule/price · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]. [P, from list] Runs take hours of wall-clock time under provider rate limits (R12-065), and the serving provider changed Vending-Bench scores (R12-002). · Data: none needed · Model: A harness wrapper classifies each error as infrastructure or model, retries infrastructure errors with the clock frozen, logs counts, and voids runs above the threshold (malformed actions stay in R12-029). · [G1-036]</sub>
   * **Simulator correctness checks**: Automatic invariant checks in every run: each car is in exactly one state, each request is served at most once, nothing changes the past, energy is never negative, and the money ledger balances. *Values:* Invariants: rider payment = driver pay + platform take + taxes, fees and pass-throughs (to the cent); car-state conservation; request life cycle; monotone clock. Checked on every event in development and every 1,000 events in evaluation [design]. Any violation voids the run.. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]. [P, from list] R07-015 and R07-066 define ledgers but no balance checks. · Data: none needed · Model: Assertion layer in the event loop, end-of-run ledger reconciliation, and property-based tests in CI. · [G1-035]</sub>
   * **Campaign budget, parallelism and wall-clock**: Total spend across models and runs, and how fast runs finish. *Values:* 5 models x 100 runs x $20 = $10k (team estimate); concurrency limited by provider rate limits; hours of wall-clock per run. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] Vending-Bench 1 runs took 5-10 real hours for 2,000 messages and about 25M tokens (verified); [design calc, re-verified] 100 runs x 11 sim-days at the standard cadence would cost about $3.4k per model on Opus 5.5 and about $8k on Fable 5.1. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Run scheduler with rate-limit-aware concurrency and a budget ledger per model. · [R12-065]</sub>
   * **Simulator scale and runtime**: How fast the engine must run so that N-car fleets and 100 runs per model stay affordable. *Values:* one day with 5,000 cars in about 3 s (greedy, Manhattan); model calls dominate run time.. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: fleetsim cut runtime from 37.3 s to 2.8 s per simulated day at 5,000 cars by using matrix indices [S]. · Data: n/a · Model: Vectorised zone-matrix engine with cached travel-time matrices. · [R11-068]</sub>
   * **Tokens per decision and cache hit rate**: Input and output tokens per model call and the share read from the prompt cache, which together set the cost of a run. *Values:* 8k-40k input and 0.5k-3k output tokens per call; 40-150 calls per sim-day; cache hits 0-90%. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Low on in-sim profit unless charged, high on budget: [design, P prices, arithmetic re-verified] at 85% cache hits, 80 calls x 20k in / 1k out per sim-day costs about $3.1 (Opus 5.5), $1.7 (Sonnet 5.5) or $7.3 (Fable 5.1); Opus 5.5 cache reads cost $0.20 vs $4 per MTok uncached; 5-minute cache writes cost 1.25x input. · Data: Claude pricing https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing · Model: Append-only conversation to keep cache hits high; token meter per call. · [R12-063]</sub>
* **Visualisation and logging**
   * **Event visual overlays**: Map overlays that show events, closures and alerts in the live visualisation. *Values:* Venue pulse sized by attendance; closure polygons; subway status ticker. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] · Data: n/a · Model: Render event objects on the map timeline · [R03-070]</sub>
   * **Fleet visual encoding**: How the live map shows fleet cars: colour by model, status, revenue ticker and own-car density against demand. *Values:* 5 statuses (busy, idle, repositioning, charging, out of service); 1-5 model colours.. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; visualisation plan in the meeting notes. · Data: n/a · Model: The event log feeds a map layer; time-lapse with day and night shading. · [R11-069]</sub>
   * **Model colour coding and live revenue overlay**: Visual encoding that lets viewers follow each model's cars and money in real time. *Values:* One colour per model (colour-blind-safe palette); per-model revenue and net-profit tickers; leaderboard panel. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] team visual plan; [P] TripsLayer colours trips by a categorical attribute (vendor in the example). · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Colour map from the run manifest; overlay reads the ledger stream. · [R12-071]</sub>
   * **Playback speed, lighting and clip export**: How fast simulated time plays on screen, how day and night are shown, and how clips are cut for social media. *Values:* 1 sim-hour = 10-60 s; trail length; sun-position lighting vs fixed; automatic clips around surges, storms and event egress. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] deck.gl trips example defaults (verified): loopLength 1800, trailLength 180, fixed ambient light plus one point light with no time of day; sun position from the NOAA solar calculator [S via sibling dossier]. · Data: NOAA solar calculator https://gml.noaa.gov/grad/solcalc/ · Model: Renderer maps the sim clock to light level; clip exporter keyed on the event log. · [R12-070]</sub>
   * **Replay and audit log**: Everything needed to re-simulate and audit a run. *Values:* Seeds, config hashes, model ids, every observation, tool call, response and token count, ledger entries. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P via D10] reproducibility comes from re-simulating a logged action stream because LLM sampling is not deterministic: [P] batch_invariant_ops README: 'out of 1000 random length 100 completions we see 18 unique samples' with vLLM, and one after the batch-invariant fix [uncertain: that the requests used an identical prompt is implied, not stated; model not named]. · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Append-only JSONL per run plus a sealed scoring step. · [R12-072]</sub>
   * **Sub-trip position trace**: Second-by-second position along the route, needed for the live map and for meter time and distance accounting. *Values:* tick 1-10 s of simulated time [design]. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] team notes ask for a live map with moving cars and sped-up time · Data: route geometry from the router · Model: Interpolate along the route polyline using edge-level times and emit positions to the visualiser. · [R10-066]</sub>
   * **Time compression and day-night lighting**: How fast simulated time runs in the live map and how lighting follows the sun for the social-media visualisation. *Values:* Speed-up 60x-1440x; lighting from sun elevation (civil dusk to dawn). `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] from team visualisation notes · Data: NOAA solar position (computed) · Model: Renderer maps sim clock to frame time; sky colour from sun elevation · [R02-069]</sub>
   * **Vehicle state log for the live map**: The per-vehicle, per-tick record the visualisation reads. *Values:* Vehicle id, model id, timestamp, lon/lat, heading, status (P1 / P2 / P3 / charging / offline), battery or fuel, cumulative revenue, costs and net; event markers. `Low · Benchmark setting · hidden · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] deck.gl TripsLayer (MIT) animates trips from per-trip path and timestamps arrays coloured by a categorical attribute (vendor); its demo view is centred on New York (-74, 40.72) (verified in app.tsx). · Data: none needed (harness setting) · Model: Simulator emits path plus timestamps per vehicle segment and a ledger stream. · [R12-069]</sub>
   * **Weather and daylight rendering on the live map**: Showing rain, snow, fog, floods and night lighting on the live map so clips show why earnings jumped. *Values:* Rain/snow overlay, fog dimming, night lighting by sun elevation, flooded links highlighted [design]. `Low · Benchmark setting · agent sees · visualisation`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; team notes on night/day cycle and clippable visuals · Data: Simulator weather state; astral sun elevation · Model: Visualiser reads the same weather state as the simulator. · [R04-061]</sub>

### 19. Calibration targets and KPIs (outputs, not inputs) (20)
*Quantities the simulation produces and is checked against; never set them as inputs.*

* **Driver-level outcomes (utilisation, idle gap, pings, cruising share)**
   * **Cruising and idle share**: Share of time or miles without a passenger while waiting for the next request. *Values:* ~41% of HVFHV time without a passenger in Manhattan below 96th St (2019); TLC cap adopted Aug 2019 (35 RCNY 59D-21: 36% from Feb 2020, 31% from Aug 2020, weekdays 6 AM-11 PM and weekends 8 AM-11 PM in the Congestion Zone; en-route time to pickups counts as cruising; WAVs exempt) [P*], still printed in the rules but annulled by a state court in Dec 2019 and not enforced [U]; ~33% of ride-hail VMT waiting or cruising (six US metros). `High · Changes during run · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: 35 RCNY 59D-21 [P*]; TLC 2019 cruising rule documents [S]; Fehr & Peers 2019 [S] · Data: TLC reports; HVFHV records lack a vehicle ID so gaps between trips cannot be rebuilt [P*: no vehicle or driver ID in the HVFHV schema] · Model: Emerges from the simulation; report it as a metric and use the 41% figure as a sanity check for baseline policies. · [R10-051]</sub>
   * **Idle gap between trips**: Minutes from one drop-off to the next pickup. *Values:* Manhattan core: 8 min (yellow) and 11 min (app cars) in 2017; longer in the outer boroughs.. `High · Changes during run · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: Schaller 2017 [S]. · Data: Schaller report; TLC trip records (cannot be chained per car publicly) · Model: Emergent; validation target. · [R06-037]</sub>
   * **Ping rate per idle hour**: How many offers an idle driver gets per hour in a given zone and hour. *Values:* About 1.8 trips per online hour citywide, so about 4-8 offers per idle hour; 1-15 by zone [design].. `High · Changes during run · agent sees · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design] from ~667k trips/day [S] and ~58% utilisation [S]; arithmetic re-checked by fact-check (667k trips / ~363k online vehicle-hours = ~1.8). · Data: Derived from HVFHV trips and the supply field · Model: Emerges from the matcher; report as a metric; check against TLC trips per vehicle per hour. · [R06-035]</sub>
   * **Utilisation rate**: Share of online time with a passenger; the main multiplier on hourly pay and an input to the TLC pay formula. *Values:* About 59% in the Manhattan core (app cars empty 41% of the time south of 96th St, 2019) and about 58% citywide (2024) [S]; platforms held to at least 50-53% (2024) [uncertain: not re-checked]; since 1 Aug 2025 the pay floor divides by fixed applied rates of 53.3% (per minute) and 68.5% (per mile) instead of each company's actual utilisation [corrected by fact-check: P*, 35 RCNY 59D-22(b)].. `High · Changes during run · agent sees`
      * <sub>Evidence: TLC data cited in the 2019 cruising-cap ruling [S]; 2024 city deal (Lyft utilisation at least 50%) [S]; 2024 citywide ~58% (Bloomberg, via R07) [S]; TLC defines utilisation as the share of available time or distance spent carrying passengers [P*, 35 RCNY 59D-03(j)]. · Data: Comptroller letter https://comptroller.nyc.gov/reports/letter-to-taxi-and-limousine-commission-on-utilization-rates-for-uber-and-lyft-drivers · Model: Track per driver; platform-wide utilisation drives lockouts (R06) and pay (R07). · [R06-036]</sub>
* **Market-level outcomes (rider wait, surge frequency)**
   * **Hourly rider wait (market tightness)**: Time from request to pickup by hour, a public proxy for how short of drivers the market is. *Values:* Median request-to-pickup 3.3 min (weekday 10-11h) to 5.2 min (Sat 23h, Sun 06h); 8.6 min on NYE 01h; JFK pickups 9.6 min at 22-23h. `High · World input · hidden · human app driver, robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc, TLC HVFHV Jan 2023; on-scene times only for Uber (HV0003) · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records, https://www.nyc.gov/site/tlc/about/tlc-trip-record-data.page · Model: Calibration target for supply model (R06); simulate driver supply per hour so waits match · [R02-019]</sub>
   * **Surge frequency calibration target**: How often, when and how strongly app prices rise above normal, estimated from public trip fares and used to calibrate the surge module. *Values:* HVFHV Jan 2023 weekdays, non-shared trips of normal duration (<= 1.1x the OD median time): 6.9-7.8% priced >= 1.3x the median fare for the same platform and OD pair, and 2.6-3.0% >= 1.6x (the range covers two filters). By request hour: 12.4-13.0% at 07-08h, 8.4-9.0% at 17-18h, 3.2-3.9% at 02-04h (our re-check vs the critic). Drivers get a lower share of surged fares (median pay/fare 0.67-0.68 vs 0.72).. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden · human app driver; robotaxi; fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own calc (g1/a2.py), re-run in this fact-check, plus an hourly re-check with holidays excluded; it agrees with the gap critic within 1 point. This is a proxy: the duration filter keeps faster-than-median trips, and the fare ratio includes upfront-price noise and route differences. · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV records (base_passenger_fare by OD and time) · Model: Tune surge thresholds (R07-020) until the simulated distribution of fare ratios by hour matches the target. Calibration fails if it is off by more than a set number of percentage points. · [G1-029]</sub>
* **Fleet-level outcomes (empty miles, clustering, saturation, availability)**
   * **Empty miles from rebalancing**: Unpaid miles the fleet drives to pickups, repositioning and charging, which cost energy, wear and tolls. *Values:* about 0.9-1.0 empty miles per trip without repositioning [corrected by fact-check: greedy pickup deadhead was 0.90-1.10 mi per trip across 1,500-6,000 cars and 0.85-0.87 with batching; 0.906 at 5,000 cars] and about 1.7 with it (Manhattan robotaxi sim); about one third of Manhattan cars empty at any moment (R06-002).. `High · Changes during run · agent sees · all, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: fleetsim: repositioning raised empty miles per trip 87% (0.906 to 1.697), about 11% more empty miles per 1% of wait removed [S]. · Data: TLC trip records (trip miles) for calibration · Model: Summed per car; cost per mile from R08; empty-mile share reported as a KPI. · [R11-022]</sub>
   * **Fleet availability (uptime share)**: Share of fleet car-hours in service rather than charging, cleaning, in maintenance, in repair or parked. *Values:* robotaxi about 75-90% (20-22 h a day per car, R09-044) [design] [corrected by fact-check: 20-22 h a day is 83-92%; 75% would be 18 h]; double-shift taxi 16-22 h a day [U]; single-shift human 8-12 h.. `High · Changes during run · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: The Waymo-Lyft Nashville depot runs 24/7 in three shifts to charge, clean and maintain cars (R09-044) [S]; Waymo about 20 trips per car-day (R06-057) [S]. · Data: none public (company releases) · Model: Status machine per car; availability = in-service hours / total hours. · [R11-042]</sub>
   * **Fleet saturation curve (value of the N-th car)**: How revenue and utilisation per car fall as the fleet grows in a fixed market. *Values:* Manhattan robotaxi sim: utilisation 57% at 1,500 cars, 50% at 3,000, 34% at 5,000, 28% at 6,000.. `High · Changes during run · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: fleetsim greedy sweep [S]; own-fleet cannibalisation (R06-062); Vazifeh et al. 2018: about 30% fewer cars could serve NYC taxi demand (R06-062) [S]. · Data: n/a · Model: Run N and N+dN on the same seed; report marginal profit per added car. · [R11-030]</sub>
   * **Own-car clustering (herding)**: Fleet cars piling into the same hotspot because they react to the same signal, leaving gaps elsewhere. *Values:* own idle cars per zone vs forecast requests; dispersion index from 0 (spread) to 1 (all in one zone).. `High · Changes during run · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [U] DiDi's multi-agent RL for fleet repositioning (Lin et al., KDD 2018) was built to stop agents chasing the same orders [uncertain: paper not re-read this session]; surge chasing herds human drivers (R06-011) [S]. · Data: n/a (emerges in the run) · Model: Compute a dispersion metric every epoch; show it on the map. · [R11-028]</sub>
   * **Fleet profit variance and correlation**: Day-to-day spread of fleet profit and how correlated the cars' earnings are. *Values:* daily profit coefficient of variation 5-30% [design]; high correlation for cars in the same zone or queue.. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees · fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: [design]; the team plans 100 runs per model. · Data: n/a · Model: Report mean, standard deviation and CVaR over seeds, plus a diversification index. · [R11-064]</sub>
   * **Robotaxi trips per car per day**: Trips per robotaxi per day, a realism ceiling for the robotaxi agent. *Values:* About 19-24 trips per car per day (about 500k rides/week across 3,000-3,800 cars, Mar 2026) [corrected by fact-check: arithmetic range; 2025 figures of 450k+ rides/week on ~2,500 cars give ~26, S, GitHub company profile].. `Med · World input · public/forecast · robotaxi, fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: Waymo 500k paid rides/week (Mar 2026) [S]; division [design]. · Data: Waymo statements; CPUC quarterly AV reports [U] · Model: Validation target for robotaxi runs. · [R06-057]</sub>
   * **Share of fleet charging at peaks**: Fraction of the fleet plugged in during demand peaks, the visible cost of a poor charging plan. *Values:* 0-20% at 5-8 pm [design]; robotaxis charge about 1-3 h a day (R06-058).. `Med · Changes during run · agent sees · EV fleet, robotaxi fleet`
      * <sub>Evidence: offpeak ablation: removing trough top-ups cut riders served from 96.2% [corrected by fact-check: the 6-seed ablation baseline is 96.2%; 96.1% is the 10-seed headline] to 95.1%, removing early unplugging for waiting riders to 95.8% [S]. · Data: n/a · Model: Hourly KPI; option to unplug early when riders are waiting. · [R11-041]</sub>
* **Validation targets and sim-to-real checks**
   * **Calibration target and acceptance error**: Which observed statistic the travel-time model must match and how closely. *Values:* zone-pair x hour-of-week median and P90 of TLC durations; target MAPE 15-20% or better on a held-out month [design]; OD neighbour averaging beat route-based methods and map APIs on 173M 2013 NYC trips. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: Wang et al. 2016 SIGSPATIAL, arXiv 1512.08580 [S] · Data: TLC yellow, green and HVFHV trip records · Model: Hold out a month; report MAPE by distance band, hour and borough; fail the build above threshold. · [R10-062]</sub>
   * **Calibration targets and sim-to-real validation**: Real-world statistics the simulator must reproduce before any model is scored. *Values:* Zone x hour pickups (sim-vs-real WAPE near the real day-to-day reference of 0.12-0.13); HVFHV pay about $54 per passenger-on-board hour (weekdays, Jan 2023); median request-to-pickup 3.5-4.2 min (weekdays, Jan 2023); speeds by hour (R10). `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] own computation on the TLC Jan 2023 HVFHV and yellow files (re-verified against the script output; 0.12-0.13 is a reference level, not a proven floor [corrected by fact-check]). · Data: NYC TLC trip records; NYC DOT Traffic Speeds https://data.cityofnewyork.us/d/i4gi-tjb9 (minute-level data; portal update frequency listed as Daily) · Model: CI suite runs scripted policies and checks the targets on held-out days. · [R12-066]</sub>
   * **Leftover easy-profit gap (no-free-lunch check)**: The largest allowed gap between the best simple fixed strategy, such as always waiting at zone X at hour Y, and the average background driver's net hourly earnings. *Values:* Target: the best fixed heuristic beats the median bot by no more than 10-15% in net $/h [design]. Real-world reference: across 20 non-holiday weekdays in Jan 2023, HVFHV driver pay per engaged hour stayed between $52.42 and $55.82 ($55.43-59.30 with tips) while daily trips ranged from 478k to 701k (r = -0.16) [P, re-verified exactly by fact-check].. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] Own re-check on the local HVFHV Jan 2023 file; [S] Buchholz 2022 (REStud) on the spatial equilibrium of NYC taxi search; [S] Hall, Horton & Knoepfle 2023 (NBER w30883) on re-equilibration (via R06-012). · Data: NYC TLC HVFHV trip records (driver_pay, tips, trip_time) · Model: A CI test grid-searches simple fixed policies (zone x hour waits, always-airport) against the bots, and calibration fails if any beats the target margin persistently across seeds. · [G2-027]</sub>
   * **Supply calibration targets**: Public statistics the background supply must reproduce. *Values:* TLC monthly: unique drivers, unique vehicles, vehicles per day, hours per day per vehicle and per driver, trips per day, minutes per trip; HVFHV: on-scene minus request by zone and hour (Uber rows only; Lyft leaves on_scene_datetime empty) [corrected by fact-check], driver_pay per trip-hour (no driver ID, so not per online hour); FHV base report: trips and unique vehicles per base; Schaller empty share and idle gaps.. `High · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: TLC monthly report columns [S, read in GitHub import code; re-read by fact-check: trips_per_day, farebox_per_day, unique_drivers, unique_vehicles, vehicles_per_day, avg days and hours per vehicle and per driver, avg_minutes_per_trip, trips_per_day_shared]; HVFHV columns [S]. · Data: TLC monthly CSV; HVFHV records; FHV Base Aggregate Report · Model: Fit log-on curves and the archetype mix by simulated method of moments; hold out months for validation. · [R06-069]</sub>
   * **Trip duration distribution (validation target)**: Distribution of in-car time per trip, used to check that simulated trips take as long as real ones. *Values:* Yellow 2023: median 12.8 min, mean 16.5 min; HVFHV Jan 2023: median 15.0 min, mean 18.1 min [fact-check: confirmed from data, was [U]]; longer in PM peak. `High · World input · public/forecast`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P*] own calc on local TLC Parquet copies (trip_time, pickup/dropoff times); time per trip is the denominator of profit per hour; cross-link R10 · Data: TLC trip records (pickup/dropoff times, trip_time) · Model: Not generated directly; harness compares simulated vs empirical duration by OD and hour · [R05-008]</sub>
   * **Back-tests on real policy changes and shocks**: Checks that the sim, calibrated on data from before a real policy change or shock, reproduces the change observed after it. *Values:* Candidate tests: the CBD toll from 5 Jan 2025 (speeds and trips, R10-005); HVFHV lockout rules (R07-030); the distance-factor reset on 13 Mar 2023 (R07-028); storms such as 29 Sep 2023 (R04-025). Pass if the predicted change is within +/-30-50% of the observed one [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P, from list] Congestion-pricing speed regime (R10-005); yellow trips +19% from 2024 to 2025 (R02-033); [design]. · Data: NYC TLC trip records before and after each change; NYC DOT traffic speeds; MTA CBD toll data (data.ny.gov) · Model: Calibrate on the pre-period, switch the rule or shock on, and compare simulated vs observed changes in trips, speeds, waits and pay. · [G1-031]</sub>
   * **Fare and pay engine validation**: Recompute fare components and driver pay for replayed trips from the rule tables and the simulated route, and compare them with the recorded TLC fields against a threshold. *Values:* Target: median absolute error under 5% per trip for base fare and driver pay, and an exact match for fixed fees (congestion surcharge, airport fee, black car fund) [design].. `Med · Benchmark setting · hidden`
      * <sub>Evidence: [P] TLC files itemise fares (schemas re-read by fact-check from local 2023 files): HVFHV base_passenger_fare, tolls, bcf, sales_tax, congestion_surcharge, airport_fee, tips, driver_pay; yellow fare_amount, extra, mta_tax, tip_amount, tolls_amount, improvement_surcharge, congestion_surcharge, Airport_fee. R12-066 checks only pay per hour. · Data: NYC TLC yellow and HVFHV trip records · Model: Run a sample of trips through the fare engine, using the rule vintage for the date (R07-065) and the simulated route. Report error by component and fail CI above the threshold. · [G2-028]</sub>

## Still missing or open

- Driver taxes: after-tax profit (15.3% self-employment tax, IRS mileage deduction) has no variable of its own; it survives only as an optional note in R07-066, which is merged into R08-067.
- Macro-economy and labour-market regime: in a recession more people drive for the apps and discretionary trips fall. No variable covers this; R02-033 only covers year-to-year drift in the data.
- Public-health or pandemic shock scenario (a COVID-style demand collapse, rider limits, partition rules). It appears only as a data-era caveat.
- Price of the main alternatives: subway and bus fares and fare changes (OMNY cap, fare-free bus pilots), LIRR/Metro-North CityTicket, and Citi Bike or e-scooter prices. R05-025 lists the modes but not what they cost.
- Other ride-hail competitors are not named in supply: a Revel-style all-EV service with employee drivers, black-car bases, and outer-borough dollar vans (G1-013 may cover the vans).
- Rider-matching preferences and anti-discrimination rules: women-rider preference features, and a ban on refusing or rating riders by neighbourhood, race or disability. Only the R12-059 scoring panel touches this.
- Large groups split across several cars (one party requesting two or more cars instead of an XL), which changes how demand turns into requests.
- Platform algorithm drift: pay or dispatch changes partway through the period, and pay personalised per driver. G1-001 covers how much pay varies per offer, not how it changes over time.
- Local market power: the agent's share of idle supply in each zone and hour. R11-056 and R06-061 judge impact citywide, but surge and ETAs are set per zone, and 30-50 cars at a venue or airport can dominate it.
- Own-app density economies: pickup ETA falls roughly with the square root of idle-car density, so profit per car can rise before saturation. R11-030 assumes a steady fall, and the N ladder {1, 10, 100} in R11-067 needs intermediate values.
- One toll-liability rule for loaded, return and repositioning legs. It must reconcile R08-050 (the app rider pays the return toll on out-of-city trips) with R01-021 and R07-038 (empty-return tolls are unreimbursed). The driver's real cost is the deadhead time and energy back, plus tolls on voluntary repositioning.
- Information footing of the baselines: R12-052 (OR planner) and any R12-051 heuristic that reads the generator's expected rates use insider information under R12-021. Give them the same L1/L2 data as the model, or label them semi-oracles.
- Unserved-demand uplift as an explicit calibrated parameter (R05-066's '5-20%' design guess), varied in the G2-032 parameter-uncertainty runs and fitted on request_datetime per G2-021.
- A single ODD object (polygon, hours, road classes, river crossings, weather limits, endpoint rule). The parts exist as R01-008, R07-060, R02-061, R04-039, R10-023, G2-036 and G1-038, but nothing ties them together.
- Impact ratings to fix after the merges: weather speed effect (R04-008, which absorbs R10-006) should be medium (high in snow, low in rain). Cold-weather EV range (R08-010, which absorbs R04-037) should be medium. For the robotaxi track, the remote-assistance rate (R09-048) and pickup-point placement (R01-033) should be medium. R11-042's corrected 83-92% availability replaces the 75% in R09-044.
